# MUTCD 11th Edition — full reading log

## Method (the engineer's instruction)

1. Read the whole manual in order, from the PDF itself.
2. For every table: number, caption, every column heading, superscripts on headings,
   every value, every footnote. For every figure: caption and notes.
3. Read each section as a whole and write down what it means.
4. **Change nothing in the graph while reading.** Flag every finding here.
5. Only when the whole manual is read: look across all flags for contradictions,
   then decide how they touch the graph.

## Reading principle (set by the engineer)

The manual is the authority. It is not judged, graded or corrected here.
- **Errors** are recorded only for *our* material: the extracted chunks, the tables file, the tagger and the graph.
- Where provisions seem to differ, first apply the manual's own rules for reading them together:
  text governs figures, tables and their notes (1A.04 ¶5); a Standard governs Guidance and Options (1C.01);
  a specific provision governs a general one (2A.15 ¶1, "except as otherwise provided");
  a table's own heading states its scope (Table 4C-4).
- Only if those rules do not settle it, record a **question about how the provisions are applied together**,
  addressed to the engineer — never a claim that the manual is wrong.

## Flag types

| Code | Meaning |
|---|---|
| DATA | the extracted data differs from the printed manual |
| RULE | a reading rule (how the manual's language works) — seen holding here |
| BREAK | a place where a reading rule does **not** hold |
| MEAN | something the manual means that the graph cannot yet represent |
| GAP | the manual does not decide |
| TERM | a term: where it is defined, or that it is not defined |
| X | a cross-reference worth recording (section, table, figure, outside document) |

## Register of reading rules

Each rule records where it came from, where it has been seen holding, and where it
breaks. A rule with a BREAK entry must not be applied everywhere.

| # | Rule | From | Holds at | Breaks at |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | "shall not exceed N" / "should not be more than N" is a maximum | 4A.07 | | |
| R2 | A sentence opening "No …" negates its comparison ("No part … more than 4 inches" = at most 4) | 4A.07 | | |
| R3 | "equals or exceeds N" is at least N (inclusive) | 4C.04, 4C.08 | | |
| R4 | In "N … or M …" the second number shares the first's comparison | 4C.04 | | |
| R5 | A number's unit limits what it can measure (mph → a speed; hours → a time; vph → hourly volume) | 4C.02, 4C.04 | | |
| R6 | "for each of any N hours" / "the same N hours" is a time window | 4C.02, 4C.09 | | |
| R7 | "within N feet of X" is a distance from X, at most N | 4C.10 | | |
| R8 | A list's opening sentence carries the rule, the authority, and any/all ("either of", "both of", "one of", "all of") | 4A.08, 4A.10 | | |
| R9 | When the opening does not say, the joining word at the end of an item ("; or", "; and") says any/all | 4A.08 | | |
| R10 | A sentence ending "the following …:" followed by "1. 2. 3." is a list inside a list, with its own any/all | 4C.04, 4C.08 | | |
| R11 | A note under a table or figure is filed with it, not as a paragraph | 4C.02 | | |
| R12 | "Items C, D, and E in Paragraph 3 … are not relevant when …" is an exception that names the rule it relaxes | 4B.05 | | |
| R13 | A Title-Case number-word joined to a unit and followed by a capitalised word is a name ("Eight-Hour Vehicular Volume") | 4C.01 | | |
| R14 | "For example …" sentences give examples, not thresholds | 4C.10 | | |
| R15 | "Table X may be used in place of Table Y" records which replaces which; "respectively" pairs in order | 4C.03–4C.08 | | |
| R16 | A table's own heading states its scope (Table 4C-4: "Community less than 10,000 population or above 40 mph on major street") | 4C.08 (engineer) | | |
| R17 | "unless otherwise directed by …" / "except as such movement is modified by …" is an open-ended override by other devices | 4A.03, 4A.04 | | |
| R18 | "the rules applicable after making a stop at a STOP sign" point outside the manual (UVC / state law) | 4A.03–4A.05 | | |
| R19 | A minimum/maximum word sets the comparison only when it sits on the same quantity noun | 4C.01 | | |
| R20 | A sentence with no shall/should/may takes its paragraph's heading (1C.01) | 4C.01 | | |

## Changes already made to the graph before this method — PROVISIONAL

These were applied manual-wide from Part 4 evidence only. Each stays provisional
until the whole manual has been read and its rule has no BREAK entry.

- Opening sentences of lists restored (R8, R9); table and figure notes refiled (R11)
- Negated limits (R1, R2); "equals or exceeds" (R3); paired values (R4)
- Unit decides kind (R5); time windows (R6); "within N of" (R7)
- Lists inside lists (R10); backward exceptions (R12); names (R13); examples (R14)
- Which-replaces-which (R15); open overrides (R17); outside rules (R18); min/max (R19)
- Plain bugs (not language rules): "%" pattern, empty item-letter nodes, plural table
  and figure references, sentence splitter carrying item numbers

## Flags found before this log (Part 2C, 4A–4C, exception rulings)

- MEAN 4C.01: warrants are never sufficient; ¶6 and ¶7 are necessary conditions; ¶20 permits installing with no warrant met after a documented study.
- MEAN 4C.02–4C.10: several warrants depend on the whole set ("the only warrant met", "none of the other eight").
- MEAN 4C.06: Warrant 5 compares two measured counts (adequate gaps vs minutes).
- MEAN 4C.05/4C.08: which chart/table replaces which (now recorded, R15).
- GAP Table 4C-7: rows only for 0%, 2%, 4%, 6%+ buses.
- GAP Table 6B-4: 41–44 mph.
- TERM "low-speed", "high-speed", "built-up area", "isolated community", "adequate trial", "unusual cases", "adequate gaps": not defined.
- TERM "average day" (1C.02 #18) and "short duration" (6N.01) ARE defined.
- TERM "lagging", "cycle-by-cycle": engineering usage, not defined in the manual.
- DATA 8B.05, 8C.05, 8E.10, 9D.10: sections misfiled under the preceding section (missing PDF bookmarks).
- DATA 2B.26 / 2H.14: figure notes filed as paragraph 1.
- 3B.12: ¶6 says "operating speeds less than 25 mph" where its twin ¶13 says "low-speed".
- Exceptions: 45 rulings recorded in EXCEPTION_RULINGS.md / rulings.py.

# Reading, in order

## Front matter (PDF 1–41)

- X: the PDF is the **11th Edition with Revision 1 (December 2025)** incorporated; revised text is marked "Rev. 1". The paper and the data say "11th Edition" — state the revision.
- List of tables: **68 tables**, matching the tables file. Manual page = PDF page − 41.

## Chapter 1A — General (PDF 42–44)

**1A.01** Purpose and uniformity. Applies whatever the ownership or funding (¶5). Assumes a trained user (¶6).
**1A.02** What a traffic control device is. Islands, curbs, speed humps, rumble strips are **not** devices; fencing, lighting, barriers, attenuators appear only for context. Items A–E list things in the right-of-way that are not devices (maintenance markers, enforcement aids including speed-measurement markings and red-light indicator lights, utility markers, non-traffic ordinances, civic notices).
**1A.03** Road users, including driving automation systems; vulnerable users per 23 U.S.C. 148(a).
**1A.04** ¶2 **Standard**: decisions the manual leaves to judgment shall be made by an engineer or someone under an engineer's supervision, by engineering judgment or study as the provision requires. ¶5: tables, figures and notes may be Standard, Guidance, Option or Support — **their class comes from the text that refers to them**. ¶6–¶7 Guidance: numerals, names and route numbers on device images in figures are examples only unless the text requires them. ¶9–¶10: citation structure; paragraphs are numbered **without counting figure or table text**; items can be nested ("Item A.1 of Paragraph 1").
**1A.05** ¶1 **Standard**: the latest *Standard Highway Signs* and the color specifications (23 CFR 655 Subpart F appendix) are part of the manual where referenced. Other publications are not regulatory. Not a design manual.
**1A.06** UVC: a model code, not enforceable; road-user obligations belong in state statute (Guidance).

Flags
- RULE R11 **holds, by the manual's own statement** (1A.04 ¶10).
- RULE R10 holds: nested items exist by design (1A.04 ¶10, "Item A.1").
- RULE R14 holds and is **wider than found**: every numeral on a device image in a figure is an example unless the text requires it (1A.04 ¶6).
- RULE R18 holds (1A.06).
- RULE **R21 (new)**: the authority of a table, figure or note comes from the text that refers to it, not from its own wording (1A.04 ¶5). **The graph gives notes their own authority — conflicts with R21.**
- MEAN: every delegated-judgment answer should say the decision is an **engineer's** (1A.04 ¶2 Standard), not just "the agency's".
- X / GAP: *Standard Highway Signs* is part of the manual by reference; sign layouts, alphabets and dimensions there cannot be checked from this corpus.
- MEAN: unnumbered sub-chapter headings group Sections (e.g. 2B.06–2B.20 "Signing for Right-of-Way at Intersections"); the graph does not record them.

## Chapter 1B — Legal requirements (PDF 45–53)

**1B.01** ¶1 Standard: the MUTCD is the national standard (23 CFR 655 Subpart F). ¶2 Standard: applies to **all** of A–E — public or private streets open to public travel; site roadways open to public travel (defined here, ¶2B); public toll roads; private toll roads open to the public; grade crossings of publicly-owned roads. ¶3 Standard: does **not** apply to A–C — private gated properties restricted at all times; grade crossings of privately-owned roads; **parking areas and their driving aisles**, public or private.
**1B.02** ¶2 Standard: state MUTCDs/supplements conform within 2 years of a Final Rule. ¶3: agency policies that modify provisions **are supplements**.
**1B.03** ¶1, ¶3–¶5 Standard: substantial conformance; new or reconstructed devices meet the new edition; Federal-aid projects before opening; existing non-compliant devices upgraded systematically. ¶7 Standard: target compliance dates are **Table 1B-1**. ¶8 Standard: devices not adopted in the manual are **prohibited** unless 1B.04–1B.08 are followed. ¶9: **absence of a prohibition is not permission.** ¶12 Standard / ¶13 Option: replace in kind by engineering judgment if A **and/or** B.
**Table 1B-1** (Target Compliance Dates). Columns: MUTCD Section(s) · Subject Area · Specific Provision · Compliance Date. Rows: 2B.64 ¶14 Weight Limit — 18 Jan 2029; 2C.25 ¶1 W12-2 — 18 Jan 2029; 2C.25 ¶8 W12-2a/2b — 18 Jan 2029; 3A.05 retroreflectivity method — **6 Sep 2026**; 8B.16 ¶3, ¶7 — 18 Jan 2029; 8D.09–8D.12 — 18 Jan 2034. No footnotes. Marked Rev. 1.
**1B.04** Interpretations: FHWA issues them; request contents A–D (Guidance).
**1B.05** ¶2 Standard: no non-compliant device without FHWA approval to experiment. ¶10 Standard: request contains **all** of A–K. ¶12: semi-annual reports by 1 Aug (Jan–Jun) and 1 Feb (Jul–Dec). ¶13: two missed reports terminate the experiment; restore within 3 months. ¶14: final report within 3 months; none and no notice within 6 months → terminated; restore within 3 months. **Figure 1B-1**: flow of the experimentation process (Support).

Flags
- MEAN: **applicability (1B.01 ¶2–¶3) is a condition on every rule**. A question about a parking area, a private gated property, or a private-road grade crossing is outside the manual. The graph does not gate on this.
- RULE **R22 (new)**: silence is not permission (1B.03 ¶9); devices or uses not provided for are prohibited unless via 1B.04–1B.08 (¶8). **The certificate's STANDARD_SILENT must never be read as "allowed".** For "may I use X?" questions where the manual is silent, the honest answer is "not provided for; requires FHWA interpretation, experimentation or interim approval".
- MEAN / X: state supplements and agency policies can modify provisions (1B.02 ¶3). An answer from the national MUTCD may differ from the state's (e.g. Texas MUTCD). Not in the corpus.
- RULE R21 holds: Table 1B-1's authority comes from 1B.03 ¶7 ("shall be as shown").
- RULE R9 holds: "and/or" at the end of 1B.03 ¶13 item A reads as ANY.
- X: Table 1B-1's 3A.05 compliance date (6 Sep 2026) has passed as of this reading.
**1B.05 (cont.)** ¶15: an experimental device may stay after the experiment if under consideration for interim approval. ¶16 Standard: legally-binding public-domain statement (no patent, trademark, copyright; see 1D.06). ¶17: individual components may be protected; the device and its function may not.
**1B.06** Changes: request contents A–C (Guidance). **Figure 1B-2**: process for incorporating new devices; its "*" note: interim approval is considered when the benefit is substantial and national and waiting would not be in the public interest.
**1B.07** Interim approvals: optional use only, **no new mandate** (¶3). ¶9 Standard: written FHWA permission before use. ¶10 Standard: request contains **all** of A–D; **D has sub-items 1–2** introduced by "An agreement to:". ¶11 Option: statewide request. ¶12–¶13 Standard: users under a statewide approval inform the State and keep location records.
**1B.08** ¶2 Standard: requests by e-mail to FHWA, except ¶3 (Option: by mail).

Flags
- BREAK **R10 trigger too narrow**: 1B.07 ¶10 item D opens a sub-list with "An agreement to:", not "the following:". The idea holds; the trigger must be "an item ending in ':' followed by numbered sub-items".
- X / GAP: **interim approvals** (FHWA memoranda on the MUTCD website) permit optional use of devices not in the manual. Outside the corpus, like official interpretations; an answer may be incomplete without them.
- RULE R22 reinforced: 1B.05 ¶2 Standard — a non-compliant device shall not be used without approval to experiment.

## Chapter 1C — Definitions (PDF 54–71)

**1C.01** Standard — the four headings:
- **Standard**: required, mandatory or prohibitive. *"In limited, location-specific cases, the results of a documented engineering study (see Section 1D.03) might indicate a deviation from one or more requirements of a Standard provision to be appropriate."* Bold type. Uses "shall"; **never "should" or "may"**. Sometimes modified by Options.
- **Guidance**: recommended; deviation allowed by engineering judgment or study. Italic. Uses "should"; never "shall" or "may".
- **Option**: permissive; no requirement or recommendation; may modify a Standard or Guidance. Uses "may"; never "shall" or "should".
- **Support**: informational; none of the three verbs.

**1C.02** ¶1 Standard: words not defined here or elsewhere in the manual take the meaning in the **UVC, the AASHTO Transportation Glossary, or other appropriate publications**. ¶2: the manual's definitions govern over other sources. ¶3: definitions 1–294 follow.

Definitions 1–43 read. Noted:
- #21 Beacon, with types (a)–(g); (f) Stop Beacon supplements only STOP, DO NOT ENTER, WRONG WAY — consistent with 4A.04 C.4.
- #34 Center line markings "need not be at the geometrical center".
- #37 Circular intersection includes roundabouts, rotaries and traffic circles.
- #39 Clear Storage Distance — **"when used in Part 8"**; contains its own "shall" (the 6-ft distance shall be measured perpendicular to the nearest rail).

Flags
- MEAN **every Standard carries an implied exception**: a documented engineering study under 1D.03 may justify a location-specific deviation (1C.01 A). The certificate for a Standard must say so; "required" is never unconditional.
- RULE **R23 (new)**: heading and verb agree — Standard/shall, Guidance/should, Option/may, Support/none (1C.01). **Check to run after reading**: any chunk whose label disagrees with its verbs is a DATA flag. The PDF's **bold (Standard) and italic (Guidance)** type can verify every label.
- RULE **R24 (new)**: "shall be permitted to" and "shall not be required to" inside Standards are **permissions**, not obligations (4F.01 F.4, 4C.02 ¶5). The graph reads every "shall" as an obligation. **Conflicts with how STANDARD is used in the graph.**
- TERM **correction**: terms not defined in the manual ("low-speed", "high-speed", "built-up area", "isolated community", …) fall back to the UVC / AASHTO Transportation Glossary / other publications (1C.02 ¶1). Earlier flags saying "not defined anywhere" must read "not defined in the manual".
- TERM #39 is scoped "when used in Part 8", yet 4C.10 ¶3B invokes it for Warrant 9 explicitly ("as defined in Section 1C.02"). The explicit reference carries it into Part 4.
- MEAN definitions can contain rules (#39's "shall"). A definition is not only a meaning.

Definitions 44–112 read. Noted:
- #50 Crosswalk: (a) the unmarked extension of sidewalk lines at an intersection, (b) any marked crossing. "Crosswalk" includes **unmarked** crosswalks.
- #56 Design Vehicle: the longest vehicle permitted **by statute** — outside the manual.
- #58 Detectable: a continuous edge within 6 inches of the surface.
- #60 Detection Plate: **Standards inside a definition** — bottom edge ≤ 2 in above the walkway, top edge ≥ 8 in; same vertical plane as the hand-trailing edge.
- #64 Driving Aisle: exempt from the MUTCD (agrees with 1B.01 ¶3C).
- #76 Engineering Judgment: by a professional engineer or under supervision; **documentation not required**.
- #77 Engineering Study: same; **shall be documented in writing**.
- #83 Expressway (partial access control) / #91 Freeway (full access control).
- #84 Fail-Safe: scoped "when used in Part 8"; contains "shall".
- #86 Flasher: approximately once per second.
- #99 HOV: at least two persons, or more if signs say.
- #103 Highway Traffic Signal: (a)–(d); **(d) a traffic control signal does not include pedestrian hybrid beacons or emergency-vehicle hybrid beacons**; special signals (1)–(7); (4) supplemental near-side faces are not pre-signals.
- #112 Intersection: (b) **a driveway, alley or site-roadway junction is not an intersection unless the public road there is controlled by a device**.

Flags
- MEAN **judgment ≠ study**: the graph's DELEGATED_JUDGMENT conflates them. A provision requiring an engineering **study** requires a written record (#77); one requiring **judgment** does not (#76). The certificate's reason must say which.
- MEAN **scope of "traffic control signal"** excludes hybrid beacons (#103(d)). Rules stated for traffic control signals must not be applied to PHBs or EVHBs.
- MEAN **scope of "intersection"** excludes uncontrolled driveway/alley/site-roadway junctions (#112(b)).
- MEAN definitions can carry Standards (#39, #60, #84, #112(b)); these are rules and need authority STANDARD.

Definitions 113–195 read. Noted:
- #112(c)–(d) Intersection: divided highways are separate intersections if opposing left-turn paths cross and there is sufficient interior storage for the design vehicle (Figure 2A-5); at signals, a median roadway with no stop line, yield line or crosswalk makes one intersection.
- #117 Lane Drop / #119 Lane Reduction: **mutually exclusive by definition**.
- #129 Low-Volume Rural Road: AADT < 400 and outside "built-up or urbanized areas" — uses the undefined "built-up".
- #131 / #139 Major / Minor Street: **by volume** ("normally carrying the higher/lower volume").
- #136 Median excludes turn lanes. #138 Minor Interchange: exit volumes < 100 vpd in the design year → "local".
- #144 Multi-Lane: a multi-lane approach counts **turning lanes**.
- #152 **Opposing** (straight ahead) vs **conflicting** (left or right) traffic.
- #155 Parking Area: a lot or garage separated from the roadway; **on-street spaces are not a parking area**.
- #158 Pathway includes shared-use paths, not sidewalks. #161 Pedestrian includes devices "determined by local law".
- #167 Permissive Mode: CIRCULAR GREEN permits turns "unless otherwise prohibited by another traffic control device" (R17 again).
- #170 Plaque: cannot be used alone; "P" suffix. #187 Professional Engineer: by State licensure law.
- #193 Queue Clearance Time: "when used in Part 8". #195 Quiet Zone: 49 CFR 222.

Flags
- MEAN general vs specific: #144 counts turning lanes in a multi-lane approach; 4C.01 ¶9 lets the engineer decide one vs two lanes for warrant analysis. The specific provision governs the warrants. **The graph has no notion of a specific provision governing a general one.**
- MEAN scope: 1B.01 ¶3C (parking areas excluded) + #155 → on-street parking **is** covered.
- RULE R17 holds (#167).
- TERM "built-up" appears inside a definition (#129) and is itself undefined in the manual.

Definitions 196–271 read. Noted:
- #196 **Rail Traffic**: trains, locomotives, other equipment, and LRT in exclusive or semi-exclusive alignments. **LRT in a mixed-use alignment is a vehicle, not rail traffic.**
- #197 Raised Pavement Marker: height "not exceeding approximately 1 inch" (permanent) / 2 inches (temporary flexible).
- #204 Roadway: excludes sidewalk, berm and shoulder; separate roadways are each "a roadway".
- #206 Roundabout: yield at entry, counter-clockwise deflection.
- #210 School: K–12 or as the State defines — consistent with 4C.06 ¶1 ("elementary through high school").
- #212 Semi-Actuated: at least one, not all, phases actuated.
- #216 Serviceable: judgement language ("beyond reasonable repair").
- #224 Sign excludes signals, markings, delineators, channelizing devices.
- #226 Sign Illumination: street or highway lighting **shall not** count (Standard in a definition).
- #227 Legend excludes the border.
- #247 Site Roadways Open to Public Travel: excludes gated-at-all-times roads, private grade crossings, parking areas and driving aisles (agrees with 1B.01).
- #248 Special-Purpose Road: "a low-volume, low-speed road" — two terms undefined in the manual.
- #262 TTC zone: work zone **or incident**.

Flags
- BREAK **speed category "SPEED_TRAIN"** (graph): treats all LRT speeds as rail speeds "never comparable with a highway speed". #196 says mixed-use LRT is a vehicle. The category must distinguish rail traffic (trains; LRT in exclusive or semi-exclusive alignment) from mixed-use LRT.
- BREAK **R1 with "approximately"**: "not exceeding approximately 1 inch" is an approximate maximum; the tagger keeps only "approximately" and loses "at most" (#197).
- TERM "low-volume" in #248 is not tied to #129's AADT < 400; "low-speed" undefined in the manual.
- RULE: #204 confirms the 9D.06 ruling (signs "used on roadways" do not cover shared-use paths).

Definitions 272–295 read (**295 definitions in total**). Noted:
- #275 Train: yields right-of-way by law at highway-rail grade crossings. #277 Traveled Way: excludes shoulders, berms, sidewalks, parking lanes.
- #278 Turn Bay: a through lane becoming a turn lane is a **lane drop, not a turn bay** (third term fenced off from lane drops).
- #280 Uncontrolled Approach: not controlled by a signal, hybrid beacon, STOP or YIELD sign.
- #282 Urban Street / #208 Rural Highway: **described in words only, no numbers** ("relatively low speeds", "frequent intersections").
- #284 Vehicle: mixed-use LRT is a vehicle (agrees with #196).
- #290 Warrant (as recorded in Part 4).

**1C.03** Standard: acronyms 1–62, including **L = taper length**, **N = one line segment plus one gap**, PRT, SPF, TA, VPH.

Flags
- DATA: all 295 definitions are in the chunks; the **graph's glossary parser drops one** (code, not data).
- MEAN "urban street" and "rural highway" are classifications by judgement, not thresholds; 2C.06 ¶5 ("urban streets") therefore needs a judgement input.
- RULE: 1C.03 gives the symbols L and N used in Part 6 and Part 3 formulas — the COMPUTED pieces should resolve their variables here.

## Chapter 1D — Provisions for devices in general (PDF 72–79)

**1D.01** ¶3 Guidance: a device should (A–E) fulfil a need, command attention, convey a clear meaning, command respect, give time to respond. ¶6 Standard: devices on site roadways have the same shape, color and meaning **"except as provided otherwise elsewhere in this Manual"**.
**1D.02** Standard: responsibility rests with the agency / official / private owner; **all regulatory devices shall be supported by laws, ordinances or regulations** (¶2); devices placed only as authorized; non-traffic signs must not interfere.
**1D.03** ¶2: study and judgement are fundamental; most device selection is **not** required by a Standard; Standards may require study or judgement "by explicit language … **or by implication**"; deviation allowed only where compliance is **impossible or impracticable** at the location, **fully documented through an engineering study**. ¶3 **Standard: the Manual "shall not be a legal requirement for their installation."** ¶4: the engineer develops the solution; **the agency makes the final decision to implement**. ¶5 Guidance: device decisions by study or judgement; the Manual is not a substitute for judgement. ¶7: seek engineering help if none on staff.
**1D.04** ¶2 **Option: minor design modifications except to symbols and colors**, by study or judgement, keeping essential appearance; ¶3 Guidance: only for demonstrated need in unusual circumstances; ¶4 example W1-10.
**1D.05** Standard: 13 colors A–M (coral and light blue reserved); used only as prescribed; tolerances in 23 CFR 655 appendix.
**1D.06** Standard: public domain, except Interstate Shield, 511 pictograph, National Scenic Byway graphic, FHWA-owned items; components may be protected.
**1D.07** Standard: no advertising on devices or supports; Acknowledgment, Specific Service and TODS signs are not advertising.
**1D.08** ¶1 Standard: use Table 1D-1 abbreviations. ¶2 Standard: Table 1D-2 only on portable CMS unless asterisked. ¶3 Guidance: prompt words. ¶4 **Standard: Table 1D-3 abbreviations shall not be used.** ¶5–¶6 Guidance: one abbreviation per jurisdiction; no punctuation.
**Table 1D-1** Acceptable Abbreviations — two parts: General (Word Message · Standard Abbreviation) and Days of the Week (Day · Standard Abbreviation). Footnotes: \* only in a roadway name (see Table 2D-3); \*\* only within a proper name; \*\*\* TUES/THURS **may** be TUE/THU on a CMS when rewording cannot fit; Note: all upper-case allowed on low-resolution CMS. Five entries defer to Table 1D-2 (Bridge, Interstate, Lane, State route, US route).
**Table 1D-2** (2 sheets) PCMS-only abbreviations — columns: Word Message · Standard Abbreviation · Prompt Word Preceding · Prompt Word Following · Example. Footnotes: \* with its prompt word, usable on devices other than PCMS; \*\* a space and no hyphen before the route number. Note: see Chapter 2L.
**Table 1D-3** Unacceptable Abbreviations — columns: Abbreviation · Intended Word · Common Misinterpretation. 11 rows: ACC, CLRS, DLY, FDR, L, LT, PARK, POLL, RED, STAD, WRNG.
**1D.09** ¶1 Standard: all necessary devices in place before opening. ¶2 Option: TTC devices in place of permanent ones not yet installed. ¶6 Guidance: remove unnecessary devices; good condition is no reason to defer.
**1D.10** Maintenance (Guidance). **1D.11** Standard: crashworthiness where various Sections require it; MASH 2016 (external).

Flags
- MEAN **"required" is not "legally required"** (1D.03 ¶3). The certificate's REQUIRED must mean "required by an MUTCD Standard". Tension, recorded not resolved: many Standards say "shall be installed".
- **Correction to the 1A.04 flag**: two decisions — the engineering determination (engineer, 1A.04 ¶2; 1D.03 ¶4–¶5) and the decision to implement (agency, 1D.03 ¶4). The answer should name the right one.
- MEAN every Standard can be deviated from where compliance is impossible or impracticable, with a documented engineering study (1D.03 ¶2, restating 1C.01 A). Every design provision can be minorly modified except symbols and colors (1D.04 ¶2).
- MEAN open exception lists ("except as provided otherwise elsewhere in this Manual", 1D.01 ¶6): exceptions must be sought manual-wide.
- MEAN ambiguous abbreviations resolved only by footnotes: **St** (Saint vs Street), **MIN** (Minimum vs Minute), **AM** (radio vs morning); **Center** → Ctr / CNTR; **Crossing** → X-ING / XING / RR XING.
- **R21 refine**: a note with its own verb takes its class from that verb (Table 1D-1 \*\*\* "may" is an Option within a Standard table; 1C.01 forbids "may" in Standards). R21 decides only for notes without a verb.
- Scope, not contradiction: Table 1D-3 forbids "L" (Left) on devices; 1C.03 defines L = taper length for the Manual's text.
- X: legal basis for regulatory devices (1D.02 ¶2), crashworthiness (MASH), color tolerances (23 CFR 655) — outside the corpus.

**Part 1 complete.**

# PART 2 — SIGNS

## Chapter 2A — General (PDF 80–105)

**2A.01** Functions of signs; list of Chapters 2B–2N. ¶4 Guidance: permanent signs should not be used to confirm rules of the road; temporary signs for education campaigns.
**2A.02** ¶3 Guidance: signs only where justified by engineering judgement or studies (1D.03). ¶6 Standard: each standard sign only for its prescribed purpose; necessary signs in place before opening; condition signs removed when conditions cease.
**2A.03** Standard: regulatory / warning / guide defined by function.
**2A.04** Design. ¶3: detailed drawings are in *Standard Highway Signs*. ¶6–¶14 Standard: legend = words, symbols, arrows; uniformity of shape, color, dimensions, legend, letter style, border, illumination; minor changes allowed to symbol orientation, border width, word layout — **shapes and colors as indicated**; symbols unmistakably similar or mirror images; standard wording where applicable; no word legend in place of a symbol, no alternative design where a standard sign applies — each "except as provided in this Manual". ¶15 Option: special word-legend signs by engineering judgement, **no experimentation needed** (unlike colors or symbols). ¶18 Standard: no phone numbers, URLs, e-mail, hashtags, QR codes on signs, except ¶19 (Option: faces not visible to drivers — pedestrians, parked vehicles, driving automation systems) and maintenance items (¶21–¶23). ¶20 Standard: pictographs only where provided; official seals; no mascots. ¶23 Standard: maintenance lettering ≤ ¾ of border width, or ≤ 1.75 in within 2 in of the edge.
**2A.05** Shapes. ¶1 Standard: shapes per **Table 2A-1**, exclusively, unless otherwise provided. ¶2 Standard: Crossbuck exclusive to R15-1, not obscured. ¶3 Guidance: do not obscure exclusive shapes by back-mounted signs; methods A–C. ¶4 Guidance: lateral constraints — methods A–E (angle up to 45°, relocate with distance plaque, reduce size plus duplicate, asymmetric mounting). ¶5 Option: vertical rectangle for horizontal-rectangle legends; horizontal rectangle overhead. ¶8 Standard: no cutting of shapes. ¶9 Option: "Where the methods described in **Paragraph 3** … are impracticable", warning legend in a vertical rectangle.
**Table 2A-1** Use of Sign Shapes — columns Shape · Signs. Octagon\* Stop R1-1\*\*; downward triangle\* Yield R1-2\*\*; Circle\* W10-1\*\*; Pennant\* W14-3\*\*; upward Pentagon\* School S1-1 (squared bottom corners)\*\* and County Route M1-6 (tapered lower sides)\*\*; Crossbuck\* R15-1\*\*; Diamond — warning series; Rectangle incl. square — regulatory, guide\*\*\*, warning; Trapezoid\* — Rec & Cultural Interest guide series, National Forest Route M1-1\*\*. Footnotes: \* shape limited exclusively to the sign(s); \*\* sign shall be exclusively the shape shown; \*\*\* guide series includes general service, specific service, TODS, general information, rec & cultural, emergency management. Note: standardized designs not modified to another shape except as provided.
**2A.06** Colors. ¶1 Standard: per Sections; coordinates in 23 CFR 655. ¶2 Standard: no color gradients except 2J.03 business panels. ¶4: "white" includes silver retroreflective. ¶7 Option: fluorescent red, yellow, green, orange. (Table 2A-2 next.)
**2A.07** ¶1: *Standard Highway Signs* gives sizes. ¶2 Standard: size tables apply unless engineering judgement determines otherwise; except ¶3, never below the minimum; **Minimum-column sizes smaller than Conventional Road only on "low-speed roadways, alleys, site roadways open to public travel, and on low-volume rural roads with operating speeds of 30 mph or less; and only where the reduced legend size would be adequate … or where physical conditions preclude the use of larger sizes."**

Flags

The graph cannot hold sentence-internal and/or structure. "30 mph or less" attaches only to low-volume rural roads.

**Table 2A-2** Common Uses of Sign Colors (read from the printed page; the text layer loses the grid).
Columns: Type of Sign · Legend (Black, Green, Red, White, Yellow, Orange, Fluorescent Yellow-Green, Fluorescent Pink) · Background (Black, Blue, Brown, Green, Orange¹, Red¹, White, Yellow¹, Purple, Fluorescent Yellow-Green, Fluorescent Pink). Superscript ¹ on **column headings** (column-scoped).

**2A.07 (cont.)** ¶3 Option: alleys — height and width may be reduced up to 6 in. ¶4–¶5 Guidance: freeway sizes on higher-speed conventional roads; oversized sizes by judgement. ¶6 Guidance: except ¶7, increases in 6-in increments. ¶7 Standard: never above a prescribed maximum. ¶8 Standard: standard shapes and colors whatever the size; proportions as practicable. ¶9 Guidance: scale plaques with signs.
**2A.08** Word messages. Standard: horizontal, left to right (¶1); standard wording (¶2); Standard Alphabets (¶3); place names mixed case, other legends upper case (¶4); lower-case loop height = **¾ of the initial capital** (¶5); no stretched letters (¶6); fractions **1.5 × numeral height** (¶14); distances in fractions of a mile, not decimals (¶15). Guidance: **1 in of letter height per 30 ft of legibility distance** (¶8); minimal abbreviations; no punctuation; solidus only for fractions; initials spaced ½–¾ of letter height; "I-50". ¶12 Option: second language only on faces not visible to drivers. ¶19: loop height defined.
**2A.09** Symbols. Standard: unmistakably similar; a category's symbol not used for another category except as authorized; rec & cultural symbols never outside rec areas and never on regulatory or warning signs. Option: mirror images; educational plaques may stay while serviceable. Guidance: educational plaques during transition.
**2A.10** Borders. Standard: border same color as legend unless otherwise provided; rounded corners except STOP. Guidance: 30-in light-background signs ½–¾ in border, ½ in from edge; light border 1 in; ≤ major-letter stroke width; signs > 72 × 120 in: 2 in; very large signs 2.5 in, ≤ 3 in.
**2A.11** Enhanced conspicuity (Option, A–…); **Figure 2A-1** examples A–G (NEW plaque, flags, W16-18P, retroreflective strips, beacon, LEDs in border).

## Chapter 2B — Regulatory signs, barricades, gates (PDF 106–185)

Chapter opens with a Subchapter and Section Organization (unnumbered sub-chapters: General; Signing for Right-of-Way at Intersections 2B.06–2B.20; Speed Limit; Movement and Lane Control; Passing/Keep Right/Slow Traffic; Selective Exclusion; DNE/WW/One Way; Parking/Standing/Stopping; Pedestrian; Traffic Signal; Road Closed and Weight Limit; Other; Barricades and Gates).
**2B.01** Standard: regulatory signs inform of selected laws and their applicability; at or near where regulations apply; retroreflective or illuminated.
**2B.02** Standard: rectangular unless designated otherwise; per *Standard Highway Signs*; LED part-time prohibitory messages approximate the red circle and diagonal; white LED symbol on black.
**2B.03** ¶1 Standard: minimum sizes per **Table 2B-1** except 2A.07. ¶3 Standard: multi-lane conventional roads — Multi-Lane column, except ¶5 and ¶6. ¶4 Standard: low-volume rural roads ≤ 30 mph operating — the **minimum** size is the Minimum column. ¶5 Option: posted ≤ 35 mph on multi-lane, other than STOP — Single Lane column. ¶6 Option: left-side duplicate, other than STOP — Single Lane for both. ¶7–¶8 Guidance: ramp sizes by mainline classification / Single Lane or Multi-Lane.
**Table 2B-1** Regulatory Sign and Plaque Sizes, 6 sheets (data: 6 records). Columns: Sign or Plaque · Designation · Section · Conventional Road (Single Lane, Multi-Lane) · Expressway · Freeway · Minimum · Oversized. Read sheets 1–6. Noted: STOP — no Freeway size; YIELD Freeway 60×60×60; WRONG WAY Expressway 36×24 (smaller than multi-lane conventional 42×30); Movement Prohibition R3-1…27 24×24 / 36×36. Footnote **\* → "See Table 9A-1 for minimum size required for signs on bicycle facilities"** (on Minimum-column cells such as STOP 30×30\*, R4-1 18×24\*). Note 1: larger signs may be used when appropriate.

Flags
- MEAN "the minimum size … shall be as shown in the Minimum column" (2B.03 ¶4) is a **floor**, not an instruction to use that size; 2A.07 ¶2 separately restricts when Minimum-column sizes may be used. Consistent, but easily misread.
- MEAN a footnote can be a **pointer to another table** (Table 2B-1 \* → Table 9A-1), not a condition on the cell's value.
- (Earlier finding stands: 2B.03 ¶3's list omits ¶4, inserted when Part 5 was dissolved.)
**2B.04** STOP. Standard: STOP sign when a full stop is always required (¶1); no secondary legends (¶2); never on a CMS (¶3); ALL-WAY plaque (white on red) under each STOP where all approaches stop (¶4); no 2-WAY/3-WAY/4-WAY plaques (¶5). ¶7 Guidance: W4-4aP / W4-4bP where STOP controls all but one approach, unless that approach is one-way. ¶8 Option: EXCEPT RIGHT TURN plaque if an **engineering study** finds special geometry and volumes. **Figure 2B-1** STOP and YIELD signs and plaques.
**2B.05** YIELD. ¶2 Standard: never on a CMS.
**2B.06** (sub-chapter "Signing for Right-of-Way at Intersections"). ¶2: **roundabouts and traffic circles are not traffic control devices**; conversion criteria are not in the MUTCD. ¶3 Guidance: least restrictive control that is safe and efficient. ¶4: order A–D (none, yield, minor stop, all-way). ¶5 Guidance: factors A–G; **A defines "units/day or units/hour" = total of motor vehicle, bicycle and pedestrian volume.** ¶6 Standard: **YIELD or STOP signs shall not be used for speed control.** ¶8 Standard: **YIELD or STOP signs shall not be used in conjunction with any traffic control signal operation, except A (flashing red at all times), B (minor street or driveway with extremely low conflict potential), or C (channelized turn lane separated by an island and not signal-controlled).** ¶9 Standard: no STOP and YIELD on conflicting or opposing approaches of one unsignalized intersection, except 2B.10 ¶3 A and B. ¶10 Standard: portable or part-time STOP/YIELD only for emergencies and TTC. ¶11 Standard / ¶12 Option: folding STOP signs during power outages.
**2B.07** Minor road — one or more of A–E ("and/or"); equal roads — factors A–C.
**2B.08** Before a more restrictive control — alternatives A–R (O: all-way stop "if the warrant is satisfied").
**2B.09** No control — Guidance: engineering judgement; Option factors A–G (C: < 1,000 units/day or 80 units peak hour; F: angle 90–75°).
**2B.10** Yield control. ¶2 Guidance: **all** of A–F — adequate sight distance; single-lane approaches; C **one of** (1) ≥ 2 correctable crashes in 12 months (from no control), (2) ≤ 2 crashes in 12 months (from stop control); D < 1,800 units/day or 140 peak hour; E angle 90–75°; F local–local or local–collector. ¶3 Option: YIELD when **any** of A–E (second intersection of a divided crossing; **channelized turn lane even if adjacent lanes are signal- or STOP-controlled**; special problems; merges; low-volume rural roads).

Flags
- **Question for the engineer — how are these two read together?** (both verified on the printed pages):
  - **4A.08 ¶6**: "STOP signs shall not be used in conjunction with any **highway** traffic signal operation, except in **either** of the following cases: A … or B …"
  - **2B.06 ¶8**: "YIELD **or** STOP signs shall not be used in conjunction with any **traffic control** signal operation, except … A; B; **or C** (channelized turn lane separated by an island and not controlled by a traffic control signal)."
  - They differ in devices (STOP vs STOP/YIELD), in signal scope (highway traffic signal ⊃ traffic control signal, #103), and in exceptions (A,B vs A,B,C). **A STOP sign on an island-separated, unsignalized channelized turn lane at a signalized intersection is allowed by 2B.06 ¶8C and forbidden by 4A.08 ¶6.** YIELD in that case is consistent (2B.06 C, 2B.10 ¶3B; 4A.08 silent on YIELD).
- DATA check passed: the 4A.08 ¶6 chunk matches the printed page.
- TERM **"units"** defined inside a Guidance item (2B.06 ¶5A): motor vehicles + bicycles + pedestrians. Used by 2B.09 ¶2C, 2B.10 ¶2D (and expected in the all-way warrants). **The tagger has no unit "units"** — these thresholds are not captured.
- MEAN scope: roundabouts are geometry, not devices (2B.06 ¶2); "should I build a roundabout?" is outside the MUTCD.
- RULE R10 holds (2B.10 ¶2 C: "One of the following" inside an ALL list).
**2B.10 (cont.)** ¶4 Guidance: YIELD on minor approaches; equal roads — the approach conflicting most with pedestrian, school, bicycle activity. ¶5 Standard: YIELD at roundabout entrances, never the circulatory roadway. ¶6 Standard: YIELD not on all approaches except at roundabouts.
**2B.11** Minor road stop — Guidance: **one or more** of A–D; B (nested): four-leg ≥ 3 crashes in 12 months **or** ≥ 6 in 36; three-leg ≥ 3 in 12 **or** ≥ 5 in 36; correctable by stop control. ¶2: low-volume rural roads.
**2B.12** All-way stop. ¶1: warrants are not a substitute for judgement; a met warrant "is not conclusive justification to install or not install"; the engineering study is the basis. ¶3 Guidance: study including warrants A–E. ¶4 Option: site roadways — engineering **judgement** suffices. ¶5 **Standard: satisfaction of a warrant shall not in itself require installation.**
**2B.13** Warrant A (Option): four-leg ≥ **five** crashes in 12 months **or** ≥ **six** in 36; three-leg ≥ **four** in 12 **or** ≥ **five** in 36; correctable type.
**2B.14** Warrant B (Option): inadequate sight distance on stop-controlled minor approaches.
**2B.15** Warrant C (Option): interim measure before a signal or before yield control at a circular intersection.
**2B.16** Warrant D (Option): A major-street approaches ≥ **300 units/hour** for each of any 8 hours of a **typical day**; **and** B minor-street ≥ **200 units/hour** for each of any of **the same** 8 hours. ¶2 Option: if the **85th-percentile** approach speed on the major street > 40 mph, "the minimum **vehicular** volume warrants may be reduced to 70 percent of the values given in Items A and B".
**2B.17** Warrant E (Option): other factors, "may include, but are not limited to" A–C.
**2B.18** Placement. Standard: near side, right-hand; Stop/Yield Ahead where visibility is restricted (¶1); as close as practicable (¶2); STOP and YIELD never on the same post (¶3); never two STOP (YIELD) signs on one support facing the same way (¶18). Guidance: ≤ 50 ft from the pavement edge (¶5); only specified plaques (¶6); with a marked crosswalk, in advance of its near edge (¶12; YIELD except at roundabouts and channelized right-turn lanes, ¶13); multi-lane roundabout — left-side YIELD (¶15). Option: TO ONCOMING TRAFFIC, TO TRAFFIC IN CIRCLE, TO ALL LANES plaques; single-lane roundabout left YIELD (¶16); additional signs (¶17); NO MERGE AREA plaque (¶19).

## Chapter 2C — Warning signs and object markers (PDF 186–243)
Chapter opens with its Subchapter and Section Organization (General; Horizontal Alignment 2C.05–2C.13; Vertical Grade; Roadway Geometry; Roadway and Weather; Traffic Control and Intersection; Merging, Two-Way, No Passing; Miscellaneous; Supplemental Plaques 2C.57–2C.69; Object Markers 2C.70–2C.73).
**2C.01** Standard: use of warning signs **based on an engineering study or engineering judgement**; retroreflective or illuminated. Guidance: keep to a minimum; remove seasonal signs when not applicable.
**2C.02** ¶1 Standard: diamond, black on yellow, except ¶2 (Option: larger than Oversized → may be rectangular or square) or as designated. ¶6 Option: minor design changes except symbols — **but W1-10 and W2 series symbols may be modified to match the geometry** (an explicit exception to "symbols not modified", allowed by 2A.04 ¶9). ¶7 Option: new word warning signs. ¶8 Option: pedestrian, bicycle, playground — yellow **or** FYG. ¶9 Standard: school bus and school signs — **FYG required** (7B.01). ¶10 Option: Warning Beacon (4S.03).
**2C.03** ¶1 Standard: sizes per Table 2C-1 except 2A.07. ¶3 Standard: diamond signs on **multi-lane conventional roads with posted speed > 35 mph** ≥ **36 × 36**, except ¶5. ¶4 Standard: plaques not in Table 2C-1 per Table 2C-2. ¶5 Option: left-side duplicate — Single Lane size. ¶6 Option: larger (2A.11). ¶7–¶9 Guidance: ramps at major interchanges — mainline class; other ramps 36 × 36; low-volume rural ≤ 30 mph — Minimum column ("typical size").
**Table 2C-1** Warning Sign and Plaque Sizes, 4 sheets (columns as Table 2B-1). Footnote **\* "The minimum size required for diamond-shaped warning signs facing traffic on multi-lane conventional roads shall be 36 x 36 per Section 2C.03"**; Notes: larger signs when appropriate; width × height in inches. Noted: Hairpin W1-11, 270-degree W1-15, Intersection W2 series, Stop/Yield/Signal Ahead W3-1/2/3 — **Multi-Lane column 30 × 30**; No Passing Zone pennant 48×48×36 / 64×64×48 / 40×40×30.
**Table 2C-2** Minimum Size of Supplemental Warning Plaques (Size of warning sign · Rectangular 1 line / 2 lines · Square · Arrow); notes: larger allowed; W × H inches.
**2C.04** ¶2: PRT defined; **Table 2C-3 "is provided as an aid"**; distances "can be adjusted". ¶3 Guidance: adequate PRT; **apply with engineering judgement**. ¶4–¶5 Guidance: spacing by PRT; periodic day/night evaluation.
**Table 2C-3** (read from the page; data verified correct) Guidelines for Advance Placement. Row key: **"Posted or 85th-Percentile Speed"** 20–85 mph. Condition A² (speed reduction and lane changing in heavy traffic): 225 · 325 · 460 · 565 · 670 · 775 · 885 · 990 · 1,100 · 1,200 · 1,250 · 1,350 · 1,475 · 1,600 ft. Condition B (deceleration to advisory speed) columns 0³, 10⁴–80⁴, e.g. 55 mph: 495 · 275 · 225 · 200 · 125 · N/A⁵ · — ; 85 mph: 1,010 · 825 · 800 · 750 · 675 · 575 · 450 · 300 · 150. ## Chapter 2D — Guide signs, conventional roads (PDF 244–327)
Chapter opens with its Subchapter and Section Organization (General Design 2D.01–2D.08; Route Signs 2D.09–2D.28; Sign Assemblies 2D.29–2D.34; Destination and Distance 2D.35–2D.44; Street Name and Parking 2D.45–2D.48; Freeway Interchange Approach 2D.49–2D.50; Weigh Station etc. 2D.51–2D.54; Other 2D.55–2D.59; Airports 2D.60).
**2D.01** ¶1 Standard: applies to roads other than freeways and expressways, except as otherwise provided. ¶3–¶8: destinations meaningful and on official maps; guide signs on **low-volume roads** mainly to return users to major roads.
**2D.02** ¶2 Standard: white message and border on green, retroreflective, except as provided. ¶4 Standard: **color coding by colored panels, never by different backgrounds** (except as provided). ¶5 Option: letter or numeral on panels. **Figure 2D-1**.
**2D.03** ¶1 Standard: minimum sizes per **Table 2D-1** (except 2A.07). ¶5 Option: reduced letter height, interline and edge spacing where size must be limited. ¶6–¶7 Guidance: do not squeeze letter spacing except for unusual lateral constraints; stay close to the standard design.
**2D.04** Standard: *Standard Highway Signs* alphabets; place names mixed case, loop height ¾ of the capital (repeats 2A.08 ¶5); other legends upper case; no stretched letters; word-length changes only by 2D.03 methods.
**Table 2D-1** sheet 1 (Designation · Section · Conventional Road · Minimum · Oversized): route shields 24×24 (1–2 digits) / 30×24 (3 digits); Forest Route minimum 18×18; M2-2 **60×48\***; D1-1e **Varies×42\***; D1 series "Varies × 18 / 30".

Flags
- TERM "low-volume roads" (2D.01) — a third variant beside "low-volume rural roads" (#129, AADT < 400) and "low-volume local streets" (2C.17); only #129 is defined.
- CHECK passed: 2D.04 ¶2 = 2A.08 ¶5 (¾ ratio); 2A.08 ¶7's pointer to 2D.03 matches 2D.04 ¶4.
**2D.05** Lettering size. ¶2 Standard: layouts per *Standard Highway Signs*. ¶3 **Standard** (except as otherwise provided): post-mounted principal legend **≥ 6 in** capitals (or 6 in / 4.5 in loop); **on low-volume roads with speeds ≤ 25 mph and urban streets with speeds ≤ 25 mph, ≥ 4 in** (4 / 3). ¶4 Standard: overhead ≥ 6 in (6 / 4.5). ¶5–¶6 Guidance: consistent sizes per class; exceed Table 2D-2 where legibility needs it.
**Table 2D-1** sheet 2: footnote **\* "The size shown is for a typical sign. The size should be determined based on the amount of legend"** (a third meaning of \*); notes: larger allowed; W × H inches.
**2D.06** Guidance: ≤ 3 lines of destinations except as provided.
**2D.07** Abbreviations. ¶2 **Standard: NORTH, SOUTH, EAST, WEST not abbreviated as cardinal directions of highways on guide signs.** ¶3–¶5 Guidance: minimal; descriptors per **Table 2D-3**; short descriptors (Table 2D-4) not abbreviated; no punctuation; hyphen not solidus. **Figure 2D-2** notes: cardinal direction "shall not be abbreviated"; descriptors "should be abbreviated"; pre-directional "South" and "Mount" "may be abbreviated"; **"Peak" (no recognizable abbreviation) "shall not be abbreviated"**.
**Table 2D-2** Recommended Minimum Letter and Numeral Sizes by Speed (2 sheets). A Post-mounted (Single-Lane / Multi-Lane × **< 30, 30–40, > 40 mph**) and B Overhead (**< 35, 35–55, > 55 mph**); rows grouped A (advance guide / entrance direction) and B (destination and other). Overhead cells carry **two values — "(min.)" and "(des.)"**. E.g. post-mounted single-lane < 30 mph, B. destination names: capital **4**, lower case **3**. Footnotes: \* "Except as provided otherwise in this Manual" (on the sheet headings) · \*\* minimum listed for 3-digit shields; see SHS · \*\*\* loop height from the capital (2A.08). Notes: inches; Street Name signs → Table 2D-6; 18-in shield not for independent use.
**2D.08** Arrows. Standard: down arrows only over lanes restricted to the destination, one per lane, over the lane centre, and only if every usable lane gets one (¶2–¶3); exit arrows angled to the exit alignment (¶4); Type E arrow only at circular intersections (¶5). Guidance: Type A arrow for destinations (¶6).

Flags
- **Settled by 1C.01 (a Standard governs a recommendation)** — Table 2D-2 recommends sizes within 2D.05 ¶3: 2D.05 ¶3 (Standard) requires **≥ 6 in** principal legend on post-mounted guide signs unless the road is low-volume or urban **with speeds ≤ 25 mph**. Table 2D-2 ("Recommended Minimum", Guidance by 2D.05 ¶6) gives **4 in** for destination names on single-lane roads **< 30 mph**. On a non-low-volume, non-urban road at 26–29 mph the table recommends less than the Standard requires. The bands also differ (≤ 25 vs < 30).
- MEAN cells with **two values** (min. and des.) in Table 2D-2 (overhead sheet).
- MEAN **\*** has a third meaning in Table 2D-1 ("typical size; determine by legend"). Footnote symbols are table-local (see Tables 2B-1, 2C-1).
- R21 tension: Figure 2D-2's note "'Peak' … shall not be abbreviated" states a **Standard not found in the text** (2D.07 ¶3 is Guidance). Per 1A.04 ¶5 the text classifies the figure; the note's own verb says Standard.
- CHECK passed: 2D.07 ¶2 (no abbreviated cardinal directions of highways on guide signs) narrows Table 1D-1 (N, S, E, W acceptable in general) — specific over general, consistent.
- MEAN speed bands differ across tables and text (2D.05: 25; Table 2D-2: 30/40 post-mounted, 35/55 overhead); "speeds" in 2D.05 ¶3 is an unqualified speed.

## CORRECTION — parts not fully read earlier, now read from the page images

The engineer asked whether reading is from the PDF itself. It is: page text is extracted from the PDF by a script, and grid tables are read from page images; chunks and the tables file are opened only afterwards, to check them. Two lapses were found in my own steps and are now fixed:
1. For some size tables I had hidden the value lines and read only row names and footnotes (Table 2B-1 sheets 4–6, Table 2C-1 sheets 2–4, Table 2C-2, Table 2D-1 sheet 2).

**2D.08 (cont.)** Guidance ¶7–¶11, ¶18–¶20: Type A for through arrows beside a group; Type B beside a single destination or horizontal beside a group; Type C in advance of required turns; Type D mainly for non-guide signs; Type E per 2D.26–2D.29. Option ¶12–¶17, ¶22–¶23: A-Extended; Type C for sharp exits and beneath overhead legends over mandatory lanes; **Type D on post-mounted conventional-road signs at lower operating speeds if text ≤ 8 in**; Type E at circular intersections. ¶21 Standard: Type C shaft bent 90° or oblique. ¶24 Guidance: arrowhead width **1.5–1.75 ×** capital height (A, B, C); **≥ 1 ×** (D); **≈ 2 ×** for down arrows. **Figure 2D-3** (note: details in SHS).
**Table 2D-3** (read from the page) Acceptable Abbreviations for Street Name Descriptors — 38 entries in two columns (Descriptor · Standard Abbreviation), e.g. Avenue Ave, Boulevard Blvd, Lane **La, Ln**, Street St, Trail Tr, and the eight directions E, N, NE, NW, S, SE, SW, W marked \*. Footnote \* "For pre-directional or post-directional designations or cardinal orientations, such as E Main St or 3rd St SW".
**Table 2D-4** (read from the page) Street Name Descriptors Not Acceptable for Abbreviation — 24 entries (Alley, Belt, Beltway, Close, Cove, Edge, Gate, Green, Grove, Hill, Loop, Mews, Oval, Pass, Passage, Path, Ridge, Row, Run, Trace, Turn, View, Vista, Walk).
**2D.09** ¶5 Standard: route preference Interstate, U.S., State, county — highest on top or left, **except ¶7** (Option: another order where it better fits road-user expectancy). ¶6 Standard: FHWA approves Interstate numbering.
**2D.10** ¶1 Standard: all numbered routes identified by route signs and plaques, **except as provided in Paragraph 9 of Section 2D.29**. ¶2 Standard: each system's signs only on that system and approaches.
**2D.11** Standard: designs per SHS; Interstate shields on all Interstate routes; **24×24 (1–2 digits) / 30×24 (3 digits)** minimum; M1-1a only in Route Sign assemblies; Off-Interstate Business LOOP / SPUR white on green, never "INTERSTATE"; U.S. route black on white shield in a black rectangle, same sizes; State route signs designed by States with Standard Alphabets; **county routes need a statewide policy and uniform numbering; pentagon, yellow on blue, ≥ 24×24**; lettered routes count as numbered. Guidance ¶12–¶13, ¶16; Option ¶17 (county name may be omitted on guide signs). **Figure 2D-4**.
**2D.12** ¶1 Standard: word plaques ≥ 24×12; Junction and arrow plaques ≥ 21×15; colors match the route sign. ¶2 Guidance / ¶3 Option.

Notes
- Agreement across the manual: Table 2D-1 sizes = 2D.11 ¶3, ¶9, ¶14 and 2D.12 ¶1; County Route pentagon = Table 2A-1; yellow on blue = Table 2A-2; Table 2D-3's Trail, Street, Circle = Table 1D-1.
- Scope keeps two "Lane" entries apart: Table 2D-3 (Lane as a street-name descriptor, La/Ln) and Table 1D-2 (travel lanes on PCMS, LN/LNS).
- Two abbreviations allowed for one word (Lane: La, Ln) → 1D.08 ¶5 (one per jurisdiction) applies.
- CHECK to make when 2D.29 is read: 2D.10 ¶1 points to **¶9** of 2D.29; the earlier exception ruling was recorded against **¶8** of 2D.29. Confirm which paragraph is which on the printed page.
- MEAN relative quantities again (arrowhead widths as multiples of letter height).
**2D.12 (cont.)** ¶4 Standard: combined route/plaque guide sign — green background, white auxiliary legends; plaques never mounted on a guide sign.
**2D.13** Standard: JCT plaque at the top of the Junction assembly, **≥ 21×15**. **2D.14** Combination Junction (Option; Standard: green, white JUNCTION). **Figure 2D-5**.
**2D.15** Cardinal direction plaques (Guidance: direction of the entire route). ¶2 **Standard: first letter "ten percent larger, rounded up to the nearest whole number size"**. ¶3 Standard: directly above the route sign (or alternative-route plaque).
**2D.16–2D.24** Alternative-route, ALTERNATE, BY-PASS, BUSINESS, TRUCK, TO, END, BEGIN, TEMPORARY plaques — each Option/Guidance to use; each **Standard for its mounting position** ("if used, shall be mounted directly above …"). 2D.17 ¶3 Standard: ALT not for routes not officially designated (e.g. incident or emergency detours); ¶4 Guidance: the shorter or better route keeps the number. 2D.24 ¶3 Standard: TEMPORARY plaques removed promptly when abandoned. **2D.25**: see Chapter 6I.
**2D.26–2D.28** Arrow and lane plaques. Standard: M5-1P/2P below the route sign in Advance Route Turn assemblies; M5-3P only at circular intersections; M5-4P–6P only for mandatory movement lanes, beside the full-width lane (not the taper); M6 arrows below all plaques in Directional assemblies; **no double-headed arrows at or before circular intersections**; M6-2aP only at the far corner. **Figure 2D-6**.
**2D.29** Route Sign assemblies. ¶1 Standard: on all approaches where numbered routes intersect, **except ¶9**. ¶2–¶4 Standard: order Interstate, U.S., State, county (then lower numbers first); left/right/through arrangement; lowest sign at single-sign height. ¶5–¶6 Guidance: common supports; **more than four route signs → a guide sign** (Figure 2D-7). ¶7 Option: on unnumbered roads with appreciable traffic to the route. **¶8 Option: omit or combine where overlapping routes or multiple turns would confuse, by engineering judgement** (ruled earlier: YES_DELEGATED). **¶9 Option: omit internal-numbering routes and routes not maintained seasonally.**
**2D.30** Junction assembly. ¶2 Standard: before **every** intersection of numbered routes. ¶3–¶5 Guidance: urban — the block before; **where speeds are low, ≤ 300 ft** in advance; rural **≥ 400 ft**; 200 ft from a Destination sign or Advance Route Turn assembly; "where speeds are high", greater. ¶6 Option: single JCT plaque or M2-2. **Figure 2D-7** (2 sheets; notes: one direction shown; **spacings are for rural intersections**, see 2D.29–2D.33, 2D.42, 2D.44 for low-speed/urban).

Notes
- CHECK passed: 2D.10 ¶1 → 2D.29 **¶9** (named exception); the earlier ruling was on **¶8** (unnamed) — both correct on the printed page.
- **Question for the engineer — how 2D.15 ¶2 and Table 2D-2 are applied together**: 2D.15 ¶2 makes the first letter "ten percent larger, rounded up to the nearest whole number size"; Table 2D-2 pairs first letter / rest of word as 6/5, 8/6, 10/8 (10% of 6 and 8 give 7 and 9). How is "whole number size" read in practice (standard letter sizes?).
- MEAN COMPUTED rule: first letter = ceil(1.1 × rest-of-word height) as written (2D.15 ¶2).
- MEAN many "Option to use, Standard for position" pairs (2D.16–2D.24): "if used, shall be mounted …" — conditional Standards.
- TERM "where speeds are low / high" (2D.30 ¶3, ¶5) — undefined; "appreciable amount of traffic" (2D.29 ¶7).
**Figure 2D-8** (4 sheets): directional assemblies; notes — northbound signs only; spacings for rural intersections.
**2D.31** Advance Route Turn. ¶1 Standard: route sign + arrow or word plaque (+ cardinal plaque if needed), where a turn is needed to stay on the route. ¶4 Option: lane designation plaques when judgement indicates, overhead is impracticable, **and** the lane is a mandatory movement lane. ¶5 Guidance: **low-speed areas ≥ 200 ft, high-speed areas ≥ 300 ft** before the turn; rural — 200 ft from Destination or Junction signs. ¶6 Standard: **no intersection between the assembly and the turn**.
**2D.32** Directional assembly. ¶1 Standard: uses A–D (C: two assemblies with single arrows **or** one with a double-headed arrow). ¶2–¶3 Guidance: through movements only alongside turn assemblies; near right corner; far corner for major, Y or offset intersections. ¶4 Support: readability matters more than absolute uniformity.
**2D.33** Confirming / Reassurance. ¶1 Standard: composition. ¶2 Guidance: **25–200 ft beyond the far shoulder or curb line**. ¶3 Guidance: between urban intersections and "beyond the **built-up area** of any incorporated city or town".
**2D.34** Trailblazer. ¶2 Standard: TO + route (or byway) sign + single-headed arrow (+ alternative-route plaque). ¶3–¶5 Option / Guidance.
**2D.35** ¶3 Guidance: route shields on Destination signs **≥ 2 × capital height and ≥ 18 in** (Table 2D-2 note 3: 18-in shield not for independent use — consistent).
**2D.36** D1 series. ¶1 Standard: horizontal rectangle, name + arrow, except on interchange approaches (2D.49). ¶2 Option: distances; several names; one arrow per group. ¶5 Standard: right arrow at the extreme right, left or up arrow at the extreme left; distances right of names (except as otherwise provided). ¶6 Option: up arrow at the right when the sign is left of traffic. ¶7–¶9 Guidance: horizontal or vertical arrows; equal widths in groups; where used (A–B).

Notes
- TERM "low-speed areas", "high-speed areas" (2D.31 ¶5), "built-up area" (2D.33 ¶3) — not defined in the manual.
- MEAN nested alternatives inside a Standard item (2D.32 ¶1 C.1 or C.2).
**2D.36 (cont.)** ¶10 Standard: ≤ 3 destination names where Advance/Supplemental guide signs show ≤ 3; ≤ 4 where they show 4. ¶11 Guidance: four destinations on two signs if space permits. ¶12 Option: single sign; arrow below when all in one direction. ¶13 Standard: four-name assembly — heavy separator line (or separate signs) by direction. ¶14 Guidance: straight-ahead nearest at top, then left, then right; next county seat or principal city; one destination per direction per route where routes overlap. ¶15 **Standard: nearer destination above farther** in the same direction. ¶16–¶18: overhead destination and Arrow-per-Lane signs. **Figure 2D-9**.
**2D.37** Overhead Arrow-per-Lane (conventional roads). ¶1 Support: unlike D15-1, can assign lanes that are not mandatory movement lanes. ¶3 **Standard: only on multi-lane intersection approaches that have an option lane.** ¶4 Standard: at least one arrow over each mandatory turn lane and a bifurcated arrow for the option lane. ¶6 Standard A–F (arrow over lane centre; through arrows up; turn arrows curved with a black-on-yellow ONLY panel; bifurcated option arrow; white separator line; lanes shown = lanes present — never downstream added lanes). ¶7–¶8 Guidance: one destination per movement, ≤ 3 per sign; diverging arrowheads lower; separator position. ¶9 Standard: minimum arrow heights per **Table 2D-5**. ¶10 Guidance: larger letters → larger arrows. ¶11 Option: curved-stem arrows at circular intersections. **Figure 2D-10** (3 sheets; notes \* Arrow-per-Lane only for destinations with an optional movement lane; \*\* conventional guide sign for mandatory-lane destinations).
**Table 2D-5** (read from the page) Principal Legend Letter Height · Straight Arrow · Turn Arrow (inches): 13.33 · 32 · 25.333 / 10.67 · 25.5 · 20.188 / 8 · 21 · 16.625.
**2D.38** D15-1 (Option). ¶4 **Standard: only where the lane is a mandatory movement lane; never for optional lanes.** ¶5 Standard: green with white border; lane-use sign at the bottom, destination at the top; over the lane centre.
**2D.39** Circular intersections. ¶1 Standard: per 2D.36 except as provided here. ¶2–¶6 Option: exit destination signs, curved-stem arrows, diagonal arrows, Arrow-per-Lane with curved stems, diagrammatic D1-5/D1-5a. ¶7 Guidance: consistent arrow types across signs and markings. ¶10 **Standard: diagrammatic signs never depict the number of lanes.** **Figure 2D-11**.
**2D.40** Standard: jughandle destination signs per 2D.36. **2D.41** Indirect turns (Guidance; Figure 2D-13).
**2D.42** Location. ¶1 Guidance: **high-speed areas — ≥ 200 ft** in advance, after Junction / Advance Route Turn assemblies; rural — 200 ft spacing. ¶2–¶3 Option: shorter in urban areas; Destination sign may be dropped where spacing is limited (lower importance).
**2D.43** Distance signs. ¶1 **Standard: ≤ 3 names; distance to the nearest mile.** ¶2 Standard: numerals right of names. ¶3–¶5 Guidance / Option: where distance is measured to (central business district, or the perceived centre); top, middle, bottom name selection.
**Figure 2D-12** sheet 1 (notes: rural spacings; one direction; see 2B, 2C, 3D).

Notes
- Agreement: 2D.37 (Arrow-per-Lane for option lanes) and 2D.38 (D15-1 only for mandatory lanes) divide the cases cleanly.
- Specific over general: 2D.43 ¶1 (Distance signs to the nearest mile) within 2A.08 ¶15 (fractions of a mile rather than decimals).
- TERM "option lane" is defined in #153 for freeway/expressway exits and splits (and conventional-road multi-lane exits or splits); 2D.37 ¶3 applies it at intersection approaches — how the defined term extends to intersections is not spelled out.
- MEAN Table 2D-5 keys on three letter heights only (13.33, 10.67, 8); other heights are covered only by ¶10's "corresponding increase" (Guidance).
**Figure 2D-12** sheets 2–4 (same notes). **Figure 2D-13** (3 sheets) indirect left turns A–C (no notes).
**2D.43 (cont.)** ¶6 Guidance: control city unchanged until reached. ¶7–¶8 Option: alternate two distant points; destinations in an adjacent State.
**2D.44** Distance sign location (Guidance): leaving municipalities and beyond rural route intersections; just outside municipal limits or "at the edge of the **built-up area**"; ~300 ft beyond a route separation; concurrent-route naming.
**2D.45** Street Name signs. ¶2 Guidance: in urban areas at **all** street intersections; rural — important roads. ¶3 Guidance: never name the freeway at an exit-ramp terminal. ¶4 Option: D3-1a with route shield. ¶5 Standard: mixed case. ¶6–¶7 Guidance: letter height factors; per **Table 2D-6**. ¶8 **Option: two-lane local roadways with speed limits ≤ 25 mph — 4 in / 3 in.** ¶9 Support: **speed limit is that of the roadway the sign faces, not of the named street**. ¶10: 12 / 9 in overhead. ¶11–¶12 Option: different heights per facing street; smaller descriptors. ¶13 Guidance: descriptors ≥ ⅔ of the name height, ≥ 3 in / 2.25 in. ¶14 Guidance: abbreviations per Tables 2D-3 / 2D-4. ¶15–¶17: block numbers. ¶18 Option / ¶19–¶20 Standard: pictographs only on D3-1, ≤ capital height; ¶21–¶22 Guidance. ¶23 Standard: retroreflective or illuminated. ¶24–¶25 border may be omitted. ¶26–¶29: alternative backgrounds — **only blue, brown or white; white legend on green/blue/brown, black on white; none on D3-2** (Standard ¶27–¶28). ¶30–¶33 Guidance/Option: corners, both faces, overhead, above STOP/YIELD. ¶34 Option: two names with Type D arrows. ¶35 **Option: historic street name signs on lower-speed roadways (36 CFR 60.4) may remain without complying with ¶5, 7, 8, 13, 14, 20, 21, 23 and 28–30.** ¶36 Guidance: memorial names not on a second Street Name sign. **Figure 2D-14**.
**Table 2D-6** (read from the page) Minimum Letter Heights on Street Name Signs — Type of Mounting · Type of Street · Speed Limit · Recommended Minimum Letter Height\* (Initial Upper-Case · Lower-Case): Overhead · all · all · 12 · 9 / Post-mounted · multi-lane · > 40 mph · 8 · 6 / Post-mounted · multi-lane · ≤ 40 mph · 6 · 4.5 / Post-mounted · 2-lane · all · 6\*\* · 4.5\*\*. Footnotes: \* (on the column heading) heights are for the street name; descriptors ≥ 3 in · \*\* two-lane local streets ≤ 25 mph — 4 / 3 may be used.

Notes
- **Read together by the manual's own rules**: 2D.05 ¶3 (Standard, "Except as otherwise provided in this Manual") and 2D.45 ¶8 (an **Option**, which 1C.01 allows to modify a Standard) → 4-in street names on two-lane local streets ≤ 25 mph are provided for. Table 2D-2 is Guidance (recommended), which cannot lower a Standard — so its 4-in values apply only where 2D.05 ¶3 already permits 4 in. Both settled; no question.
- CHECK passed: Table 2D-6 (6 in on 2-lane and multi-lane ≤ 40 mph) agrees with 2D.05 ¶3; Table 2D-2 note 2 sends Street Name signs to Table 2D-6.
- MEAN **which road the condition belongs to**: the speed limit is the facing roadway's, not the named street's (2D.45 ¶9). The graph attaches conditions to "the site" without saying which road.
- MEAN an Option exempting from a list of named paragraphs, including Standards (2D.45 ¶35: ¶5, ¶20, ¶23, ¶28 …) — the R12 pattern.
- CHECK passed: Table 2A-2 (street name: white on green) is a general overview; 2D.45 ¶28 adds blue, brown, white — specific provision.
- TERM "lower-speed roadways" (2D.45 ¶35), "built-up area" (2D.44 ¶1) — undefined in the manual.
**2D.46** Advance Street Name. ¶2 Standard: supplements, never replaces, the D3-1. ¶4–¶5 Guidance: rural arterials — before all signalized intersections and mandatory turn lanes; urban major arterials — before all signalized intersections unless too closely spaced. ¶6 Guidance: letter heights per **2D.05** (not Table 2D-6). ¶7–¶9 Standard: white on green, **no alternative backgrounds**; names on top, distance or NEXT SIGNAL etc. below; **no pictographs**. ¶10–¶12 Option; ¶13 Guidance: name order; ¶14 Option: W16-8P plaque instead.
**2D.47** Parking Area. ¶2 **Standard: the 18×15 size only on "minor, low-speed streets"** (= Table 2D-1 minimum). ¶3 Guidance: ≤ four blocks from the parking area.
**2D.48** PARK - RIDE. Standard: legend and direction; pictograph/carpool symbol on top, **≤ 18 in** high; white on green; transit pictograph color by the transit authority.
**2D.49** Interchange approaches. ¶2 **Standard: on multi-lane conventional roads, guide signs shall show which turn and/or lane leads to each freeway direction.** ¶3 Guidance: one-lane sequence A–E; ¶6 Option: minor interchanges A–C; ¶7 Guidance: multi-lane sequence A–E. ¶4, ¶9 Standard: Entrance Direction and Advance Entrance Direction — white on green, shield(s) and cardinal direction(s). ¶12 Standard: post-mounted diagrammatic — only two successive turns on the same side, no lane lines or action messages.

Notes
