- X State law decides which trucks must enter (2D.51 ¶5).
**2D.55** Community wayfinding. Standard: **conventional roads only**, never on freeway/expressway mainlines or ramps (direction from freeways only by Supplemental guide + ramp Destination sign) (¶4); **never for primary destinations, routes or streets** (¶5); not where spacing to higher-priority signs is inadequate, never obscuring devices (¶6); **never overhead** (¶7); boundary informational sign white on green, not on freeways (¶15); color codes by neighborhood/subarea only, panels left of the name, **≤ 2 × capital height** (¶16); **backgrounds never red, orange, yellow, purple, their fluorescent versions, FYG or fluorescent pink** (¶19); **contrast ≥ 0.70** (¶20); retroreflective (¶21); rectangular (¶22); letter heights and spacing **per 2A.08 and 2D.05** (¶29–¶30); lettering per 2D.04 except ¶34 (¶31); mixed case destinations (¶32); alternative lettering conventional, not italic/script/decorative (¶35); **no URLs or scanning graphics** except for pedestrian-only viewing (¶36); arrows and order per 2D.08, 2D.36 (¶37); pictograph **≤ 2 ×** capital height (¶39); no unapproved symbols, no logos or advertising (¶40–¶41); identification markers smaller than the sign and not resembling devices (¶45). Guidance: municipal-level systems (¶8); evaluate existing signs (¶9); **pedestrian wayfinding signs away from the street and not retroreflective** (¶11–¶12); ≤ 3 destinations (¶24); separator lines (¶26); marker area **≤ ⅕** of the sign (¶46). Option: boundary sign (¶14); letters on panels (¶17); **backgrounds other than green, including colors beyond those approved in 2A.06** (¶18); other lettering if an engineering study shows equal or better legibility (¶34); pictographs, markers (¶38, ¶42, ¶44). **Figures 2D-30 to 2D-32**.
**2D.56** Named highways. ¶3–¶5 Standard: **names never replace route numbers**; **memorial or honorary names never on supplemental or information signs** (see 2M.10); route signs only for official guidance. ¶6–¶7: unnumbered routes may be named consistently; one name per highway.
**2D.57** National Scenic Byways (Option / Guidance). ¶7–¶9 Standard: design only on designated routes; route signs keep primary visibility; never as panels on guide signs. **Figure 2D-33**.
**2D.58** State byways, historic trails, auto tour routes. ¶11 Standard: never resembling route signs. ¶16 **Standard: not on freeways/expressways except for continuity between conventional-road segments, then as independent trailblazers at less frequent intervals.** ¶17 Standard: never as panels on guide signs. Guidance ¶4, ¶10, ¶12, ¶15. **Figures 2D-34, 2D-35**.

Notes (read together by the manual's own rules)
- 2D.02 ¶4 (Standard: no color-coding by background, "except as otherwise provided") + 2D.55 ¶18 (Option: wayfinding backgrounds other than green) → provided for.
- 1D.05 ¶3 (Standard: the 13 colors used only as prescribed) + 2D.55 ¶18 (Option: background colors beyond those approved in 2A.06, except those in ¶19) → wayfinding may use colors outside the 13; the ¶19 list keeps the critical meanings reserved. Recorded as how the two are read together.
- 2A.21 ¶3 (Standard: retroreflective or illuminated, "unless otherwise provided … for a particular sign or group of signs") + 2D.55 ¶12 (Guidance: pedestrian wayfinding signs not retroreflective) → provided for.
- CHECK passed: 2D.55 ¶36 agrees with 2A.04 ¶18–¶19; 2D.56 ¶4 agrees with 2D.45 ¶36.
- MEAN ratio and area limits (≤ 2 × capital height; ≤ ⅕ of sign area; contrast ≥ 0.70) — relative quantities.
**Figure 2D-36** historic trails from a freeway (no notes).
**2D.59** Emergency routing. ¶2 Option: EMERGENCY ROUTE signs and plaques permanently installed on conventional roads. ¶4 **Standard: only at departure points and along diversion routes around incident-prone segments; at each turning decision point until the original route is rejoined or other directional signs take over.** ¶5 Standard: white on green. ¶6 Option: yellow panel with black letters. ¶7 **Standard: no orange or pink on permanent rerouting signs**; with a route shield the wording is EMERGENCY ROUTE TO. ¶8–¶10 Option: M4-11bP (white on green) or M4-11cP (black on yellow) on route assemblies; **a combination warning and regulatory sign** "WHEN FLASHING [ROUTE] CLOSED AHEAD / USE EMERGENCY ROUTE …" with beacons; END EMERGENCY ROUTE. **Figures 2D-37, 2D-38**.
**2D.60** Airports. ¶1–¶2 Support: most airport roads are conventional roads; **those with full or partial access control and operating speeds higher than 45 mph are freeways or expressways for signing** (Chapter 2E). ¶3: ACRP Report 52. ¶5 Guidance: reduce speeds where spacing is inadequate; larger letters; airlines on separate signs in sequence; **no CMS rotating airline names**. **Figure 2D-39** (3 sheets; notes: major guide signs only, illustrative; terminal loop one-way counter-clockwise).

Notes
- CHECK passed: 2D.59 ¶7 (no orange or pink on permanent rerouting signs) is consistent with Table 2A-2, where orange and fluorescent pink belong to TTC and incident management.
- MEAN a sign that is both warning and regulatory is allowed specifically here (2D.59 ¶9), although 2A.03 classifies signs by one function.
- MEAN a classification threshold stated in Support: airport roads > 45 mph with access control are treated as freeways/expressways (2D.60 ¶2).

**Chapter 2D complete.**


## Re-examined: what I was missing (engineer's guidance: find the connection before asking)

Each apparent difference below is resolved by understanding how the provisions connect. Confidence noted.

1. **4A.08 ¶6 and 2B.06 ¶8 (STOP signs at signals)** — understood. 4A.08 forbids STOP signs "in conjunction with any highway traffic signal operation". An island-separated channelized turn lane **not controlled by the signal** is outside that operation. 2B.06 ¶8 C spells out a case 4A.08 does not reach; the two agree. (High.)
2. **2A.05 ¶9 → "Paragraph 3"** — understood. ¶3 concerns a sign mounted on the back of an exclusive shape (e.g. a warning diamond behind a STOP octagon) whose points would protrude. A **vertical rectangle** fits within the octagon's outline, so ¶9 answers exactly ¶3's problem when ¶3's methods are impracticable. The reference is correct. (High.)
3. **2D.15 ¶2 and Table 2D-2 (cardinal first letter)** — understood. "Rounded up to the nearest whole number size" means the next size in the standard letter-height series (4, 5, 6, 8, 10, 12 …): 5 → 5.5 → **6**; 6 → 6.6 → **8**; 8 → 8.8 → **10** — exactly Table 2D-2's pairs 6/5, 8/6, 10/8. (High.)
4. **2B.21 ¶27 → "Paragraphs 9 and 10"** — understood. ¶9–¶10 govern how an appropriate limit is set; a variable speed limit sign must display limits set by those same principles at the proper times and locations. The reference is correct. (Medium-high.)
5. **2B.16 ¶2 (70 percent)** — understood. The object of the reduction is "the values given in Items A and B" — the combined-unit values: 300 → 210 and 200 → 140 units per hour. "Vehicular volume warrants" names the warrant. (Medium-high.)
6. **2C.18 / 2C.19 at 16–18 ft** — understood. 2C.19 B/C add specific conditions (high proportion of commercial vehicles; short sight distance); where they hold, the bridge functions as one lane and the more specific ONE LANE BRIDGE provision applies. (Medium.)
7. **Differentials between table rows when the 85th-percentile speed is used** — proposed reading: Table 2C-6's top row "15 mph or more" shows the rows are **lower bounds of bands** (5 to < 10, 10 to < 15, ≥ 15); Chart B likewise (≥ 20 at the top). A 12-mph differential reads as the 10-mph row. (Medium — **to confirm with the engineer**; widened to point-row tables keyed on "posted/85th-percentile or speed limit": Tables 2C-3 and 3B-1 — which speed is used, and how measured speeds between rows are applied.)

Also understood on re-reading:
- Figure 2A-3 note and Table 2C-1's \* note are short summaries that point to their Sections ("See Section 2A.16", "per Section 2C.03"); the Sections' conditions travel with the pointer.
- Table 2D-2's own heading carries \* "Except as provided otherwise in this Manual" — the table itself defers to 2D.05 ¶3 and 2D.45 ¶8.

Open questions for the engineer: **3** (item 7; Figure 2G-17 note 3 with 2G.10 ¶11; 3C.08 ¶4 bar-pair spacing — minor).


## Chapter 2E — Guide signs, freeways and expressways (PDF 328–…)

Chapter opens with its Subchapter and Section Organization (General 2E.01–2E.07; Sign Design 2E.08–2E.18; Installation 2E.19–2E.20; Guide Signing for Interchanges 2E.21–2E.45; Other 2E.46–2E.56; Route Diversion 2E.57; Interface with Conventional Roads 2E.58–2E.59).
**2E.01** ¶3 Standard: applies to any highway meeting the definition of freeway or expressway (#83, #91). Expressway requirements exceed conventional roads but are less than freeways (¶1).
**2E.02** Planned system for unfamiliar users; Guidance ¶3–¶4: consistency; functions A–G.
**2E.03** Classification A–P. **2E.04** Urban features A–H; treatments A–H; ¶3: **lower urban speeds do not justify lower signing standards**. **2E.05** Rural (Guidance: place signs at natural target locations). **2E.06** Named highways per 2D.56.
**2E.07** ¶1 **Standard: destinations clearly identified; continuity; at any decision point a destination is indicated by only one route** (Figure 2E-1). ¶2 Guidance: control cities A–E; ¶4: control cities selected by the States (AASHTO Guidelines, 5th ed., 2016).
**2E.08–2E.10** Design aims; Standard: white on green (except as provided); legends and borders retroreflective, backgrounds retroreflective unless illuminated; Guidance: overhead signs illuminated unless an engineering study shows retroreflection suffices.
**2E.11** Interchange classes (Support): major (a) with freeways/expressways, (b) with high-volume multi-lane highways, principal urban arterials or major rural routes with heavy or unfamiliar interchanging traffic; intermediate; minor — local and very light traffic; **exit volumes < 100 vpd in the design year → minor**.
**2E.12** Sizes. ¶1 Standard: Table 2E-1 (except 2A.07). ¶4 **Standard: loop height ¾; cardinal directions, action messages and special characters in upper case, ≥ 8 in.** ¶5 Standard: message dimensions first; minimum sizes per Tables 2E-2/2E-3 (expressway) and 2E-4/2E-5 (freeway); overhead signs use the Overhead column unless the interchange column is larger. ¶6 Standard: names mixed case, FHWA Series E (modified). ¶7 Standard: **same letter sizes rural and urban**. ¶10 Guidance: freeway sizes where expressway design is comparable.
**Table 2E-1** (read from the pages; 3 sheets; one column "Minimum Size"): E1 advance guide "Varies"; exit number plaques E1-5P 114×30 … E1-5eP 186×30; left exit plaques 114×54 … 186×54; E1-5mP LEFT 72×30; E2-1P/1aP Varies×24/×36; E5-1 72×60; E5-1a 78×60 … 156×60; E5-1bP 42×30 … 120×30; E5-1c 60×90\*; E11-1,1d **174\*\*×36**; E11-1a/1b 66×18; E11-1c 120×18; E11-1e,1f **222\*\*×36**; E11-2 60×18; E13-2 162×24; Interstate/U.S./State route shields **36×36 (1–2 digits) / 45×36 (3 digits)**; M2-1P 30×21; M2-2 60×48\*; M3–M4 plaques 36×18; M5-1P… 30×21; M5-4P… 36×24; M10-1 24×24; D1–D3 heights; D4-2 36×48; D8-1 **96×72 (F) / 78×60 (E)**, D8-2 108×90 (F) / 84×72 (E), D8-3 84×78 (F) / 66×60 (E); D13-1,2 78×42; D17 series. Footnotes: \* typical size — determine by legend · \*\* width is the minimum; increase to match the guide sign. Notes: larger allowed; W×H inches; **two sizes → larger for freeways (F), smaller for expressways (E)**.
**Table 2E-2** Expressway letter sizes by interchange class (Major a / Major b / Intermediate / Minor / Overhead\*): A. advance guide, exit direction, overhead (exit number words 10·10·10·8·10; numerals 15·15·15·12·15; Interstate numerals 14\*\* (major a, overhead only); U.S./State numerals 18·18·18·12·18, shields 36×36/45×36, minor 24×24/30×24; text ID 18·15·15·12·15; **cardinal first 18·15·12·10·15 / rest 15·12·10·8·12**; auxiliary words 15·12·10·8·12; destination names upper 20·16·13.33·10.67·16 / lower 15·12·10·8·12; distance numbers 18·15·12·10·15; fractions 12·10·10·8·10; distance words 12·10·10·8·10; action words 10·10·10·8·10); B. gore (words 10·10·10·8·—; numerals 12·12·12·10·—). Footnotes: \* **larger interchange-class size governs overhead signs** · \*\* minimum for 3-digit shields.
**Table 2E-3** Expressway sizes by sign type (A Pull-Through … L see Table 2E-5), e.g. Pull-Through destinations 13.33 / 10, cardinal 12 / 10; Supplemental place names 10.67 / 8; Distance words 8 / 6; **Reference Location words 4**, numerals 10 (see Chapter 2H); Exit Only words 12. Footnote \* 3-digit shields.
**Table 2E-4** Freeway letter sizes by interchange class (same layout; Rev. 1): e.g. Interstate numerals **18/14\*\*** major a, shields **48×48/36×36**, 60×48/45×36; U.S./State numerals 24/18 major a; **cardinal first 18·15·15·10·15 / rest 15·12·12·8·12**; destination upper 20·20·16·13.33·16 / lower 15·15·12·10·12; gore words 12·12·12·8, numerals 18·18·18·12. Notes: \* larger class size governs overhead; \*\* 3-digit shields; **"/" = desirable / minimum**.
**Table 2E-5** Freeway sizes by sign type: Pull-Through destinations 16/12, cardinal 15/12; Supplemental place names 13.33/10; Distance words 8/6; Reference Location words 4, numerals 10; Exit Only words 12; **L. Overhead Arrow-per-Lane** (arrowhead 20/16.25; shaft 7.5/6.094; arrow heights through 48/39, left or right only 38/30.875, optional-diverge 48/39, optional-split 42/34.125; separator width 2; spacings 6.5/5.0, 12/9, 12; EXIT and ONLY panels 54×18); M. Diagrammatic (arrowhead 13.5; stem 30; spacing 12). Footnotes: \* 3-digit shields · \*\* **"XX/XX" = 20-in / 16-in destination legend sizes**.
**2E.13** Guidance: interline ≈ ¾ of the average adjacent capital height; top/bottom spacing = adjacent letter height; side spacing ≈ largest letter height.
**2E.14** Borders (Guidance): > 120×72 → 2 in; smaller → 1.25 in; very large → 2.5 in, ≤ 3 in; never above the principal-legend stroke width; corner radius ≈ ⅛ of the minimum dimension, ≤ 12 in. Option: trim outside the radius.
**2E.15** Guidance: ≤ 2 destinations per Advance Guide or Exit Direction sign; avoid city + street together; ≤ 3 per multi-sign display; ≤ 3 lines. **2E.16** Standard: abbreviations per 2D.07. **2E.17** Symbols (Support / Guidance: General Information panels left of the legend, sized like a route shield).

Notes
- **Understanding strengthened (re-examined item 3)**: "rounded up to the nearest whole number size" = the next standard letter size **that is a whole number** (10.67 and 13.33 are skipped). Checked against every cardinal pair: Table 2D-2 (6/5, 8/6, 10/8), 2E-2 (18/15, 15/12, 12/10, 10/8), 2E-3 (12/10), 2E-4 (18/15, 15/12, 10/8), 2E-5 (15/12) — **all fit**.
- MEAN **slash notation is table-local**: Table 2E-4 "/" = desirable / minimum (note 2); Table 2E-5 "/" = 20-in / 16-in legend variants (footnote \*\*); Table 2D-2 labels pairs "(min.)" / "(des.)". Table 2E-1 uses "(F)" / "(E)" pairs.
- MEAN overhead signs take the larger of the Overhead column and the interchange-class column (Tables 2E-2, 2E-4 footnote \*; 2E.12 ¶5).
- Read together: 2E.12 ¶4 (cardinal, action and special legends ≥ 8 in) and Tables 2E-3/2E-5 row H (Reference Location words 4 in) — reference location signs are General Information signs (Chapter 2H, classified separately in 2E.03 I); the row itself points to Chapter 2H. Consistent by scope. All other upper-case word rows are ≥ 8 in — CHECK passed.
- Specific over general: 2E.14 (freeway guide-sign borders 1.25 / 2 in) within 2A.10 ¶3; freeway route shields 36×36 (Table 2E-1) where 2D.11 ¶3 sets 24×24 "except as otherwise provided".
- CHECK passed: 2E.12 ¶7 (same sizes rural and urban) agrees with 2E.04 ¶3.

**2E.18** Arrows. Standard: types of Figure 2D-3 and 2D.08 (¶1); Exit Direction arrows point diagonally upward — beside the legend on overhead signs, centred below on post-mounted — except Arrow-per-Lane (2E.40), lane drops (2E.28) and ¶5 (¶2); multi-lane exit arrows below the legend over each lane (¶4); down-arrow rules as in 2D.08 ¶2–¶3 (¶5–¶6). ¶3 Option: arrow below the legend over the taper at the theoretical gore.
**2E.19** Overhead installations (Support; see 2A.14, 2A.15).
**2E.20** Lateral offset. ¶1 **Standard: ≥ 6 ft outside the usable shoulder (or curb) for post-mounted freeway/expressway signs and overhead supports, unless shielded by a rigid barrier; crashworthy or shielded in the clear zone.** ¶2 Guidance: ≥ 10 ft from the nearest lane where practicable; large guide signs preferably ≥ 30 ft. ¶3 Guidance: expressway medians ≤ 12 ft — span both roadways. ¶5 Standard: no butterfly or other non-crashworthy overhead supports in gores or unshielded clear-zone locations. ¶6 Option: ramps not generally less than 6 ft.
**2E.21** ¶2 Standard: Advance guide and Exit Direction signs with consistent destinations. ¶3–¶5 Guidance: no new or dropped destinations in a sequence; **≥ 800 ft** between signs before the deceleration lane; minimise Supplemental guide signs; ¶8 most direct exit for each direction (may differ by direction). **Figure 2E-2** (typical sequence: 800 ft MIN spacings, ½ mile, 1,000 ft, 500 ft).
**2E.22** Exit numbering. Standard: **reference-location (mile-based) numbering; consecutive numbering not used**; numbers match Reference Location signs (¶1); every freeway exit numbered; plaque on top of Advance, Exit Direction and Gore signs; single line with EXIT(S) (¶4); **suffix letters only for more than one exit at a mile point, never for downstream ramp splits; space ½–¾ of the suffix height; A upward in the increasing direction, descending to A in the other; no cardinal initials** (¶5); **no plain "EXIT 256" where "EXIT 256 A" exists** (¶6); suffixes counted per direction (¶9); numbers only for departures from the mainline, except C-D roads (2E.36) (¶10); plaque at top right for right exits (¶11); **LEFT exits — Left Exit Number plaque at top left, LEFT black on yellow** (¶12); numbering begins at the **southernmost or westernmost terminus within the State** (¶15); circumferential routes clockwise from the south (¶16). ¶7 Guidance examples A–D (crossroad at or after Mile 15 and before 16 → EXIT 15). ¶8 Option. **Figures 2E-3 to 2E-5**.

Notes
- MEAN COMPUTED: exit number = the whole mile number at or before the crossroad (2E.22 ¶7A) — a floor function, Guidance-level.
- Specific over general: 2E.20 ¶1 (Standard, freeway signs 6 ft outside the usable shoulder) within the general lateral-offset provisions of 2A.16.
- CHECK passed: 2E.18 ¶5–¶6 repeats 2D.08 ¶2–¶3 (down arrows).

**Figures 2E-6, 2E-7, 2E-8** loop, spur and overlapping-route numbering (note on 2E-6/2E-7: the interchange where a loop or spur begins "may be called either Exit 1 or Exit 0").
**2E.22 (cont.)** Standard: loop routes numbered from the south or west junction (¶17); spurs ascending from the mainline (¶18); cross-State routes coordinated (¶19); **overlapping routes — continuity for only one route; an Interstate keeps continuity over a non-Interstate** (¶20). ¶21 Guidance: that route also keeps reference-location continuity.
**2E.23** Interchange Advance guide. ¶2 **Standard: at least one for every interchange class, except ¶16 and 2E.25 ¶18.** ¶3–¶4 Guidance: major and intermediate — ½ mile and 1 mile (and 2 miles if spacing permits); minor — ½ to 1 mile from the gore. ¶6 Standard: same legend as the Exit Direction sign with the distance as last line; "XX MILE(S)" with exit numbers, else "EXIT(S) XX MILE(S)". ¶7 Guidance: **> 1,000 ft → nearest ¼ mile; ≤ 1,000 ft → feet, nearest 100 ft**. ¶8 Standard: fractions, never decimals (= 2A.08 ¶15). ¶9–¶12 Standard: Exit Number plaque abutting on top — right exits top right; left exits Left Exit Number plaque top left; unnumbered left exits LEFT plaque. ¶14 **Standard: multi-lane exits with an option lane carrying the through route at major interchanges, and splits with an option lane → Overhead Arrow-per-Lane signs (2E.39, 2E.40).** ¶15 Option: interchanges 1–2 miles apart; median duplicates. ¶16 **Guidance: < 800 ft between theoretical gores → Interchange Sequence signs instead.** ¶17 Guidance: LAST EXIT BEFORE TOLL plaque. **Figure 2E-9** (note: delete EXIT(S) if an exit number is used).
**2E.24** Interchange Sequence. ¶2–¶3 Guidance: < 800 ft between gores; over the entire urban length. ¶5–¶8 Standard: in a series; next 2 or 3 interchanges with distances to the nearest ¼ mile; first sign before the first Advance guide sign; LEFT panel for left exits; **never a substitute for Exit Direction signs**. ¶9 Guidance: in the median, midway between interchanges. ¶10 Standard: median signs at overhead height. ¶11 Option: exit numbers. **Figures 2E-10, 2E-11**.
**2E.25** Exit Direction. ¶2 **Standard: at major and intermediate interchanges; no populations.** ¶3 Guidance: at minor interchanges. ¶5 Guidance: post-mounted at the start of the deceleration taper; overhead over the exit lane near the theoretical gore; **< 300 ft from taper start to gore → overhead**. **Figure 2E-12**.

Notes
- MEAN **a Standard's exception list naming a Guidance paragraph** (2E.23 ¶2 → ¶16). Understood: the reference imports ¶16's condition (< 800 ft between gores) as the exception to the Standard, and ¶16 adds the recommendation to use Sequence signs there. The Standard is relaxed exactly where the Guidance applies.
- MEAN figure notes carrying permissions ("may be called either Exit 1 or Exit 0", Figures 2E-6, 2E-7) — R21: class from the text (2E.22), which is silent on 0 vs 1; the note supplies the permission.
- CHECK passed: 2E.23 ¶8 agrees with 2A.08 ¶15; 2E.24 ¶8 keeps Exit Direction signs (2E.25 ¶2) required.

**2E.25 (cont.)** ¶6 Standard: where a through lane is dropped at an exit, the Exit Direction sign is overhead at the theoretical gore (except Arrow-per-Lane cases). ¶7 Standard: where Arrow-per-Lane signs serve as Advance guide signs for option-lane exits or splits, an Arrow-per-Lane sign also replaces the Exit Direction sign, near but not downstream of the divergence (except 2E.40 ¶4). ¶8 Standard: overhead Exit Direction content (A) and EXIT ONLY black-on-yellow for lane drops (B). ¶9–¶11 Standard: plaque positions as 2E.23. ¶13–¶14 Guidance: multi-exit interchanges; overpass/underpass placement. ¶15–¶16 Option: E13-2 advisory speed panel, with beacons. ¶17 Standard: beacons ≥ 12 in from panel, sign and legend edges; per Chapter 4S. ¶18 **Option: Interchange Sequence signs may replace an Advance guide sign where restricted sight distance makes the required spacing impossible** (the exception named in 2E.23 ¶2). ¶19 Guidance: LAST EXIT BEFORE TOLL. **Figures 2E-13, 2E-14**.
**2E.26** Exit Gore. ¶2 Standard: **defines the gore** as the area between the main roadway and the ramp just beyond the branch; an Exit Gore sign for **each** ramp leaving a freeway, expressway or C-D roadway; EXIT or EXIT XX with an upward diagonal arrow; suffix spacing ½–¾ of its height; breakaway or yielding supports. ¶3 Guidance: arrow at the departure angle. ¶4–¶5 Option / Guidance: narrow E5-1c only where E5-1a lacks lateral offset; arrow may point down if mounted ≥ 14 ft. ¶6–¶8 Option: W13-1aP below; **Type 1 object marker 4 ft above ground on each support**; E5-1bP plaque on existing E5-1 signs. ¶9 Standard: E5-1a when that assembly is replaced. **Figure 2E-15**.
**2E.27** Pull-Through. ¶2 Guidance: where the through roadway is unclear; with down arrows on curved through alignments, unclear lane counts, multi-lane exits with through-lane reduction; not with full-width Arrow-per-Lane signs. ¶3 Standard: route shield and cardinal direction. ¶4 Option: control city, down arrows. **Figure 2E-16** (note: E6-2/E6-2a add down arrows).
**2E.28** Lane drops without an option lane. ¶1 Standard: scope (option-lane cases → 2E.39, 2E.40, 2E.42). ¶2 **Standard: major guide signs overhead, except ¶14; EXIT ONLY panel for all lane drops where the through route stays on the main roadway.** ¶3–¶4 Standard: EXIT ONLY down-arrow panels on overhead Advance guide signs, one arrow per dropped lane; yellow bottom of the Exit Direction sign with a black upward arrow per dropped lane; word placement for one- and two-lane drops (except ¶5). ¶5–¶6 Option: arrow position under constraints; retrofit panels. ¶7 Standard: retrofit placement. ¶8–¶9 Guidance: no distance message within 1 mile, except auxiliary lanes < 1 mile between interchanges. ¶10 **Guidance: where the dropped lane carries the through route, no EXIT ONLY panel.** ¶14 Guidance: RIGHT (LEFT) LANE ONLY panel where overhead placement is impossible, alternating with W9-7. ¶15 Guidance: warning signs where a mainline lane ends just after an exit. **Figures 2E-17 to 2E-19**.
**2E.29** Signing by type (Guidance: similar layouts; per-direction type). **2E.30** Minor interchange. ¶3 **Standard: at least one Interchange Advance guide sign and an Exit Gore sign** (per 2E.23, 2E.26). ¶4 Guidance: Exit Direction sign too.

Notes (read together)
- CHECK passed: 2E.23 ¶2's exception "Paragraph 18 of Section 2E.25" is the Option to substitute Interchange Sequence signs.
- 2E.25 ¶18 says "required minimum spacing between major guide signs (see Section 2E.23)"; the spacing is stated as Guidance (2E.21 ¶4, 800 ft) and drawn as "800 ft MIN." in Figure 2E-2. "Required" here refers to that minimum spacing.
- 2E.26 ¶7 (Type 1 marker on gore sign supports) and 2C.72 ¶5 (no Type 1 for obstructions **adjacent to** the roadway): the gore lies **between** roadways, like an island nose — the within-the-roadway case of 2C.71 (Type 1 allowed at island ends, ¶2). 4 ft matches 2C.70 ¶4. Consistent.
- 2E.28 ¶2 and ¶10 are two halves of one rule: EXIT ONLY where the through route stays on the main roadway; none where the dropped lane itself carries the through route.
- Pattern again: a Standard's exception pointing to a Guidance paragraph (2E.28 ¶2 → ¶14), as in 2E.23 ¶2 → ¶16.
- TERM "gore" defined inside a Standard (2E.26 ¶2) alongside #168 physical gore and #263 theoretical gore.

**Figures 2E-20 to 2E-26** (dropped auxiliary lanes, auxiliary lane ≥ ½ mile, mainline terminations, intermediate within major, downstream ramp split, minor interchange; notes point to 2C.12 and Figure 2D-16).
**2E.31** Diamond. ¶3 Guidance: **no Advisory Exit Speed sign when no speed reduction is needed**; ¶4 placement per 2C.12. ¶5–¶6: Stop/Signal Ahead on the ramp (Option), in pairs on two-lane ramps (Guidance). ¶7 Guidance: ramp Destination sign with every destination shown on the mainline signs for that exit. **Figure 2E-27**.
**2E.32** Urban diamond (Option: Advance guide signs closer, distances adjusted). **Figure 2E-28**.
**2E.33** Cloverleaf. ¶2 Guidance: two place names, first exit on top. ¶3 **Standard: overhead assembly at the first gore (Exit Direction for exit 1 + Advance guide for exit 2); overhead Exit Direction over the auxiliary lane for exit 2.** ¶4 Standard: suffixes (2E.22). ¶5 Standard: no Diagrammatic signs except as 2E.41 provides. **Figure 2E-29**.
**2E.34** Cloverleaf with C-D roadways. ¶3 Standard: C-D exits — overhead Exit Direction at the gore. ¶2, ¶4 Guidance: same destinations; suffixes; EXIT vs EXITS on plaques. **Figure 2E-30**.
**2E.35** Partial cloverleaf (Guidance: sign on or just before the overcrossing). **Figure 2E-31**.
**2E.36** C-D roadways for successive interchanges (Guidance: line limits per 2E.15, extra destinations on Supplemental signs; exit numbers on mainline signs; Exit Gore at the C-D departure).
**2E.37** Freeway-to-freeway (Guidance: route shield, cardinal direction, next control city only).

Notes
- Read together: 2E.31 ¶3 (no Advisory Exit Speed sign when no reduction is needed) and 2C.12 ¶3 (Standard: required when the limit/advisory difference ≥ 20 mph) — no reduction needed means no advisory speed and no difference; consistent.

**Figures 2E-32, 2E-33** (successive C-D interchanges; freeway-to-freeway A–B).
**2E.37 (cont.)** ¶3: off-route movement defined (the movement not following the through route). ¶4 **Standard: the off-route roadway is signed as an exit** (numbering per 2E.22, distances per 2E.23). ¶5 **Standard: overhead signs at 1 mile and at the theoretical gore of each connecting ramp.** ¶6 Option: ½-mile and 2-mile signs overhead. ¶7 Guidance: W13-2 where a study shows a reduction is needed.
**2E.38** Splits with dedicated lanes. Standard: Figure 2E-34 designs; arrows = lanes present; overhead, arrow over each lane; non-through roadway signed as an exit; Advance guide per 2E.23; **Exit Direction sign carries EXIT ONLY** (¶8). Guidance: signs at the theoretical gore; down arrows (straight) or upward diagonal (curved). **Figure 2E-34**.
**2E.39** Option lanes. ¶2 **Standard: Overhead Arrow-per-Lane signs for all multi-lane exits at major interchanges with an optional exit lane carrying the through route, and for all splits with an option lane; never for other exits or splits** (single-lane exits, splits without option lanes). ¶3 Guidance: consider at intermediate interchanges (factors). ¶4 Guidance: otherwise per 2E.42.
**2E.40** Arrow-per-Lane design. ¶2 **Standard: required at all new or reconstructed locations and where support structures are replaced; located at or near where the lanes begin to diverge; never at or near the theoretical gore.** ¶3 Option: an existing gore Exit Direction sign may remain with new Arrow-per-Lane advance signs. ¶4 Standard: then no Arrow-per-Lane sign at the gore; **no new Exit Direction or Pull-Through signs with Arrow-per-Lane signs on new or reconstructed facilities**. ¶5 Guidance: ½ mile, 1 mile, and 2 miles where possible. ¶6 Standard criteria A–J (arrow per lane; through arrows vertical unless the through lanes curve; exit arrows curved with EXIT and ONLY panels — not for splits of overlapping routes; bifurcated option arrow; split option arrow into two curves; white separator line; distance at 1 and 2 miles; lanes shown = lanes present; exit number plaques). ¶7 Guidance A–E (≤ 1 destination per movement, ≤ 2 per sign; arrowhead heights; placement of shields and cardinals; separator). ¶8 Standard: never depict a downstream ramp split on a mainline sign. ¶10 Standard: arrow heights per **Table 2E-6**. ¶11–¶12 Option: E13-2 panel, beacons. ¶14 **Option: where there is ≥ 800 ft between the start of the diverge and the theoretical gore, Exit Direction and Pull-Through signs may be added near the gore.** **Figures 2E-35 to 2E-38**.
**Table 2E-6** (read from the page) Principal Legend Letter Height · Through · Turn · Through with Turn · Split (inches): 20 · 48 · 38 · 48 · 42 / 16 or less · 39 · 30.875 · 39 · 34.125.
**2E.41** Diagrammatic Advance guide. ¶1 Support: **unlike earlier editions, it does not depict lanes** (which, how many, added or reduced). ¶2 Option: before the sequence or in place of the 2-mile Advance guide sign. ¶3 Standard criteria A–H (plan-view schematic; no symbols or shields as arrowheads; not at the Exit Direction location; no EXIT ONLY; exit number plaques; no deceleration or auxiliary lanes; no lane lines in shafts; off-movement destinations on the departure side). …

(Understood; medium-high.)
- 2E.39 ¶2 sets both a requirement and a prohibition (required for option-lane exits at major interchanges and option-lane splits; prohibited elsewhere) — Arrow-per-Lane at intermediate interchanges is by Guidance (¶3), which fits between them only because ¶2's prohibition names "other types of exits or splits", not other interchange classes.

**Figures 2E-39, 2E-40** narrow gores at option-lane splits and exits (note: other advance signs not shown).
**2E.41 (cont.)** ¶4 Guidance A–G (one destination per movement; diverging arrowhead lower; equal shaft widths; shields and arrowheads related; cardinal beside shield; through control destination omitted when two exits are shown; distance-legend placement). ¶5 **Standard: not at cloverleafs to depict separate C-D departures.** ¶6 **Standard: never depict a downstream exit-ramp split on a mainline sign.** ¶8–¶9 Option: E13-2 panel, beacons. ¶10 **Option: may be used on any class of roadway and modified for conventional-road intersections.** **Figures 2E-41, 2E-42**.
**2E.42** Option lanes at intermediate and minor interchanges. ¶1 Support: peak-period option lanes. ¶2 Guidance: **partial-width** Arrow-per-Lane signs (arrows only for the option and exit lanes). ¶3 Standard: no through route or destination on the partial-width sign. ¶4 Standard: located per 2E.40. ¶5–¶6 Option: conventional signing (¶7–¶9); retain an existing gore Exit Direction sign. ¶7–¶9 Guidance: option lane not signed on Advance guide signs; Exit Direction at the gore with an arrow per departing lane, no EXIT ONLY (auxiliary-lane case); lane-drop case per 2E.28 with E11-1e; **post-mounted R3-8 lane-use signs** where the lanes have developed. ¶10 Support: **down arrows are read as dedicated lanes**. ¶11 **Standard: no down arrow over an option lane on overhead Advance guide signs.** **Figures 2E-43 to 2E-47**.
**2E.43** Overhead installations (Guidance): only essential signs; one sign with one or two destinations for single exits; **no regulatory signs such as speed limits with overhead guide installations**; **≤ 3 guide signs at one location**; sign spreading A–B. ¶2 Option: more signs for multiple exits or complex geometry. **Figure 2E-48** (note: signs at locations 1 and 2 face southbound).
**2E.44** Closely-spaced interchanges. ¶2 Guidance: Interchange Sequence signs. ¶3 **Standard: Advance guide signs show only one interchange.**
**2E.45** Tunnels. ¶2 **Option (tunnels only): side-by-side legend elements; exit plaque at the side, EXIT(S) ≥ 6 in and numerals ≥ 12 in; names ≥ 10.67 in; Series D for very long names.** ¶3 **Standard: only within the tunnel, not its approaches or departures.** ¶5–¶7 Guidance: illuminate tunnel signs; Interchange Sequence signs before the tunnel; supplementary pavement markings.

Notes (read together)
- 2E.45 ¶2 (Option: 6-in EXIT word in tunnels) and 2E.12 ¶4 (Standard: word legends ≥ 8 in): an Option may modify a Standard (1C.01); ¶3 confines it to the tunnel. Consistent.
- CHECK passed: 2E.41 ¶6 = 2E.40 ¶8 (no downstream ramp split on mainline signs); 2E.41 ¶10 matches 2D.08 ¶25's pointer to 2E.41 for conventional-road intersections; 2E.33 ¶5's "except as otherwise provided in Section 2E.41" is 2E.41 ¶2 (supplementing signs), bounded by ¶5.

- MEAN R3-8 lane-use signs (2B.30, intersection signs) used at freeway exits by 2E.42 ¶9, post-mounted — consistent with 2B.30 ¶7.

**2E.46** Next Exit plaques. ¶2 Guidance: only if successive interchanges are **> 5 miles** apart. ¶3 Guidance: E2-1P vs E2-1aP by width. ¶4 Standard: NEXT EXIT XX MILES; below the Advance guide sign nearest the interchange; not impairing breakaway. **Figure 2E-49**.
**2E.47** Post-interchange sequence (Guidance): **500 ft beyond the end of the acceleration lane** a Route Sign assembly, then Speed Limit and Distance signs, **1,000 ft apart** (Figure 2E-2); omit where space or local traffic dictates. ¶3 Option: Distance sign least important.
**2E.48** Post-Interchange Distance. ¶1 Standard: 1–3 lines; top line = next meaningful interchange (community, else route). ¶6 Standard: bottom line = a control city of national significance (if any). ¶7 Guidance: same destinations no more often than every 5 miles; distances to the destination, not the exit; per 2D.43. **Figure 2E-50**.
**2E.49** Travel Time sign. Standard: replaces the Distance sign; changeable time element followed by **MINS**; **no travel times on Interchange guide signs** (¶5). **Figure 2E-51**.
**2E.50** Distance and Travel Time / Comparative Travel Time. Standard: one destination only (E7-5); two alternative routes each on its own line (E7-6); MINS; **comparative times not used to promote different modes** (¶6). Guidance: locations. **Figure 2E-52**.
**2E.51** Supplemental guide. ¶1: the **AASHTO Guidelines for the Selection of Supplemental Guide Signs … "is incorporated by reference in this Section"**. Guidance: demonstrated need; State/agency policy using the AASHTO guidelines; **≤ 1 per interchange approach**; ≤ 2 destinations, ≤ 3 lines, with exit number or NEXT RIGHT; midway between Advance guide signs or ≥ 800 ft after a single one; not detracting from another interchange's signs. Standard: **≤ 2 supplemental generators per approach and ≤ 4 per interchange** (¶8); never at the same location as interchange signs (¶9); park-and-ride and recreational/cultural destinations are Supplemental guide signs (¶10–¶11); transit pictograph on the carpool line, **≤ 2 ×** capital height (¶14–¶15). **Figures 2E-53, 2E-54**.
**2E.52** Community Interchanges Identification (Guidance: "[Community] Exits" with distances to the nearest ¼ mile, before the first Advance guide sign; community name then omitted from interchange signs). ¶5 Option: Next Exits sign where more than three. **Figures 2E-55, 2E-56**.
**2E.53** Next Exits (Option / Guidance: "NEXT XX EXITS"; region name not repeated on interchange signs). **Figures 2E-57, 2E-58**.
**2E.54** Weigh stations (freeway/expressway). ¶3 **Standard: four signs — D8-1 at 1 MILE; D8-1 at ½ MILE or D8-2; D8-3; gore sign.** ¶4 Option: adjust distances by engineering judgement. ¶5 Option: R13-1 where State law requires. ¶6–¶7: COMMERCIAL VEHICLE INSPECTION AREA; R13-1 then reads INSPECTION AREA (Standard). ¶8 **Standard: an OPEN/CLOSED changeable legend shall be included.** ¶9 Guidance: its position. ¶10 Option: OPEN WHEN FLASHING plaque with beacons **in place of** the changeable legend. **Figure 2E-59** (notes: OPEN/CLOSED white on black; alternate legend on all D8 signs).
**2E.55** Route signs and trailblazers (Guidance: cut-out shields on guide signs; Interstate numerals at least as large as others; along the freeway **36×36 / 45×36**; route signs just beyond the exit if used instead of Pull-Through signs). Option ¶3, ¶5–¶6: trailblazers on approach roads; toll-highway names or trailblazers at listed locations. **Figure 2E-60**.

Notes (read together)
- Two contexts, two authorities: OPEN/CLOSED **required** at freeway weigh stations (2E.54 ¶8) and **recommended** on conventional roads (2D.51 ¶7); four-sign sequence on freeways (2E.54 ¶3) vs three on conventional roads (2D.51 ¶3); alternate legends differ ("… INSPECTION AREA" on freeways).
- 2E.54 ¶10 (Option, "in place of") modifies ¶8 (Standard) — as 1C.01 allows.
- 2E.51 ¶4 (≤ 1 sign per approach, Guidance), ¶5 (≤ 2 destinations per sign) and ¶8 (Standard: ≤ 2 per approach, ≤ 4 per interchange along the main roadway — two directions) fit together.
- X another outside document **incorporated by reference** into a Section (2E.51 ¶1), like 1A.05's.
- CHECK passed: MINS (2E.49, 2E.50) is the Table 1D-1 abbreviation for minutes; 2E.51 ¶15 (≤ 2 × capital) = 2D.55 ¶39; 2E.55 ¶2 sizes = Table 2E-1; 2E.47 distances = Figure 2E-2.

**2E.56** Eisenhower Interstate System signs (Option). Standard: M1-10a only where viewed by parked occupants or pedestrians, never on mainlines or ramps (¶2); neither sign in Junction, Turn, Directional or Trailblazer assemblies or on guide signs (¶3). **Figure 2E-61**.
**2E.57** Route diversion by vehicle class (Guidance: signs before the decision point; Option: regulatory/warning panels on the last exit's guide signs; R14-3, W12-2 as supplements). **Figure 2E-62**.
**2E.58–2E.59** Pointers to 2D.49, 2B.48, 2D.50.

**Chapter 2E complete.**

## Chapter 2F — Toll road signs (PDF 420–…)
Chapter opens with its Subchapter and Section Organization (General 2F.01–2F.03; Regulatory 2F.04–2F.05; Warning 2F.06–2F.11; Guide 2F.12–2F.19).
**2F.01** ¶1–¶2: toll highways are typically freeways/expressways, may be conventional roads; this Chapter covers facilities with **all lanes tolled** (managed lanes → 2G). ¶3 **Standard: other Part 2 Chapters apply except where this Chapter specifically provides.**
**2F.02** ¶1 Standard: sizes per **Table 2F-1** (except 2A.07). ¶4 Standard: ETC pictograph prominent for account holders and non-holders alike. ¶5–¶7 Guidance: horizontal-rectangle pictograph **≥ 1.5 ×** capital height, width **≈ 2–3 ×** its height; as principal legend — route-shield height; other shapes of similar area.
**2F.03** Purple. ¶1 **Standard: per 1D.05 and 2A.06; purple background only for the ETC-account requirement (except 2F.05, 2F.16); never for destinations or action messages.** ¶2 Standard: ETC-only lanes/highways — pictograph + ONLY; on green, a white panel on a purple underlay with white border (unless the pictograph is predominantly purple, then a white border). ¶3 Standard: plaques and header panels purple with white border. ¶4 Standard: purple underlays and backgrounds only as ¶1–¶3 describe. **Figure 2F-1**.
**Table 2F-1** (read from the page) Toll Road Sign and Plaque Minimum Sizes (columns as Table 2B-1): R3-28 114×48 (expwy/fwy only); R3-29P, R3-30P 24×18; R3-31 24×24 · 24×24 · 36×36 · 36×36 · min 24×24 · 36×36; R3-32P 24×12 … 36×18; W9-6 96×66; W9-6a 114×66; **W9-6bP 312\*×30**; **W9-6cP 264\*×30**; W9-6dP, W9-6e, W9-6f 114×48; W9-6gP 216×30; W9-6hP 282×30; **W16-16P 252\*×36** (expwy/fwy); W16-16aP 144×54; W16-17P 24×12 … 36×18; M4-17, M4-18 48×48. Footnote \* (**on the width number inside the cell**): the width is the minimum, increased to match the guide sign. Notes: larger allowed; W×H inches.
**2F.04** Toll plaza regulatory signs. Guidance: lane-specific signs clearly related to lanes, over the lane on or just before the canopy; **Speed Limit signs for ETC-only through lanes, set by an engineering study** (¶6); **no Speed Limit sign for a lane controlled by a STOP sign or where a stop is required** (¶7); down arrows to show lane applicability (¶8). ¶9 **Standard: no STOP sign for an ETC-only lane designed for collection while moving.** ¶10 Option: STOP for attended or exact-change lanes (with PAY TOLL / TAKE TICKET plaques). ¶11 Option: lower STOP mounting height if the toll island constrains it. **Figure 2F-2**.

**2F.04 (cont.)** ¶12 Option: regulatory signs in toll islands — lateral offset down to **1 ft** from the island face. ¶13 Guidance: STOP near the payment point. ¶14–¶17: Toll Rate R3-28 (Option), between the plaza and the first advance sign, ≤ 3 lines, one amount per line (Guidance); fuller rates on the booth (Option). **Figure 2F-3** (note: the pictograph shown is an example; **the facility's adopted ETC pictograph shall be used**).
**2F.05** ¶1 **Standard: on ETC-only routes, R3-31 directly below the route sign — white border, purple background, pictograph and "ONLY" in black on a white panel.** ¶2 Option: NO CASH plaque (black on white).
**2F.06** Pay Toll / Take Ticket advance warning. ¶1 Standard: distance and (except ticket systems) the 2-axle toll; **variable tolls → changeable element**. ¶2–¶4 Guidance: ~1 mile and ~½ mile before mainline plazas with stops; overhead. ¶5–¶7 Option: plaques instead where space is short; a 2-mile sign; ramp plazas with limited visibility.

**2F.08** Stop Ahead Pay Toll / Take Ticket. ¶1 Standard: toll shown, changeable if variable. ¶2–¶5 Guidance: downstream of the ½-mile sign, overhead where the lanes begin to widen, clearly tied to the plaza lanes (not open-road lanes). ¶6–¶7 Option. **Figure 2F-4**.

**2F.13 (cont.)** ¶9 Option: other accepted accounts on a separate information sign.
**2F.14** Conventional toll plazas (Guidance): overhead advance signs showing lanes per payment method (lane numbers, LEFT/CENTER/RIGHT LANE(S) or down arrows) and vehicle restrictions, **≥ ½ mile** before mainline plazas; another set **between ~¼ mile and 800 ft**; ramps, bridges, tunnels by study or judgement. **Figure 2F-14**.
**2F.15** Plazas on alignments diverging from open-road ETC-only lanes (Guidance): overhead signs at **~1 mile and ~½ mile** before the divergence (A ETC-only sign with down arrows; B plaza-lane signs; C restrictions); a set at the theoretical gore (A–C); a set **~800 ft** before the plaza. ¶5 Standard: arrows per 2D.08. **Figure 2F-15**.

## CORRECTION — my page reader removed content lines (pages 1–445)

The reader I use to extract page text removed running headers and footers with a pattern that also deleted **any line beginning with "Part" and a number**. Checking every line it removed on PDF pages 1–445: 134 were true footers ("Sect. 2A.01 to 2A.02"); **6 were content**, now read:
- p. 61, definition #125 Locomotive Horn: "… (see 49 CFR Part 229.129) mounted on a locomotive or control cab car. The terms 'locomotive horn,' 'train whistle,' and 'train horn' are used interchangeably in the railroad industry."
- pp. 137, 153, 158, 159 (Figures 2B-7, 2B-15, 2B-19, 2B-20 notes): "Pavement marking provisions are found in **Part 3. The pavement markings illustrated** on this figure are shown for clarification purposes only."
- p. 443, 2F.16 ¶7: "Part 6 contains information regarding the closing of a lane for temporary traffic control purposes."
None changes a finding. The reader now removes only exact header/footer lines. A second check against the chunks (41 differences on pages 1–445) found only the chunker's added "Notes" prefix on figure notes.


## Chapter 2G — Preferential and managed lane signs (PDF 446–…)
Chapter opens with its Subchapter and Section Organization (General 2G.01–2G.02; Regulatory 2G.03–2G.07; Warning 2G.08–2G.09; Guide 2G.10–2G.15; Managed Lanes, Part-Time Shoulder, Lane-Use Signals, Variable Speed Limits 2G.16–2G.26).
**2G.01** Support: preferential lanes (HOV, LRT, bus, taxi); barrier-, buffer-separated or contiguous; continuous or restricted access; constant or reversible; schedules. ¶4: **open-road tolling and toll-plaza lanes are not preferential lanes.** ¶5–¶7: managed lanes restrict access to designated points; under some strategies a managed lane is a special type of preferential lane. ¶10 **Standard: this Chapter does not apply to bicycle lanes unless otherwise provided** (bicycle preferential lanes → 9B.04).
**2G.02** Standard: sizes per **Table 2G-1** (except 2A.07).
**Table 2G-1** (read from the pages; 2 sheets; columns Conventional Single/Multi-Lane, Expressway, Freeway, Oversized — **no Minimum column**): R3-10/10a and R3-11 series 30×42 · 30×42 · 36×60 · 78×96 · 78×96; R3-11hP 30×15 … 78×36; R3-12 series 30×42 … 48×84; R3-12f/g 30×36 … 48×60; R3-12h 36×48 … 60×84; R3-13 66×36 … 144×78; R3-14/14a 72×60 · 96×72 · 144×108; R3-14b 72×60 … 120×96; R3-14c 90×60 … 156×102; R3-15 66×36 … 102×60; R3-15a 78×48 · 114×72 · 144×84 · 150×108; R3-15b/c 66×36 … 102×60; R3-15d/e 42×36 … 72×60; priced managed-lane R3-40 … R3-48a (expressway/freeway only; R3-48/48a "Varies"); part-time shoulder R3-51 … R3-57P and W3-9 (36×48 expwy, 48×60 fwy); W16-11P 24×12 … 30×18; E8-1 … E8-6. Notes: larger allowed; W×H inches.
**2G.03** Preferential-lane regulatory signs. ¶1 **Standard: when a preferential lane is established, its regulatory signs and markings (Chapter 3E) shall be used.** ¶2: types A–D (occupancy definition, operation, advance, ends). ¶3 Standard: distinguished from general-purpose signs by symbols and/or words. ¶5 Option: CMS may supplement or substitute. ¶7 Standard: CMS at the required size, letter height and format for the facility and design speed. ¶8–¶9 Guidance: post-mounted vs overhead by study on conventional roads; separate laterally from general-purpose signs. ¶10 Standard: overhead R3-13/14/15 on the side of the preferential-lane entrance. ¶11 Option: post-mounted in wide medians. ¶13 Guidance: not projecting beyond the median barrier. ¶14 **Option: signs ≤ 72 in wide on median barriers may be skewed up to 45° or raised to ≥ 17 ft clearance.** ¶15 **Standard: signs > 72 in wide overhanging roadway or shoulder — overhead clearance per 2A.15.** ¶16 Guidance: spacing on conventional roads by judgement. ¶18–¶19 **Standard: the diamond symbol only for HOV lanes; never on bus, taxi or bicycle preferential-lane signs.** ¶20 Standard: minimum occupancy shown right after "HOV". ¶21 Support: operating agencies set occupancy; Federal-aid HOV guidance (external). **Figure 2G-1** (2 sheets; notes: occupancy may vary; occupancy may be added to R3-12a–d / R3-15b–c; **legends are examples — "should be based upon local conditions, ordinances, and State statutes"**; R3-13/R3-15 may be post-mounted in wide medians; a lane-use control signal may be incorporated for reversible status).
**2G.04** Vehicle occupancy definition. ¶1 Standard: R3-10/R3-13/R3-13a where agencies decide to define minimum occupancy. ¶2 Guidance: ILEV R3-10a only for HOV lanes, post-mounted by judgement. ¶3 Support: ILEVs defined by EPA (40 CFR §88.311-93, §88.312-93(c)) — cf. #107. ¶5 **Standard: freeway/expressway preferential lanes with access only at designated points — overhead R3-13/13a ≥ ½ mile before the start or initial entry point, and displayed only before entry points.** ¶6 **Standard: buffer-separated or contiguous HOV lanes with restricted access — R3-11a then R3-10, post-mounted, at intervals ≤ ½ mile along legal access gaps and in enforcement areas.** ¶7 Option: the same pair at ~½-mile intervals along the whole lane. **Figures 2G-2, 2G-3** (continuous-access lanes; note: "Sets of R3-10 and R3-11a signs **should** be placed following entrance ramps and at ½-mile intervals").

Notes (read together)
- CHECK passed: 2G.03 ¶14 (skew up to 45°) = 2A.05 ¶4A; "≥ 17 ft" = 2A.15 ¶16; ¶15 defers to 2A.15.
- CHECK passed: the diamond symbol, allowed "instead of HOV" on lane-control plaques (2B.28 ¶9, 2B.30 ¶2), is exclusive to HOV lanes (2G.03 ¶18–¶19).
- Scope: 2G.04 ¶5–¶7 cover lanes with access **restricted to designated locations**; Figures 2G-2/2G-3 show **continuous-access** lanes, where their note 6 recommends the R3-10/R3-11a sets at ½-mile intervals (Guidance by the note's verb). Different lane types, different provisions. **Confirmed**: 2G.04 ¶9 (Standard) requires the R3-11a + R3-10 pair at intervals ≤ ½ mile along continuous-access HOV lanes; the figure notes' "should" is weaker than the text, and by 1A.04 ¶5 the text classifies the figure.
- X local ordinances and State statutes govern preferential-lane legends (Figure 2G-1 note 3).

**2G.04 (cont.)** ¶8 Option: barrier-separated HOV lanes — R3-11a + R3-10 at ~½-mile intervals, intermediate entries, enforcement areas. ¶9 **Standard: continuous-access buffer-separated or contiguous HOV lanes — R3-11a + R3-10 (+ R3-10a) at intervals ≤ ½ mile along the lane.** ¶10 Guidance: signs within a sequence **800–1,000 ft apart** on freeways/expressways. ¶11 Guidance: judgement on conventional roads. ¶12 Standard: R3-10 (+ R3-10a) at the start of every direct-access ramp.
**2G.05** Operation signs. ¶2 Guidance: taller signs for more time periods. ¶3 Standard: R3-11 adjacent to the lane, R3-14 directly over it. ¶4–¶5 Standard: legend order (lanes; eligible uses; times/days — overhead: uses, then times above the down arrow; stacked when long). ¶6 Standard: full-time restrictions — no period legend, **except ¶7** (Option: "24 HOURS"). ¶9 Standard: full-time designs never for part-time restrictions. ¶10–¶11 Option: additional movements ("HOV 2+ AND RIGHT TURNS ONLY"); MOTORCYCLES ALLOWED plaque (¶12 Standard: below R3-11/11a/11b). ¶13 **Standard: access-restricted lanes — overhead R3-14 at the start and every legal entry point (freeways, expressways, and conventional roads where the lane is not the outermost); post-mounted R3-11 only as a supplement there.** ¶14 **Standard: continuous-access lanes — overhead R3-14 at the start on freeways and expressways.** ¶15–¶16 Option: conventional roads may use R3-11 instead of or with R3-14 (except where ¶13 requires overhead); more signs along the lane. ¶17 Standard: post-mounted R3-11 at the start of direct-access ramps. ¶18–¶19 Option: overhead R3-14 on ramps; lane-use control signals.
**2G.06** Advance signs (Guidance): R3-12/12f/15/15d for added lanes; R3-12e/15a where a general-purpose lane becomes preferential — on freeways **≥ 1 mile** in advance; on conventional roads by judgement, leaving room for ineligible vehicles to leave. Option: modified legends; more signs.
**2G.07** Ends signs. Standard: freeways — R3-12b/12h **≥ ½ mile** before a lane termination (¶1); R3-12a/12g where lane and restriction end and traffic merges, **except ¶7** (¶2); R3-12d **≥ ½ mile** before the lane becomes general-purpose (¶3); R3-12c where it does, **except ¶8** (¶4). ¶5 Guidance: conventional roads by judgement. ¶7–¶8 Option: overhead R3-15b/15e or R3-15c instead of or with the post-mounted signs.
**2G.08** Barrier-mounted warning signs. ¶1 Option: vertical rectangle on median barriers (instead of W16-11P + diamond). ¶2 **Standard: top — white symbol/legend on black with white border; bottom — the standard warning legend black on yellow.** ¶3 Guidance, ¶4 Option (≤ 72 in: skew ≤ 45° or ≥ 17 ft), ¶5 Standard (> 72 in: overhead clearance, 2A.15). **Figure 2G-4** (note: other lane types' symbol or words **shall** be shown white on black).
**2G.09** W16-11P HOV plaque (Option; diamond may replace "HOV"; LANE or ONLY may be added).
**2G.10** Preferential-lane guide signs. ¶1: Sections 2G.10–2G.15 apply to freeways and expressways except as provided. ¶2 Guidance: conventional roads per 2D. ¶4–¶6 Guidance: combinations by study; lateral separation; priority Advance Guide → Entrance Direction → Exit Destination. Standard: distinguished by symbols/words (¶7); **HOV diamond on every E8-3, E8-2/2a and E8-1/1a sign at entry and exit points; never on bus or taxi lanes** (¶9); variable-occupancy HOV lanes too (¶10); **diamond at every HOV entry and exit point; guide signs never show the occupancy requirement** (¶11); **access-restricted lanes — guide + regulatory signs before and at every entry** (¶12); **continuous-access lanes — regulatory signs alone** (¶13); white diamond on black at the left edge (¶14); barrier-mounted vertical guide signs — white on black top, green bottom (¶16); > 72 in overhead clearance (¶19); CMS at required size (¶22); **never "EXIT" for entry points on the same route** (¶23); **LEFT plaque for left-side entry points; never on regulatory signs** (¶26). Guidance ¶17, ¶24–¶25 ("ENTRANCE"). Option ¶15, ¶18, ¶20–¶21. **Figures 2G-5 to 2G-7** (notes: other lane types — symbol or words white on black).
**2G.11** Initial entry points. ¶1 **Standard: except for continuous-access lanes (Figures 2G-2, 2G-3), an Advance Guide sign ≥ ½ mile before the initial entry point and an Entrance Direction sign at it; no "EXIT".** ¶2 **Standard: a general-purpose lane becoming an access-restricted preferential lane — also an Advance Guide sign at ~1 mile; both signs carry a yellow panel with a down arrow and ONLY.** ¶3 Guidance: ~1-mile Advance Guide otherwise for access-restricted lanes. ¶4 Option: ~2 miles. ¶5 Standard: access-restricted lanes on freeways — Advance Guide and Entrance Direction signs overhead. ¶6 Guidance: Exit Destination guide signs before the initial entry. ¶7 Standard: upper section black on white, e.g. "HOV EXITS", with the diamond at the left for occupancy lanes. **Figure 2G-8** (notes: right-side mirror; geometry illustrative; occupancy and hours vary; see 3E).
**2G.12** Intermediate entry points. ¶1 Standard: overhead Entrance Direction sign at intermediate entries of access-restricted lanes. ¶2 Guidance: an Advance Guide sign where entry is by a separate lane or ramp. ¶3 Guidance: where entrance and egress share an access location, the Entrance Direction sign between ½ and ¼ of the entry-area length from its downstream end.

Notes (read together)
- Guide vs regulatory division: regulatory signs **shall** show the occupancy after "HOV" (2G.03 ¶20); guide signs **shall not** show it (2G.10 ¶11). Consistent — each sign type has its job.
- Access-restricted vs continuous-access lanes carry different sign systems throughout (2G.04 ¶5–¶9, 2G.05 ¶13–¶14, 2G.10 ¶12–¶13, 2G.11 ¶1) — a site attribute the graph must ask for.
- CHECK passed: 2G.08's vertical rectangle for warning signs on barriers is a case of 2A.05 ¶5 (lateral constraint); 2G.11 ¶2's yellow ONLY panel follows the EXIT ONLY convention of 2E.28.

**Figures 2G-9 to 2G-11** (entrance, intermediate entry, egress and end of access-restricted HOV lanes; notes: mirror for right side; **geometry illustrative only — use locally applied geometric criteria**; occupancy and hours vary; see 3E; warning signs not shown).
**2G.12 (cont.)** ¶4 Standard: Advance Guide signs for intermediate entries, if used, overhead. ¶5 Option: ½, 1 and 2 miles. ¶6 Standard: no "EXIT". ¶7 Guidance: Exit Destination signs before intermediate entries.
**2G.13** Egress to general-purpose lanes. ¶1 **Standard: restricted-egress lanes — post-mounted E8-6 and E8-5 in the median or on the barrier before and at each egress, except ¶4 and ¶5.** ¶2 Standard: legends name the next general-purpose exit(s); **egress never called an exit**. ¶4–¶5 Guidance: overhead signs for two or more preferential lanes; overhead with a Pull-Through sign where egress is by a separate lane or ramp. ¶6–¶7 Standard: white diamond on black at the left edge; Pull-Through upper section black on white ("HOV LANE") with the diamond. **Figures 2G-12, 2G-13**.
**2G.14** ¶1 Standard: advance guide signs on surface streets leading through a transit facility to a direct-access ramp. **Figure 2G-14** (notes include: non-HOVs to be told not to enter; "ENTRANCE" where the ramp does not pass through a park-and-ride).
**2G.15** Direct exits to another highway. ¶1 Standard: contiguous left-side lanes — Advance Guide, Exit Direction and E8-4 Exit Gore signs specific to the preferential lane. ¶2 **Standard: upper section black on white — "HOV LANE" (Pull-Through) or "HOV EXIT" (Advance Guide, Exit Direction) — with the diamond for occupancy lanes.** ¶3 Guidance: overhead; Pull-Through with the Exit Direction sign. ¶4 Standard: no vertical barrier-mounted signs for these. ¶5 Standard: overhead Advance Guide and Exit Direction signs for freeway-to-freeway preferential ramps. **Figures 2G-15 to 2G-17** (2G-17 note 3: **"If the vehicle occupancy levels vary between HOV facilities, then the occupancy level should be added to the guide signs"**).
**2G.16** Managed lanes (non-priced). Standard: 2G.03–2G.07 apply; **CMS or changeable elements show regulatory messages only when in effect** (¶1); changeable open/closed status for vehicle-type restrictions (¶2); **changeable display of the occupancy in effect when it varies** (¶3).
**2G.17** Priced managed lanes. ¶1–¶4 Support: definition; naming by operation is impractical; a uniform naming convention. ¶5 **Standard: priced managed lanes beside general-purpose lanes on the same route are signed EXPRESS / EXPRESS LANE(S) when any of A–D** (all tolled; HOV free; HOV discounted; registered HOV free or discounted). ¶6 **Standard: not for all-managed highways; not for managed ramp alternatives (except ramps leading directly to a managed lane, 2G.14); not for open-road tolling lanes bypassing plazas.**
**2G.18** Priced-lane regulatory signs. Standard: 2G.03–2G.07 apply except as here; messages only when in effect (¶1); preferential signs adapted (Figure 2G-18) (¶2); **toll shown; variable → R3-48/R3-48a with the amount in effect** (¶3); ETC-account-only → pictograph + ONLY per 2F.02, 2F.03 (¶4); **HOV free without registration → R3-10/R3-13 with the diamond deleted (R3-40, R3-43)** (¶5); R3-44 series at the start and every legal entry of access-restricted lanes (¶6); changeable non-toll occupancy (¶7); ends signs R3-42/42a/45 (merge) vs R3-42b/42c/45a (lane becomes general-purpose) (¶9). ¶8 Option: registration information on a separate sign. **Figure 2G-18** (notes: adopted pictograph **shall** be used; **changeable elements shall be used for variable-toll numerals**).
**2G.19** Priced-lane guide signs. ¶1 Standard: lanes follow 2G.10–2G.15; all-managed highways follow 2E; tolled all-managed highways also 2F. ¶2 Standard: ETC pictographs per 2F.02, 2F.03, 2F.17. ¶3 **Standard: "EXPRESS LANE(S)" at entries of priced lanes open to general-purpose passenger vehicles, with the ETC pictograph in a header panel (except ¶5); where HOVs travel free without registration, the header is in regulatory format — pictograph and minimum occupancy, black on white.** ¶4 Standard: HOV-only managed lanes follow 2G.10–2G.12, 2G.14. ¶5 **Standard: no registration required → no ETC pictograph on primary guide signs; a black-on-yellow TOLL header instead.** ¶6–¶7 Option: TOLL BILLED BY MAIL ONLY / … OR [pictograph] on a separate sign. **Figure 2G-19** (notes: pictographs are examples; examples are for registration-required facilities).

Notes (read together)
- "Exit" is reserved for leaving the route: entries to preferential lanes (2G.10 ¶23) and egress to general-purpose lanes (2G.13 ¶2) are never "exits"; direct exits to another highway are "HOV EXIT" (2G.15 ¶2). Consistent.
- The diamond marks HOV lanes only (2G.03 ¶18); a priced managed lane is not an HOV lane, so its occupancy signs delete the diamond (2G.18 ¶5). Consistent.
- 2G.19 ¶5 matches 2F.17 ¶9 (no pictograph where registration is not required) and uses the TOLL panel of 2F.12 ¶5.
- Pattern again: a Standard excepting Guidance paragraphs (2G.13 ¶1 → ¶4, ¶5), as in 2E.23 ¶2 and 2E.28 ¶2.
- **Question for the engineer (with my proposed reading) — Figure 2G-17 note 3 and 2G.10 ¶11**: the note says the occupancy level "should be added to the guide signs" where it differs between HOV facilities; 2G.10 ¶11 (Standard) says guide signs "shall not display the occupancy requirement". Proposed reading: ¶11 forbids occupancy in the guide legend; the manual carries occupancy on guide signs through **regulatory panels within them** (2G.19 ¶3 Standard — "regulatory format" header with the minimum occupancy; 2F.12 ¶10). The note's addition would be such a panel. (Medium.)

**Figures 2G-20 to 2G-24** (entrances, intermediate entries and egress for priced managed lanes; case notes: (1) all vehicles need an account; (2) all except HOVs — **"If registration is required for non-toll travel by HOV traffic, case (1) signing shall be used"**; (3) no registration, billed by mail; geometry illustrative only).
**2G.19 (cont.)** ¶10–¶11 Guidance: Exit Destination supplemental signs before initial entries (per 2G.11 ¶6); **CMS with comparative travel times or congestion levels before entry points of managed lanes that are alternatives on the same route**. ¶12 Option: comparative CMS on nearby non-managed highways. ¶13 **Standard: egress and direct exits per 2G.13 and 2G.15, with white-on-green header messages — A "LOCAL EXITS" on Advance Guide for egress; B "LOCAL EXITS" on Intermediate Egress Direction; C "EXPRESS EXIT" for direct exits; D "EXPRESS LANE(S)" on Pull-Through signs.** **Figures 2G-25 to 2G-31** (2G-26 note: **CMS elements shall be used for travel-time numerals**).
**2G.20** Part-time shoulder travel (Support: peak-period use; lane-use control signals or blank-out signs; guide signs for both conditions). ¶3 **Standard: a shoulder opened permanently and full-time is a travel lane, signed and marked as such.**
**2G.21** Part-time shoulder regulatory signs. ¶1 **Standard: regulatory signs give the periods; R3-51 for fixed periods; R3-51d with two flashing beacons above it for variable periods.** ¶2 Standard: R3-51aP/51bP plaques for excluded classes; restricted classes shown on the signs. ¶3 Option: R3-51cP EMERGENCY STOPPING ONLY OTHER TIMES (if no exclusion plaque). ¶4 Guidance: R3-52c in advance, then R4-17 downstream. **Figure 2G-32** (4 sheets; footnotes: \* lane-use control signals **every ½ mile or less**; \*\* R3-51d with beacons may replace them at the same intervals; \*\*\* R3-51 **every ½ mile or less** for fixed periods; note: consider blank-out signs instead of R3-51/R3-52).

Notes
- Header conventions differ by lane type: HOV/preferential lanes — black-on-white upper section with the diamond (2G.11 ¶7, 2G.13 ¶7, 2G.15 ¶2); priced managed lanes — white-on-green headers "LOCAL EXITS", "EXPRESS EXIT", "EXPRESS LANE(S)" (2G.19 ¶13).
- CHECK passed: travel-time numerals by changeable elements (Figure 2G-26 note) as in 2E.49 ¶4 and 2E.50 ¶4–¶5.
- **DATA (chunks)**: the chunk for Figure 2G-32's notes interleaves two notes ("Consideration should be given to using … beacons may be used in lieu of lane …"). The printed page keeps them separate (the \*\* footnote and the Note).

**2G.21 (cont.)** ¶5 **Standard: R3-52a ~½ mile before shoulder travel ends; R3-52 at the transition back to permanent lanes; R4-17 after it.** ¶6–¶9 Guidance: BEGIN EXIT LANE where a deceleration lane interrupts the shoulder; BEGIN RIGHT (LEFT) TURN LANE where a shoulder becomes a mandatory turn lane on conventional roads; EMERGENCY STOPPING ONLY at turn-outs; TO TRAFFIC ON SHOULDER plaque under YIELD on ramps. **Figure 2G-32** sheets 3–4.
**2G.22** W3-9 Traffic Using Shoulder (Guidance on ramps; Option second sign; conventional roads that stop or yield).
**2G.23** Guide signs. ¶2 **Standard: where an interchange lane drop exists only while the shoulder is open, Advance and Exit Direction signs are overhead with a blank-out or changeable EXIT ONLY message (per 2E.28) shown only during those periods.** ¶3 Standard: otherwise per 2D and 2E. ¶4 Guidance: D17-5/D17-6 at emergency turn-outs (2D.54).
**2G.24** Lane-use control signals for shoulders. ¶2 Option: overhead. ¶3 Standard: per Chapter 4T except as here; **not required over the adjacent lanes; steady RED X when the shoulder is for emergency stopping only.** ¶4 **Standard: variable periods — signals every ~½ mile or less, centred over the shoulder: DOWNWARD GREEN ARROW when open, steady YELLOW X just before closing, RED X when closed; while open, a signal ~½ mile before the end shows YELLOW X continuously; RED X at all times at the end.** ¶5–¶6 Option: R3-51d with beacons instead at the same intervals; R3-51e with a signal.
**2G.25** Active lane management. ¶2 **Standard: per 4T; steady YELLOW X when the next downstream signal over that lane shows RED X.** ¶3 Option: earlier YELLOW X as warranted. ¶4 Support: too many YELLOW X dilute the message. ¶5 **Standard: with planned temporary closures, lane-use signals only supplement Part 6 TTC devices.** ¶6–¶7 Guidance: **½-mile spacing**, closer where views are limited; minimise combining signals with sign structures. **Figure 2G-33**.
**2G.26** Variable speed limits (ATM). ¶3 **Standard: displayed speeds per 2B.21 ¶13 (multiples of 5 mph) and SHS.** ¶4 Guidance: clearly tied to the regulated lanes; **not on overhead guide sign installations** (2E.43). ¶5 Guidance: spacing by engineering study, besides "the post-interchange Speed Limit sign (see **Paragraph 17 of Section 2E.47**)".

## Chapter 2H — General information signs (PDF 500–…)
**2H.01** Scope (Support). Option: I3/I4 symbol signs with educational plaques; M5/M6 plaques white on green for directional assemblies; Recycling Center (Guidance: not on freeways); Ferry. ¶7 Guidance: not within a series of guide signs or critical locations; simple, dignified. ¶8 **Standard: no promotional descriptions ("Scenic", "Historic") except in official names.** ¶9 **Standard: white on green rectangles, except State Welcome (2H.07), Acknowledgment (2H.13), Alternative Fuels Corridor (2H.14).** **Figure 2H-1**.

**2H.03** Airports. ¶1 Option: signs from the nearest freeway or highway, normally ≤ 15 miles; I3-5 with name plaque; trailblazers. ¶2 **Standard: no airport pictographs or graphics with or in place of the airport name.** ¶3 Guidance: adequate trailblazers.
**2H.04** Traffic Signal Speed I1-1 "SIGNALS SET FOR XX MPH" (Option; changeable numerals; blank-out for part-time coordination). ¶3 Standard: electronic section white on black or green. ¶4 Guidance: near each change in timed speed and every few blocks.
**2H.05** Jurisdictional Boundary I2-1 (Option: State, county, municipality, unincorporated community, Tribal Nation, district where jurisdiction, maintenance or emergency response changes). Guidance: at the boundary; statutes on separate regulatory signs; unincorporated communities on official maps. ¶4 Standard: rectangle, white on green; only the name and ENTERING / STATE LINE / County / municipal class. ¶5 **Standard: no elected officials' names or promotional messages.** ¶6–¶7: pictograph — official seal, left of the legend, **≤ 2 ×** capital height (Standard). ¶8 Guidance: not for special-purpose districts.
**2H.06** Geographical Feature I2-2 (Option; Guidance: name only).
**2H.07** State Welcome signs. ¶1: distinct from boundary signs. ¶2–¶3 Option: near the State line; **seal or flag, motto, and the Governor's name; colors other than standard**. ¶4–¶5 **Standard: separate from other signs; no CMS or electronic displays, no promotional advertising, no Acknowledgment or business logos, no phone numbers, URLs or scanning graphics.** ¶6–¶7 Guidance: farther from the road; size like other guide signs.

**2H.09** Project Information I2-5. ¶2 **Standard: legend limited to A route/road name, B project title, C completion month or season, D agency name.** ¶3 Option: a start date if installed more than a week before work. ¶4 **Standard: never more than one month before work begins; a jurisdiction policy on timing; removed at the end of work even before final inspection.** ¶5 Standard: one per direction; not interfering with TTC devices. ¶6 Standard: white on green; no URLs, e-mail or phone numbers. **Figure 2H-3** (note: the actual State name should replace "State").

**2H.11** Reference Location signs. ¶1: integer (D10-1–3) and tenth-mile intermediate (D10-1a–3a). ¶2 **Standard: on all freeways, and on expressways on routes with reference-location continuity (unless enhanced signs are used).** ¶3–¶4 Option: any highway or ramp; intermediate signs at 0.1, 0.2 or 0.5-mile intervals. ¶5 Standard: with intermediate signs, integer signs show ".0". ¶6 **Standard: ≥ 4 ft mounting height, not governed by 2A.15.** ¶7–¶8 **Standard: continuous numbering per route in a State; overlaps — continuity for one route only, the Interstate if one is; that route also carries exit-number continuity.** ¶9 Guidance: after an overlap, show the total distance. ¶10–¶11 Standard: measured on northbound/eastbound roadways, opposite roadways matched; **zero at the south and west State lines** or route beginnings. ¶12 Standard: right side except ¶13 (Option: median; one side back-to-back on two-lane roads; up to 30 ft from the pavement). ¶14 Option: move up to 50 ft. ¶15 Guidance: **omit if not within 50 ft**. **Figures 2H-5, 2H-6**.
**2H.12** Enhanced Reference Location D10-4 / D10-5 (Option on freeways, expressways instead of D10, or conventional roads). ¶4 Standard: ".0" at integer points with intermediates. ¶5 Standard: vertical, green with white legend, route shield in its standard colors; lines — cardinal direction, shield, mile, tenth. ¶6: 2H.11's height, numbering and placement apply. **Figure 2H-7**.
**2H.13** Acknowledgment signs and plaques. ¶2 Guidance: agency policy with non-discrimination. Standard: the policy includes this Section's provisions (¶3); only where spacing to higher-priority signs is adequate, never obscuring devices (¶4); **never on or around other devices or their supports or bridge piers, nor at key decision points (ramps, merges, lane ends, intersections, grade crossings, toll plazas, TTC zones, limited sight distance)** (¶5); **white on blue, independent post-mounted only, never overhead** (¶6); rest-area acknowledgment on the mainline — **one per direction, ≥ 500 ft from other devices**, "REST AREA SPONSORED BY", not combined with Rest Area signs (¶8); program names only on Acknowledgment signs (¶11); design A–K (¶16: no contact information, slogans, URLs, QR codes; upper-case Standard Alphabets; primary logo only; **sponsor area not at the top, ≤ 8 sq ft and ≤ ⅓ of the sign**; **assembly ≤ 24 sq ft**; nothing resembling devices; **no illumination or animation**; **plaque ≤ the lesser of ⅓ of the General Service sign or 24 sq ft**; no size increase with oversized GS signs; no promotion of the program); plaques only below the listed General Service signs, never alone (¶18–¶19); content limits apply to plaques (¶20). ¶12 Guidance: spacing to other signs (except parking) **150 ft (< 30 mph) · 200 ft (30–45 mph) · 500 ft (> 45 mph)**. ¶13 Guidance: relocate, cover or remove when a higher-priority device conflicts. Option ¶7, ¶9–¶10, ¶14, ¶17. **Figure 2H-8**.
**2H.14** Alternative Fuels Corridor D9-19. ¶1 Option: corridors designated "Corridor Ready" by the Secretary of Transportation. ¶2 **Standard: only FHWA-designated "Corridor Ready" segments; with General Service fuel signs; fuels limited to EV charging, CNG, LNG, liquified petroleum and hydrogen.** Standard: post-mounted only (¶4); no State variations or sponsor acknowledgments (¶5); **one sign at the start per direction, except ¶7** (Option: more on long corridors) (¶6); no General Service signs in the END assembly (¶10); **LAST IN CORRIDOR plaque where a fuel stops being available** (¶11); not a directional sign or combined (¶14); **GS symbols ≤ 18×18 with the 24×24 sign, ≤ 24×24 with the 36×36** (¶16); on freeways, GS signs at interchanges with the fuels, repeated at ramp terminals and at each turn (¶18); spacing; relocate on conflict (¶20); only on designated routes (¶21). Option ¶8–¶9 (BEGIN/END white on blue), ¶12, **¶13 (Next EV Charging where the gap is > 50 miles)**, ¶15 (up to three GS symbols). ¶17 Guidance: more than three fuels → letter-designation plaque. **Figures 2H-9 to 2H-11** (2H-10 note: exit numbers may replace arrows on the mainline).

**Chapter 2H complete.**

## Chapter 2I — General service signs (PDF 516–…)
**2I.01** Standard: sizes per Table 2I-1 (except 2A.07).
**2I.02** Conventional roads. ¶1 Support: services usually visible; urban signing mainly for hospitals, police, tourist information, camping. ¶2 Option: where services are infrequent and only on a crossroad. ¶3 **Standard: all General Service signs and panels white on blue.** ¶4–¶5 Guidance: in advance of the turn-off; statewide policy or warrant. ¶6 Option: any alternative fuels. ¶7 **Standard: EV Charging signs only for DC fast chargers per 23 CFR 680.106, operating ≥ 16 hours a day, 7 days a week.** ¶8 Standard: directional message at intersections. ¶9–¶10 Option: M5/M6 plaques white on blue; symbols or words. ¶11 Standard: **symbols and words never intermixed on one sign.** ¶12 **Standard: Pharmacy D9-20 only for a pharmacy open 24/7 with a licensed pharmacist on duty, within 3 miles of an interchange on the Federal-aid system; with a 24 HR plaque.** ¶13 **Standard: Hospital D9-2 / HOSPITAL plaque only for facilities operating 24/7.** ¶14 Option: Emergency Medical Services D9-13 for part-time emergency care.

**2I.02 (cont.)** ¶16 Option: Next Services plaque where the next services are **10 miles or more** away. ¶17 Option: International Symbol of Accessibility D9-6 where accessible ramps and restrooms exist. ¶18 Guidance: VAN ACCESSIBLE plaque "when the D9-6 sign is used in accordance with **Paragraph 16**". ¶19–¶21 Option: RV Sanitary Station; Litter Container; **EMS symbol D9-13** for facilities in a State/local EMS signing policy. ¶22 **Standard: EMS symbol only for qualified hospitals, ambulance stations and free-standing emergency treatment centers; supplemented by a sign or plaque "as provided in Paragraph 22"** identifying the service. ¶23 Option: HOSPITAL, AMBULANCE STATION, EMERGENCY MEDICAL CARE, TRAUMA CENTER plaques; with Telephone, Channel 9, POLICE signs. ¶24 **Standard: EMERGENCY MEDICAL CARE only for qualified free-standing emergency treatment centers.** ¶25–¶26 Guidance: State EMS policy criteria A (ambulance: 24/7, two State-certified persons, radio to hospital, EVOC-trained operator), B (hospital: 24/7, emergency department with physician or nurse with physician on call, licensed, radio), C (Channel 9: 24/7 monitoring endorsed by government). **Figure 2I-2**.

repeated smaller at the ramp terminal with arrows if services are not visible from a single-exit ramp.** ¶3–¶5 Guidance: exit number on the sign; distances on ramp signs if > 1 mile; only where the user can return in the same direction. ¶6 Guidance: statewide eligibility criteria A–G (fuel: vehicle services, sanitary facilities, ≥ 16 h/day 7 days; food: licensing, two meals a day ≥ 6 days; lodging; telephone; **hospital: physician on duty 24/7 — with priority order 1 physician in ED, 2 RN in ED with physician in hospital on call, 3 RN in ED with physician on call from office or home**; **24-hour pharmacy: licensed pharmacist 24/7 within 3 miles of an interchange**; camping). ¶7 **Standard: EV Charging only for DC fast chargers per 23 CFR 680.106, ≥ 16 h/day, 7 days.** ¶9 Guidance: alternative-fuel policy (2H.14). ¶10 **Standard: seasonal services — signs removed or covered off-season.** ¶11 **Standard: between the Advance Guide and Exit Direction signs.** ¶12 Option: Next Services plaque below the Exit Direction sign if **greater than 10 miles**. ¶13 **Standard: format per 2I.02; ≤ 6 services per sign including plaques; only Food, Gas, EV Charging, Lodging, Camping, Phone, Hospital, 24-Hour Pharmacy, Tourist Information.** ¶14 Standard: fixed positions. ¶15–¶16, ¶18 Guidance: blank space for absent services; word order (FOOD PHONE / GAS LODGING; HOSPITAL, 24-HOUR PHARMACY, CAMPING on separate lines); symbol layouts for six, four, three services. ¶17, ¶19–¶20, ¶22–¶26 Option: substitutions (EV, DIESEL, LP-GAS, D9-6; lower right or extreme right); rural interchange plaques D9-18dP/eP/fP (one to three services); telephone; RV; "services not available"; Truck Parking; TRUCK EXTERNAL POWER. ¶21 Standard: replace the rural plaque with a full sign when more than three services exist. **Figure 2I-3**.
**2I.04** Interstate Oasis (FHWA policy of 18 Oct 2006, external). Guidance: statewide policy; D5-12 ≥ 800 ft from other guide signs, before the Advance Guide or between it and the Exit Direction sign; else D5-12aP below a D9-18 sign. Option: OASIS on business panels; business name below the legend. ¶6–¶7 Standard: D5-12b on the ramp where OASIS is not shown on ramp Specific Service signs and the Oasis is not visible; trailblazers as needed. **Figure 2I-4**.
**2I.05** Rest and roadside areas. ¶1 Standard: retroreflective white on blue. ¶2 **Standard: REST AREA only where parking and restrooms are both available.** ¶3–¶7 Guidance: PARKING AREA / PICNIC AREA / SCENIC AREA legends otherwise; tourist centers per 2I.08; conventional roads D5-1/1a in advance and D5-5 (or D5-2) at the turn-off; freeways D5-1 at 1 and/or 2 miles. ¶8 Standard: D5-2a at the exit gore. ¶9–¶10, ¶12–¶14 Option: D5-1a or a second D5-1 with fractional distance; Next Rest Area; D9-6, TDD, Wi-Fi symbols. ¶11 Standard: letter sizes per Tables 2E-2 to 2E-5; sizes per Table 2I-1. **Figure 2I-5** (note: alternate legends).
**2I.06–2I.07** Brake Check and Chain-Up areas (Guidance: advance sign and entrance sign). **Figure 2I-6**.
**2I.08** Tourist information and welcome centers. ¶2 Guidance: ≤ 3 supplemental panels. ¶3 **Standard: white on blue; operation (staffed or unstaffed) ≥ 8 hours a day, 7 days a week.** ¶4 Standard: seasonal — removed or covered off-season. ¶5 Guidance A–D (signing per 2I.03; legends; supplemental panels; gore sign REST AREA only). ¶6 Option: D9-10 below the REST AREA sign. **Figure 2I-7**.
