## Chapter 2J — Specific service signs (PDF 533–…)
**2J.01** Eligibility. ¶1 **Standard: Specific Service signs = guide signs with business identification and direction; categories limited to gas, food, lodging, camping, attractions and EV charging.** ¶2–¶3 Guidance/Support: primarily rural areas; abundant services mean General Service signs fit better. ¶4 Option: any class of highway by engineering study. ¶5 Guidance: not where users cannot re-enter in the same direction. ¶6 Standard: facilities comply with public-accommodation non-discrimination and licensing laws. ¶7 Standard: attractions only with the primary purpose of amusement, historical, cultural or leisure activities. ¶8 Guidance: **≤ 3 miles**, except ¶9 (Option: extend in **3-mile increments to 15 miles** until a facility participates). ¶10 **Standard: a statewide policy with eligibility criteria if an agency provides these signs.** ¶11 Guidance: criteria A–E (**gas: ≥ 16 h/day 7 days on freeways/expressways, ≥ 12 h/day on conventional roads**; food: two meals a day ≥ 6 days; lodging; camping; attraction: regional significance, parking). ¶12 **Standard: EV CHARGING panels only for DC fast chargers per 23 CFR 680.106, ≥ 16 h/day 7 days.** ¶13 Option: proprietary EV networks if eligible.
**2J.02** Application. ¶3 **Standard: ≤ 4 Specific Service signs per approach; order in the direction of travel — attraction, camping, lodging, food, EV charging, gas — except ¶4** (Option: EV charging anywhere spacing allows). ¶5–¶6 Guidance: siting; a service on two signs in succession. ¶7 **Standard: category word, a directional legend (EXIT XX, NEXT RIGHT, arrows) and panels; no distances on approach signs.** ¶8 **Standard: an alternative-fuel-only business never on GAS signs** (¶9 Option: General Service signs instead). ¶11 Guidance: ATTRACTION signs ≤ 4 panels. ¶13 **Standard: ≤ 3 service types per sign or assembly; ≤ 6 panels; three types → 2 each; two types → 3 + 3 or 4 + 2; no other arrangements.** ¶14 Standard: a service type on no more than two signs. ¶15 Standard: blue background, white border and upper-case legend. ¶16–¶18: remove or cover when a service ends; not installed without a current service. ¶19 Option: General Service signs for eligible services not on Specific Service signs. **Figures 2J-1 to 2J-3** (2J-2 note: ramp signs ≥ 100 ft from the gore sign, each other and the terminal).
**2J.03** Logos and panels. Standard: trademark or word message, one per panel, customary colors and shape, not resembling devices (¶1); **no scanning graphics visible from the road** (¶2); supplemental messages within the panel at Table 2J-1 heights, **never promoting products or amenities unrelated to the category or unavailable to non-patrons** (car wash, ATM, Internet, lottery, pool) (¶7); no promotion of panel space (¶8); **EV CHARGING supplemental message only if charging is open to the public without buying the primary service, with DCFC (gas, food, attraction) or DCFC and/or AC Level 2 (camping, lodging)** (¶9); **one business per panel, one name or logo, no slogans** (¶18). Guidance: logos consistent with on-premise signs; word panels white on blue; one supplemental message, black on yellow; statewide policy; RV ACCESS criteria. Option: supplemental messages (¶6), alternative fuels on GAS panels (¶10), EV CHARGING (¶11), OASIS (¶17). **Figure 2J-4**.
**2J.04** ¶1 Guidance: sizes by legend and panels; equal panel sizes. ¶2 Standard: ≤ 6 panels per sign or assembly.
**Table 2J-1** (read from the page) Minimum Letter and Numeral Sizes — Freeway or Expressway · Conventional Road or Ramp: A. service categories 10 · 6; exit number words 10 · —; exit numerals and letters 10 · —; action words 10 · 6; distance numerals — · 6; fraction numerals — · 4. B. panel words and numerals 8 · 4; trademark/logo proportional; supplemental message 5 · 2.5. Note: inches.

Notes (read together)
- 2I.12 ¶4 refers to the 511 pictograph "on the standard sign size … in Table 2H-1"; the D12-5 sign's standard sizes are in **Table 2I-1** (48×60 · 66×72); Table 2H-1 does not list it. The sign named identifies the table meant — recorded by content.
- 2I.14 ¶2 (longer phone numbers and domain names on Carpool signs) and 2F.19 ¶2 (phone numbers ≤ 4 characters) are specific cases of 2A.04 ¶18's "unless otherwise provided … for a specific sign".
- CHECK passed: 2I.14 ¶3 (≤ 18 in) agrees with 2D.48 ¶4; 2J.01 ¶11A (16 h freeways) agrees with 2I.03 ¶6A; EV charging criteria identical in 2I.02 ¶7, 2I.03 ¶7, 2J.01 ¶12; 2J.04 ¶2 = 2J.02 ¶13 (six panels).
- MEAN eligibility thresholds (hours of operation, distances, meal counts) are Guidance for State policies, while EV-charging and pharmacy criteria are Standards — authority differs by service.

**2J.04 (cont.)** ¶3 Option: more than six eligible businesses — extra panels per ¶4. ¶4 **Standard: ≤ 12 panels of one type on ≤ 2 signs; ≤ 6 per sign; ≤ 4 signs per approach.** ¶7 **Standard: panels horizontal rectangles; ≤ 60×36 on freeways/expressways, ≤ 30×18 on conventional roads and ramps (Table 2J-2); spacing ≤ 8 in vertical, ≤ 12 in horizontal.**
**Table 2J-2** (read from the page) Maximum panel sizes: Freeway or Expressway 60×36 · Conventional Road or Ramp 30×18 (W×H inches).
**2J.05** ¶1 Standard: letter sizes per Table 2J-1. ¶2 Guidance: mixed case on panels.
**2J.06** Interchanges. ¶1 **Standard: between the preceding interchange and ≥ 800 ft before the Exit Direction sign.** ¶2 **Standard: not at freeway-to-freeway interchanges, except where the ramp also reaches a conventional road within the interchange** (Figure 2J-5). ¶3–¶4 Guidance: ≥ 800 ft between signs; ramp signs ≥ 100 ft beyond the gore sign, from each other and from the terminal; ≥ 200 ft from ramp Destination signs. ¶5 Guidance: **Next EV Charging where the next EV-charging exit is 50 miles or greater**, just after the fuel General Service sign.
**2J.07** Single-exit interchanges. ¶1 Standard: service type + exit number on one line (NEXT RIGHT/LEFT if unnumbered). ¶2 Standard: ramp signs where traffic may turn either way and the facilities are not visible; arrows; panels duplicated, reduced (2J.04 ¶7). ¶3 Option: distances on ramp signs (2A.08 ¶14–¶15). ¶4 Guidance: < ¼ mile → nearest 1/10 mile.
**2J.08** Double-exit interchanges. ¶2 Standard: top section first exit, bottom second; service type + exit number (or NEXT/SECOND RIGHT); ≤ 6 panels in total. Guidance ¶1, ¶3; Option ¶4–¶6.
**2J.09** C-D roadways. Standard: ≤ 4 Specific Service signs on the mainline approach (¶3); C-D panels duplicate mainline panels (¶4); **if more than four would be needed, General Service signs on the mainline instead** (¶5). **Figure 2J-6**.
**2J.10** Trailblazers. ¶2 **Standard: required along crossroads where the route turns, is unclear or needs guidance; a business that cannot be trailblazed is not eligible; not required where the business and access are visible.** ¶3 Guidance: ≤ 500 ft before the turn. ¶4–¶6 Standard: other devices take precedence; ≤ 4 panels, duplicates of ramp panels; white arrows/legends on blue. ¶7–¶8 Option. **Figure 2J-7**.
**2J.11** Intersections. ¶1 Guidance: TODS and Specific Service signs spaced apart. ¶2 **Standard: without room for all, guide, warning and regulatory signs have priority; TODS and/or Specific Service signs are omitted.** ¶3 Guidance: ≥ 300 ft before the intersection. ¶4 Guidance: no panels for a visible qualified facility. ¶5 Standard: service name above its panels with NEXT RIGHT (LEFT) or an arrow. ¶6 Option: crossroad signs.
**2J.12** ¶1 **Standard: each agency using Specific Service signs establishes a signing policy** (besides 2J.01's statewide eligibility policy). ¶2 Guidance: contents A–F.

**Chapter 2J complete.**

## Chapter 2K — Tourist-oriented directional signs (PDF 546–550)
**2K.01** ¶2 **Standard: eligible only if a facility draws most of its income or visitors in the normal season from road users not residing in the area.** ¶3 Option: seasonal agricultural products. ¶4 **Standard: only on rural highways (#208); never on conventional roads in urban or urbanized areas, nor on freeway or expressway mainlines or ramps.** ¶5 Option: with General Service signs.
**2K.02** Design. ¶1 Standard: rectangular, white on blue (except ¶7). ¶2 **Standard: one facility per sign; no promotional advertising.** ¶3 Guidance: ≤ 2 lines, ≤ 1 symbol, arrow and distance; arrow positions. ¶4–¶6 Option: GS and rec symbols; hours by judgement; logos. Standard: rec symbols white on brown (¶7); appropriate size (¶8); **panels ≤ 24×15, no logos resembling devices** (¶9); TOURIST ACTIVITIES white on blue, upper case, above the signs (¶11). **Figures 2K-1, 2K-2** (notes: optional panel and legend; use if there is an intervening intersection).
**2K.03** ¶1 Guidance: upper case ≥ 6 in; panels mixed case. ¶2 Standard: SHS letter design.
**2K.04** ¶1 **Standard: total height of the directional signs ≤ 6 ft (plus TOURIST ACTIVITIES and action messages).** ¶2–¶4 Guidance: ≤ 3 assemblies per approach (left, right, straight), ≤ 3 signs each; left above right when combined; order by distance; ≤ two lines; ≤ 6 ft wide. ¶5 Option: three or fewer facilities on one sign.
**2K.05** Advance signs (Guidance: only where needed; same design, arrows and distances replaced by NEXT RIGHT/LEFT or AHEAD). ¶3 Standard: action messages white on blue, upper case. ¶4–¶5 Option: RIGHT (LEFT) ½ MILE; extra height allowed.
**2K.06** Locations (Guidance): intersection signs ≥ 200 ft before the intersection, ≥ 200 ft apart and from other devices; advance signs ~½ mile, 500 ft apart, order left, right, straight. ¶3 Guidance: per 2A except as here. ¶4 Option: farther from the road. ¶5 Standard: other devices take precedence.
**2K.07** State policy. ¶1 **Standard: non-discrimination and licensing compliance; each State using TODS adopts a policy.** ¶2 Guidance: contents A–M (maximum distance **5 miles**; Highway Beautification Act 23 U.S.C. 131; fees; seasonal covering).

**Chapter 2K complete.**

## Chapter 2L — Changeable message signs (PDF 551–…)
**2L.01** ¶1–¶2 Support: definition; applies to permanent and portable CMS with electronic displays (PCMS also 6L.05), generally not to manually or electromechanically changed signs. ¶3: **a CMS is a traffic control device at all times**, whatever it displays; this Chapter is not stand-alone. ¶4–¶5: habituation; use judiciously (Guidance). ¶6 Standard: non-electronic CMS legends per 2A–2K, 2M, 2N; others per this Chapter, 2A and specific-sign provisions. ¶7 **Standard: only inventory/maintenance items on the front or back; no manufacturer, brand or model names or logos.** ¶8 Guidance: fixed-legend blank-out signs follow the conventional sign's provisions and SHS legend.
**2L.02** Applications. ¶1 **Standard: only traffic operational, regulatory, warning and guidance information, except as this Chapter provides; no advertising or unrelated messages on the CMS or its supports.** ¶2–¶3 Option: safety campaigns (2L.07), transportation-related, homeland-security emergency and AMBER messages; alternatives, ETC, carpooling reminders. ¶4 Support: licence renewals, recalls, maintenance are **not** transportation-related messages. ¶5 Examples. ¶6–¶7 Guidance: not where respect for the sign suffers; no vague messages alone ("CONGESTION AHEAD"); not in place of conventional signs for unchanging conditions (blank-out part-time signs excepted); only the changing elements electronic (R3-48 prices). ¶8 Support: purposes A–L. ¶10 **Standard: agencies with permanent CMS issue and maintain a message policy** (types, priority, syntax, timing). ¶11–¶12 Guidance.

Notes (read together)
- Next EV Charging sign — two contexts, different authority and boundary: **"50 miles or greater"** (2J.06 ¶5, Guidance, Specific Service exits) and **"greater than 50 miles"** (2H.14 ¶13, Option, within Alternative Fuels Corridors).
- 2K.01 ¶4 limits TODS to "rural highways" as defined in #208 — a descriptive definition without thresholds, so the classification is a judgement.
- CHECK passed: 2J.04 ¶7 = Table 2J-2; 2J.06 ¶4 (100 ft) = Figure 2J-2's note; 2J.07 ¶2's pointer "Paragraph 7 of Section 2J.04" is the panel-size paragraph; 2L.01 ¶7 agrees with 2A.04 ¶21 (only inventory/maintenance items on sign backs) and 1D.07.
- MEAN two policies for Specific Service signs: statewide eligibility (2J.01 ¶10) and each agency's signing policy (2J.12 ¶1) — both Standards.

**2L.02 (cont.)** ¶13 Guidance: AMBER alerts never pre-empt traffic messages; brief; point to other sources. ¶14 **Standard: no other "alert" types unrelated to traffic or travel.** ¶15 Standard: no display format resembling advertising. ¶17 **Standard: homeland-security and emergency messages only in declared emergencies with an imminent threat; never generic security messages; never promotional or advisory in design.** ¶18 Guidance: scrutiny; traffic-message format. ¶19–¶22: pointers (2B.21 VSL, 2C.13 speed feedback, 2H.04 signal speed, 5B.01 LEDs for driving automation).
**2L.03** Legibility and visibility. ¶1–¶3 Support: definitions. ¶4 Guidance: **on roads with speed limits ≥ 55 mph, visible from ½ mile; legible from ≥ 600 ft at night and ≥ 800 ft by day**; otherwise fewer units, single phase. ¶5 Guidance: electronic standard regulatory/warning signs at Chapter 2B/2C sizes.
**2L.04** Message design. ¶1 **Standard: no animation, flashing, dissolving, exploding, scrolling or other dynamic elements, except ¶2** (portable CMS used as arrow boards — per 6L.05, 6L.06). ¶4 Standard: same design principles as other signs. ¶5 Guidance: supplement, not substitute, except blank-out/part-time displays. ¶7 Guidance: **no Warning Beacons on CMS; ≤ 3 lines, ≤ 20 characters per line.** ¶8 Guidance: spacing — characters 25–40 %, words 75–100 %, lines 50–75 % of letter height (**Table 2L-1**). ¶9 Guidance: upper case; **≥ 18 in where speed limits are ≥ 45 mph, ≥ 12 in below 45 mph; 18 in for two-phase high-load messages.** ¶10 Option: replicas at conventional-sign size. ¶11 Support: > 18 in gains little. ¶12 Guidance: width/height 0.7–1.0; stroke 0.2 (¶13: 5×7 matrix). ¶14 **Standard: automatic brightness adjustment.** ¶15, ¶17 Guidance: luminance contrast 8–12; positive contrast (¶18: negative contrast ≥ 25 % shorter legibility). ¶19 **Standard: colors per Table 2A-2.** ¶20 Standard: on black backgrounds the legend takes the standard sign's background color, except ¶21 (Option: yellow/amber-only CMS). ¶22 **Standard: green or blue backgrounds by lighted pixels (whole sign lit).** ¶24 Guidance: pixel pitch **> 20 mm → words only, no symbols or shields**. ¶25 Guidance.
**Table 2L-1** (read from the page) Letter height · character spacing · word spacing · line spacing (inches): 12 · 3–5 · 9–12 · 6–9 / 18 · 4½–7 · 13½–18 · 9–13½.
**2L.05** Message length. ¶1 Guidance: a unit of information ≤ 4 words. ¶4 **Standard: ≤ 2 phases; ≤ 3 lines per phase; each phase understood alone and the message the same in either order; lines centred; where more than one CMS is visible only one shows a sequential message (toll plazas excepted).** ¶5 Option: left/right justification for replicas. ¶6 Standard: abbreviations per 1D.08. ¶7 Guidance A–G: **minimum phase time = 1 s per word or 2 s per unit, whichever is less, never < 2 s; cycle ≤ 8 s; ≤ 0.3 s between phases; ≤ 3 units per phase; ≤ 4 units per message at operating speeds ≥ 35 mph, ≤ 5 below 35 mph; one unit per line.** ¶9–¶10 Option: units over two lines; downstream repeats; series of two-phase messages. **Figure 2L-1** (notes: pixel pitch ≤ 20 mm near-conventional legends; > 20 mm monochrome words; pitch centre to centre; metric because manufacturers use metric). **Table 2L-2** example units (MAJOR CRASH / AT EXIT 12 / TO NEW YORK / USE ROUTE 46 → two phases).
**2L.06** Travel time messages. ¶2 Guidance: tied to a destination distance; per 2E.49–2E.50; one destination with time and distance; **distances rounded to the nearest whole mile**. ¶3–¶4 Option: comparative times without distances; reference-location exit numbers in place of names, then up to two travel times without distances.
**Tables 2L-3, 2L-4** (read from the pages) examples of message construction for CMS and portable CMS (Example · Phase · Potential · Improved · Comments) — e.g. "SEAT BELTS SAVE LIVES" → "STATE LAW FASTEN SEAT BELTS"; "RT LN CLSD 1 MI" → "RIGHT LANE CLOSED 1 MILE". Notes: examples for single-color CMS > 20 mm pitch in upper case (full-matrix ≤ 20 mm use mixed case and proper colors); portable CMS ≈ 8 characters per line.
**2L.07** Traffic safety campaigns. ¶2 **Standard: traffic control messages have priority.** Guidance ¶3–¶7: simple, relevant; **no humour, pop-culture references, slogans or statistics**; shortened per 2L.05; emphasise the regulation and penalties with coordinated enforcement; only within active campaigns using other media, coordinated with NHTSA's calendar. ¶8 examples.
**2L.08** Permanent CMS locations (Guidance A–E: upstream of bottlenecks and diversion points; not within interchanges except toll plazas or managed lanes; not where information load or lane changing is high).

**Chapter 2L complete.**

## Chapter 2M — Recreational and cultural interest area signs (PDF 561–…)
**2M.01** Scope (Support; Option: on freeways with direct access per 2M.09; off the road network). **2M.02** Application (Guidance: agency policy; Option: non-vehicular amenities). **2M.03** Standard: regulatory and warning signs in these areas comply with the rest of the Manual. **2M.04** ¶1–¶2 Standard: General Information and General Service symbols outside these areas keep their Chapter 2H/2I colors. ¶3 Option: inside, white on brown. ¶4 **Standard: except 2M.09, square or rectangular, white symbol/message and border on brown; six series A–F.** ¶6 Option: mirror images. **2M.05** Sizes (Guidance: 24×24, 6-in increments; 30×30 on freeway guide signs; Option: 18×18 on low-speed, low-volume roads).
**Table 2M-1** (read from the page) symbol categories — General, Accommodations, Services, Land, Water, Winter Recreation (RS- codes). Footnote \* **"For use only within recreational and cultural interest areas where speed limits are 25 mph or less."**

**2M.06** Educational plaques (Guidance: with all initial installations, same width; Option: omit for well-known symbols).
**2M.07** ¶1 **Standard: for non-road prohibitions without a Chapter 2B sign — the rec symbol in black inside a red circle and diagonal (upper left to lower right) on white.** ¶2 Standard: red circle retroreflective like other elements. **Figure 2M-1** (note: "Standard regulatory signs **shall** be used where provided elsewhere in this Manual").
**2M.08** Placement. ¶1 Standard: per 2A; symbols on top, directions below. ¶2 Standard: on destination guide signs the symbol goes below the name. ¶3–¶4, ¶8 Option: symbols below the sign; 18×18 secondary symbols; M5/M6 arrows white on brown; groups of up to four. ¶5 Standard: non-road placement per the authority. ¶7 Guidance: ≤ 4 symbols per assembly.
**2M.09** Destination guide signs. ¶1 **Standard: on Supplemental guide signs — rectangle, white on green or brown.** ¶2 Option: trapezoids on conventional roads. ¶3 **Standard: trapezoids white on brown; right-angled with the wide base on top and the diagonal leg toward the arrow for arrow signs; isosceles for advance legends.** ¶4 Option: brown destination and supplemental signs; brown Advance and Exit Direction signs where the crossroad leads only to the area. ¶5 **Standard: Exit Gore signs always white on green; exit number plaques match the sign below.** ¶6 **Standard: white on green where the crossroad also serves other destinations.** **Figures 2M-2 to 2M-10** (2M-3 note: reduced offsets per 2A.16; symbol figures: \* only within areas with speed limits ≤ 25 mph).
**2M.10** Memorial or dedication signing. ¶1–¶3 Support: not named highways; not needed for navigation. ¶4–¶6 Guidance: **not on or along the highway or on bridges; a marker at a rest area, overlook or similar place with parking**; white on brown; mixed-case name. ¶7 **Standard: if on the mainline — never on directional guide signs; not interfering with devices or safety; one independent post-mounted sign per direction.** ¶8 **Standard: rectangle; only the name and a simple message such as "Dedicated to"; no biography, graphics, pictographs, logos or symbols; SHS lettering; no route number or official name.** ¶9–¶10 Standard: never implying renaming; never on supplemental or other information signs. ¶11 Guidance: freeways and expressways not signed as memorial highways.

**Chapter 2M complete.**

## Chapter 2N — Emergency management signs (PDF 572–…)
**2N.01** Guidance: evacuation contingency plans (controlled designated highways; essential-traffic operations; emergency aid centres).
**2N.02** ¶1 **Standard: Emergency Management signs shall be used to guide and control traffic during an emergency.** ¶2–¶3 Guidance: cover conflicting regulatory/warning signs; remove EM signs afterwards (except Evacuation Route signs) and restore the normal ones. ¶4 **Standard: advance planning is the responsibility of State and local authorities.** ¶6 Standard: sizes per **Table 2N-1**. ¶9–¶10 Guidance: replace with standard signs as conditions permit; light, economical materials. ¶12 Option: contamination symbol on EM4-1b/1c. **Figure 2N-1** (note: HURRICANE is one example; other types may be used).
**Table 2N-1** (read from the page) Emergency Management Sign Sizes — Minimum Size: Evacuation Route **"EM1-1, EM-1a, EM1-2"** 24×24\*; EM2-1 30×24; EM2-2 30×24; EM2-3 24×30; EM2-4 24×30; EM3-1…1c 30×24; EM4-1…1c 30×24. Footnote \* **18×18 may be used on low-volume roadways or roadways with speeds of 25 mph or less.** Notes: larger allowed; W×H inches.
**2N.03** Evacuation Route. ¶1 **Standard: M5 or M6 arrow plaque (white on blue) below every EM1-2.** ¶2 Option: HURRICANE or another type above EVACUATION ROUTE on EM1-1a. ¶3 Standard: white arrow — vertical, horizontal, or bent for advance turns. ¶4 Guidance: 150–300 ft before and at each turn; confirmation where needed. ¶5 **Standard: urban — right side, ≥ 7 ft above the curb, ≥ 1 ft back from its face; rural — right side, ≥ 7 ft above the pavement edge, 6 to 10 ft right of the roadway edge.** ¶6 **Standard: not conflicting with other signs; regulatory signs take precedence.** ¶7 Option: may take precedence over guide or warning signs. ¶8–¶9 Guidance: placement supervised by the officials over other signs; continuity agreements; EM1-1a and EM1-2 only where different evacuations use different routes.
**2N.04** AREA CLOSED (Guidance: on the shoulder or on a portable mounting/barricade in the roadway; **bottom ≤ 4 ft above the pavement**; not creating a complete blockage without advance warning; at intersections offering a detour).
**2N.05** TRAFFIC CONTROL POINT (Guidance: where traffic stops to be checked; **with a STOP sign, mounted directly below it**).
**2N.06** MAINTAIN TOP SAFE SPEED (Option: may cover an existing Speed Limit sign). ¶3 Support: **no numeric minimum can be set**. ¶5 **Standard: rural — right side, 6–10 ft from the roadway edge, ≥ 5 ft high; urban — ≥ 7 ft above the curb, ≥ 1 ft back.**

# PART 3 — MARKINGS

## Chapter 3A — General (PDF 578–580)
**3A.01** ¶1–¶2 Support: markings supplement or act alone; forms include road-surface and curb markings, delineators, colored pavements, channelizing devices. ¶3 **Standard: each standard marking only with its prescribed meaning, including in applications the Manual does not describe.** ¶4 **Standard: markings needed at night retroreflective unless adequately lit (except Chapter 3H); all Interstate markings retroreflective.** ¶5 Standard: inapplicable, confusing markings removed or obliterated as soon as practicable. ¶6 Option: temporary masking with non-reflective pavement-colored tape.
**3A.02** Materials (Guidance: color over life; minimise tripping and loss of traction; Option: droplet systems).
**3A.03** Colors. ¶1 **Standard: yellow, white, red, blue or purple, to standard highway colors.** ¶2 Option: black for contrast. ¶3 **Standard: yellow longitudinal lines — A opposite directions, B left edge of divided highways, one-way streets and ramps, C two-way left-turn and reversible lanes.** ¶4 **Standard: white — A same direction, B right edge, C both edges of a reversible roadway.** ¶5 **Standard: red RPMs or delineators — A truck escape ramps, B roadways or lanes not to be entered from the direction they are visible.** ¶6 Standard: blue supplements white for accessible parking. ¶7 Standard: purple per Chapter 3F for ETC-account-only toll plaza lanes. ¶8 Standard: route-shield markings in route-sign colors (2D.11).
**3A.04** Functions, widths, patterns. ¶1 **Standard: double — maximum or special restriction; solid — discourages or prohibits crossing; broken — permissive; dotted lane line — warns of a downstream change in lane function; dotted extension — guides through intersections, tapers, ramp areas.** ¶2 **Standard: normal 4–6 in; wide ≥ 2 × normal; double — two parallel lines with visible pavement between (except contrast markings); broken — normal segments with gaps; dotted — shorter segments and gaps; extensions at least as wide as the line extended.** ¶3 Guidance: double-line space ≤ 2 × single-line width. ¶5 Support: 6-in lines help run-off-road safety and driving automation systems. ¶6–¶7 Guidance: **broken 10-ft segments / 30-ft gaps (or similar ratio); dotted lane lines 3 ft / 9 ft; extensions 2 ft / 2–6 ft.**
**3A.05** Marking retroreflectivity. ¶1 **Standard: a method to maintain ≥ 50 mcd/m²/lx (dry) for longitudinal markings on roads with speed limits ≥ 35 mph, except ¶5.** ¶2 Guidance: ≥ 100 on roads ≥ 70 mph. ¶3 Guidance: FHWA-SA-22-028 (2022) methods or a study. ¶4 Support: 30-m geometry (88.76° entrance, 1.05° observation). ¶5 Option: exclusions A–F (**lit areas; ADT < 6,000**; dotted extensions; curb, parking-space and shared-use-path markings). ¶6: not for transverse, word/symbol/arrow, crosswalk, chevron/diagonal/crosshatch markings. ¶7–¶8 Support: special circumstances; **compliance holds if the agency takes reasonable timely action under its method**.

## Chapter 3B — Pavement and curb markings (PDF 581–…)
**3B.01** Yellow center lines. ¶1 Standard: separate opposite directions; yellow. ¶2–¶3 Option: not at the geometric centre; short sections (curves, hills, grade crossings, bridges). ¶4 **Standard: two-lane two-way roads use A broken yellow (passing both ways), B broken + solid (passing one way), or C double solid (no passing).** ¶5 **Standard: never a single solid yellow center line on a two-way roadway.** ¶6 **Standard: undivided two-way roads with ≥ 4 through lanes always available → double solid yellow, except reversible or two-way left-turn lanes.** ¶8 Guidance: three through lanes — two lanes one way by no-passing markings. ¶9 Option: curved transitions. **Figures 3B-1 to 3B-3** (notes: see 3B.11; see 3B.20, 3B.23).
**3B.02** Center line warrants. ¶1 **Standard: all paved undivided two-way urban arterials and collectors with traveled way ≥ 20 ft and ADT ≥ 6,000; and all paved undivided two-way roads with ≥ 3 through lanes.** ¶2 **Guidance: urban arterials/collectors ≥ 20 ft and ADT ≥ 4,000; rural arterials/collectors ≥ 18 ft and ADT ≥ 3,000; others by study.** ¶3 Guidance: judgement below 16 ft. ¶4 Option: others ≥ 16 ft. ¶5 Option: ADT estimated by judgement where no count exists.
**3B.03** No-passing zones. ¶1 Standard: one- or two-direction no-passing markings. ¶2 **Standard: at A two-way lane-reduction transitions, B approaches to obstructions passed on the right, C grade-crossing approaches (8C.02), D crosswalk approaches.** ¶3 Standard: on two- and three-lane roads with center lines, at curves and other locations where a study shows passing must be prohibited. ¶4 **Standard: at curves where passing sight distance is less than Table 3B-1 "for the 85th-percentile speed or the speed limit".** ¶5–¶6 Support: 3.5-ft eye/object heights; zone ends at points a and b. ¶7 Guidance: **zones < 400 ft apart are connected**. ¶9 **Standard: three-lane roads whose center lane changes direction — a no-passing buffer (flush median island ≥ 50 ft) with lane-reduction transitions at each end.**
**Table 3B-1** (read from the page) Minimum Passing Sight Distances — "85th-Percentile or Speed Limit" · distance: 25 · 450 · 30 · 500 · 35 · 550 · 40 · 600 · 45 · 700 · 50 · 800 · 55 · 900 · 60 · 1,000 · 65 · 1,100 · 70 · 1,200 (mph · feet).
**3B.04** Reversible lanes. ¶1 Standard: broken double yellow on each side. ¶2 Standard: supplemented by signs (2B.34) and/or lane-use control signals.
**3B.05** Two-way left-turn lanes. ¶1 Standard: broken yellow toward the TWLTL, solid yellow toward traffic. ¶2 Guidance: white TWLTL arrows at or just after the start.

Notes (read together)
- Graded authority by threshold (3B.02): Standard (urban ≥ 20 ft, ADT ≥ 6,000; any ≥ 3 lanes), Guidance (urban ADT ≥ 4,000; rural ≥ 18 ft, ADT ≥ 3,000), Option (≥ 16 ft), judgement (< 16 ft).
- 3A.05 ¶8 and 2A.22 ¶3 share the same shape: compliance means having and using a method, not every marking or sign meeting the value at every moment. Table 1B-1 dates the 3A.05 method (6 Sep 2026).
- CHECK passed: 3A.03 ¶7 (purple for ETC-only lanes) agrees with 1D.05 J and 2F.03; 3A.03 ¶8 with 2D.11; #34 ("need not be at the geometrical center") with 3B.01 ¶2.
- **Item 7 widened** (the one open question on rows): 3B.03 ¶4 and Table 3B-1 key on "85th-percentile **or** speed limit", with point rows (25, 30 … 70 mph). A measured 85th-percentile speed (e.g. 43 mph) falls between rows, and speeds below 25 or above 70 are not listed. Same question as the curve tables: how the rows are applied in practice, and which speed is used.

**Figures 3B-4, 3B-5** (notes: opposing no-passing zones may or may not overlap; buffer length per 3B.03, taper L per 3B.12, flush island per 3J.03).
**3B.05 (cont.)** ¶3 Option: more TWLTL arrows by judgement. ¶4 **Standard: no single-direction arrow in a lane bordered on both sides by TWLTL markings.** ¶5–¶6 Guidance: with signs (2B.32); **TWLTL markings do not extend into intersections (as defined in 1C.02)**. ¶7 Option: transitions to mandatory left-turn lanes or painted islands. **Figures 3B-6, 3B-7** (B: intersecting driveways — consistent with #112(b)).
**3B.06** White lane lines. ¶1 Standard: white. ¶2 **Standard: on all freeways and Interstate highways.** ¶3 Guidance: roadways with two or more lanes one way; congested locations. ¶5 Standard: **broken white where crossing with care is allowed** (except 3B.07 ¶1). ¶6 Standard: **solid white where crossing is discouraged.** ¶7 Guidance: solid on approaches — through vs mandatory turn lanes, between turn lanes, toll lanes. ¶8–¶10 Option: solid between through lanes on approaches, beside auxiliary lanes, before multi-lane crosswalks; wide solid; curved transitions. ¶12 **Standard: double solid white where crossing is prohibited.** **Figure 3B-8**.
**3B.07** Non-continuing lanes. Standard: **normal dotted white between a through lane and a deceleration or acceleration lane** (¶1); dotted extensions from the taper to the theoretical gore (parallel decel, ¶2) or from the gore through the taper (tapered decel, ¶3); parallel acceleration lanes — dotted from the gore to at least half-way to the end of the taper (¶4). ¶5–¶6 Option: extensions to the end of the taper. ¶7 **Standard: wide dotted white — A lane drops at exit ramps, B splits with dedicated lanes, C splits with an option lane, D auxiliary lanes between entrance and exit, E lane drops at intersections, F auxiliary lanes between intersections.** ¶8 Guidance: at freeway exits, **begin ≥ ½ mile before the theoretical gore**. ¶10 Guidance: at intersections, by judgement, not closer than the most upstream related sign. ¶11 Guidance: **3-ft segments, 9-ft gaps**. ¶14–¶15 Option: a solid portion (not all); curved transitions. **Figures 3B-9 to 3B-11**.
**3B.08** Channelizing lines. ¶1 Support: neutral areas (ramps, managed-lane access, toll bypasses, separated left-turn lanes), speed management. ¶3 **Standard: solid wide or double solid white, except 3E.04 and 3J.05 ¶6.** ¶5 **Standard: both sides of the neutral area at all exit ramps and at entrance ramps with parallel acceleration lanes.**

Notes (read together)
- CHECK passed: 3B.06 ¶5–¶6, ¶12 apply 3A.04 ¶1 (broken permissive, solid discourages, double restricts); 3B.07 ¶11 = 3A.04 ¶7 (3 ft / 9 ft); 3B.08 ¶3 = #36 (channelizing line "a solid wide or double white line"); 3B.05 ¶6 relies on #112 (driveways are not intersections unless controlled).
- Terms kept apart by definition show up in markings too: lane drops (#117) get **wide dotted** lines (3B.07 ¶7); lane reductions (#119) get transition markings (3B.12).

**Figures 3B-11, 3B-12** (lane-drop markings A–F; conventional road A–B; note: word-marking spacing per 3B.20).
**3B.08 (cont.)** ¶6 Standard: tapered acceleration lanes — channelizing lines both sides to at least half-way to the theoretical gore. ¶7–¶8 Standard: both sides of neutral areas at open-road-tolling bifurcations and managed-lane access/egress points. ¶9 Option: to the gore. ¶10 **Standard: other markings in the neutral area are white.** ¶12 Option: curved transitions.
**3B.09** Edge lines. ¶1 Standard: right or left roadway edges. ¶2 **Standard: right edge — normal solid white** (except 3E.04). ¶3 **Standard: left edge on divided highways, one-way streets, ramps — normal solid yellow** (except 3E.04). ¶5–¶6: wide lines; ≥ 6 in beneficial everywhere. ¶7 Guidance: not through intersections except dotted extensions or the far side of a T.
**3B.10** Edge line warrants. ¶1 **Standard: freeways, expressways, and rural arterials ≥ 20 ft with ADT ≥ 6,000.** ¶2 Guidance: rural arterials and collectors ≥ 20 ft with ADT ≥ 3,000; others by study. ¶3 Guidance: not where study or judgement shows they would decrease safety. ¶4–¶7 Option: with or without center lines; omit by judgement (curbs, parking); omit outside a marked bike lane; to keep traffic off weak shoulders.
**3B.11** Through intersections and interchanges. ¶1 **Standard: extensions the same color as the lines they extend.** ¶2 Guidance: at least the same width. ¶3 Guidance: dotted extensions **2-ft segments, 2–6-ft gaps** at complex intersections. ¶4 Guidance: solid lines or channelizing lines for greater restriction. ¶5 **Standard: center line extensions are dotted.** ¶6 Guidance: a single line for a double line. ¶7 **Standard: no solid edge-line extensions through intersections except where there is no intersecting approach (far side of a T).** ¶8–¶9 Guidance: edge lines discontinued across intersecting approaches; **maintained across driveways that are not intersections (1C.02)**. ¶10 Option: dotted edge extensions. **Figure 3B-13**.
**3B.12** Lane-reduction transitions. ¶1 Support: definition (narrowing or on-street parking, not at an interchange or intersection). ¶4 **Standard: A a no-passing zone in the direction of convergence and through the transition (not on one-way streets, expressways, freeways); B an edge line in that direction, except ¶6.** ¶5 Guidance: edge line from the Lane Ends sign past the start of the narrower roadway. ¶6 **Option: edge line may be omitted where operating speeds are < 25 mph and curbs define the edge, or a through lane becomes a parking lane.** ¶7 Guidance: A delineators (except ¶13, 3G.03 ¶2); **B lane-reduction arrows where the speed limit is ≥ 45 mph**; **C broken lane line ends ¼ of the advance placement distance (2C.04, Table 2C-3) between the Lane Ends sign and the start of the taper.** ¶8 **Guidance: speed limit ≥ 45 mph → L = W·S; speed limit < 45 mph → L = W·S²/60; S = the 85th-percentile speed or the speed limit, whichever is higher.** ¶9 Guidance: **minimum taper 100 ft urban, 200 ft rural.** ¶10 Guidance: longer where observed speeds exceed limits. ¶11–¶16 Option: < 100 ft where operating speed < 25 mph; design speed on new construction; **delineators omitted on "low-speed urban roadways"** with curbs or parking-lane transitions; arrows below 45 mph; arrows in long acceleration lanes; dotted line to the taper start. **Figure 3B-14** (notes: L, W, AP defined; see 2C.47).
**3B.13** Obstructions. ¶1 **Standard: tapered lines from the center or lane line to 1–2 ft right (or both sides) of the obstruction's approach end.** ¶2–¶3 Guidance: the same L = W·S / W·S²/60 formulas and 100/200-ft minimums. ¶4 Option: < 100 ft under 25 mph. ¶5 **Standard: pass-right-only obstructions — two-direction no-passing marking at least twice the diagonal length.** ¶6 Option: yellow diagonals in the flush median. ¶7 **Standard: pass-either-side — two channelizing lines diverging, preceded by a wide solid or double solid white line for the diverging length.** ¶8 Option: white chevrons and other white devices in the neutral area. **Figure 3B-15** (note: "For **speeds** 45 mph or more: L = WS; for speeds less than 45 mph: L = WS²/60; S = 85th-percentile speed or the speed limit, whichever is higher"; minimums 100 / 200 ft; extend for sight distance).
**3B.14** Raised pavement markers. ¶1 Standard: color matches the marking they guide, supplement or replace. ¶2–¶3 Option: red on the wrong-way side; RPMs at curbed median and island ends. ¶4 **Standard: internally illuminated RPMs steady, never flashed.** ¶5 Support: **flashing RPMs are In-Roadway Warning Lights (Chapter 4U).** ¶6 Guidance: spacing follows the broken-line pattern.

Notes (read together)
- **COMPUTED taper length (3B.12 ¶8, 3B.13 ¶2)**: the formula is chosen by the **speed limit** (≥ 45 → L = W·S; < 45 → L = W·S²/60), and S = **max(85th-percentile speed, speed limit)**. E.g. limit 40, 85th 50 → L = W·50²/60 ≈ 41.7·W. Floors: 100 ft urban, 200 ft rural (Guidance).
- Figure 3B-15's note says "For **speeds** 45 mph or more"; the text says "roadways having a **speed limit** of 45 mph or greater". By 1A.04 ¶5 the text classifies the figure — the branch is on the speed limit.
- Where the manual wants a particular speed it says so ("whichever is higher", 3B.12 ¶8). Tables 2C-3 and 3B-1 say only "posted or 85th-percentile" / "85th-percentile or speed limit" — the open item 7.
- **Earlier flag re-read (3B.12 ¶6 vs ¶13)**: ¶6 (edge line omission, "operating speeds less than 25 mph") and ¶13 (delineator omission, "low-speed urban roadways") are Options for **different devices**; their conditions need not match, and ¶6's number does not define ¶13's "low-speed".
- 3B.12 ¶7C is a cross-table computation: the lane line ends at ¼ of the Table 2C-3 advance placement distance.
- CHECK passed: 3B.09 ¶2–¶3 = 3A.03 ¶3–¶4 (yellow left edges, white right edges); 3B.11 ¶9 = #112(b); 3B.14 ¶5 = #103(b) (in-roadway warning lights are highway traffic signals).
- Part 6 cross-check to make: Table 6B-4's taper formulas (previously flagged gap for 41–44 mph) against 3B.12 ¶8's "< 45 / ≥ 45" split.

**3B.14 (cont.)** ¶7 **Standard: N = one line segment plus one gap of the broken lines used** (= 1C.03). ¶8 Option: closer spacing by judgement or study.
**3B.15** RPMs as positioning guides (Option; Guidance **2N**; Option N or less on curves and transitions, **3N** on straight, level freeways).
**3B.16** RPMs supplementing markings (Guidance): pairs for double and wide lines; **solid lines ≤ N, channelizing and edge lines ≤ N/2; broken lines ≤ 3N, reversible-lane broken lines ≤ N**; one per segment on intersection extensions; ≤ N through interchanges. ¶2 Guidance: not on right edge lines unless benefits outweigh effects on bicyclists and they do not simulate a broken line when wet. ¶3–¶4 Option: islands, gores, obstructions, wrong-way arrows; curves plus **~5 seconds of travel** in advance.
**3B.17** RPMs substituting for markings. ¶1 Option. ¶2 Guidance: simulate the pattern. ¶3 **Standard: non-retroreflective RPMs never alone as a substitute.** ¶5 **Standard: for broken lines — groups of 3–5 markers spaced ≤ N/8; if N ≠ 40 ft, equally spaced over the segment (½, ⅓, ¼ points); at least one retroreflective marker per group or one midway in each gap.** ¶6 **Standard: for solid lines — ≤ N/4, retroreflective ≤ N/2.** ¶7 Guidance: right edge lines as 3B.16 ¶2. ¶8 **Standard: for dotted lines — ≤ N/4, ≥ 1 per segment, a retroreflective marker every N.** ¶9 Option: side-by-side for wide lines.
**3B.18** Curb markings for parking (Guidance: signs preferred, especially where snow hides curbs; a word marking if curbs are used without signs). ¶3 Option: plain curb markings for **statutory** prohibitions near STOP/YIELD signs, driveways, hydrants, crosswalks. ¶4 **Option: local agencies may prescribe special colors for curb markings.**
**3B.19** Stop and yield lines. ¶1 Option: stop lines with STOP, R1-5b, R1-5c, "**R-5e**" or other stop-requiring devices (not YIELD, except at passive grade crossings). ¶2 **Standard: solid white across the approach lanes.** ¶3 **Standard: no stop lines where drivers must yield (YIELD, R1-5, R1-5a, R1-5d, or by State law at uncontrolled approaches), except 8C.03.** ¶4–¶5 Guidance: at signals (4D.08); **12–24 in wide**. ¶6 Option: omit at ramp meters. ¶8–¶10 **Standard: yield lines only with a yield-requiring device; never where a stop is required; a row of solid white isosceles triangles pointing toward approaching traffic.** ¶12 Guidance: triangle base **12–24 in**, height **1.5 × base**, gaps **3–12 in**. ¶13 Guidance: **≥ 4 ft before the nearest crosswalk line** (not roundabout yield lines or midblock crosswalks); without a crosswalk, at the stopping point, **≤ 30 ft and ≥ 4 ft from the intersecting traveled way**. ¶14 **Standard: yield (stop) lines at uncontrolled multi-lane crosswalks require R1-5 series signs.** ¶15–¶16, ¶18 Guidance: **20–50 ft** in advance; no parking between line and crosswalk; not at circular intersections. ¶20 Option: staggered by lane. **Figure 3B-16**.
**3B.20** Word, symbol, arrow markings. ¶1 Support: symbols preferable to words. ¶4 **Standard: white, except 3A.03 and 3B.22.** ¶5 Standard: SHS designs. ¶6–¶9 Guidance: ≤ 3 lines; **spacing ≥ 4 × character height on low-speed roads, never > 10 ×** (TWLTL arrows and 3B.26 B, D excepted); ≤ one lane wide (except SCHOOL); scaled to the facility. ¶10–¶11 Option: smaller on narrow low-speed paths; **25 % smaller where operating speed < 25 mph**.
**3B.21** Word markings. ¶1 Guidance: **letters ≥ 6 ft** (except 9E.01, 9E.15). ¶2 Guidance: read in the direction of travel, first word nearest. ¶3 **Standard: STOP not before a stop line unless every vehicle must always stop.** ¶4–¶7: ONLY with lane-drop signs and arrows (Guidance); ONLY in turn bays, with preferential markings (Option); 25 % reduction < 25 mph. ¶8 **Standard: ONLY never in a lane shared by more than one movement.** **Figure 3B-17**.
**3B.22** Symbol markings. ¶3–¶5: elongated route-shield markings (Option; Guidance: in option lanes if in any lane; in sequence). ¶8 Guidance: accessibility symbol in each accessible space. ¶9–¶10 Option: blue background; yield-ahead triangle or YIELD AHEAD words. ¶11 **Standard: yield-ahead markings only where a YIELD sign is in place; triangle per Figure 3B-20.** ¶12 Option: pedestrian symbol. **Figures 3B-18 to 3B-20** (3B-20: separate designs for speed limits ≥ 45 and < 45 mph).
**3B.23** Lane-use arrows. ¶3–¶5 Guidance: in exclusive turn lanes and bays (unless clearly discouraged otherwise) and where movements depart from normal rules; ≥ 2 arrows in turn lanes; at offset left-turn lane ends. ¶6 Option. ¶8 **Standard: through lanes becoming mandatory turn lanes → turn arrows required, with standard signs (2B.28).** ¶9 Guidance: ONLY words too, repeated well in advance. ¶10 Option: freeway exit lanes.
**3B.24** Wrong-way arrows (Guidance: lane-use arrows on exit ramps near the crossroad; Option: wrong-way arrows, Drawing G). **Figure 3B-21** (notes: SHS; scaling per 3B.20).
**3B.25** Chevrons and diagonals. ¶2 Option A–G locations. ¶5 **Standard: chevrons white, pointing toward approaching traffic (except 3J.03).** ¶6–¶7 Option: diagonals between opposing directions, flush medians, buffers; shoulders, no-parking zones. ¶8 **Standard: diagonals between opposing directions or on left shoulders — yellow, slanting away from adjacent traffic.** ¶9 **Standard: on right shoulders or no-parking zones — white, slanting away.** ¶10 Guidance: **≥ 12 in wide where speed limit ≥ 45 mph, ≥ 8 in where < 45 mph**; angle 30–45°. ¶11 Option: 4 in in no-parking zones or below 25 mph operating speed. **Figure 3B-22**.
**3B.26** Do Not Block Intersection. ¶1 Option: near signals, railroad crossings; elsewhere if authorized by law. ¶2 **Standard: one of A (outline), B (outline + DO NOT BLOCK/KEEP CLEAR), C (outline + cross-hatching), D (words only).** ¶3 **Standard: accompanied by R10-7, R8-8 or similar signs.**
**3B.27** ¶1 Standard: on-street parking space markings white.

Notes (read together)
- CHECK passed: 3B.19 ¶14–¶16, ¶18 = 2B.19 ¶2–¶3, ¶6–¶7 (R1-5 signs with lines at uncontrolled multi-lane crosswalks; 20–50 ft; no parking; not at circular intersections); 3B.19 ¶10 = #295 (yield line); 3B.21 ¶8 parallels 2B.29 ¶3 (ONLY never for shared movements); 3B.23 ¶8 with 2B.28 and 2C.47 ¶11 (lane drops get regulatory signs and arrows); 3B.24 = 2B.48 ¶2B; 3B.14 ¶7 = 1C.03 N; "N ≠ 40 ft" matches the 10-ft/30-ft broken line of 3A.04 ¶6.
- 3B.18 ¶4 (Option: special colors for curb markings) and 3A.03 ¶1 (Standard: yellow, white, red, blue, purple): an Option may modify a Standard (1C.01), specifically for curb markings.
- 3B.19 ¶1 prints "R-5e"; the sign named (Stop Here for Trail Crossing) is **R1-5e** in 3B.19 ¶9, 2B.19 and Table 2B-1. Recorded by the sign named.
- MEAN COMPUTED spacings in units of N (3B.15–3B.17): N, N/2, N/4, N/8, 2N, 3N, with equal division when N ≠ 40 ft.
- MEAN a time-based distance: "approximately 5 seconds of travel time" (3B.16 ¶4).
- X statute-dependent: curb-only parking prohibitions (3B.18 ¶3); Do Not Block markings elsewhere "if authorized by law" (3B.26 ¶1).

**Figure 3B-23** parking space markings. **3B.27 (cont.)** ¶3 Option: blue lines supplementing accessible spaces.
**3B.28** Speed reduction markings. ¶1–¶2 Support: progressively closer transverse lines; effective with curve warning signs. ¶3 Option: before unexpectedly severe curves where signs have not achieved the reduction. ¶4 Guidance: only for unexpected curves or by judgement; **not on long tangents or where drivers are mostly local (e.g. school zones)**; supplement, never substitute. ¶5 **Standard: white transverse lines on both sides of the lane, perpendicular to the longitudinal lines.** ¶6–¶7 Guidance: spacing decreasing downstream; **≤ 12 in wide, ≤ 18 in into the lane**. ¶8 **Standard: only in lanes with a longitudinal line on both sides.** **Figures 3B-24** (note: the outline defines the area; need not be rectangular), **3B-25**.
**3B.29** Speed hump / table markings. ¶1 **Standard: white; Figure 3B-26 options A–C for humps without crosswalks; Figure 3B-27 A–B for humps that are crosswalks or tables.** ¶2 Option: longitudinal lines may stop on the hump profile. ¶3 **Standard: a hump or table functioning as a raised crosswalk gets crosswalk markings (3C).**
**3B.30** Advance speed hump markings (Option; BUMP/HUMP words; signs per 2C.27). ¶3 **Standard: eight white 12-in transverse lines, longer and closer toward the hump, per Figure 3B-28.** ¶4 Guidance: in each approach lane.
**3B.31** Diamond interchange with transposed crossroad (DDI). ¶2 **Standard: each direction treated as a one-way roadway; yellow and white edge lines per 3B.09.** ¶3 **Standard: a lane-use arrow in each approach lane at the crossovers.** ¶5 **Standard: no flush median islands between the inverted flows.** ¶6 Guidance: extensions through the crossovers. **Figure 3B-29**.

## Chapter 3C — Crosswalk markings (PDF 630–635)
**3C.01** Support: guidance and alerting; ¶3 **at non-intersection locations, crosswalk markings legally establish the crosswalk** (cf. #50(b)); ¶4 detectable warnings — ADA Standards 2010, 28 CFR 35/36 (external).
**3C.02** Application. ¶1 Guidance: at signals, install crosswalk markings; ¶2 Option: omit by judgement. ¶3 Guidance: STOP/YIELD approaches where judgement shows a need. ¶4 **Guidance: at uncontrolled approaches an engineering study before marking, considering A–N** (lanes, median, distance to controlled crossings, volumes, paths, ages and abilities, delays, transit, ADT, speed limit or 85th-percentile speed, geometry, consolidation, lighting, other). ¶5 **Standard: crosswalk markings at legally established non-intersection crosswalks.** ¶6 **Guidance: consider further measures (speed reduction, shorter crossings, awareness, active warning) where any of A four+ lanes without a raised median/refuge and ADT ≥ 12,000; B four+ lanes with one and ADT ≥ 15,000; C posted speed limit ≥ 40 mph; D multiple-threat crashes predominate; E visibility cannot be secured by parking prohibitions.** ¶7–¶13: pointers (4J PHB, 4L RRFB, 4S.03, 4U.02, 7C, 7D, 9E.13).
**3C.03** Design. ¶2 Support: transverse-line vs high-visibility. ¶3 **Standard: white; transverse lines 6–24 in wide.** ¶4 Support: near 24 in where no stop/yield line or approach speeds exceed 35 mph. ¶5–¶6 **Standard: minimum width 6 ft, except 8 ft at non-intersection crosswalks where the posted speed limit is ≥ 40 mph.** ¶7–¶8 Guidance: **high-visibility markings and warning signs at all non-intersection crosswalks**; parking prohibitions on approaches. ¶9 **Standard: curb ramps within the extension of the markings.** ¶10 Guidance: full width. ¶12 Standard: white paving materials as crosswalk lines — white additives, retroreflective. **Figure 3C-1** (note repeats the 8-ft minimum).
**3C.04** Transverse-line crosswalks (Guidance: at signals or STOP/YIELD approaches).
**3C.05** High-visibility (Option; Support: bar, ladder, bar pair; benefits A–F). ¶4 **Standard: at least three longitudinal elements; a bar pair counts as one.** ¶5–¶7 Guidance: uniform dimensions and spacing (across approaches and both sides of a median); parallel to traffic. ¶8 Option: stagger spacing to avoid wheel paths and lines.
**3C.06** Longitudinal bars. **Standard: bar width 12–24 in; spacing 12–60 in and ≤ 2.5 × bar width.**
**3C.07** Ladder. **Standard: transverse lines 6–24 in; interior bars 12–24 in; spacing 12–60 in and ≤ 2.5 × bar width.** ¶7 Option: bars rotated up to 45° for foreshortening.
**3C.08** Bar pairs. ¶3 **Standard: each bar 8–12 in; gap within the pair = one bar width.** ¶4 **Standard: spacing between pairs "not … less than 24 inches or greater than 60 inches, or 2.5 times the width of the total width of a bar pair".** ¶5 Standard: no transverse lines with bar pairs.
**3C.09** Circular intersections. ¶1 **Standard: no crosswalk markings to or from a roundabout central island.** ¶2–¶3 Guidance: mark across entrances and exits; **≥ 20 ft from the circulatory roadway** at YIELD approaches and uncontrolled exits.
**3C.10** Diagonal crossings with exclusive pedestrian phases (Option: Figure 3C-2; Guidance: diagonal segments not high-visibility; note: high-visibility perimeter crosswalks allowed).
**3C.11** DDI crosswalks (Guidance: mark at crossovers and ramp terminals; **≥ 20 ft** from intersecting ramps at yield-controlled movements).
**3C.12** Pedestrian islands and medians (Support; ADA Standards for refuge widths).

## Chapter 3D — Circular intersection markings (PDF 636–…)
**3D.01** Guidance: markings and signs designed together; consistent approach and circulatory messages. Support: types; figures 3D-1 to 3D-8.
**3D.02** Lane lines. ¶1 **Standard: multi-lane approaches have lane lines.** ¶2 **Standard: a through lane becoming a dropped lane at a roundabout gets the dotted white lane line of 3B.07.** ¶3 Guidance: lane lines within multi-lane circulatory roadways through the departure. ¶4 **Standard: never continuous concentric lane lines within the circulatory roadway.** ¶5 Option: channelizing lines, chevrons, diagonals.
**3D.03** Circulatory edge lines. ¶1–¶2 Guidance: white outer edge line — solid beside splitter islands, **wide dotted across entering lanes**. ¶3 **Standard: no edge lines or extensions across roundabout exits.** ¶4 Option: yellow inner edge line.

Notes (read together)
- MEAN COMPUTED maximum spacing: longitudinal and ladder bars — max spacing = min(60 in, 2.5 × bar width) (3C.06 ¶3, 3C.07 ¶6); e.g. 12-in bars → 30 in.
- **Question for the engineer (minor, with proposed reading) — 3C.08 ¶4**: bar-pair spacing "not … greater than 60 inches, **or** 2.5 times the … total width of a bar pair". A bar pair is 3 × bar width (24–36 in), so 2.5 × is 60–90 in. Read like 3C.06/3C.07 (an additional cap, the lesser), 60 in always governs; read as alternatives, up to 90 in with 12-in bars. Proposed: the lesser (60 in), by the pattern of the sister provisions.
- Speeds of different kinds: 3C.03 ¶4 (Support) speaks of approach speeds > 35 mph; ¶6 (Standard) and 3C.02 ¶6C use the **posted speed limit** ≥ 40 mph.
- CHECK passed: 3C.01 ¶3 fits #50(b) (marked crosswalks anywhere); 3C.03 ¶3 (white) = 3A.03 ¶4 principles; 3B.27 ¶3 = 3A.03 ¶6 (blue supplements white); 3B.29 ¶3 ties humps functioning as crosswalks to 3C; 3D.02 ¶2 applies #117 (lane drop) and 3B.07; 3C.09 ¶3 (≥ 20 ft) matches 2C.54 ¶11 / 2C.55 ¶6 (crosswalk ≥ 20 ft before the yield point).

**Figures 3D-1 to 3D-8** roundabout marking examples (one-lane, two-lane with one/two-lane approaches and exits, double left, double right, diamond interchange with roundabout terminals).
**3D.04** Yield lines (Option, per 3B.19; TO ALL LANES plaque per 2B.18). **3D.05** YIELD / YIELD AHEAD words and symbols; route shields (Option).
**3D.06** Arrows. Guidance: **not on single-lane approaches**; **should be used on approaches with double left or double right turns**; style matches the regulatory lane-use signs; normal arrows within the circulatory roadway. ¶3 **Standard: no lane-use arrows between the crosswalk and the wide dotted line across the entry.** Option: normal or curved-stem; oval or circle for the island.
**3D.07** Other circular intersections (Option by judgement; mini-roundabout Figure 2B-21).

## Chapter 3E — Preferential lane markings (PDF 647–…)
**3E.01** Support: HOV, ETC, price-managed, bus, taxi, LRT lanes; bicycles in Part 9; colored pavement in 3H.
**3E.02** Longitudinal markings. ¶3 **Standard: edge and lane lines for preferential lanes beside same-direction general-purpose lanes per Table 3E-1.** ¶4 **Standard: between two or more preferential lanes — normal broken white.** ¶5 Standard: regulatory signs per 2G.03–2G.07. ¶7 Guidance: supplemental channelizing devices by judgement. ¶8 Guidance: **buffers wider than 4 ft where crossing is prohibited — chevrons in the buffer.** ¶9 Guidance: conventional-road buffers not mistaken for parking or bike lanes. ¶10 Option: contiguous lanes with wide broken lines — shorter skip, wider line. ¶11 **Standard: dotted white lines separate direct-exit deceleration lanes (and tapers) from the continuing preferential lane.** ¶12 **Standard: signs and/or lane-use control signals supplement reversible markings for part-time counter-flow preferential lanes.** ¶13 **Standard: preferential lanes beside opposite-direction general-purpose lanes per Table 3E-2.** ¶15 Option: cones, tubular markers.
**Table 3E-1** (read from the page) Standard Edge Line and Lane Line Markings — Type · Left-Hand Line · Right-Hand Line: barrier-separated non-reversible — yellow edge · white edge; **barrier-separated reversible — white edge · white edge**; buffer-separated left side — yellow edge · wide solid double white on both buffer edges (crossing prohibited) / wide solid single (discouraged) / wide broken on both edges or within the buffer (permitted); buffer-separated right side — the same three plus **wide dotted within the buffer where crossing is permitted for right turns** · normal white edge line (if warranted); contiguous left side — yellow edge · wide solid double / wide solid single / wide broken lane line; contiguous right side — wide solid double / single / broken / dotted (right turns) · normal white lane line (if warranted). Notes: **1 lane lines between preferential lanes "shall be normal broken white lines"**; 2 "provided in a tabular format for reference". **Figures 3E-1 to 3E-3**.
**Table 3E-2** (read from the page) Counter-Flow Preferential Lanes on Divided Highways — Center Line on Left · Edge Line on Left: part-time contiguous — **normal broken double yellow** · normal white (if warranted); part-time buffer-separated — broken double yellow on both buffer edges · same; full-time contiguous — **normal solid double yellow** · same; full-time buffer-separated — solid double yellow on both buffer edges · same. **Figure 3E-4**.
**3E.03** Word and symbol markings. ¶2 **Standard: every preferential lane marked with one or more of A HOV — white diamond ≥ 2.5 ft wide and 12 ft long with lines ≥ 6 in, or "HOV"; B ETC account-only — the system name or pictograph (e.g. E-Z PASS ONLY), except ¶8; C price-managed — EXPRESS / EXPRESS LANE(S); D BUS ONLY / BUS STOP; E TAXI ONLY / TAXI STAND; F LRT ONLY; G other appropriate words.** (Figure 3E-2 note: HOV symbols at **¼-mile** intervals or by judgement.)

Notes (read together)
- Table 3E-1's note 2 ("for reference") and 3E.02 ¶3 (Standard: "shall be in accordance with Table 3E-1"): by 1A.04 ¶5 the text classifies the table — the markings carry Standard force. Note 1's "shall" repeats 3E.02 ¶4.
- CHECK passed: barrier-separated reversible lanes with white lines both sides = 3A.03 ¶4C; Table 3E-2's part-time broken double yellow = the reversible-lane marking of 3B.04 ¶1; full-time solid double yellow = 3B.01 ¶4C; 3E.02 ¶12 = 3B.04 ¶2; 3E.03 ¶2A/C agree with 2G.03 ¶18 (diamond for HOV only) and 2G.17 ¶5 (EXPRESS); 3E.02 ¶5 = 2G.03 ¶1.

**Figure 3E-3** contiguous preferential lanes (notes: HOV symbols at ¼-mile intervals or by judgement; double yellow center line if the lane's left side is a two-way center line).
**3E.03 (cont.)** ¶3 Guidance: every allowed use marked where a lane has several. ¶4 **Standard: no motorcycle or ILEV markings for lanes they are allowed to use** (¶5: their eligibility is conveyed by regulatory signs). ¶6 **Standard: static or changeable regulatory signs (2G.03–2G.07) with the word/symbol markings.** ¶7 **Standard: markings white, centred in the lane.** ¶8–¶9 Option: omit at toll plazas; lane-use arrows in curb lanes for turning non-preferential traffic. ¶10–¶12 Guidance: markings end near the Preferential Lane Ends sign; spacing by judgement; at decision points, direct exits (EXIT / EXIT ONLY), access openings. ¶13 Option: occupancy numeral after the diamond or HOV. **Figure 3E-4**.
**3E.04** Part-time shoulder travel. ¶2 Support: a permanently opened shoulder is a travel lane (as 2G.20 ¶3). ¶3 **Standard: shoulders open to all traffic — no word or symbol markings.** ¶4 **Standard: shoulders for particular classes — BUS ONLY, TRANSIT ONLY, HOV or the diamond, right after ramps and after intersections; with regulatory signs (2G.03).** ¶5–¶6 **Standard: wide dotted channelizing lines through the shoulder at entrance and exit ramps** (and the entrance extension where ramp traffic stops or yields to shoulder traffic). ¶7 Standard: an additional outside solid edge line per 3B.09–3B.10. ¶8 Guidance: pattern changes at regulatory signs. ¶9–¶10 Option: dotted edge lines at entry/exit points; **red-colored pavement for transit-only shoulders** (3H.07). ¶11 Standard: red pavement discontinued through ramp influence areas. **Figures 3E-5, 3E-6** (notes: see 2G; red pavement optional).

## Chapter 3F — Toll plaza markings (PDF 658)
**3F.01** Support (purple pavement in 3H.08). **3F.02** Guidance: solid white lane lines separating toll lanes and payment methods, from the full-width lane to the plaza. ¶3 Option: purple solid lines inside the lines of an ETC-account-only lane. ¶4 **Standard: purple lines ≥ 3 in wide** (¶5 Guidance: not wider than the line supplemented). ¶6 **Standard: booths and islands are obstructions — markings per 3B.13 and 3J.** ¶7 Option: omit lines beside booth islands.
**3F.03** ¶2 **Standard: ETC-account-only approach lanes carry the 3E.03 word marking or pictograph, except ¶4.** ¶3 **Standard: account-only open-road tolling lanes bypassing a mainline plaza — words or pictographs on the approach to the divergence.** ¶4 Option: omit where physical conditions preclude. ¶5 Guidance: unseparated ORT lanes beside cash lanes — channelizing devices and/or no-lane-change markings from where cash-lane speeds drop below **30 mph** (off-peak) to where departing cash traffic regains 30 mph.

## Chapter 3G — Delineators (PDF 659–662)
**3G.01** Support: guidance devices (not warning); visible when wet or snow-covered. Option: long sections or short alignment changes.
**3G.02** ¶1 **Standard: retroreflective, clearly visible at 1,000 ft under high beams; crashworthy supports.** ¶2 **Standard: elements ≥ 3 in (or 3-in diameter).** ¶3–¶4: single, double, vertically-elongated (Option in place of double).
**3G.03** Application. ¶1 **Standard: color matches the edge lines (3A.03, 3B.09).** ¶2 **Standard: single delineators on the right side of freeways and expressways and on at least one side of interchange ramps, except A (tangents where RPMs are used continuously on lane lines and roadside delineators lead into all curves — both) or B (continuous lighting between interchanges).** ¶3–¶5 Option: other roads; left side; chevrons instead (2C.08). ¶6 **Standard: left-side delineators on two-way roadways are white.** ¶7–¶10 Guidance: outside of ramp curves; a double yellow delineator at official crossovers; **double or elongated delineators at ~100-ft intervals along acceleration and deceleration lanes**; along guardrails and barriers. ¶11 Option: red on the wrong-way side. ¶12–¶14 Guidance: at lane-reduction transitions (except 3B.12 ¶13), for the full length; carried through transitions. ¶15 **Standard: red on truck escape ramps** (¶16 Guidance: both sides). **Figure 3G-1** (notes: constant offset with smooth transitions around obstructions; all delineators shown are white; chevrons may be used).
**3G.04** Placement and spacing (Guidance): **~4 ft high** (Option lower on barriers); **2–8 ft outside the shoulder edge** or in line with a barrier ≤ 8 ft out; constant offset, transitioned inside obstructions; not a pedestrian obstacle; **200–530 ft apart on mainline tangents, 100 ft on ramp tangents**; ¶10 **on curves, the approximate spacing of Table 3G-1 "should be used"**; truck escape ramps ≤ 50 ft at the entrance. Option: closer spacing; relocation ≤ ¼ of the spacing at driveways and intersections (else omitted); transitions before lane changes or obstructions; continuous "ribbon" on barriers.
**Table 3G-1** (read from the page) Approximate Spacing on Horizontal Curves — R · S (feet): 50 · 20 / 115 · 25 / 180 · 35 / 250 · 40 / 300 · 50 / 400 · 55 / 500 · 65 / 600 · 70 / 700 · 75 / 800 · 80 / 900 · 85 / 1,000 · 90. Notes: 1 interpolate for other radii; 2 minimum 20 ft; 3 ≤ 300 ft on curves; 4 **beyond the curve: 2S, 3S, 6S, ≤ 300 ft**; 5 **S = 3√(R − 50)**; 6 rounded to the nearest 5 ft.

## Chapter 3H — Colored pavement (PDF 663–…)
**3H.01** ¶2 Support: **non-retroreflective purely aesthetic colored pavement is not a traffic control device**, even between crosswalk lines. ¶3 **Standard: colored pavement that communicates information (in the traveled way, islands or shoulders), or that is retroreflective, is a traffic control device, limited to this Chapter's colors and uses.** ¶4 **Standard: only where the regulation, warning or guidance applies at all times** (except 3H.07 ¶5). ¶5 Guidance: significant contrast. ¶7 **Standard: only to supplement other markings.** ¶8: longitudinal markings, crosswalks, symbols and route markers are not colored pavement.
**3H.02** Materials (Option retroreflective or not; Guidance traction; Support skid differentials).
**3H.03** Aesthetic treatments. Standard: not interfering with devices (¶5); not confusing to pedestrians relying on tactile cues (¶6); **colors outside the traffic-control chromaticity ranges** (¶7); no advertising or retroreflectivity (¶8); **crosswalk interiors not inviting users to linger or interact** (¶9). Guidance: no degrading of contrast or look-alike patterns; **gap ≥ ½ the transverse-line width and ≥ 6 in beside crosswalk lines**; gap ≥ the longitudinal line width (one line of a double); no pictographs, illusions or symbols. **Figure 3H-1**.
**3H.04** Yellow-colored pavement. ¶2 **Standard: only A median islands between opposing flows, B left shoulders of divided highways, C left shoulders of one-way streets or ramps.** ¶3–¶4 **Standard: never in reversible or two-way left-turn lanes; never on channelizing islands with same-direction traffic on both sides.**

Notes (read together)
- **Table 3G-1 and its formula**: S = 3√(R − 50) rounded to 5 ft reproduces every row except R = 300 ft (formula 47.4 → 45; table 50); R = 50 gives 0, and the table's 20 is note 2's minimum. 3G.04 ¶10 directs the **table's values** ("the approximate spacing shown in Table 3G-1 should be used"); the formula in note 5 explains their derivation. Settled: use the printed values for listed radii, interpolate per note 1 for others. Recorded that the formula alone gives ≈ 47 ft at R = 300.
- CHECK passed: 3E.04 ¶2 = 2G.20 ¶3; 3E.03 ¶4 with 2G.04 ¶2 (ILEV conveyed by signs); 3F.03 with 3E.03 ¶2B; 3G.03 ¶1 and ¶6 with 3A.03 ¶3–¶4 (white left delineators on two-way roads, since left edge lines are yellow only on divided/one-way roads); 3G.03 ¶15 = 3A.03 ¶5A (red for truck escape ramps); 3G.03 ¶12 with 3B.12 ¶7A, ¶13; 3H.04 ¶2 = 3A.03 ¶3 (yellow for opposing flows and left edges).
- MEAN colored pavement is a device or not depending on purpose and retroreflectivity (3H.01 ¶2–¶3) — a classification the graph must make from the site's use, not the material.

**3H.04 (cont.)** ¶5 Option: whole or partial length. **Figure 3H-2** (note: colored pavement optional).
**3H.05** White-colored pavement. ¶2 **Standard: only A same-direction channelizing islands, B right shoulders, C exit gores, D entrance gores.** ¶3 Guidance: on shoulders, only areas not for traffic (emergency shoulders excepted). ¶4–¶5 Option: partial length; **instead of chevrons in neutral areas**. **Figure 3H-3**.
**3H.06** Green-colored pavement (bicycles). ¶2 **Standard: only A bike lanes, B their intersection extensions, C weaving areas at mandatory turn lanes, D two-stage turn boxes, E bike boxes, F detector-symbol backgrounds.** ¶3 **Standard: never in EV parking, crosswalks, behind shared-lane markings, or instead of required bike markings.** ¶4 Standard: patterns match dotted lines. ¶5–¶6 Guidance: separated bikeways only at entrances and conflict points; shared-use paths only where modes are separated. ¶7–¶8 Option. ¶9 Guidance: related signs (9B, 9D). **Figure 3H-4**.
**3H.07** Red-colored pavement (transit). ¶2 Option: where judgement expects higher transit speeds, shorter service times, or less illegal use. ¶3 **Standard: only where general traffic may not use, queue, wait, idle or occupy.** ¶4 **Standard: full lane width.** ¶5–¶7 Option: **full- or part-time**; partial length; **broken or dotted pattern where general traffic may enter** (e.g. before a turn). ¶8 **Standard: regulatory signs establish the use, and any entry for turns or bypassing.** ¶9 Guidance: separate alignments — only at entrances. **Figure 3H-5**.
**3H.08** Purple-colored pavement. ¶1 **Standard: only ETC-account-only toll plaza approach lanes and account-only ORT facilities.** ¶2 **Standard: not in an approach lane that also leads to other payment methods downstream.** ¶3–¶4 Standard: flanked by solid white lane lines (plazas) or edge and lane lines (ORT). ¶5 Option. **Figure 3H-6** (notes: pictographs are examples — the adopted ones **shall** be used; optional).

## Chapter 3I — Channelizing devices for emphasis (PDF 673–675)
**3I.01** ¶1 Option: cones, tubular markers, panels, separators, drums, barricades for emphasis; along center lines to preclude turns, along lane lines to preclude lane changes. ¶2 Support: **raised islands are not considered traffic control devices.** ¶3 **Standard: design per Chapter 6K except color.** ¶4 **Standard: outside TTC zones, the color of the marking supplemented or replaced (3A.03)**, except ¶5 (Option: orange if not permanent — emergencies, special events). ¶7 **Standard: retroreflective or illuminated at night; outside TTC zones white bands between same-direction flows, yellow between opposing flows or along left edges of one-way roads.**
**3I.02** Tubular markers. ¶1 **Standard: permanent markers ≥ 28 in high and ≥ 2 in wide.** ¶2 Guidance: affixed; spacing ≤ N. ¶3 Option: other spacing. ¶5 Guidance: not on the same line as an R1-6 sign they supplement. **Figure 3I-1**.

## Chapter 3J — Islands and sidewalk extensions (PDF 676–…)
**3J.01** Support: island design per AASHTO Green Book 2018 (external). Option: curbs, pavement edges, markings, channelizing devices.
**3J.02** Approach-end treatment (Support; Guidance: approach ends marked; **raised bars or buttons > 1 in high marked white or yellow retroreflective by the flows they separate**). **Figure 3J-1**.
**3J.03** Flush (marked) islands. ¶1 **Standard: formed with channelizing lines — white for same-direction flows, yellow for opposing flows — except ¶2.** ¶2 **Standard: continuous flush medians — two sets of double solid yellow lines; other markings yellow except white crosswalks.** ¶3 **Standard: chevrons/diagonals the same color as the channelizing line.** ¶4–¶5 Option: both chevrons and diagonals; colored pavement per 3H. **Figure 3J-2**.
**3J.04** Curb markings for raised islands. ¶1 **Standard: colors per 3A.03.** ¶2–¶4 Guidance: **retroreflective solid yellow on approach ends where traffic keeps right; white where it may pass either side**; length showing the island's alignment. ¶5–¶6 Option: discontinue where parallel, lit or delineated; omit at median openings.
**3J.05** Raised island markings. ¶2 Option: yellow edge lines beside islands between opposing flows. ¶3 **Standard: same-direction raised islands outlined with white channelizing lines, except ¶4 and ¶6.** ¶4–¶6: omit on small islands (Option) with curb markings (Guidance); **yellow edge line where traffic passes on the right of a same-direction island of discernible size** (Guidance). **Figures 3J-3, 3J-4**.

Notes (read together)
- **Yellow channelizing lines at islands between opposing flows** (3J.03 ¶1) vs 3B.08 ¶3 (channelizing lines white, exceptions 3E.04 and 3J.05 ¶6) and #36 (white): settled by 3A.03 ¶3 (Standard — yellow separates opposing traffic) and 3B.08 ¶1 (channelizing lines describe same-direction neutral areas). 3J.03 ¶1 is the specific provision for opposing-flow islands. 3B.08 ¶3's exception list does not name 3J.03.
- 3H.07 ¶5 (part-time red pavement) is the exception 3H.01 ¶4 names; 3H.07 ¶7 (broken red where entry is allowed) is an Option within ¶3's limit.
- Color by purpose throughout Part 3: yellow — opposing flows and left edges; white — same direction and right edges; red — transit and wrong way/escape ramps; blue — accessible parking; purple — ETC account-only; green — bicycles (3A.03, 3H.04–3H.08, 3I.01 ¶7, 3J.03–3J.04).
- CHECK passed: 3H.05 ¶5 (white pavement instead of chevrons) with 3B.25; 3H.08 ¶2 with 2F.03 ¶4 (purple only for account-only requirements); 3I.02 ¶2 spacing N = 3B.14 ¶7; 3I.02 ¶5 with 2B.20 (R1-6 in-street signs).

**3J.05 (cont.)** ¶7 Support: yellow edge lines at same-direction islands help against wrong-way entry. ¶8–¶9 Option: chevrons in neutral areas; diagonals in buffers. **Figure 3J-5**.
**3J.06** Island delineation. ¶1 **Standard: delineators match the related channelizing or edge lines; red when facing only wrong-way traffic (3G.03).** ¶2 Standard: each roadway through an intersection considered separately. ¶3 Option: RPMs in front of or on curbed ends, supplementing or substituting curb markings.
**3J.07** Sidewalk extensions by markings. ¶1–¶4 Support: curb extensions; marked versions at pavement level. ¶5 **Standard: double solid lines connecting to the curb (or roadway edge); color per 3A.03.** ¶6 Support: **the area behind the double line is not part of the roadway; vehicle traversal prohibited** (distinct from single-line shoulders/gores and double-line flush medians). ¶7: single wide-line areas for speed management are not sidewalk extensions. ¶8 Guidance: tubular markers beside the double line. ¶12 **Standard: crosswalk markings not extended through marked sidewalk extensions.** ¶13: accessibility outside the Manual's scope (U.S. Access Board). ¶14 Guidance: critical signs (STOP, YIELD, pedestrian crossing) within the extension; **lateral offset measured from the centre of the double line**. ¶17 **Standard: aesthetic treatments there non-retroreflective (3H.03).** **Figure 3J-6**.

## Chapter 3K — Rumble strip markings (PDF 683)
**3K.01** ¶2 Support: **the Manual has no provisions on the design or placement of longitudinal rumble strips** (only markings used with them). ¶4 Option: lines over rumble strips (rumble stripes). ¶5 Standard: colors per 3A.03. ¶6 **Standard: no separate edge line in addition to a shoulder rumble stripe.** **Figure 3K-1**.
**3K.02** ¶2: no provisions for pavement-colored transverse rumble strips. ¶4 **Standard: colored transverse rumble strips in travel lanes are black or white (except 6M.06 in TTC).** ¶5 Guidance: white ones not confusable with stop lines or crosswalks.

**Chapter 3K complete. PART 3 complete.**

# PART 4 — HIGHWAY TRAFFIC SIGNALS
(Part 4 was read from the chunks in an earlier session — PART4_READING.md; this is the reading from the PDF.)

## Chapter 4A — General (PDF 684–689)
**4A.01** Types (Support list). **4A.02** Support: the UVC is the primary source for signal meanings; "within the intersection" per #112.
**4A.03** Steady indications (Standard): A green — CIRCULAR GREEN permits through, turns, U-turns unless modified; yields to pedestrians in crosswalks and vehicles in the intersection; left/U-turn yields to close opposing traffic; GREEN ARROW only the arrow's movement; pedestrians may cross on CIRCULAR GREEN unless otherwise directed, **not on a GREEN ARROW**. B yellow — movement terminating; red follows; pedestrians do not start. C red — stop at the stop line / before the crosswalk / before the intersection; **right turn (and left one-way to one-way) after stopping unless prohibited or a RED ARROW is shown**; RED ARROW — no entry for that movement, turn on red arrow only where a device permits it; pedestrians do not enter.
**4A.04** Flashing indications (Standard): **A flashing green has no meaning and shall not be used**; B flashing yellow — proceed with caution; flashing YELLOW ARROW — the arrow's movement, yielding; pedestrians may cross; as a beacon — extra attention / requirement now applicable; C flashing red — stop, then proceed as at a STOP sign; flashing RED ARROW likewise for the turn; pedestrians may cross; **as a beacon, only to supplement STOP, DO NOT ENTER or WRONG WAY signs, or where compliance requires a stop at a point.**
**4A.05** Bicycle indications (Standard A–F: GREEN BICYCLE for the movement shown on the adjacent Bicycle Signal sign (9B.22); YELLOW terminating; RED stop, right turn on red if no motor-vehicle lanes to the right, unless prohibited; **flashing green and flashing yellow bicycle have no meaning and shall not be used**; flashing red — stop, then proceed).
**4A.06** Pedestrian indications (Standard): **flashing WALKING PERSON has no meaning and shall not be used**; steady WALK — start to cross; flashing DONT WALK — do not start, those crossing continue (to the far side or refuge as directed); steady DONT WALK — do not enter.
**4A.07** Supports and cabinets (Guidance): Roadside Design Guide 2011, ADA Standards 2010; far from the traveled way; safety devices where clearances cannot be met; **foundations ≤ 4 in above ground** (not rigid supports); not obstructing sidewalks or ramps; medians too.
**4A.08** Signs at signals. ¶2 Guidance: signs per Part 2. ¶4 Guidance: illuminated signs without glare; signal faces dominant. ¶5 Standard: sign assembly clearances per 4D.09, 4D.10. ¶6 **Standard: STOP signs not used in conjunction with any highway traffic signal operation, except A flashing red at all times, B a low-conflict minor street or driveway within or next to the signal area.**
**4A.09** Markings (Guidance: per Part 3; signs where markings will not stay).
**4A.10** Operation and maintenance (Guidance: responsibility set before installation; A–H — timing records and authorised changes; cleaning, relamping; alternate operation during failure; skilled personnel; spares; appearance).

## Chapter 4B — Traffic control signals, general (PDF 690–691)
**4B.01–4B.02** Support: advantages A–E and disadvantages A–D; signals are not a panacea.
**4B.03** ¶1 **Guidance: consider alternatives even if warrants are met.** ¶2 Option A–N (warning signs, roundabouts, PHB/RRFB/beacons/in-roadway lights, sight distance, speed measures, beacons, lanes, geometrics, refuges, lighting, turn restrictions, multi-way stop if warranted, others).
**4B.04** Support: warrants define minimum conditions under which signals **might** be justified. ¶2 Guidance: design by engineering study.
**4B.05** Removal (Guidance: judgement reviews; removal steps A–E — **flash or cover for ≥ 90 days** with STOP control, remove if data confirm). Option: temporary signals removed after A–B; poles may remain.

## Chapter 4C — Signal needs studies (PDF 692–…)
**4C.01** ¶1 **Standard: except temporary TTC signals, an engineering study of traffic, pedestrian and physical characteristics before any signal is installed.** ¶2 **Standard: the study analyses existing operation and safety and the applicable factors of Warrants 1–9** (8-hour, 4-hour, peak hour, pedestrian, school, coordinated, crash, network, grade crossing). ¶3 **Standard: satisfying a warrant shall not in itself require a signal.** ¶5–¶7 Guidance: consider alternatives (4B.03); **no signal unless one or more factors are met and a study shows it will improve overall safety and/or operation.** ¶8–¶11 Guidance: right-turn volumes and lane counting by judgement (one lane + turn lane as one or two lanes; half of traffic turning left with adequate storage → two lanes; minor right turns with minimal conflict excluded; through/right + left lanes — sum both or the higher lane). ¶12 Guidance: estimated volumes for developing sites; **a study within 1 year of stop-and-go operation** (except Warrant 8) — remove or cease if unjustified.

Notes (read together)
- **STOP signs with signals — 2B.06 ¶8 and 4A.08 ¶6 (both Standards)**: 2B.06 ¶8 (YIELD or STOP) excepts A flashing red, B low-conflict minor street/driveway, **C island-separated channelized turn lanes not controlled by the signal**; 4A.08 ¶6 (STOP) lists A and B. 4A.08 ¶6 governs STOP signs used "in conjunction with" a signal's operation; a turn lane not controlled by the signal falls outside that phrase, and 2B.06 ¶8C, the provision specifically on STOP/YIELD signs, says so explicitly. Consistent.
- CHECK passed: 4A.04 C4 (flashing red beacons only with STOP, DNE, WRONG WAY) with 2B.04 and 2B.46–2B.47; 4A.02 ¶2 = #112 (intersection area); 4C.01 ¶3 = the all-way stop pattern (warrants never sufficient alone); 4A.04 A, 4A.05 D–E and 4A.06 A are prohibitions of indications with "no meaning".
- CHECK passed: 3J.07 ¶14 (sign offset from the double line) — a specific rule for where 2A.16's offset is measured.
- Part 4 was previously read from chunks (PART4_READING.md). Differences found in this PDF reading will be noted against that file.
