**4C.01 (cont.)** ¶13–¶16 Option: wide medians as one or two intersections (2A.23); heavy major-street left turns analysed as the "minor street"; **any four consecutive 15-minute periods = 1 hour** (non-overlapping; same hours for major and minor volumes); bicyclists counted as vehicles or pedestrians (¶17: usually by where they ride). ¶18–¶19 Option: data A–G (12-hour counts; 15-min classified counts in the 2 AM and 2 PM peaks; pedestrian counts; facilities for young, elderly, disabled; **posted or statutory speed limit or 85th-percentile speed**; condition diagram; **collision diagram ≥ 1 year**) and A–E (delay, gaps, speeds, pedestrian delay, queues). ¶20 Support: **warrants are one factor, not the overriding one; an agency may install a signal where no warrant is met, but only after an engineering study documenting why it is the best solution.**
**4C.02** Warrant 1, Eight-Hour. ¶1–¶3 Support: Condition A (minimum volume), B (interruption of continuous traffic); **a single warrant — A or B satisfies it**, else the combination. ¶4 Guidance: 100 % columns of A **or** B for each of any 8 hours. ¶5 **Standard: the same 8 hours for major and minor volumes within a condition; A and B may use different 8 hours.** ¶6: the more critical minor approach may change hour to hour. ¶7 **Option: 70 % columns where the posted or statutory speed limit or 85th-percentile speed on the major street exceeds 40 mph, or in an isolated community < 10,000.** ¶8–¶9 Guidance: combination only after adequate trial of alternatives — **80 % columns of both A and B**. ¶10 Standard: same 8 hours per condition. ¶12 Option: 56 % columns under the ¶7 conditions.
**Table 4C-1** (read from the page) — lanes major/minor · major-street vph (100 · 80 · 70 · 56 %) · critical minor-approach vph (100 · 80 · 70 · 56 %). **Condition A**: 1/1 — 500 · 400 · 350 · 280 | 150 · 120 · 105 · 84; 2+/1 — 600 · 480 · 420 · 336 | 150 · 120 · 105 · 84; 2+/2+ — 600 · 480 · 420 · 336 | 200 · 160 · 140 · 112; 1/2+ — 500 · 400 · 350 · 280 | 200 · 160 · 140 · 112. **Condition B**: 1/1 — 750 · 600 · 525 · 420 | 75 · 60 · 53 · 42; 2+/1 — 900 · 720 · 630 · 504 | 75 · 60 · 53 · 42; 2+/2+ — 900 · 720 · 630 · 504 | 100 · 80 · 70 · 56; 1/2+ — 750 · 600 · 525 · 420 | 100 · 80 · 70 · 56. Footnotes: a basic minimum; b combination after trial; c **>40 mph or isolated community < 10,000**; d combination under c's conditions.
**4C.03** Warrant 2, Four-Hour (Guidance: 4 hours above the Figure 4C-1 curve; Option: Figure 4C-2 (70 %) under the >40 mph / < 10,000 conditions). **Figures 4C-1, 4C-2** (notes: lower thresholds **115 / 80 vph** (2+ / 1 minor lanes); **80 / 60 vph** at 70 %).
**4C.04** Warrant 3, Peak Hour. ¶2 Guidance: **only in unusual cases** (office complexes, plants, HOV facilities). ¶3 Guidance: A all three for the same hour — **STOP-controlled minor approach delay ≥ 4 veh-h (one lane) / ≥ 5 (two lanes); minor volume ≥ 100 / 150 vph; total entering ≥ 650 (three approaches) / ≥ 800 (four+)**; or B above the Figure 4C-3 curve. ¶4 Option: Figure 4C-4 (70 %). ¶5 Option: flashing mode outside warrant hours if it is the only warrant. ¶6 Guidance: traffic-actuated if the only warrant. **Figures 4C-3, 4C-4** (lower thresholds **150 / 100 vph**; **100 / 75 vph** at 70 %).
**4C.05** Warrant 4, Pedestrian Volume. ¶2 Guidance: A 4 hours above Figure 4C-5 or B 1 hour above Figure 4C-6. ¶3 **Option: Figures 4C-7 / 4C-8 where the major-street speed exceeds 35 mph** (not 40) or community < 10,000. ¶4 Option: each direction separately across a wide median. ¶5 Guidance: **not within 300 ft of the nearest signal or STOP sign** on the street crossed, unless progression is unaffected. ¶6 **Standard: pedestrian signal heads (Chapter 4I).** ¶7 Guidance: A intersections — control the minor street, actuated, with pedestrian detection; B non-intersection — **≥ 100 ft from STOP/YIELD-controlled side streets**, pedestrian-actuated, a face over the traveled way per approach, **parking prohibited ≥ 100 ft before and ≥ 20 ft beyond the crosswalk** (or curb extensions), signs and markings; C coordinated within systems. ¶8 Option: **pedestrian criterion reduced up to 50 % if the 15th-percentile crossing speed < 3.5 ft/s**. ¶9 Option: not needed where adjacent signals give adequate gaps. **Figures 4C-5 to 4C-8** (lower thresholds **107 · 133 · 75 · 93 pph**; with slow walkers **53 · 66 · 37 · 46 pph**).
**4C.06** Warrant 5, School Crossing. ¶1: schoolchildren = elementary through high school. ¶2 Guidance: **adequate gaps fewer than the minutes in the crossing period, and ≥ 20 schoolchildren in the highest crossing hour.** ¶3–¶4 Guidance: other remedies first; **not within 300 ft of the nearest signal** unless progression is unaffected. ¶5 **Standard: pedestrian signal heads.** ¶6 Guidance A–C (as 4C.05 ¶7).
**4C.07** Warrant 6, Coordinated System (Guidance A one-way / B two-way platooning; **not where resulting spacing < 1,000 ft**).
**4C.08** Warrant 7, Crash Experience. ¶2 Guidance: **all of A (adequate trial of alternatives failed), B (one of: 1-year all-severity or fatal-and-injury angle + pedestrian crashes ≥ Table 4C-2; 3-year ≥ Table 4C-3), C (80 % columns of Condition A or B for 8 hours, or ≥ 80 % of the pedestrian warrant).** ¶3 Standard: the same 8 hours. ¶5 Option: under the >40 mph / < 10,000 conditions — 56 % columns and Tables 4C-4 / 4C-5. ¶6 Option: agency-calibrated HSM safety performance functions, documented in the study. ¶7 Support: the table values derive from HSM (2010) SPFs — the point where signalized outperforms stop control.
**Tables 4C-2 / 4C-3** (read from the pages) — through lanes major/minor · all severities (4 legs · 3 legs) · fatal-and-injury (4 · 3): **1-year: 5 · 4 · 3 · 3 for every lane combination; 3-year: 6 · 5 · 4 · 4 for every combination.** Footnote a: angle crashes involve vehicles on both streets.
**Tables 4C-4 / 4C-5** (read from the pages; heading **"Community less than 10,000 population or above 40 mph on major street"**) — 1-year: 1/1 — 4 · 3 · 3 · 3; 2+/1 — 10 · 9 · 6 · 6; 2+/2+ — 10 · 9 · 6 · 6; 1/2+ — 4 · 3 · 3 · 3. 3-year: 1/1 — 6 · 5 · 4 · 4; 2+/1 — 16 · 13 · 9 · 9; 2+/2+ — 16 · 13 · 9 · 9; 1/2+ — 6 · 5 · 4 · 4.
**4C.09** Warrant 8, Roadway Network (Guidance: two or more major routes; A ≥ 1,000 vph entering in the weekday peak hour with 5-year projections meeting Warrant 1, 2 or 3; or B ≥ 1,000 vph for each of any 5 hours of a Saturday or Sunday; major route characteristics A–C).
**4C.10** Warrant 9, Near a Grade Crossing. ¶2 Guidance: only after alternatives (more pavement; reassigning stop control). ¶3 Guidance: **A STOP/YIELD-controlled approach with the nearest track centre within 140 ft of the stop or yield line; and B the highest-volume rail hour above the Figure 4C-9 / 4C-10 curve for distance D (clear storage distance, 1C.02).** ¶4 Guidance: 4C-9 for one approach lane, 4C-10 for two or more; **use the curve nearest the actual D (95 ft → 90 ft)**; unknown rail times → highest-volume hour. ¶5–¶8 Option: minor volume × up to three factors — **Table 4C-6** (rail frequency; curves assume 4 per day), **Table 4C-7** (only if **≥ 2 %** are buses carrying **≥ 20 people**), **Table 4C-8** (curves assume 10 % tractor-trailers). ¶9 **Standard: if justified — A minor-street actuation, B preemption per 4F.19 and 8D.09, C flashing-light signals at the crossing (8D.02).** ¶10 Guidance: automatic gates. **Figures 4C-9, 4C-10** (curves **D = 30, 50, 70, 90, 110, 130 ft**; lower threshold 25 vph; vph after adjustment factors).
**Tables 4C-6 to 4C-8** (read from the pages): 4C-6 rail per day — 1: 0.67 · 2: 0.91 · 3–5: 1.00 · 6–8: 1.18 · 9–11: 1.25 · 12+: 1.33. 4C-7 high-occupancy buses — 0 %: 1.00 · 2 %: 1.09 · 4 %: 1.19 · 6 %+: 1.32 (footnote: ≥ 20 people). 4C-8 tractor-trailers — D < 70 ft · D ≥ 70 ft: 0–2.5 %: 0.50 · 0.50; 2.6–7.5: 0.75 · 0.75; 7.6–12.5: 1.00 · 1.00; 12.6–17.5: 2.30 · 1.15; 17.6–22.5: 2.70 · 1.35; 22.6–27.5: 3.28 · 1.64; > 27.5: 4.18 · 2.09.

Notes (read together)
- MEAN COMPUTED Table 4C-1: every column is a percentage of the 100 % column (e.g. 56 % of 500 = 280); 70 % of 75 = 52.5 printed 53 (rounded up).
- "Posted or statutory speed limit **or** the 85th-percentile speed … exceeds 40 mph" (4C.02 ¶7 etc.) is an enabling condition: either speed exceeding 40 opens the Option. Contrast Tables 2C-3 / 3B-1, where "posted or 85th-percentile" keys a lookup and the text does not say which speed (item 7).
- Two speed triggers in one Chapter: **> 40 mph** (Warrants 1, 2, 3, 7) and **> 35 mph** (Warrant 4, pedestrians).
- **Table 4C-4 / 4C-5** (Option under the >40 mph / small-community conditions) are **lower** than 4C-2 / 4C-3 for one-lane majors (4 vs 5) but **higher** for multi-lane majors (10 vs 5). As an Option "in place of", the agency chooses; the table headings state their scope (R16).
- 4C.01 ¶6 (Guidance: no signal unless one or more **factors** are met) and ¶20 (Support: a signal where **no warrant** is met, after a documented study) agree — "factors" (¶2) include the analysis of existing operation and safety, not only warrants.
- **Item 7 widened again**: Figure 4C-9/4C-10 curves are D = 30, 50 … 130 ft, and 4C.10 ¶4B says "nearest" (95 → 90); a D exactly between curves (40, 60 … 120 ft) is not resolved. Table 4C-7's point rows (0, 2, 4, 6+ %) leave 3 % or 5 % between rows. Table 4C-8's one-decimal ranges (2.5 / 2.6) imply percentages rounded to one decimal.
- CHECK passed: 4C.10 ¶3B uses #1C.02 clear storage distance; ¶9B points to 4F.19 and 8D.09 (Table 1B-1 dates 8D.09–8D.12 for 2034); 4C.05 ¶6 and 4C.06 ¶5 (pedestrian heads required) agree with Chapter 4I to be read.

## Chapter 4D — Design features of traffic control signals (PDF 707–716)
**4D.01** ¶2 Support: pretimed, semi- and full-actuated; advance detection for dilemma zones on rural high-speed approaches. ¶3 **Standard: design and operation consider all modes, including access and safety.** ¶4 **Standard: signals not in operation — faces covered, turned or taken down.** ¶5 Guidance: covers include the backplate; turned faces not reflecting the yellow border. ¶7 **Standard: a signal controls traffic only where its faces are placed.** ¶8–¶9 Guidance: **no signalized midblock crosswalk within 300 ft of the nearest signal, or within 100 ft of STOP/YIELD-controlled side streets or driveways**, unless study/judgement supports it. ¶10 Guidance: phasing and timing re-evaluated regularly. ¶11 Guidance: **signals within ½ mile along major routes coordinated**, also across jurisdictional boundaries. ¶12 Support: not between sections with different cycle lengths.
**4D.02** Pedestrians. ¶2 **Standard: pedestrian signal heads (unless crossing is prohibited) where A the signal was justified by Warrant 4 or 5; B an exclusive pedestrian phase or LPI with all conflicting vehicles stopped; C an established signalized school crossing; D existing pedestrian accommodations where multi-phase indications would confuse pedestrians.** ¶3 Guidance: for each marked crosswalk. ¶4 Guidance: time every cycle or detectors. ¶5 Guidance: R9-3 where crossings are prohibited and a barrier is impractical. ¶7 Option: other conditions.
**4D.03** Bicyclists. ¶1 **Standard: visibility-limited faces adjusted for bicyclists, or separate bicycle faces (4H).** ¶2 **Standard: timing and actuation on bikeways reviewed for bicyclists.** ¶3 Option: bicycle signal faces.
**4D.04** Transit (Option: LRT indications, 8D.15).
**4D.05** Number of faces. ¶1 **Standard: A at least two primary faces for a signalized through movement; with no through movement, two for the major turning movement (except single-lane shared left/right approaches, Figure 4F-15A); B–C left and right turns per 4F.02–4F.15.** ¶2 Option: continuously displayed single-section GREEN ARROW for never-conflicting movements. ¶4–¶5 Guidance: two or more primary turn faces for dual turn lanes or major turn movements. ¶6 Support: far-side overhead faces and one per lane are safer. ¶7 **Guidance: where the posted or statutory speed limit or 85th-percentile speed is ≥ 45 mph, for new or reconstructed signals — A Table 4D-1; B one overhead face centred over each through lane when equal in number; C overhead faces over each mandatory turn lane; D all primary faces far side; E consider supplemental faces; F all faces with backplates.** ¶8 Guidance: consider the layout for major 4+ lane arterials and slower approaches. **Figure 4D-1** (notes 1–4; shared protected/permissive face over the lane line).
**Table 4D-1** (read from the page) through lanes · total primary through faces\* · minimum overhead: 1 · 2 · 1 / 2 · 2 · 1 / 3 · 3 · 2\*\* / 4+ · 4+ · 3\*\*. Notes: \* **a minimum of two is always required (4D.05)**; may be exceeded; cone of vision per 4D.07. \*\* all overhead if practical.
**4D.06** Visibility, aiming, shielding. Guidance: visibility the highest priority; clear indication; geometry considered; **at midblock crosswalks at least one face over the traveled way per approach**; **the two primary faces continuously visible from at least the Table 4D-2 distance before the stop line** (unless physically obstructed or another signal intervenes); **else a Signal Ahead sign (2C.35)** (¶6). Option: Warning Beacon on that sign, flashing when drivers at the legal speed might meet red or a queue; supplemental faces for limited sight distance. Guidance: supplemental faces where needed; visibility-limiting for small angles between approaches. ¶13 **Standard: visors > 12 in not on free-swinging faces.** ¶14–¶15 Guidance: visors; prefer visors over louvers. ¶16 Option: visibility-limited faces. ¶17 **Guidance: backplates on all faces facing approaches with speed ≥ 45 mph**; consider below 45 where glare or backgrounds warrant. ¶19 **Standard: no ancillary legends on backplates.** ¶20 **Standard: dull black inside visors, louvers, fins and backplate fronts.** ¶21 **Option: yellow retroreflective backplate border 1–3 in wide.**
**Table 4D-2** (read from the page) Minimum Sight Distance for Signal Visibility — **85th-Percentile Speed** · distance: 20 · 175 / 25 · 215 / 30 · 270 / 35 · 325 / 40 · 390 / 45 · 460 / 50 · 540 / 55 · 625 / 60 · 715 (mph · ft). Note: stopping sight distance plus an assumed queue for 60–75-s cycles.
**4D.07** Lateral positioning. ¶1 **Standard: at least one (preferably both) of the two primary faces within ±20° of the approach centre, from a point 10 ft behind the stop line, also meeting 4D.08.** ¶2 **Standard: if both are post-mounted — far side, one right and one left.** ¶3–¶4 **Standard: through faces ≥ 8 ft apart (centres, perpendicular); separate turn faces ≥ 8 ft apart if over the roadway.** ¶5–¶6 Guidance: measuring clustered faces; turn faces ≥ 8 ft from other movements' faces, except ¶7 (Option: 3 ft for modifications). ¶10 Guidance: overhead turn faces within the turn lane's edge extensions (¶11: not supplemental ones). ¶12 **Guidance: new/reconstructed — CIRCULAR GREEN faces for permissive left turns not on the far median or over the turn lane.** ¶13 **Standard: supplemental post-mounted faces — no left arrows in near-right faces; no right arrows in far-left faces (far median = far left).**
**4D.08** Longitudinal positioning. ¶1 **Standard (unless physically impractical): the primary turn faces and at least one (preferably both) primary through faces located ≥ 40 ft and ≤ 180 ft beyond the stop line (unless a supplemental near-side face is provided), also meeting 4D.07.** ¶2 Guidance: judgement on a near-side face at 150–180 ft. ¶3 Guidance: near-side faces close to the stop line. **Figure 4D-2** (40 ft\* minimum; **120 ft\*\* maximum for 8-inch faces**; 180 ft\*\*\* maximum for 12-inch faces unless near-side; ±20° cone from 10 ft behind the stop line).
**4D.09** Mounting height. ¶1 **Standard: over any part of the highway usable by vehicles — bottom ≥ 15 ft.** ¶2 **Standard: not over the roadway — ≥ 8 ft above the sidewalk (or roadway-centre grade); ≥ 4.5 ft above a near-side median island.** ¶3 Guidance: top ≤ 25.6 ft over the roadway. ¶4 Guidance: 40–53 ft from the stop line per Figure 4D-3. ¶5–¶6 Guidance: not over the roadway — bottom ≤ 19 ft (vertical faces), ≤ 22 ft (horizontal). **Figure 4D-3**.
**4D.10** Lateral offset (Guidance: roadside faces < 15 ft high — ≥ 2 ft from the curb face or shoulder edge).
**4D.11** Temporary and portable signals. ¶3 **Standard: advance signing.** ¶4 **Standard: A conventional display and operation; B removed when not needed; C flashing or covered/turned/taken down when not operating steadily, except ¶5** (Option: semi-actuated with continuous major-street green). ¶6–¶8 Guidance: only if it improves safety/operation; crews' regular use approved by the jurisdiction; **≤ 30 days unless part of a longer TTC project**.

## Chapter 4E — Signal indications (PDF 717–…)
**4E.01** ¶1 **Standard: illuminated parts circular or arrow** (except bicycle, pedestrian, LRT, lane-use signals). ¶2 **Standard: no letters or numbers (including countdowns) in vehicular indications.** ¶3 **Standard: no strobes within or next to any indication.** ¶4 **Standard: no flashing displays within or next to indications except those Part 4 expressly allows (and preemption indications, 4F.19).** ¶5–¶6 Standard: one color per circular and arrow indication, except ¶7 (Option: bimodal GREEN/YELLOW ARROW, never both at once). ¶8–¶9 **Standard: an arrow shows one direction and is the only lit part; vertical for through, horizontal for turns ≈ 90° or more, sloped for sharper-angle turns, U-turn arrows per Figure 4E-1.** ¶10 **Standard: ITE equipment standards (incandescent) and ITE LED Circular (2005) and Arrow (2008) supplements** (external). ¶11 Guidance: intensity and distribution per those. ¶13 Guidance: automatic dimming for excessive night glare.
**4E.02** Sizes. ¶1 **Standard: 4, 8 and 12 in only.** ¶2 **Standard: 4-in only for bicycle faces (4H.07).** ¶3 **Standard: 12-in for all arrows.** ¶4 **Standard: 12-in for all circular indications in new faces, except ¶5** — Option: 8-in for A emergency-vehicle green/flashing yellow; B closely-spaced downstream approaches where visibility-limited faces are impractical; **C faces < 120 ft from the stop line where the posted or statutory speed limit or operating speed is ≤ 30 mph**; D supplemental near-side faces; E faces solely for pedestrians; F flashing beacons. ¶6 Option: mixed sizes within those limits.
**4E.03** Positions (general). ¶2 **Standard: three, four or five sections unless provided otherwise; clustered vertical faces have ≥ 3 vertical positions.** ¶3 Standard: single-section continuous GREEN ARROW permitted. ¶4–¶5 Standard: straight vertical or horizontal lines; order per 4E.04–4E.05. ¶6 **Standard: CIRCULAR YELLOW between the red and all other sections.** ¶7–¶8 Standard: U-turn arrows take the turn-arrow position of the same color; **no U-turn arrow with a same-direction turn arrow in one face.** ¶9 Option: two identical reds for emphasis.

Notes (read together)
- **DATA (chunks)**: a 4D.05 Support chunk reads "Applies to approaches with a posted, statutory, or 85th-percentile speed of 45 mph or higher." That sentence is **not printed on the page** (it restates Table 4D-1's title). The graph should not carry it as a paragraph.
- **Figure 4D-2 combines two Sections**: 40–180 ft (4D.08 ¶1) and the **120-ft maximum for 8-inch faces**, which is the 4E.02 ¶5C condition (8-in circular indications only < 120 ft from the stop line at ≤ 30 mph).
- Speed keys differ within the Chapter: 4D.05 ¶7 and 4D.06 ¶17 use "posted or statutory speed limit **or** 85th-percentile ≥ 45 mph" (enabling — either speed); **Table 4D-2 is keyed by the 85th-percentile speed only**; 4E.02 ¶5C by "posted or statutory speed limit or operating speed ≤ 30 mph". Table 4D-2's point rows (20…60 mph) belong with item 7.
- CHECK passed: 4D.01 ¶8–¶9 (300 ft; 100 ft) agree with 4C.05 ¶5, ¶7B; 4D.02 ¶2A with 4C.05 ¶6 and 4C.06 ¶5; 4D.06 ¶6 with 2C.35; 4E.01 ¶3 with 2A.12 (no strobes); 4D.07 ¶1's 10-ft point = Figure 4D-2; 4E.02 ¶2 with 4A.05 (bicycle indications).

**4E.03 (cont.)** ¶10 Option: horizontal and vertical faces on one approach if 4D.07 spacing is met. **Figure 4E-2**.
**4E.04** Vertical faces. ¶1–¶2 **Standard: all reds above yellows and greens; each YELLOW ARROW above its GREEN ARROW.** ¶3 **Standard order, top to bottom: CIRCULAR RED · left RED ARROW (steady/flashing) · right RED ARROW · CIRCULAR YELLOW · CIRCULAR GREEN · straight GREEN ARROW · steady left YELLOW ARROW · flashing left YELLOW ARROW · left GREEN ARROW · steady right YELLOW ARROW · flashing right YELLOW ARROW · right GREEN ARROW.** ¶4 Standard: a bimodal section takes the GREEN ARROW's position. ¶5 Option: same-color clusters. ¶6 **Standard: clusters only A two identical sections, B two or three different sections of one color, C the 4F.16 case (GREEN ARROW + flashing YELLOW ARROW).** ¶7 **Standard: except three-section bimodal faces (4F.04, 4F.08, 4F.11, 4F.15), a flashing yellow section is not level with and is below the steady yellow.** ¶8: hybrid beacon exceptions (4J.02, 4N.02).
**4E.05** Horizontal faces. ¶1–¶2 **Standard: all reds left of yellows and greens; each YELLOW ARROW left of its GREEN ARROW.** ¶3 **Standard order, left to right: CIRCULAR RED · left RED ARROW · right RED ARROW · CIRCULAR YELLOW · steady left YELLOW ARROW · flashing left YELLOW ARROW · left GREEN ARROW · CIRCULAR GREEN · straight GREEN ARROW · steady right YELLOW ARROW · flashing right YELLOW ARROW · right GREEN ARROW.** ¶4 Standard: bimodal positions. ¶5 Standard: flashing yellow not level with and right of the steady yellow (same exceptions).

## Chapter 4F — Steady (stop-and-go) operation (PDF 721–…)
**4F.01** General rules (all Standard). ¶1 **At least one indication lit in every face at all times.** ¶2 **A face controls the same movement in every interval of the cycle.** ¶3 A steady CIRCULAR RED — prohibit entry (turns after stop per 4A.03 C.1); with GREEN ARROWs to allow turns only (except protected-only and separate-face protected/permissive). B steady CIRCULAR YELLOW — after CIRCULAR GREEN or straight GREEN ARROW; **never between red and green**; followed by red (except entering preemption); **not to a permissive left-turn approach using a shared face unless (a) the opposing approach also shows yellow, (b) a study shows (a) is impractical, volumes are low, and a W25-1 "ONCOMING TRAFFIC HAS EXTENDED GREEN" sign is installed, or (c) occasional cases with W25-2 "… MAY HAVE EXTENDED GREEN".** C CIRCULAR GREEN — any lawful, practical direction. D steady RED ARROW — no entry for the turn; no turn on it except 4A.03 C.2. E flashing RED ARROW — turn after full stop and yielding. F steady YELLOW ARROW — after a GREEN ARROW (unless terminating with the CIRCULAR GREEN, or a straight arrow); after a flashing arrow if red follows; **never between a red/flashing arrow and a GREEN ARROW** (except preemption); **not while any conflicting vehicle has green/yellow or conflicting pedestrians have WALK/flashing DONT WALK** (except the permissive-left case ending with opposing CIRCULAR YELLOW; same-direction departures with separate lanes are not conflicts); not to end a flashing permissive left arrow unless (a)–(c) as in B.4; ended by red except preemption or continuing permissive (red clearance allowed). G flashing YELLOW ARROW — turn after yielding. H steady GREEN ARROW — **only for movements not in conflict with other vehicles on green/yellow or pedestrians on WALK/flashing DONT WALK** (U-turn and straight-arrow cases excepted); required for non-conflicting left turns; **not required on the stem of a T or for turns from one-way streets.**
¶4–¶6 Option: U-TURN YIELD TO RIGHT TURN (R10-16); **straight GREEN ARROW instead of CIRCULAR GREEN to discourage wrong-way turns (A one-way crossroads, B exit ramps, C geometry prohibiting turns, D pre-signals)**; turn arrows instead of circulars where all traffic must turn.
¶9 **Standard: no straight-through RED ARROW or YELLOW ARROW ever.** ¶10 **Standard: never on one face — CIRCULAR YELLOW with CIRCULAR RED; CIRCULAR GREEN with CIRCULAR RED; straight GREEN ARROW with CIRCULAR RED.** ¶11 Standard: not on an approach either, except by right-turn-only faces that are visibility-limited or carry an R10-10 RIGHT TURN SIGNAL sign (or ¶13). ¶12 **Standard: never on a face or an approach — CIRCULAR GREEN + CIRCULAR YELLOW; straight GREEN ARROW + CIRCULAR YELLOW; GREEN + YELLOW ARROW same direction; YELLOW + RED ARROW same direction; GREEN + RED ARROW same direction** (except ¶13). ¶13 Standard: pre-signal / queue-cutter turn-lane faces may combine A–C. ¶14 Standard: one section never shows both flashing and steady yellow (or red) in steady operation, except the listed Sections. ¶15 Guidance: no unexpected crossing conflicts unless slight, delay-saving and signed.
**4F.02** Left turns — general. ¶2 Support: modes A permissive only, B protected only, C protected/permissive, D variable. ¶3 Option: protected-only for many older drivers. ¶4 **Standard: permissive — opposing through faces show green or steady yellow; pedestrian heads not restricted.** ¶5 **Standard: protected — opposing through faces show steady CIRCULAR RED; no GREEN/YELLOW ARROW to opposing right turns unless separate departure lanes; pedestrians crossing the departure lanes see steady DONT WALK.** ¶6 **Standard: combined left/through lane — no left GREEN/YELLOW ARROW or flashing RED ARROW with the through CIRCULAR RED; no left RED ARROW with the through CIRCULAR GREEN/YELLOW.** ¶7 **Standard: no yellow change interval when changing permissive → protected.** ¶8 Standard: variable mode — each mode's rules; no circular green/yellow in protected-only; no left arrows green/yellow in permissive-only. ¶9–¶10 Option: five-section shared face with FYA for variable mode on shared lanes; signs or CMS. ¶11 Support: **shared face** (serves as a primary through face, same circular color) vs **separate face** (only the turn; only with a mandatory turn lane). ¶13: modes may differ by approach. ¶14 Option: shared left/right face (4F.16).
**4F.03** Permissive only, shared face (Standard): CR, CY, CG, one at a time; CG during the permissive turn; always the same circular color as the through faces; same face as protected/permissive if other modes are used. **Figure 4F-1**.
**4F.04** Permissive only, separate face. ¶1 **Standard: no separate left face without a mandatory left-turn lane.** ¶2 Standard: no CIRCULAR GREEN in it. ¶3 Standard (FYA): steady RED ARROW, steady YELLOW ARROW, flashing YELLOW ARROW, one at a time; FYA during the permissive turn; steady yellow after; **FYA may run while through faces are red and opposing lefts have GREEN ARROWs**; the steady-yellow section not used for FYA unless bimodal for variable mode; in flashing mode FYA only from the steady-yellow section; same face as protected/permissive when other modes are used. ¶4 Option: **flashing RED ARROW only where a study finds every vehicle must stop** (e.g. wide medians). ¶5 Standard (FRA): steady/flashing RED ARROW, YELLOW ARROW, GREEN ARROW (present for three sections but not shown in permissive-only); FRA during the permissive turn; steady yellow after; may run with opposing protected lefts; **sign not required; if used, R10-27**. ¶6 Option: clustered two RED ARROWs. **Figures 4F-2, 4F-3** (note: flashing red arrow only by engineering study).
**4F.05** Protected only, shared face. ¶1 **Standard: only if CIRCULAR GREEN and left GREEN ARROW always begin and end together**; CR, CY, CG, GA (one color at a time); both greens during the protected turn; same circular color as through faces. ¶2 Option: straight GREEN ARROW in place of CG where used for through traffic too. **Figure 4F-4** (note repeats the "begin and terminate together" Standard).
**4F.06** Protected only, separate face (Standard: mandatory lane required; RED, YELLOW, GREEN ARROWs; GREEN during the turn; steady YELLOW after; same face as protected/permissive, no flashing arrows in protected-only). **Figure 4F-5**.
**4F.07** Protected/permissive, shared face (Standard): CR, CY, CG, steady left YELLOW ARROW, left GREEN ARROW (one circular and one arrow at a time; the yellow arrow not required if the GREEN ARROW always ends with the through CG); protected — GREEN ARROW plus the through circular color; YELLOW ARROW after the GREEN ARROW unless both end together (then CY alone or CY + YA); permissive — CG only; same circular color as through faces; **sign not required; if used, R10-12**. **Figure 4F-6**.
**4F.08** Protected/permissive, separate face. ¶1–¶2 Standard: mandatory lane; no CIRCULAR GREEN. ¶3 Standard (FYA): steady RED, steady YELLOW, flashing YELLOW, GREEN ARROWs, one at a time; GREEN protected; steady YELLOW after (red clearance allowed); FYA permissive; steady YELLOW after the FYA if red follows; FYA may run with opposing protected lefts; **permissive → protected: GREEN ARROW immediately after the FYA, no steady yellow between.** **Figure 4F-7** (notes: \* not in protected-only; \*\* not in permissive-only).

Notes (read together)
- **Two different lens orders**: horizontal faces place the left-turn arrows **before** the CIRCULAR GREEN; vertical faces place them **after** the straight GREEN ARROW (4E.04 ¶3 vs 4E.05 ¶3). Both are Standards; the graph must keep both.
- CHECK passed: 4F.02 ¶7 (no yellow change permissive → protected) = 4F.08 ¶3G; "no separate left-turn face without a mandatory left-turn lane" stated three times (4F.04 ¶1, 4F.06 ¶1, 4F.08 ¶1); signs named here exist in Part 2 (R10-10, R10-12, R10-16, R10-27 in 2B.59; W25-1, W25-2 in 2C.44); 4F.01 ¶3 A.1 and D point back to 4A.03 C.1–C.2.
- MEAN "yellow trap" conditions (4F.01 B.4 and F.5, (a)–(c)) are a three-way test: opposing yellow, or a documented study plus W25-1, or occasional operation plus W25-2 — a condition graph with sign requirements.
- MEAN "conflict" has a stated exception: same-direction departures are not conflicts where each turn lane has its own departure lane shown by markings or channelization (4F.01 F.4, H.1, H.2; 4F.02 ¶5).

**4F.08 (cont.)** ¶3 H **Standard: either a four-section face (steady and flashing yellow arrows in different sections) or a three-section face with a bimodal steady/flashing yellow section**; I four-section faces — the steady-yellow section never shows FYA in steady mode; J in flashing mode FYA only from the steady-yellow section. ¶4 Option: bimodal GREEN ARROW/FYA section + steady YELLOW + RED ARROWs counts as four-section. ¶5 Option: flashing RED ARROW only where a study finds every vehicle must stop. ¶6 **Standard (FRA protected/permissive): RED (steady or flashing), YELLOW, GREEN ARROWs one at a time; GREEN protected; YELLOW after; FRA permissive; YELLOW after the FRA if red follows; permissive → protected — GREEN immediately, no yellow between; FRA may run with opposing protected lefts; sign not required, if used R10-27.** ¶7 Option: clustered red arrows. **Figure 4F-3**.
**4F.09** Right turns — general. ¶2 Support: four modes (as left). ¶3 **Standard: permissive right — conflicting U-turn-only faces show steady U-turn RED ARROWs; pedestrian heads not restricted.** ¶4 **Standard: protected right — no GREEN/YELLOW ARROW to opposing left turns unless separate departure lanes; conflicting U-turn faces show RED ARROWs; pedestrians crossing the departure lanes see steady DONT WALK.** ¶5 **Standard: combined right/through lane — no right GREEN/YELLOW ARROW or flashing RED ARROW with the through CIRCULAR RED; no right RED ARROW with the through CIRCULAR GREEN/YELLOW.** ¶6 Standard: variable mode as for left turns. ¶7–¶8 Option: five-section shared face; signs or CMS. ¶9 Support: shared vs separate faces. ¶12 Option: shared left/right face (4F.16).
**4F.10** Permissive only, shared face (Standard as 4F.03, same circular color as through faces). **Figure 4F-8**.
**4F.11** Permissive only, separate face. ¶1 **Standard: no separate right face without a mandatory right-turn lane.** ¶2 Standard: no CIRCULAR GREEN in it. ¶3 Standard (FYA): A either 1 steady RED ARROW, steady YELLOW ARROW, FYA, or **2 steady CIRCULAR RED, YELLOW ARROW, FYA — with an R10-10R RIGHT TURN SIGNAL sign if the CIRCULAR RED is sometimes shown while the through faces are not red, unless shielded from the through lanes**; B FYA permissive; C YELLOW after; **D stop-and-remain display: RED ARROW if right-on-red is not intended to be allowed, CIRCULAR RED if it is**; E FYA may run while the through faces are red; F–H as 4F.04. ¶4 Option: FRA only by study. ¶5 Standard (FRA): A 1 RED (steady/flashing), YELLOW, GREEN ARROWs (green present but not shown in permissive-only) or 2 CIRCULAR RED + RED ARROW on the top position, YELLOW, GREEN (R10-10R rule as above); B FRA permissive; C YELLOW after; D stop display as above; E FRA may run with through red and opposing protected lefts; F **sign not required; if used R10-27 (right)**. ¶6 Option: clustered reds. **Figures 4F-9, 4F-10** (notes: R10-10R **shall** be used with the circular-red faces in the stated case; FRA only by study).
**4F.12** Protected only, shared face (Standard: only if CIRCULAR GREEN and right GREEN ARROW always begin and end together; CR, CY, CG, GA; both greens protected; same circular color). ¶2 Option: straight GREEN ARROW. **Figure 4F-11** (note repeats the Standard).
**4F.13** Protected only, separate face (Standard: mandatory lane; A 1 RED/YELLOW/GREEN ARROWs or 2 CIRCULAR RED/YELLOW/GREEN ARROW with the R10-10R rule; GREEN protected; YELLOW after; stop display by right-on-red intent; same face as protected/permissive without flashing arrows). **Figure 4F-12**.
**4F.14** Protected/permissive, shared face (Standard as 4F.07 for right turns).
**4F.15** Protected/permissive, separate face. ¶1–¶2 Standard: mandatory lane; no CIRCULAR GREEN. ¶3 Standard (FYA): A 1 RED/YELLOW/FYA/GREEN ARROWs or 2 CIRCULAR RED/YELLOW/FYA/GREEN (R10-10R rule); B GREEN protected; C YELLOW after (red clearance allowed); D FYA permissive; E YELLOW after the FYA if red follows; **F permissive → protected: 1 if an opposing permissive left turn ends at the same time — steady YELLOW ARROW after the FYA (red clearance allowed); 2 if not — GREEN ARROW immediately, no yellow between**; G stop display by right-on-red intent; H FYA may run with the through red; I four-section or three-section bimodal; J–K section use in steady and flashing modes. ¶4 Option: bimodal GREEN/FYA counts as four-section. ¶5 Option: FRA by study. ¶6 Standard (FRA): as 4F.11 ¶5 for protected/permissive, permissive → protected GREEN immediately; sign not required, if used R10-27. ¶7 Option. **Figures 4F-13, 4F-14** (notes as above).
**4F.16** Approaches with no through movement. ¶1 Support: stems of T-intersections; opposite one-way approaches. ¶2 **Standard: except single-lane approaches, ≥ 2 primary faces for the major turning movement.** ¶3 Option: continuous CIRCULAR RED with arrows (safety-critical locations). ¶4 **Standard: no CIRCULAR GREEN or CIRCULAR YELLOW where A the approach's posted or statutory speed limit is ≥ 35 mph, B the opposing one-way roadway is a freeway/expressway exit ramp, or C that opposing one-way roadway's limit is ≥ 35 mph.** ¶6 Option: all-mandatory-turn-lane approaches — turns operate independently in any modes. ¶7 **Standard: a combined left/right lane — both turns start and end together, and every red on the approach is a CIRCULAR RED.** ¶8 Support: **a specific exception to the Chapter's RED ARROW requirements.** ¶9 **Standard: faces A two or more CR/CY/CG faces (with a left GREEN ARROW in the left-most face where an opposing approach exists and the left turn is protected) — except 4F.01 ¶6 and ¶4; B with extra mandatory turn lanes and no conflicts, arrow-only faces (CR, YELLOW ARROW, GREEN ARROW) and a shared CR / left YA / left GA / right YA / right GA face per 4E.04–4E.05; C as B with FYA in place of GREEN ARROW for conflicting turns.** **Figure 4F-15** (notes: horizontal faces allowed; shared faces may be five sections in a line; left GREEN ARROW section **shall** be included with an opposing one-way approach and protected phasing).

Notes (read together)
- **Right-turn rules mirror the left-turn rules, with stated differences**: separate right faces may show a CIRCULAR RED when right-on-red is intended to be allowed and a RED ARROW when not (4F.11 ¶3D, 4F.13 ¶2D, 4F.15 ¶3G, ¶6G) — which ties to 4A.03 C.1 (turn after stop on CIRCULAR RED) and C.2 (no turn on a RED ARROW unless a device permits); the R10-10R sign is required where that CIRCULAR RED may appear while the through lanes are not red, unless visibility-limited. Permissive → protected: left turns never get a yellow between (4F.08 ¶3G); right turns do **when an opposing permissive left turn ends at the same time** (4F.15 ¶3F.1).
- CHECK passed: 2B.59 lists "LEFT (RIGHT) TURN YIELD ON FLASHING RED ARROW AFTER STOP (R10-27)" and "LEFT (RIGHT) TURN SIGNAL (R10-10)" — one code for both directions; Part 4's "R10-10R" is the right-hand version (suffix convention as R3-20R/L).
- 4F.16 ¶8 states the manual's own specific-over-general reading: the CIRCULAR RED rule for combined left/right lanes is "a specific exception" to the RED ARROW provisions.
- 4F.16 ¶4 uses "posted or statutory speed limit ≥ 35 mph" (the limit only), unlike 4D.05 ¶7's "speed limit or 85th-percentile".

**Figure 4F-15** sheets 2–3 (B conflict with one turn; C conflicts with both; notes: horizontal faces allowed; five sections in a line allowed; left GREEN ARROW **shall** be included with an opposing one-way approach and protected phasing). **4F.16 (cont.)** ¶11 Option: variable lane use — independent turns when no combined lane; steady and flashing yellow arrows in one section to stay within five sections (4E.03).
**4F.17** Yellow change and red clearance. ¶1 Support: the yellow's exclusive function is to warn of termination. ¶2 **Standard: a steady yellow after every CIRCULAR GREEN, GREEN ARROW, and steady-mode FYA or FRA — except when followed immediately by a GREEN ARROW.** ¶3 **Standard: yellow duration by engineering practices.** ¶5 Guidance: red clearance where engineering practices indicate. ¶6–¶7 **Standard: red clearance duration by engineering practices; durations consistent with the determined values within the controller's capabilities.** ¶8 **Standard: yellow duration never varies cycle to cycle within a timing plan.** ¶9 **Standard: red clearance never decreased or omitted cycle to cycle, except ¶10** (Option: extended when a red-light violator is detected). ¶11 Option: lagging-left red clearance shown when the lag phase is skipped, omitted when shown. ¶12 Option: different durations in different timing plans. ¶13 **Guidance: yellow 3–6 s (longer for higher speeds); red clearance ≤ 6 s except one-lane two-way facilities (4O.02) or exceptionally wide intersections.** ¶14 **Standard: no "pre-yellow warning" displays (flashing green, vehicular countdowns, similar) at a signalized location — except Warning Beacons on advance warning signs (2C.35).** ¶15 Support: research shows they increase crashes.
**4F.18** Preemption and priority — general. ¶1–¶3 Option/Support: altered timing for vehicle classes; harder-to-stop vehicles supersede easier ones; non-intersection uses. ¶4–¶5: dark-to-steady transitions under requests — avoid misdirection (Option methods A–E). ¶6 Guidance: keep traffic moving. ¶7 **Guidance: priority order — train, boat, heavy vehicle (fire, EMS), light vehicle (law enforcement), light rail transit, rubber-tired transit.** ¶8 Option: LRT indications (8D.15) for buses in queue-jumper or semi-exclusive/mixed alignments.
**4F.19** Preemption (#1C.02). ¶2 Support: examples (emergency greens; track clearance; red to prohibit turns toward tracks). ¶3 **Standard: entering preemption, the yellow change interval and any following red clearance shall not be shortened or omitted.** ¶4 Option: **A pedestrian walk and change intervals may be shortened or omitted; B the red clearance may be omitted so that the return to the previous green follows a steady yellow in the same face.** ¶5 **Standard: during and leaving preemption — no shortened or omitted yellow or red clearance; no steady yellow → green.** ¶6–¶7 Option: distinctive indications for escorts (rail/boat preemption) and for emergency-vehicle control (UVC 11-106). ¶8 Guidance: back-up power for signals with railroad preemption or coordinated with flashing-light signals (not LRT-interconnected). ¶9 Guidance: Chapter 8D for signals near grade crossings.
**4F.20** Priority control (#1C.02). ¶3 **Standard: during and around priority — A no shortened or omitted yellow or red clearance; B walk interval not below 4I.06; C walk and change intervals not omitted unless the vehicular phase is also omitted or the pedestrian phase is exclusive; D no shortened or omitted pedestrian change interval; E no steady yellow → green.**

## Chapter 4G — Flashing operation (PDF 752–754)
**4G.01** ¶1 **Standard: 50–60 flashes per minute.** ¶2 **Standard: on-time ½ to ⅔ of the cycle.** ¶3 Standard: visibility-limiting rules apply to flashing indications, except flashing yellows for through traffic need not be shielded from separate turn lanes. ¶4 **Standard: an independent flasher per signal.** ¶5 **Standard: a manual switch and a conflict monitor (MMU) circuit (and an automatic means if appropriate) to initiate flashing.** ¶6 **Standard: flashing not ended by removing or switching off the controller or MMU.** ¶7 Option: scheduled flashing by study or judgement.
**4G.02** Into flashing. ¶1 Option: MMU or manual switch at any time. ¶2 **Standard: programmed changes only A at the end of the common major-street red, or B directly from CIRCULAR GREEN → flashing CIRCULAR YELLOW, GREEN ARROW → FYA, or FYA → FYA (in another section if separate).** ¶3 **Standard: no green or flashing yellow directly followed by steady or flashing red without a steady yellow.**
**4G.03** Indications in flash. ¶1 Guidance: flashing yellow for the major street, red for others (or red everywhere). ¶2 **Standard: all greens dark, except continuous single-section GREEN ARROWs.** ¶3 **Standard: flashing yellow on more than one approach only if they do not conflict.** ¶4 **Standard: exactly one flashing indication per face** (except ¶5: two identical reds together). ¶6 **Standard: no steady indications except continuous single-section GREEN ARROWs, which stay lit.** ¶7 Standard: where a face has circular and arrow indications of the flashed color, only the circular flashes. ¶8 **Standard: one color per approach, except separate turn faces may flash RED ARROW beside flashing yellow through faces; shared turn faces may not flash CIRCULAR RED beside flashing yellow through faces.** ¶9 Standard: all-arrow faces flash the red or yellow arrow; protected-only or FYA/FRA faces may flash the YELLOW ARROW beside flashing yellow through faces if a permissive turn without a full stop is intended.
**4G.04** Out of flashing. ¶1 **Standard: A yellow-red — at the start of the major-street green (or the major movement's green); B red-red — flashing reds to steady reds, then the major-street green.** ¶2 **Guidance: the steady red clearance from red-red flash ≥ 6 s.** ¶3–¶4 Guidance: sequences with and without a common major-street green. ¶5 **Standard: no flashing yellow directly followed by red without a steady yellow.** ¶6 Option: special midblock signals resting in flashing yellow (green position) may go straight to steady yellow.

## Chapter 4H — Bicycle signals (PDF 755–758)
**4H.01** Option A–D uses (protected phase or leading/lagging interval; bike lane right of a right-turn lane; counter-flow; complex intersections); midblock signals. Guidance: decided in the 4C.01 study (new) or by judgement (existing). ¶6: retrofits analogous to pedestrian signals, no study required. ¶8 **Standard: only for bicyclists from a designated bike lane or separate facility.** ¶9 **Standard: only where bicyclists on GREEN or YELLOW BICYCLE do not conflict with any simultaneous motor vehicle movement, including turns on red.** ¶10 Guidance: only where the bicycle movement sometimes differs from the parallel motor-vehicle movement.
**4H.02** **Standard prohibitions: no simultaneous perpendicular bicycle movements; not for bicyclists sharing a lane with motor vehicles; never with hybrid beacons.**
**4H.03** ¶2 **Standard: a Bicycle Signal sign (R10-40 … R10-41c) immediately adjacent to every bicycle face — ≥ 24 × 36 in beside overhead faces, ≥ 12 × 21 in beside post-mounted faces — except ¶3** (Option: omit beside supplemental near-side 4-in faces).
**4H.04** Steady (Standard): RED BICYCLE prohibits entry (turns after stop per 4A.05 C); YELLOW after GREEN, never red → green, followed by red; GREEN only to permit entry.
**4H.05** Flashing. ¶1 **Standard: same mode as the other faces; never dark while the others flash.** ¶2–¶3 Guidance: flashing RED BICYCLE with red approaches (or none); flashing YELLOW with yellow through faces unless red is safer.
**4H.06** Layout. ¶1 **Standard: only bicycle symbols — no circular or arrow indications.** ¶3 **Standard: A the SHS 2004 p. 6-7 symbol, horizontal, facing left; B positions as circular R/Y/G (4E.04–4E.05); C no clustered pairs of yellow or green bicycles (a specific exception to 4E.04 ¶5).** ¶4–¶5 Option: backplates; no visors on 4-in faces. **Figure 4H-1**.
**4H.07** **Standard: 4, 8, 12 in; one size per face; 4-in only in supplemental post-mounted near-side faces.**
**4H.08** Placement. ¶1 **Standard: per 4D.05–4D.08 except A one primary bicycle face suffices (specific exception to 4D.05 ¶1A); B primary faces 8 or 12 in even near side; C a supplemental near-side face where the primary is > 120 ft beyond the stop line.** ¶2 Guidance: **80–120 ft → a near-side face should be provided.** ¶3 Guidance: **≥ 3 ft from the nearest vehicular face** (measuring rules). ¶4 Guidance: maximize visibility to bicyclists; visibility-limited where drivers might be confused.
**4H.09** Height. **Standard: per 4D.09 except A not over the roadway — ≥ 7 ft; B 4-in near-side faces 4–8 ft, projecting ≤ 4 in over a sidewalk or path.**
**4H.10** Guidance: intensity per 4E.01 ¶11. **4H.11** Standard: intervals per 4F.17; Guidance: **bicycle yellow ≥ 3 s**; Support: the red clearance, not the yellow, provides crossing time.
**4H.12** Push buttons. Option; ¶3 **Standard: shared pedestrian/bicycle buttons meet accessibility (4I.05).** ¶4 **Standard: bicycle buttons with an R10-4, R10-24 or R10-26 sign (2B.58, 9B.20).**

## Chapter 4I — Pedestrian control features (PDF 759–…)
**4I.01** Support: WALKING PERSON / UPRAISED HAND; APS in 4K; PHB 4J; in-roadway lights 4U.
**4I.02** Design. ¶1 **Standard: new indications — symbols on a rectangular background (existing lettered or outline heads may serve out their life); SHS symbols; each independently displayed, single color.** ¶2 **Standard: two-section — hand above person; one-section — overlaid or side by side with the hand on the left, independently lit.** ¶3 **Standard: WALKING PERSON white; UPRAISED HAND Portland orange** (incandescent lenses obscured except the symbol). ¶4 Standard: ITE 2008 and ITE LED pedestrian 2011 standards (external). ¶5–¶6 Guidance: intensity; unlit symbols not visible from the far end. ¶7 **Standard: symbols ≥ 6 in high.** ¶8 **Standard: flashing DONT WALK 50–60 per minute, on ½–⅔.** ¶9–¶11 Guidance: recognisable to 10 ft from the far end; **≥ 9 in where pedestrians enter > 100 ft from the head**; dimming. ¶12–¶13 Option/Standard: animated eyes — white, scanning ~1/s, ≥ 12 in wide, each eye ≥ 5 × 2.5 in, lit only during the walk interval. **Figure 4I-1**.
**4I.03** ¶1 **Standard: bottom of housing 7–10 ft above the sidewalk; aimed for visibility at the start of the crosswalk.** ¶2 Guidance: separated from vehicular heads on a shared support.
**4I.04** ¶1 **Standard: countdown displays on all pedestrian heads where the pedestrian change interval is > 7 s.**

Notes (read together)
- **Preemption entry — 4F.19 ¶3 (Standard) and ¶4B (Option)**: ¶3 forbids shortening or omitting the yellow and following red clearance; ¶4B allows omitting the red clearance only for the return to the previous green after a steady yellow — the one case 4F.01 ¶3 B.3 already permits. An Option modifying a Standard for a single named case (1C.01).
- **Preemption vs priority for pedestrians**: preemption may shorten or omit walk and pedestrian change intervals (4F.19 ¶4A); priority may not (4F.20 ¶3B–D).
- CHECK passed: 4F.17 ¶14 (no flashing green or vehicular countdowns) agrees with 4A.04 A (flashing green has no meaning) and 4E.01 ¶2 (no numbers in vehicular indications); 4G.01 ¶1–¶2 flash rate = 4I.02 ¶8; 4H.07 ¶3 = 4E.02 ¶2; 4H.08 ¶1A and 4H.06 ¶3C name themselves as specific exceptions; 4H.08's 120-ft rule parallels 4E.02 ¶5C and Figure 4D-2.
- Part 9 cross-check to make: 4H.03 ¶2's Bicycle Signal sign minimum sizes (24 × 36 overhead, 12 × 21 post-mounted) against Table 9B-1.

**4I.04 (cont.)** ¶2 Option: countdowns where the change interval is ≤ 7 s. ¶3 **Standard: the countdown runs simultaneously with the flashing DONT WALK.** ¶4 **Standard: Portland orange numbers ≥ 6 in on black, beside the UPRAISED HAND.** ¶5 **Standard: begins only at the start of the change interval; dark after zero.** ¶6 **Standard: counts to the end of the change interval; never during the walk interval; never during the red clearance of a concurrent vehicular phase ending with or after the pedestrian phase.** ¶7 **Guidance: with no concurrent vehicular phase, the flashing DONT WALK ≈ 4 s less than the required clearance time, followed by an added steady DONT WALK interval.** ¶8 Guidance: **≥ 9-in numbers where pedestrians enter > 100 ft away.** ¶9–¶10 Guidance: separate timing devices; dark immediately on preemption.
**4I.05** Pedestrian detectors. Option: push buttons or passive detection. ¶4 **Guidance: ≤ 5 lbf, no tight grasping; locations A–K — ADA reach ranges and clear space; accessible route to the ramp; on the ramp side farthest from the intersection centre; ≤ 10 ft from that ramp edge; ≤ 5 ft from the outside crosswalk line; not farther than the stop line; 1.5–6 ft from the curb face or shoulder edge; face parallel to the crosswalk; ~3.5 ft high, ≤ 4 ft; 4-ft clear access route; outside ramp flares.** ¶5–¶6 Guidance: as level as feasible; ≤ 10 ft from the curb under constraints. ¶7 Guidance: **two buttons on one corner ≥ 10 ft apart**, except ¶8 (Option: closer or one pole). ¶10 **Standard: a sign (2B.58) beside every push button.** ¶12 **Standard: positions and legends show which crosswalk each button serves.** ¶13 **Standard: clearance only to a median + actuated → a detector in the median.** ¶14–¶15 Guidance: detectors on islands; locked special-purpose buttons. ¶16 **Standard: pilot lights dark until actuation, then lit until the WALK (or green).** ¶17–¶18 Option/Standard: extended press for extra time **with the R10-32P plaque**. **Figures 4I-2** (notes: dimensions are recommendations; buttons ≥ 10 ft apart), **4I-3**.
**4I.06** Pedestrian intervals. ¶1 **Standard: pedestrian indications displayed except in flashing mode, when they are not displayed.** ¶2 **Standard: during WALK or flashing DONT WALK, a steady red to conflicting perpendicular vehicles (except 4J.03 ¶3).** ¶3 **Standard: WALK only when pedestrians may leave the curb.** ¶4 **Standard: the flashing DONT WALK change interval follows the WALK; then a buffer of steady DONT WALK ≥ 2 s before conflicting vehicles are released; change + buffer ≥ the calculated pedestrian clearance time; the buffer begins no later than the red clearance.** ¶5 Option: DONT WALK during the yellow — flashing, steady, or both. ¶7 **Guidance: clearance time for a pedestrian leaving at the end of the WALK at 3.5 ft/s to the far side of the traveled way (or a median).** ¶8–¶9 Option: up to 4 ft/s where an extended-press function exists; passive detection may adjust; extra time added to walk or change interval. ¶10 Guidance: slower speeds where slower walkers or wheelchair users routinely cross. ¶11 **Guidance: walk ≥ 7 s**, except ¶12 (Option: ≥ 4 s). ¶14 **Guidance: walk + clearance ≥ the time at 3 ft/s from the detector (or 6 ft behind the curb/pavement edge) to the far side (or median in two-stage crossings); extra added to the walk.** ¶15 Option: clearance only to a median. ¶16 **Standard: then median pedestrian signals (with detectors if actuated) and an R10-3d-type sign.** ¶18 Option: shortened on preemption (4F.19). ¶19 Option: leading pedestrian interval. ¶22–¶24 **Guidance: LPI ≥ 3 s, enough to cross one lane or clear the turning path; consider turn prohibitions during it; WALK ≥ LPI + 7 s.** **Figure 4I-4** (notes: countdown optional ≤ 7 s; walk may be reduced; **\*\*\* the buffer "shall always be provided and displayed"**).
**Chapter 4I complete.**

## Chapter 4J — Pedestrian hybrid beacons (PDF 768–771)
**4J.01** ¶1: a special hybrid beacon at unsignalized marked crosswalks. ¶2 Option: where signal warrants are not met, or Warrants 4/5 met but no signal is chosen. ¶3 **Standard: with signs and markings; only at a marked crosswalk.** ¶4 Guidance: if a signal is chosen, per 4D–4I, 4K. ¶5 Guidance: by study of volumes, speeds, widths, gaps, pedestrian volumes, walking speeds, delay. ¶6–¶7 **Guidance: major-street "posted or statutory speed limit or the 85th-percentile speed" ≤ 35 mph → Figure 4J-1; > 35 mph → Figure 4J-2 (1 hour, curves by crosswalk length).** ¶8 **Guidance: interpolate between the curves for other crosswalk lengths.** ¶9 Option: pedestrian criterion reduced up to 50 % if the 15th-percentile crossing speed < 3.5 ft/s. ¶10 Option: each direction separately with a wide median. **Figures 4J-1, 4J-2** (lower threshold 20 pph).
**4J.02** Design. ¶1 **Standard: per Chapters 4D–4G, 4I, 4J except as here.** ¶2 **Standard: three sections — a CIRCULAR YELLOW centred below two horizontal CIRCULAR REDs.** ¶3 **Standard: A ≥ 2 faces per major-street approach; B stop lines; C pedestrian heads (4I) at each end; D pedestrian actuation; E STOP signs on minor-street approaches when at or next to an intersection.** ¶4 Guidance: parking prohibited ≥ 100 ft before and ≥ 20 ft beyond the crosswalk (or curb extensions); coordinated in systems. ¶5–¶6 Guidance: **both faces over the roadway where speeds exceed 35 mph** or visibility is obscured; multi-lane ≤ 35 mph — one each side (median) or at least one overhead. ¶7 Guidance: 4D.05–4D.10. ¶8–¶10 Option: R10-23 / R10-23a signs; W11-2, S1-1, W11-15 with AHEAD in advance, with Warning Beacon; backplates. ¶12 Guidance: that beacon flashes only when the PHB is not dark. ¶13 Standard: beacon per 4S.01, 4S.03. ¶14 **Standard: no bicycle signal faces at a PHB.**
**4J.03** Operation. ¶1 **Standard: dark between actuations.** ¶2 **Standard: flashing CIRCULAR YELLOW → steady CIRCULAR YELLOW → both steady CIRCULAR REDs during the walk → alternating flashing CIRCULAR REDs during the change interval → dark.** ¶3 **Standard: pedestrian heads — steady DONT WALK while dark or yellow; WALK with steady reds; flashing DONT WALK with alternating flashing reds; steady DONT WALK after.** ¶4 Option: heads dark while the beacon is dark (roundabouts, by study). ¶5–¶7 Guidance: flashing-yellow duration by judgement, not varying; coordinated start. ¶8 Option: minimum dark time. ¶10 **Standard: yellow change by engineering practices (4F.17).** ¶11 Guidance: **3–6 s**. ¶12–¶13 Option: red clearance; flashing-red buffer. ¶14 **Guidance: preempt a PHB within 200 ft of an active grade crossing** (4F.19, 8D.09). ¶15 **Standard: in flashing mode (MMU or manual) — flashing CIRCULAR YELLOW to the major street; pedestrian heads dark.** **Figure 4J-3** (sequence 1–6; note: optional red clearance after 3 and buffer after 5).

## Chapter 4K — Accessible pedestrian signals (PDF 772–…)
**4K.01** Support: ADA and Section 504 govern when APS are used; factors A–E; difficulty factors (quiet vehicles, turns on red, complex phasing, roundabouts, wide streets). ¶6 **Standard: APS with pedestrian signal timing.** ¶7 **Standard: indicate which crossing each device serves.** ¶8 **Standard: not limited by time of day or day of week in steady operation.** Option: push buttons or passive; buttons at pretimed signals; Braille/raised street names; tactile maps. ¶14 **Standard: where a press is needed for the walk, it activates both the walk and the APS.**
**4K.02** Location. ¶2 Guidance: per 4I.05, close to the outer crosswalk line and the ramp. ¶3 **Standard: two APS buttons < 10 ft apart or on one pole (except all-way simultaneous walks) → each with A locator tone, B vibrotactile walk, C speech walk message, D speech push button information message.** ¶4 **Standard: clearance only to a median with APS detectors → an APS detector in the median.**
**4K.03** Walk indications. ¶2 **Standard: both audible and vibrotactile.** ¶3 **Standard: vibrotactile arrow on the button, vibrating during the walk.** ¶4–¶5 **Standard: audible during the walk only, audible at the start of the crosswalk, same duration as the walk (unless resting in walk).** ¶6 Guidance: resting in walk — first 7 s; recalled by a press if time remains. ¶7 **Standard: APS for different phases < 10 ft apart on a corner or median → speech walk message; otherwise (midblock, single APS, median, ≥ 10 ft apart) → percussive tone.** ¶8 **Standard: tones repeat at 8–10 ticks per second, multiple frequencies with a dominant 880 Hz.** ¶9 Guidance: **≤ 5 dBA above ambient** (except beaconing). ¶10 **Standard: automatic volume adjustment up to a maximum of 100 dBA.** ¶11 Guidance: quieter at channelized right turns, multi-phase and diagonal/simultaneous crossings. ¶12 Option: alert tone. ¶17 **Standard: speech messages state that the walk is on and for which crossing.** ¶18–¶21 Guidance: models ("Broadway. Walk sign is on to cross Broadway."; "Walk sign is on for all crossings."); no extra content; no commands or "safe to cross". ¶22 **Standard: messages when the walk is not timing — optional; if given, begin with "wait", need not repeat.** ¶23 **Standard: with a pilot light, each actuation gives the speech message "wait".**

Notes (read together)
- **MEAN COMPUTED pedestrian timing (4I.06)**: required clearance C = crossing length ÷ 3.5 ft/s (Guidance; 4 ft/s allowed with extended press; slower where needed); change interval + buffer ≥ C, buffer ≥ 2 s (Standard); walk ≥ 7 s (or ≥ 4 s) (Guidance/Option); walk + C ≥ (distance from detector, or 6 ft + crossing length) ÷ 3 ft/s (Guidance); LPI ≥ 3 s and walk ≥ LPI + 7 s (Guidance). 4I.04 ¶7 (change ≈ C − 4 s with no concurrent vehicle phase) fits ¶4 because the steady DONT WALK intervals make up the rest.
- **Two ways to read a family of curves**: Warrant 9 — the **nearest** curve (4C.10 ¶4B); PHB — **interpolate** between curves (4J.01 ¶8). Each figure carries its own reading rule.
- **STOP signs at PHBs**: 4A.08 ¶6 limits STOP signs with any highway traffic signal; 4J.02 ¶3E **requires** STOP signs on minor-street approaches at a PHB at or next to an intersection. 4J.02 is the specific provision, and its own scope list (¶1: 4D–4G, 4I, 4J) does not include 4A. Settled by specific over general.
- **Item 7 widened**: 4J.01 ¶6–¶7 (and 4J.02 ¶5–¶6) select by "posted or statutory speed limit **or** the 85th-percentile speed" ≤ 35 / > 35 mph; a road with a 30-mph limit and 40-mph 85th-percentile speed meets both descriptions.
- CHECK passed: 4I.06 ¶1 (pedestrian heads dark in flash) = 4J.03 ¶15; 4I.06 ¶2's exception is the PHB's flashing-red change interval (4J.03 ¶2–¶3); 4J.02 ¶14 = 4H.02 ¶3; 4J.02 ¶2 is one of the exceptions 4E.04 ¶8 names; 4I.05 ¶10 and ¶18 point to 2B.58 and the R10-32P plaque; 4K.02 ¶3's 10-ft figure = 4I.05 ¶7.

**4K.03 (cont.)** ¶24 Option: speech messages in other languages. ¶25 **Standard: English first, then the second language, alternating while the walk times.**
**4K.04** Vibrotactile arrows and locator tones. ¶1 **Standard: a vibrotactile arrow on the button showing which crosswalk it actuates — high contrast, parallel to the crosswalk.** ¶2 **Standard: a locator tone.** ¶4 **Standard: locator tones ≤ 0.15 s, repeating at 1-s intervals whenever the audible walk is not active (including the change interval and rest-in-walk), except ¶5** (Option: silent during steady DONT WALK if passive detection activates it for pedestrians within **12 ft**). ¶6 **Standard: deactivated in flashing mode** (not where pedestrian actuation brings the signal out of flash or dark). ¶7 Standard: responsive to ambient sound. ¶8 Guidance: audible 6–12 ft or to the building line.
**4K.05** Extended press. ¶1 Option: more time, audible beaconing, information message. ¶2 **Standard: a press < 1 s actuates only the pedestrian timing and walk indication; ≥ 1 s also actuates the added features.** ¶3 **Standard: the R10-32P plaque where extra crossing time is given.** ¶4–¶5 Support: beaconing not at channelized turns or split phasing. ¶6 Guidance: consider beaconing after a study — crosswalks > 70 ft (unless a median APS divides them), skewed, irregular intersections, on request, others. ¶7 Guidance: initiated by an extended press. ¶8 **Standard: beaconing raises the locator tone during the change interval up to 100 dBA from a far-end loudspeaker 7–10 ft above the pavement.** ¶9 Guidance: speaker within the crosswalk width. ¶11–¶12 Option: louder walk; information messages (intersection, unusual phasing or geometry). ¶13 **Standard: information messages only when the walk is not timing, beginning "Wait", e.g. "Wait to cross Broadway at Grand", with any signalization or geometry information after.** ¶14 Guidance: no landmarks, detours or TTC information.
**Chapter 4K complete.**

## Chapter 4L — Rectangular rapid flashing beacons (PDF 777–780)
**4L.01** ¶1 Option: pedestrian/bicyclist-activated emphasis for pedestrian, school and trail warning signs at marked crosswalks on uncontrolled approaches. ¶2 **Standard: an RRFB functions only as a Warning Beacon (4S.03); Warning Beacon provisions apply except as here.** ¶3 **Standard: only with a post-mounted W11-2, S1-1 or W11-15 with a W16-7P plaque, or the overhead version, at a marked crosswalk.** ¶4 **Standard: not at crosswalks across YIELD-, STOP-, signal- or PHB-controlled approaches — except roundabout entries/exits and island-separated free-flow turn lanes.** ¶5–¶6 Option/Standard: an advance RRFB (with AHEAD or distance plaque) supplements, never replaces, the crosswalk RRFB.
**4L.02** Design. ¶1 **Standard: two rapidly flashed rectangular yellow LED indications, each ≥ 5 in wide × 2 in high.** ¶2 **Standard: horizontal, ≥ 7 in apart (nearest edges), not beyond the sign's outside edges.** ¶3 **Standard: never independent of the crossing sign — on the same support; overhead — directly above or below the sign.** ¶4 Option: between the sign and plaque or within 12 in above the sign (a specific exception to 4S.01 ¶6). ¶5 Option: visors, backplates. ¶6 **Standard: post-mounted — at least two assemblies (right and left) per approach, each with RRFB and W16-7P.** ¶7 Guidance: left one on the median if practicable. ¶8 **Standard: overhead — at least one sign (no W16-7P) over the lanes.** ¶9 Standard: at intersections, per ¶3. ¶10–¶11 Option: more than one crosswalk on an approach; the first crosswalk's RRFBs facing one direction. ¶12 **Standard: daytime intensity ≥ SAE J595 (2005) Class 1 yellow.** ¶13 Option: night dimming. ¶14 **Standard: push buttons (not passive detection) → an R10-25 sign.** ¶17 Guidance: an audible information device. ¶18 Option: confirmation light. **Figure 4L-1** (notes: all RRFBs start and stop together (**shall**); overhead may be over the intersection centre).
**4L.03** Operation. ¶1 **Standard: normally dark; on only after actuation; off after a set time (or when passive detection shows the crosswalk clear).** ¶2 **Standard: all units at a crosswalk (including advance) start and stop together.** ¶3 Guidance: duration per 4I.06 clearance timing. ¶5 **Standard: the flash period restarts at every detection, including during and just after flashing.** ¶6 **Standard: 75 flashing sequences per minute (a specific exception to 4S.01 ¶3).** ¶7 **Standard: each 800-ms sequence — L 50 · dark 50 · R 50 · dark 50 · L 50 · dark 50 · R 50 · dark 50 · both 50 · dark 50 · both 50 · dark 250 ms.** ¶8 **Standard: each indication ≤ 5 flashes per second over the sequence (seizure risk).** ¶9 Standard: audible devices with RRFBs use no vibrotactile or percussive indications. ¶10 Guidance: speech "Warning lights are flashing", spoken twice.

## Chapter 4M — Emergency-vehicle traffic control signals (PDF 781–782)
**4M.01** ¶2–¶3 Option: where warrants are not met (e.g. fire station access); an EVHB instead (4N.01). ¶4 Guidance: where gaps or stopping sight distance are inadequate. ¶5 **Guidance: sight distance from the obstruction for the critical lane and the posted or statutory speed limit or 85th-percentile speed, whichever is higher.**
**4M.02** Design. ¶2 **Standard: W11-8 with the W11-12P EMERGENCY SIGNAL AHEAD plaque before every emergency-vehicle signal**; beacons per 4S.01, 4S.03. ¶3 Guidance: one face per major approach over the roadway. ¶4 Guidance: 12-in steady red, yellow and arrows; **8-in green or flashing yellow** (= 4E.02 ¶5A). ¶5–¶6 **Standard: EMERGENCY SIGNAL (R10-13) sign facing each major approach, beside the overhead face if any.** ¶7–¶8 Option: one face for an emergency-only approach; other means to reduce flashing-yellow output.
**4M.03** Operation. ¶1 Standard: at signalized locations, greens obtained by preemption (4F.19). ¶2 **Standard: midblock — A green or flashing yellow (in the green position) between actuations; B on actuation, steady yellow then steady red to the major street; C no yellow required after the driveway's green.** ¶3–¶5 Guidance: intersections flash or actuated; beacons only before and during the yellow and during the red; **red ≤ 1.5 × the time for the emergency vehicle to clear**, set by test runs. ¶6 Option: manual or remote initiation.

## Chapter 4N — Emergency-vehicle hybrid beacons (PDF 783–784)
**4N.01** ¶1 **Standard: only with signs, at unsignalized locations where emergency vehicles enter or cross; actuated only by authorized emergency or maintenance personnel.** ¶2 Guidance: only if A the 4M.01 conditions are met, B a study shows it can comply and work, and **C it is not at or within 100 ft of a STOP- or YIELD-controlled side road or driveway.**
**4N.02** Design. ¶2 **Standard: three sections — CIRCULAR YELLOW below two horizontal CIRCULAR REDs.** ¶3 **Standard: ≥ 2 faces per major approach.** ¶4–¶5 Guidance: **both overhead where speeds exceed 40 mph** or visibility is obscured; multi-lane ≤ 40 mph — each side or one overhead. ¶6 Guidance: 4D.05–4D.10. ¶7 **Standard: stop lines and R10-14 / R10-14a signs for each major approach.** ¶8–¶10 Option: R10-14b; operator confirmation light; advance warning sign with beacon (¶11 Guidance: flashing only when not dark). **Figure 4N-1** (note: optional red clearance after interval 3).
**4N.03** Operation. ¶1 **Standard: dark between actuations.** ¶2 **Standard: flashing yellow → steady yellow → alternating flashing reds for the egress time (only when major-street drivers must stop, then proceed as at a STOP sign) → dark.** ¶3 Guidance: flashing-yellow duration by judgement. ¶4 **Standard: yellow by engineering practices (4F.17)**; ¶5 Guidance 3–6 s. ¶6 Option: red clearance. ¶7 Guidance: preempt within 200 ft of an active grade crossing. ¶8 **Standard: in flashing mode — flashing yellow to each major approach.**

## Chapter 4O — One-lane, two-way facilities (PDF 785)
**4O.01** Support; Guidance: sight distance and approach speed; Option: where warrants are not met but gaps are inadequate. **4O.02** ¶1 **Standard: 4D–4G apply, except A red clearances long enough to clear the one-lane section; B interconnection or other means to prevent conflicting indications (green-green) at the two ends.** **4O.03** Guidance: operate to traffic; adequate clearing time by judgement. ¶3 **Standard: flashing mode flashes red.**

## Chapter 4P — Freeway entrance ramp control signals (PDF 786–787)
**4P.01** Support: ramp metering; Guidance: engineering study of ramps, connections, streets and the freeway section; FHWA Ramp Management and Control Handbook (external).
**4P.02** ¶1 Standard: signal design standards apply except as here. ¶2 **Standard: two-section (red, green) or three-section (red, yellow, green) faces.** ¶3–¶4 Option: dark when not in use; some lanes metered (HOV bypass). ¶5 **Standard: one controlled lane, or all lanes always green together → at least two faces per ramp.** ¶6–¶7 **Standard: two or more separately-controlled lanes → one face over each lane.** ¶8 Guidance: side faces with three or more lanes. ¶9–¶10 **Option: the two faces for a single lane (or the outer lane) on one pole — a specific exception to 4D.07's 8-ft separation — lower face ≥ 4.5 ft high.** ¶11–¶13 Guidance: minimise mainline viewing; XX VEHICLE(S) PER GREEN signs (2B.61); freeway-to-freeway ramps — visibility and multiple advance signs with beacons.
**4P.03** ¶1–¶2 Guidance: strategies set before installation; **W3-8 RAMP METERED WHEN FLASHING for part-time metering**. ¶3 **Standard: W3-8 with a Warning Beacon flashing when metering; no flashing LED units in its legend or border.**

## Chapter 4Q — Movable bridges (PDF 788–…)
**4Q.01** Support: signals notify of closure, coordinated with warning and resistance gates; **resistance gates are a design feature, not traffic control devices** (AASHTO movable bridge specifications). ¶4 **Standard: both signals and gates, except A neither — other measures on roads < 400 ADT or at manual bridges without power; B signals only in urban areas where streets or driveways make gates ineffective; C warning gates only if a bridge-controlled signal is within 500 ft of the resistance gates with no intervening entrances.**
**4Q.02** ¶1 Standard: per 4D–4G except as here. ¶2 **Standard: 12-in indications for all new movable bridge signals** (¶3 Option: existing 8-in kept). ¶4 **Standard: A three-section R/Y/G faces, or B two one-section red faces in a vertical array separated by an R10-6 STOP HERE ON RED sign.** ¶5 **Standard: at least two faces per approach and a stop line.** ¶6–¶7 Guidance: three-section for frequent openings; **≤ 50 ft before the warning gate**. ¶8 Option: audible warnings.

Notes (read together)
- **MEAN COMPUTED RRFB sequence (4L.03)**: 75 sequences per minute → 800 ms each; the twelve steps sum to 11 × 50 + 250 = 800 ms. Each lamp lights 4 times per sequence (left in A, E, I, K; right in C, G, I, K) → 4 ÷ 0.8 s = **5 flashes per second — exactly the ¶8 maximum**.
- 4K.05 ¶2 (extended press = ≥ 1 s) and the R10-32P legend ("PUSH BUTTON FOR 2 SECONDS …"): users are told 2 s, which exceeds the 1-s threshold. Consistent.
- **Ramp meters (4P.02 ¶9–¶10)**: the Option names itself an exception to 4D.07's 8-ft separation; its 4.5-ft lower face is also below 4D.09 ¶2A's 8-ft minimum for faces not over the roadway. Held by specific over general and Option-modifies-Standard (1C.01). (4D.09 ¶2B already allows 4.5 ft on near-side medians.)
- **Hybrid beacon speed thresholds differ**: PHB faces overhead above **35 mph** (4J.02 ¶5); EVHB above **40 mph** (4N.02 ¶4). Sequences differ too: PHB has a steady-red walk interval before the alternating flashing reds (4J.03 ¶2); EVHB goes from steady yellow straight to alternating flashing reds (4N.03 ¶2).
- 4M.01 ¶5 states "whichever is higher" for the speed — like 3B.12 ¶8 — where the manual wants a particular speed it says so (item 7 context).
- CHECK passed: 4M.02 ¶4 = 4E.02 ¶5A (8-in emergency greens); 4O.02 ¶1A with 4F.17 ¶13 (longer red clearances for one-lane two-way facilities); 4L.01 ¶2 and 4L.02 ¶4, 4L.03 ¶6 name their exceptions to 4S.01; 4P.03 ¶3 agrees with 2C.37 and 2A.12 (no flashing LEDs in that sign's legend); 4B.03 ¶2C's list includes RRFBs.

**4Q.02 (cont.)** ¶9 Guidance: DRAW BRIDGE (W3-6) sign in advance (not where urban conditions make it impractical). ¶10 **Standard: without a continuous view of two indications for the Table 4D-2 distance — a supplemental face or a W3-6 with an interconnected Warning Beacon.** ¶11 Option: W3-6 with beacon. ¶12 Support: no signals needed at the resistance gate nearest the span when two gate sets are used. ¶13 **Standard: warning gates ≥ standard railroad size, 16-in alternate vertical fully-reflectorized red and white stripes, with flashing red lights as on railroad gates (8D.03), lit only when closed or moving.** ¶14–¶15 Guidance: top ~4 ft above the pavement; lightweight; lateral clearance when upright. ¶16 Option: delineate resistance gates similarly. ¶17–¶19: full approach width (or all lanes on barrier-divided roads); full roadway width without resistance gates; one full or two half gates. ¶20 Support: locations set by the resistance gate; resistance gates ≥ 50 ft from the span on high-speed roads (except bascule/lift). ¶21 Guidance: **warning gates ≥ 100 ft from resistance gates (or the span)**. ¶22–¶23 Guidance: do not halt traffic on spans subject to vessel impact; at least one span away; **beyond 330 ft, a second gate set ~100 ft from the gate or span**. ¶24 Guidance: a device against stopping on nearby railroad tracks.
**4Q.03** ¶1 **Standard: signals, gates and spans interlocked under the bridge tender's control.** ¶2 **Standard: three-section faces — green at all times between openings (flashing yellow allowed if no opening is expected for continuous periods > 5 hours); steady red to stop; yellow by engineering practices (4F.17).** ¶3 **Standard: vertical red pairs flash alternately only when traffic must stop.** ¶4 Guidance: interconnect and preempt adjacent signals by judgement.
**Chapter 4Q complete.**

## Chapter 4R — Toll plazas (PDF 791)
**4R.01** ¶1 **Standard: no red/green circular signals (or look-alikes) to show toll lane open/closed status.** ¶2 Guidance: not for new or reconstructed indications of ETC success/failure or cash stop-and-go.
**4R.02** ¶1 Standard: lane-use control signals per 4T. ¶2 **Standard: multi-lane plazas where lanes are sometimes closed — a lane-use control signal over the centre of each lane** (= 2F.16 ¶6). ¶3 Option: below 15 ft under a canopy but not below its clearance. ¶4 Option: for open-road ETC lanes supplementing Part 6 devices.
**4R.03** ¶1 Standard: per 4S. ¶2 Guidance: beacons at ETC-only canopy signs distinctly separate from lane-use signals (2F.16). ¶3 **Option: beacons on islands, attenuators or booth pylons below the normal 8-ft minimum.**

## Chapter 4S — Flashing beacons (PDF 792–794)
**4S.01** ¶1 Support: control (intersection beacons) or warning. ¶2 **Standard: per 4D and 4E except as here.** ¶3 **Standard: 50–60 flashes per minute; on ½–⅔.** ¶4 **Standard: no beacon within a sign border, except Exit Direction signs with advisory speed panels (2E.25).** ¶5 **Standard: 8-in and 12-in only.** ¶6 Guidance: **housing edge ≥ 12 in outside the sign or plaque edges.** ¶7–¶8 Option: night dimming; backplates.
**4S.02** Intersection Control Beacon. ¶1 **Standard: faces toward each approach, flashing CIRCULAR YELLOW or RED; only at intersections controlling two or more directions.** ¶2 **Standard: A yellow on one route (normally the major street) and red on STOP-controlled approaches, or B red on all (all STOP-controlled).** ¶3 Standard: no flashing yellow facing conflicting approaches. ¶4 **Standard: STOP signs on every approach shown flashing red.** ¶5 **Standard: two horizontal reds flash simultaneously (not to resemble railroad signals); two vertical separated reds alternately.** ¶6 **Standard: 12-in where lane-use signals are seen at the same time, or where the nearest face is > 120 ft beyond the stop line without a near-side face.** ¶7 Guidance: 12-in above 40 mph (posted/statutory or 85th) or with post-mounted faces only. ¶8 Guidance: pedestals only on islands. ¶9–¶11 Option: supplemental faces; where crash rates suggest a need; usually over the centre.
**4S.03** Warning Beacon. ¶1 Support uses A–E — **regulatory signs except STOP, DO NOT ENTER, WRONG WAY and SPEED LIMIT** (which have their own beacons); WHEN FLASHING legends (no flashing LEDs in those signs, 2A.12). ¶2 **Standard: flashing CIRCULAR YELLOW sections.** ¶3 **Standard: only to supplement a warning or regulatory sign or marker.** ¶4 Standard: at intersections, not facing conflicting approaches. ¶5–¶7 Guidance: location by the condition; illuminate obstructions; **operate only while the condition or regulation exists.** ¶8–¶10 Option: alternate or simultaneous; with W3-4 + W16-13P interconnected with signals; actuated by road users. ¶11 Guidance: audible devices with pedestrian-actuated beacons. ¶12 **Standard: those audible devices use no vibrotactile or percussive indications.** ¶13 Guidance: "Warning lights are flashing", twice.
**4S.04** Speed Limit Sign Beacon. **Standard: only with a Speed Limit sign; flashing CIRCULAR YELLOW; two or more flash alternately.** Option: fixed or variable signs; flashing to show the limit is in effect.
**4S.05** Stop Beacon. ¶1 **Standard: only with STOP, DO NOT ENTER or WRONG WAY signs** (= 4A.04 C4). ¶2 **Standard: flashing CIRCULAR RED; horizontal pairs simultaneously, vertical pairs alternately.** ¶3 Guidance: **housing 12–24 in from the sign's edge.**

## Chapter 4T — Lane-use control signals (PDF 795–797)
**4T.01** Support: reversible lanes, non-reversible uses, toll plazas. ¶3 Guidance: study — static signs vs signals; signals where A more than one lane reverses, B turn lanes change between periods, C complex operations, D crash history with static signs, E a study shows benefit. ¶4 **Standard: pavement markings (3B.04) with reversible-lane signals.** ¶5–¶6 Option: open/closed status on freeways (merges, lane ends, incidents) and at facility access points; R10-8 sign.
**4T.02** Meanings (Standard): **A DOWNWARD GREEN ARROW — lane open; B YELLOW X — about to close, followed by RED X (same or downstream face); C RED X — closed in the direction viewed; D WHITE TWO-WAY LEFT-TURN ARROW — left turns from either direction, not through; E WHITE ONE-WAY LEFT-TURN ARROW — left turns in that direction only.** **Figure 4T-1**.
**4T.03** Design. ¶1 **Standard: rectangular faces, opaque backgrounds; DGA, YELLOW X, RED X ≥ 18 in; white turn arrows ≥ 30 in — except ¶13.** ¶2–¶4 **Standard: reversed or closed lanes — at least DGA and RED X; reversible lanes that become turn lanes — the white arrow too; lanes adjacent to reversible lanes — DGA one way, RED X the other.** ¶5 **Standard: left to right — RED X, YELLOW X, DGA, WHITE TWO-WAY, WHITE ONE-WAY.** ¶6–¶10 Guidance: **visible ≥ 2,300 ft**; over the lane centre; intermediate faces for longer or curved sections (see one, preferably two); straight line across; away from signals. ¶11 **Standard: bottom 15–19 ft above the pavement, except ¶12** (Option: lower on canopies, not below clearance). ¶13 Option: **12-in and 18-in faces where clutter is minimal and speeds < 40 mph (not toll plazas).** ¶14–¶16 Option: larger faces; non-adjacent lanes; separate or superimposed sections.
