**4T.04** Operation. ¶1 **Standard: coordinated along the section; reliably guarding against prohibited combinations.** ¶2 **Standard: never over one lane to both directions — A DGA/DGA, B YELLOW X/YELLOW X, C one-way left/one-way left, D DGA/YELLOW X, E white turn arrow/DGA, F two-way left/one-way left, G one-way left/YELLOW X.** ¶3 **Standard: moving condition ended by RED X directly or YELLOW X then RED X.** ¶4 Standard: RED X long enough to clear the lane before opposing movement. ¶5 Standard: DGA → two-way left: RED X to the opposite direction long enough to vacate. ¶6 **Standard: manual override of automatic control.** ¶7 Guidance: automatic or manual operation. ¶8 **Standard: continuous operation, except special-event signals and non-reversible freeway lane signals may be dark; transitions to and from dark only with indications matching the lane use; other devices show lane use when dark.**

## Chapter 4U — In-roadway warning lights (PDF 798–799)
**4U.01** ¶1 Support: uses — marked school, midblock, uncontrolled-approach and **roundabout-advance crosswalks**, other pedestrian crossings. ¶2 **Standard: no applications other than this Chapter's.** ¶3 **Standard: flashed, never steady** (¶4: steady in-pavement lights are internally illuminated RPMs, 3B.14). ¶5 Option: flicker effect. ¶6 Guidance: **≤ ¾ in above the surface.**
**4U.02** Crosswalks. ¶1 Option: by study or judgement. ¶2 **Standard: only at marked crosswalks with warning signs; not at crosswalks controlled by YIELD, STOP, signals or PHBs.** ¶3 **Standard: A both sides spanning the whole crosswalk (except ¶7–¶8); B pedestrian-actuated, ending after a set time or when the crosswalk clears; C flashing yellow at 50–60 per minute; any flicker not repeating at 5–30 per second; D between the crosswalk line and 10 ft outside it; E facing away from (and across, if bidirectional) the crosswalk.** ¶4 **Standard: one-lane one-way — ≥ 2 on the approach side; two-lane — ≥ 3 each side; more than two lanes — ≥ 1 per lane each side.** ¶5–¶6 Guidance: lane centres, center line, edges; away from tire paths. ¶7–¶9 Option: omit the departure side on one-way streets or left of a median; pedestrian-facing confirmation lights. ¶10 Guidance: **operation long enough to cross at 3.5 ft/s** (slower where needed). ¶11 Guidance: audible device. ¶12 **Standard: push buttons → R10-25 sign.** ¶13 **Standard: operation only to a median → median actuators.** ¶14 Standard: no vibrotactile or percussive audible indications. ¶15 Guidance: "Warning lights are flashing", twice.

**Chapter 4U complete. PART 4 complete.**

# PART 5 — TRAFFIC CONTROL DEVICE CONSIDERATIONS FOR AUTOMATED VEHICLES

## Chapter 5A — General (PDF 800–…)
**5A.01** Support: considerations for devices designed to accommodate automated vehicles; **this Part does not require agencies to use these provisions**; the MUTCD does not address digital infrastructure, geometric design, maintenance levels or pavement conditions.
**5A.02** Support: AVs, sensors (camera, radar, LiDAR, ultrasonic, infrared), connected vehicles (V2V, V2I).
**5A.03** Support: SAE J3016 terms A–G (ADAS, ADS, automation levels, cooperative automation, driving automation system, DDT, ODD).
**Table 5A-1** (read from the page) Automation Levels — Level · description · category · type: 0 — human performs all of the DDT · None\* · None; 1 — sustained assistance with steering **or** speed · ADAS · Driving Automation System; 2 — steering **and** speed · ADAS · Driving Automation System; 3 — ADS performs the DDT within an ODD, human responds to requests · ADS · Driving Automation System; 4 — ADS even if the human does not respond · ADS · Driving Automation System; 5 — full-time ADS under all conditions a human could manage · ADS · Driving Automation System. **Merged cells: "ADAS" spans Levels 1–2, "ADS" Levels 3–5, "Driving Automation System" Levels 1–5.** Note \*: Level 0 may have ADAS features treated as warning or momentary intervention.
**5A.04** ¶1 Support: automation systems tolerate little non-uniformity (A CMS refresh rates and device quality, B sign color, C marking contrast, D signal photometrics, E TTC devices and flaggers, F grade crossings, especially passive). ¶3–¶4 **Guidance: maintenance policies considering human drivers and automation (1D.10, 2A.19, 3A.05, 4A.10); engineering judgement considers uniformity for both.** ¶6 Guidance A–D: uniform devices by roadway type and situation; timely replacement; planned TTC with compliant devices; remove extraneous devices.

Notes (read together)
- **Crosswalks at roundabouts — two wordings, one outcome**: RRFBs are barred at crosswalks "across **approaches** controlled by YIELD signs" with an explicit roundabout exception (4L.01 ¶4); in-roadway lights are barred at crosswalks "**controlled by** YIELD signs" with no exception, yet roundabout crosswalks are a listed use (4U.01 ¶1). A roundabout crosswalk lies ≥ 20 ft before the yield point (3C.09 ¶3): it crosses a YIELD-controlled approach, but the crosswalk itself is not YIELD-controlled. Both allow roundabout crosswalks. (Medium-high.)
- **Two seizure rules of different form**: RRFB — each lamp **≤ 5 flashes per second** (a ceiling, 4L.03 ¶8); in-roadway flicker — **not between 5 and 30 per second** (a forbidden band, 4U.02 ¶3C).
- Beacon placement: general beacons ≥ 12 in from a sign's edge (4S.01 ¶6); Stop Beacons 12–24 in (4S.05 ¶3) — the specific rule adds a maximum.
- CHECK passed: Stop Beacon uses (4S.05 ¶1) = 4A.04 C4; the regulatory exclusions of 4S.03 ¶1D are the signs with their own beacons (4S.04, 4S.05); 4T.01 ¶4 ↔ 3B.04 ¶2 (markings and signals required together, each way); 4U.01 ¶4 ↔ 3B.14 ¶5 (steady in-pavement lights are RPMs; flashing ones are warning lights); 4R.02 ¶2 = 2F.16 ¶6; 4S.01 ¶4's exception = the 2E.25/2E.41 E13 panel with beacons; 4Q.02 ¶13 gate stripes (16 in) match the 2B gate-arm stripes.
- CHECK passed (tables file): Table 5A-1's merged cells are expanded to each row, matching the printed table.
- 4Q.03 ¶2 has a duration threshold: flashing yellow between openings only if none is expected for continuous periods **> 5 hours**.

**5A.04 (cont.)** ¶6 E–F (as listed) — uniformity principles.

## Chapter 5B — Provisions for traffic control devices (PDF 803–804)
**5B.01** Signs. ¶2 **Standard: scanning graphics used for driving automation must be invisible to the human eye, with no apparent loss of resolution or recognition.** ¶3 Guidance A–D: signs clearly tied to their lane or road; information spreading (2A.20); non-standard signs designed on standard practice; LED refresh rates.
**5B.02** Markings (Guidance A–G): **6-in normal lines and edge lines**; dotted extensions to the end of acceleration tapers (3B.07); chevrons in exit gores; RPMs only as supplements; contrast markings on light pavements; uniform broken-line segment and gap lengths.
**5B.03** Signals (Guidance A–E): consistent overhead vs post mounting along corridors; consistent face counts and displays; LED refresh rates; **backplates with retroreflective borders**; **FYA for permissive turns**. ¶2 Support: CIRCULAR GREEN faces over mandatory turn lanes are harder for automation to read (cf. 4D.07 ¶12). ¶3: V2I redundancy.
**5B.04** TTC (Guidance A–F): consistent sign type, spacing, height; END ROAD WORK (G20-2) at the end; wider retroreflective material or closer channelizing devices; continuous markings at zone starts and lane transitions; temporary RPMs only as supplements; prompt removal of inapplicable markings (6J.01). ¶2 Support: scars and residual markings mislead automation.
**5B.05** Grade crossings (Guidance: consistent placement; remove devices at out-of-service crossings, 8A.09). **5B.06** Bicycle facilities (Guidance: END plaque with BIKE LANE sign; W9-5 / W9-5a signs; Support: vertical separation aids detection).

**PART 5 complete.** (All Guidance and Support except 5B.01 ¶2.)

# PART 6 — TEMPORARY TRAFFIC CONTROL

## Chapter 6A — General (PDF 806–809)
**6A.01** ¶1: TTC = temporary traffic control. ¶2 **Standard: the needs and control of all road users (motorists, bicyclists, pedestrians including persons with disabilities) through a TTC zone are an essential part of construction, utility work, maintenance and incident management — on highways and site roadways open to public travel.** ¶3–¶10 Support: functions, worker safety, typical applications, low-volume roads, public relations, ITS. ¶11 **Standard: TTC plans and devices are the responsibility of the public body, official, or site-roadway owner with jurisdiction.** ¶12 Guidance: adequate statutory authority. ¶13–¶14 Support: rural vs urban characteristics; **high- vs low-volume status is decided by the agency with jurisdiction.**
**6A.02** Guidance: the seven fundamental principles — A plans (design like permanent roads; a TTC plan understood before occupying the site; changes approved by a knowledgeable official); B inhibit movement as little as practical (drivers slow only when they see a need; avoid abrupt changes; schedule to minimise closures; reduce demand; queue assessment in design; bicyclist and pedestrian passage; off-peak/night work; coordinate with cross-street and emergency officials); C clear guidance (warning, delineation, channelization; remove or cover conflicting devices — in shorter operations use emphasizing devices; accessible formats; flagging); D routine day and night inspection (trained persons with authority to halt work; monitoring; crash studies); E roadside safety (recovery areas; crashworthy detectable channelizing devices; storage); F training appropriate to each person's decisions; only trained persons supervise device selection, placement and maintenance; G public relations (notice, media, abutters, emergency services, railroads and transit, commercial vehicles, schools).
**6A.03** ¶1 Guidance: all road users including persons with disabilities. ¶2 **Standard: definition of traffic control devices (signs, signals, markings, channelizing devices, others using colors, shapes, symbols, words, sounds or tactile information to regulate, warn or guide) on streets, highways, pedestrian facilities, bikeways, pathways and site roadways open to public travel.** ¶3 **Standard: all devices for construction, maintenance, utility or incident operations comply with the Manual.** ¶4 **Standard: removed as soon as practical when not needed; inappropriate devices removed or covered during short suspensions.**
**6A.04** Support: crashworthiness provisions apply to all public roads and site roadways. **6A.05** Support: night work considerations (6N.18).

## Chapter 6B — TTC elements (PDF 810–…)
**6B.01** TTC plans. Support ¶1–¶4 (variables; plan scope; truck and hazmat routing, 2B.67–2B.68). Guidance ¶5–¶11: **a TTC plan for planned activities (and unplanned where practicable)**; from planning to restoration; prepared by knowledgeable persons, devices by engineering judgement; coordinate adjacent projects; planning before occupying the zone for all work including minor jobs; **special events — plans approved by the agencies with jurisdiction**; accessible pedestrian paths. Option ¶12–¶13: contractor alternate plans; modifications (¶14 Guidance: approved before use). ¶15–¶16 Guidance: transit (accessible temporary stops), railroad, property access. ¶17–¶19 **Guidance: avoid reduced speed zoning; use only where restrictive features exist; avoid frequent changes; design for a reduction of no more than 10 mph; more than 10 only where restrictive features require it, with added notification, stepped down, and more warning devices.** ¶20 Support: large reductions increase speed variance and crashes. ¶21–¶22: typical applications (Chapter 6P, Table 6P-1). ¶23 Guidance: alter typical applications to fit. ¶24–¶25 Option: add devices; lengthen spacings and tapers; delete devices labelled optional.
**6B.02** Zones (Support): TTC zone; **work zone — from the first warning sign or high-intensity vehicle lights to the END ROAD WORK sign or the last TTC device**; incident zone; planned special event.
**6B.03** Four areas (advance warning, transition, activity, termination) by judgement. **Figure 6B-1**.
**6B.04** Advance warning area. ¶2 Option: a single sign or vehicle lights up to a series. ¶3 Guidance: **freeways/expressways — ½ mile or more.** ¶4 **Guidance: urban streets — nearest sign at 4 to 8 × the speed limit (ft)**; longer on high-speed arterials (Table 6B-1). ¶5 Option: as short as 100 ft with a single sign on low-speed residential streets. ¶6 **Guidance: rural — first sign 8 to 12 × the speed limit (ft); 1,500 ft or more for open highways.** ¶7 **Guidance: Table 6B-1 distances are approximate, for guidance, applied with judgement and adjusted to field conditions.** ¶9 Option: omit where the activity is clear of the road users' path.
**Table 6B-1** (read from the page; Rev. 1) Recommended Advance Warning Sign Minimum Spacing — A · B · C: **urban low speed\* 100 · 100 · 100 ft; urban high speed\* 350 · 350 · 350; rural 500 · 500 · 500; expressway/freeway 1,000 · 1,500 · 2,640.** \* speed category determined by the agency or site owner. \*\* A = transition/point of restriction to the first sign (nearest the zone); B = first to second; C = second to third.
**6B.05** Transition area. ¶2 **Standard: except mobile operations, redirection by channelizing devices, traffic control devices and/or TTC methods.** ¶3 Option: vehicle-mounted devices (arrow boards, PCMS, high-intensity lights) for mobile operations.
**6B.06** Activity area (Support: work space, traffic space, buffer space). ¶4 Guidance: sign each work space. ¶7 **Guidance: no work or storage in the buffer space.** Option ¶8–¶11, ¶14: longitudinal and lateral buffers; separating opposing flows in one lane (Figure 6B-2); **Table 6B-2 for longitudinal buffer length**. ¶13 Support: with a shadow vehicle, arrow board or CMS in a closed lane, only the area upstream of it is buffer.

Notes (read together)
- CHECK passed: Table 6B-1 against 6B.04's rules — urban low-speed A = 100 ft = 4 × 25 mph; urban high-speed 350 ft lies within 4–8 × 45 mph (180–360 ft); rural 500 ft lies within 8–12 × 55 mph (440–660 ft); freeway C = 2,640 ft = ½ mile (6B.04 ¶3).
- CHECK passed: 5B.01 ¶2 (automation scanning graphics invisible to people) is consistent with 2A.04's ban on visible scanning graphics; 5B.02 ¶2A–B match 3A.04 ¶5 and 3B.09 ¶6 (6-in lines); 5B.03 ¶2 agrees with 4D.07 ¶12.
- MEAN "speed category" (low vs high speed urban) and "high/low volume" are agency determinations in Part 6 (Table 6B-1 note \*, 6A.01 ¶14) — site attributes the graph must ask for, not compute.
- MEAN COMPUTED advance-warning distances from the speed limit: urban 4–8 × S, rural 8–12 × S (feet), Guidance.

**Figure 6B-2** Types of tapers and buffer spaces (notes: S = speed in mph; see Table 6B-4 for L).
**6B.06 (cont.)** ¶15 Guidance: lateral buffer width by judgement. ¶16 Option: staging space for incident responders on congested facilities.
**Table 6B-2** (read from the page) Stopping Sight Distance as a Function of Speed — 20 · 115 / 25 · 155 / 30 · 200 / 35 · 250 / 40 · 305 / 45 · 360 / 50 · 425 / 55 · 495 / 60 · 570 / 65 · 645 / 70 · 730 / 75 · 820 (mph · ft). Footnote \*: speed = **posted speed, off-peak 85th-percentile speed before work, or the anticipated operating speed**. (Used per 6B.06 ¶11 for longitudinal buffer length — Option.)
**6B.07** Termination area (Support: to the last TTC device such as END ROAD WORK). Option: END ROAD WORK, Speed Limit or other signs; a longitudinal buffer before the downstream taper.
**6B.08** Tapers. ¶1 Option: used in transition and termination areas; lengths adjusted near ramps, crossroads, curves. ¶3 Support: **longer is not necessarily better**; the test is observed driver performance. ¶4 **Guidance: taper length per Tables 6B-3 and 6B-4.** ¶5–¶6: merging tapers longest; long enough to warn and merge before the transition ends. ¶8 **Guidance: shifting taper ≈ ½ L.** ¶10 **Guidance: shoulder taper ≈ ⅓ L; a shoulder used as a travel lane gets a merging or shifting taper.** ¶12 **Guidance: downstream taper 50–100 ft, devices ~20 ft apart.** ¶14 **Guidance: one-lane two-way taper 50–100 ft, devices ~20 ft apart, plus a downstream taper.** **Figure 6B-3**.
**Table 6B-3** (read from the page) Taper Length Criteria — merging **at least L**; shifting **at least 0.5 L**; shoulder **at least 0.33 L**; one-lane two-way **50 ft min, 100 ft max**; downstream **50 ft min, 100 ft max**. Note: use Table 6B-4 for L.
**Table 6B-4** (read from the page) Formulas — **S "40 mph or less": L = W·S²/60; S "45 mph or more": L = W·S**. W = offset width (ft); **S = posted speed limit, or off-peak 85th-percentile speed before work, or the anticipated operating speed (mph)**.
**6B.09** Detours (onto existing highways; Guidance: signed over their entire length) and diversions (onto temporary alignments).

## Chapter 6C — Pedestrian and worker safety (PDF 819–823)
**6C.01** **Standard: Part 6 pedestrian and worker provisions applied by knowledgeable (trained/certified) persons after evaluation and engineering judgement.**
**6C.02** Pedestrians. ¶2 Guidance: advise users before closing a sidewalk. ¶3 **Standard: where a TTC zone affects pedestrians, adequate pedestrian access and walkways shall be provided.** ¶4–¶5 Option: alternate means (free bus, assistance); attended short-duration/short-term zones may pause work instead of an alternate route. ¶7–¶9 Guidance: no conflicts with vehicles, equipment or operations; an accessible path replicating the existing; not severed for parking; separate from work and traffic; advance signs at intersections (not midblock) where pedestrians must cross. ¶11 **Guidance A–G: accessible continuity; transit stops kept; smooth hard surface per ADA 2010; existing width if practical — else ≥ 60 in, or a 60 × 60-in passing space at least every 200 ft; audible or detectable information; continuous detectable edging (6M.04); objects below 7 ft project ≤ 4 in.** ¶13 Option: close the site to pedestrians. ¶14–¶17 Guidance: fencing without sight restrictions or impact hazards (wood not a substitute for barriers); low minimal ballast; minimise vehicle crossings of paths (flaggers); minimise worker access across walkways. ¶18–¶19: canopied walkways, sturdy and lit. ¶20–¶21 Guidance: temporary barriers where paths come close. ¶23 **Standard: normal vertical curbing is not a substitute for temporary traffic barriers when barriers are needed.** ¶24 Option: barriers or longitudinal channelizing devices to deter entry and midblock crossing. ¶26 Guidance: reroute or barrier where vehicle incursion is likely. ¶28 **Guidance: no tape, rope or plastic chain as pedestrian control (not detectable).** ¶29–¶30 Guidance: preserve urban routes; regular inspection.
**6C.03** Accessibility. ¶1 Support: NCHRP 117B (2008), ADA Standards 2010 (external). ¶3 Guidance: extent by judgement or the responsible individual. ¶4 **Standard: disrupted or relocated facilities — temporary facilities detectable, with the existing accessibility features; a long-cane-detectable barrier across the full width of a closed facility.** ¶6 Guidance: audible, tactile/vibrating and detectable devices. ¶7 Support: speech messages best (passive actuation most desirable). ¶8 Guidance: push buttons with locator tones.
**6C.04** Worker safety. ¶3 Guidance A–E: training; temporary barriers by clearance, speed, duration, time, volume; **speed management** (zoning, funneling, lane reduction, **speed safety cameras**, officers, flaggers); minimise backing; a hazard assessment by a trained person per the **OSH Act General Duty Clause 5(a)(1)** and **29 CFR 1926.20(b)(2)** (external). ¶4 Option A–E: shadow vehicles with attenuators; road closure; law enforcement; lighting; special devices (rumble strips, CMS, beacons, flags, intrusion alarms). ¶5 Support: overuse lessens effectiveness.
**6C.05** High-visibility apparel. ¶1 **Standard: all workers, including emergency responders, within the right-of-way in the TTC zone — ANSI/ISEA 107-2015 Performance Class 2 or 3 (or equivalent revisions), day and night, except ¶4; class chosen by the employer's designated safety person.** ¶2 **Standard: background fluorescent orange-red, fluorescent yellow-green, or both; retroreflective material orange, yellow, white, silver, yellow-green or fluorescent versions.** ¶3 **Standard: uniformed law enforcement directing traffic, investigating crashes, handling closures or disasters — the same apparel.** ¶4 Option: responders and law enforcement may wear ANSI/ISEA 207-2006 public safety vests instead. ¶5 **Standard: firefighters and other responders in the right-of-way wear it, except ¶6** (Option: retroreflective turn-out gear when exposed to flame, heat or hazmat, per NFPA). ¶7 Guidance: **night flaggers — Class 3.**

## Chapter 6D — Flagger control (PDF 824–…)
**6D.01** Guidance: trained flaggers with abilities A–E.
**6D.02** STOP/SLOW paddle. ¶1 Guidance: **primary and preferred device** (Table 6G-1). ¶2 **Standard: octagonal, on a rigid handle; retroreflectorized at night.** ¶3 Option: STOP/STOP or SLOW/SLOW of the same size and shape (6D.05). ¶4 Guidance: light semi-rigid material. ¶6 Option: flashing lights — white or red on STOP, white or yellow on SLOW, patterns A–E. ¶7–¶9 **Standard: STOP-face lights all white or all red; SLOW-face all white or all yellow; more than eight lights must convey the octagon/diamond; 50–60 flashes per minute.**
**Figure 6D-1** (paddle preferred; flag for emergencies only).
**6D.03** Flags. ¶1 Guidance: **emergencies only.** ¶2 **Standard: red or fluorescent orange-red, ≥ 24 in square, on a ~36-in staff.** ¶3 Guidance: weighted edge. ¶4 **Standard: retroreflectorized at night.**
**6D.04** Flashlights. ¶1 Option: red glow cone to supplement paddle or flag in emergencies at unlit night stations. ¶2 **Standard: flashlight in the left hand, paddle/flag in the right; stop — arm down, slow arc left to right ≤ 45° from vertical; proceed — point at the bumper then toward the open lane and hold (no waving); alert/slow — quick figure-eight toward traffic.**
**6D.05** Procedures. ¶2 **Standard: flaggers use a STOP/SLOW paddle, flag or AFAD (6L.02–6L.04); hand movements alone are prohibited in one-lane two-way operations, except emergency responders at incidents (6O.01) or uniformed law enforcement.** ¶3 **Standard paddle signals: stop — STOP face stationary, arm extended, free palm raised above the shoulder; proceed — SLOW face stationary, free hand motions to proceed; alert/slow — SLOW face stationary.** ¶4 Option: free hand up and down, palm down, to slow further. ¶5 Standard flag signals: A stop — staff horizontal across the lane, flag hanging, free palm raised …

Notes (read together)
- **Part 3 / Part 6 taper formulas compared (the cross-check flagged at 3B.12)**: Part 3 (3B.12 ¶8, 3B.13 ¶2 — permanent lane-reduction and obstruction markings) splits by the **speed limit** at < 45 / ≥ 45 mph with S = **the higher** of 85th-percentile and speed limit. Part 6 (Table 6B-4 — TTC tapers) splits at **"40 mph or less" / "45 mph or more"** with S = posted limit **or** off-peak 85th-percentile **or** anticipated operating speed, no "whichever". Different contexts, different rules; not to be merged.
  - For **posted limits** the 41–44 mph gap does not arise: limits are set in multiples of 5 mph (2B.21 ¶13, Standard).
  - For a **measured or anticipated** S of 41–44 mph the table has no row, and the three alternatives for S are not ranked. **Item 7 widened** to include Table 6B-4 (and Table 6B-2's same three-way speed key).
- Table 6B-3 ("at least 0.5 L", "at least 0.33 L") and 6B.08 ¶8, ¶10 ("approximately ½ L", "approximately ⅓ L"): the table sets the minimum, the text the typical length. Consistent.
- CHECK passed: 6B.08 ¶12, ¶14 (50–100 ft, ~20-ft spacing) = Table 6B-3; 6C.02 ¶11G (≤ 4 in projection below 7 ft) matches 4H.09 ¶1B and the ADA protruding-object rule; 6D.02 ¶9 flash rate = 4G.01 ¶1, 4S.01 ¶3.
- X external standards govern apparel (ANSI/ISEA 107-2015 Class 2/3; 207-2006; NFPA turn-out gear) and worker hazard assessment (OSH Act; 29 CFR 1926.20).

**6D.05 (cont.)** ¶5 **Standard flag signals: B proceed — flag and arm lowered out of view, free hand motions; flags never used to signal proceed; C alert/slow — slow sweeping wave from shoulder level to straight down, arm not above horizontal, free hand down.** ¶6 Guidance: stand on the shoulder or closed lane before stopping traffic; enter the moving lane only after traffic stops; visible to the first road user; far enough ahead to warn workers (horns, whistles); alone. ¶7 Option: STOP/STOP or SLOW/SLOW paddles.
**6D.06** Flagger stations. ¶1 **Standard: located so approaching road users have sufficient distance to stop at the intended point, except ¶2** (Option: modified by judgement where stopping sight distance is not achievable). ¶3 Option: **Table 6B-2 distances**, increased for downgrades. ¶4 Guidance: extra stopping space; an escape route. ¶5 **Standard: except emergencies — preceded by advance warning signs, and illuminated at night.**

## Chapter 6E — One-lane, two-way traffic control (PDF 828–829)
**6E.01** ¶1 **Standard: movements from each end coordinated, except ¶4** (Option: self-regulating on short low-volume sites with full visibility). ¶2–¶3 Guidance: flaggers, flag transfer, pilot car, signals, stop/yield control; control points allowing easy passing.
**6E.02** Flagger method (Guidance: a flagger at each end, one coordinating; communication not mistakable for flagging; single flagger (Option) only where visible end to end, on the shoulder opposite the work).
**6E.03** Flag transfer (Support; Guidance: **≤ ~1 mile**).
**6E.04** Pilot car. ¶2 Guidance: contractor or authority name displayed. ¶3 **Standard: PILOT CAR FOLLOW ME (G20-4) on the top or rear.** ¶4 **Standard: coordinated with flagging or other control at each end.** ¶5 **Standard: an AFAD in pilot-car operation is operated by a flagger near and within sight of it, never unattended.** ¶6 Guidance: wait-time signs with temporary signals.
**6E.05** Temporary signals (Option; Figure 6P-12; Chapter 4O). **6E.06** STOP or YIELD control (Option: low-volume roads with visibility end to end; Guidance: a single sign faces traffic on the closed side).

## Chapter 6F — TTC zone signs, general (PDF 830–833)
**6F.01** ¶2–¶3 Option: fluorescent orange wherever orange is required. ¶4–¶5 Option/Standard: orange flags, beacons, warning lights with signs, **never blocking the sign face**. ¶6 **Standard: sizes per Tables 6G-1, 6H-1, 6I-1 (except 2A.07); the Minimum column only on low-volume rural roads, local streets, or roadways with operating speeds ≤ 30 mph.** ¶7–¶8: larger sizes allowed; deviations in 6-in increments (Guidance). ¶10: special word messages (2A.04). ¶11 **Standard: night signs retroreflective or illuminated, same shape and similar color day and night.** ¶12 **Standard: street, highway or strobe lighting does not satisfy sign illumination.** ¶13–¶14 Option: internal or external illumination; rigid or flexible materials.
**6F.02** Placement. ¶1 Guidance: right side. ¶2 Option: both sides; portable supports in the roadway; on barricades. ¶4 **Standard: rural roadside signs — bottom ≥ 5 ft above the pavement edge.** ¶5 **Standard: business, commercial, residential areas (parking, pedestrians, obstructed views) — ≥ 7 ft above the curb (or traveled-way edge).** ¶6 **Standard: above sidewalks — ≥ 7 ft.** ¶7 **Standard: on barricades or portable supports — bottom ≥ 1 ft above the traveled way.** ¶8 Option: secondary signs 1 ft lower. ¶9 Guidance: supports not on sidewalks or bicycle/pedestrian areas. ¶10 **Standard: mounted per Section 307 of the 2010 ADA Standards.** ¶11 **Guidance: low portable signs (below Part 2 heights) ≤ 3 days, except ¶12** (Option: R9-8 to R9-11a, R11, W1-6 to W1-8, M4-10, E5-1 and similar may stay longer). ¶14 Guidance: **signs on Type 3 barricades cover ≤ 50 % of the top two rails or ≤ 33 % of the three rails' area.** ¶15 **Standard: signs and supports crashworthy; signs > 50 sq ft on multiple breakaway posts ≥ 7 ft clearance.** ¶16 Option: on work, shadow vehicles or trailers for mobile operations. **Figures 6F-1, 6F-2**.
**6F.03** Maintenance (Guidance: clean, visible, retroreflective, positioned; replace illegible signs; 2A.21 retroreflectivity).

**6G.04** Road Closed R11-2 series. ¶1 Guidance: closed to all but contractors or authorized vehicles; with warning and detour signs. ¶2 Option: STREET CLOSED, BRIDGE OUT, PATH CLOSED. ¶3 Guidance: at or near the centre on or above a Type 3 barricade. ¶4 **Standard: not used where traffic continues on fewer lanes or where the closure is some distance beyond.**
**6G.05** Local Traffic Only R11-3 series, R11-4 (Guidance: where the closure is beyond and locals may reach it; rural legend ROAD CLOSED XX MILES AHEAD LOCAL TRAFFIC ONLY; Option: urban R11-4 and substitutions).

## Chapter 6H — TTC zone warning signs (PDF 839–…)
**6H.01** ¶2–¶3 **Standard: Part 2 and SHS Standards; sizes per Table 6H-1.** ¶4 **Standard: diamond, black on orange — except the W10-1 Grade Crossing Advance Warning sign, black on yellow — except ¶5–¶9.** Option: **fluorescent yellow-green where Parts 2 or 7 require or recommend it; existing applicable yellow signs may stay; fluorescent pink for incident management; rectangular for mounting or space; yellow in emergencies when orange/pink are not at hand.** ¶10–¶12 Guidance: larger signs for emphasis; advance warning well ahead of obstructions; audible information or detectable barriers for pedestrians with vision disabilities. ¶14–¶15 Option: signs singly or combined; distance plaques.
**6H.02** Position. ¶1 **Guidance: per Table 6B-1; the closest of a series ~100 ft (low-speed urban) to ≥ 1,000 ft (freeways, expressways).** ¶2 **Guidance: ROAD WORK AHEAD (W20-1) first in a series.** ¶4 Option: AHEAD instead of a distance.

Notes (read together)
- **Minimum-column rules — two contexts**: Part 2 (2A.07, Standard) — only on low-speed roadways, alleys, site roadways, and low-volume rural roads at ≤ 30 mph operating speed, **and** only where the reduced legend is adequate or physical conditions preclude larger signs. Part 6 (6F.01 ¶6, Standard) — only on low-volume rural roads, **local streets**, or roadways with operating speed ≤ 30 mph, with no added condition. 6F.01 ¶6 is the specific rule for the TTC tables (6G-1, 6H-1, 6I-1); 2A.07 governs the Part 2 tables. Recorded separately.
- CHECK passed: Table 6G-1's \* ties multi-lane conventional roads back to Table 2B-1; 6F.02 ¶4–¶6 heights = 2A.15 (5 ft rural, 7 ft urban and above sidewalks); 6H.02 ¶1 (~100 ft to ≥ 1,000 ft) = Table 6B-1 column A; 6D.06 ¶3 uses Table 6B-2; 6H.01 ¶4's W10-1 exception keeps the grade-crossing sign yellow (Part 8 color).
- Color system for warning signs by context: permanent — yellow (FYG for school/pedestrian/bicycle as provided); TTC — orange (fluorescent orange allowed); incidents — fluorescent pink; W10-1 always yellow (6H.01 ¶4–¶9, 1D.05).
- MEAN duration thresholds in Part 6: low portable signs ≤ 3 days (6F.02 ¶11); temporary signals ≤ 30 days unless longer projects (4D.11 ¶8).

## Chapter 6J — TTC zone pavement markings (PDF 854–855)
**6J.01** ¶3 **Standard: existing markings maintained in all long-term stationary zones per 3A and 3B (except 6J.02 temporary markings); markings match the alignment at both ends; markings along the entire length of paved detours or temporary roadways before they open.** ¶4 Guidance: remove or obliterate inapplicable markings as soon as practical, minimising scarring. ¶5 **Standard: painting over with black paint or spraying asphalt is not acceptable as removal or obliteration.** ¶6 Option: removable non-reflective pavement-colored tape.
**6J.02** Temporary markings. ¶2 Guidance: **≤ 14 days after the surface treatment or final surface**, unless justified. ¶3 Guidance: per State/agency policy. ¶4 **Standard: warning signs, channelizing devices and delineation where markings cannot provide a clear path.** ¶5 **Standard: temporary no-passing markings per 3A and 3B; temporary broken lines use the permanent cycle length with segments ≥ 2 ft.** ¶6 Guidance: day and night review. ¶7 Option: half-cycle lengths (≥ 2-ft stripes) on severe curves. ¶8 Option: **for ≤ 14 days on two- or three-lane roads, R4-1, R4-2 and W14-3 signs instead of no-passing markings**; longer on low-volume roads per policy. ¶9–¶10 Guidance: placement per 2B.36, 2B.37, 2C.53, 6H.29.
**6J.03** Temporary RPMs. ¶1 Option: substitution. ¶2 **Standard: color and pattern simulate the markings.** ¶3 **Standard: for broken segments — ≥ 3 retroreflective markers ≤ 5 ft apart, every 40 ft.** ¶4 **Standard: for solid lines — ≤ 10 ft apart, retroreflective ≤ 20 ft.** ¶5 Option: ≥ 2 markers at each end of 2–5-ft segments. ¶6 Guidance: on surfaced detours and new alignments. ¶7 Option: as supplements.
**6J.04** Delineators. ¶2 **Standard: combined with or supplementing other devices; crashworthy supports; per 3G.** ¶3 Guidance: curve spacing per 3G.04.

## Chapter 6K — TTC zone channelizing devices (PDF 856–…)
**6K.01** ¶1 **Standard: designs per Figure 6K-1; all channelizing devices crashworthy.** ¶4 **Guidance: spacing of cones, tubular markers, panels, drums and barricades ≤ 1 × speed limit (ft) in tapers and ≤ 2 × speed limit in tangents.** ¶5 Guidance: extend 2 × speed limit beyond the transition where devices could lead traffic astray (Figure 6P-39). ¶6–¶8 Option: ≤ 2-in drainage gap; warning lights in fog, snow, curves; sequential lights on merging tapers (6L.07). ¶10 **Standard: retroreflective material similar in color day and night.** ¶11 **Standard: no owner or manufacturer identification visible to approaching road users, except ¶12** (Option: agency/contractor/supplier name and phone on the non-retroreflective surface). ¶13 **Standard: that area non-retroreflective and ≤ 2 in high.** ¶15 **Standard: devices no longer serviceable (#1C.02) are replaced.**

Notes (read together)
- **NEXT XX MILES plaque (6H.33 ¶3 vs Table 6H-1)**: the table's conventional size is 24 × 18, but the Standard requires ≥ 30 × 24 when the plaque accompanies a sign ≥ 36 × 36 — the usual conventional warning-sign size. By 1A.04 ¶5 the text governs; the table's 24 × 18 fits only with smaller (e.g. 30 × 30 minimum-size) signs; note 1 allows larger plaques. **The plaque size depends on the sign it is mounted with** — a pairing rule the graph must carry.
- Table 6H-1's footnote \* is printed with no marked cell; read by its content (signs facing multi-lane conventional roads → Table 2C-1 minimums). The tables file keeps it at table level, matching the page.
- **CHECK passed (Part 3 ↔ Part 6)**: temporary RPM spacings (6J.03 ¶3–¶4) equal Part 3's N-based rules for N = 40 ft — broken: 3+ markers at ≤ N/8 = 5 ft (3B.17 ¶5); solid: ≤ N/4 = 10 ft with retroreflective ≤ N/2 = 20 ft (3B.17 ¶6). 6J.01 ¶5–¶6 agree with 3A.01 ¶5–¶6 (removal; temporary tape). 6J.02 ¶5's temporary broken lines keep the permanent cycle (3A.04 ¶6).
- Clean partition: LOW SHOULDER ≤ 3 in (Option) / Shoulder Drop-Off > 3 in (Guidance) (6H.26 ¶2–¶3).
- MEAN COMPUTED device spacing: taper ≤ 1 × S ft, tangent ≤ 2 × S ft, extension 2 × S ft (6K.01 ¶4–¶5, S = speed limit in mph).
- MEAN duration thresholds: NEW TRAFFIC PATTERN ≤ 2 weeks (6H.14); temporary markings ≤ 14 days (6J.02 ¶2); sign-based no-passing ≤ 14 days (6J.02 ¶8); G20-1 for zones > 2 miles (6H.35); NO CENTER LINE every 2 miles (6H.29).

**Figure 6K-1** channelizing devices (notes: warning lights optional; **rail stripes 6 in, or 4 in where rails are < 36 in long (shall)**; traffic-facing barricade sides retroreflective).
**6K.02** Pedestrian channelizing devices. ¶2–¶3 Guidance: where work affects pedestrian facilities or the temporary route lacks the existing accessibility features; used to close sidewalks and delineate alternates. ¶5 **Standard: crashworthy when exposed to traffic.** ¶6 **Standard: detectable by long-cane users and visible to pedestrians with vision disabilities.** ¶7 **Standard: a sidewalk closure covers the entire width.** ¶8 **Standard: continuous detection plates and hand-trailing edges — plate bottom ≤ 2 in, plate top ≥ 8 in; hand-trailing edge top 32–38 in, smooth; both in one vertical plane.** ¶9 Guidance: gaps between devices ≤ 1 in. ¶11 Guidance: ≥ 2-in gap between the hand-trailing edge and its support. ¶12 **Standard: retroreflective sheeting (6K.01 ¶10) where visible to traffic.** ¶13 Guidance: otherwise ≥ 6 in of contrasting light/dark material on top. ¶14–¶15, ¶18, ¶20 Option: non-retroreflective pedestrian side; vertical or 45° stripes; continuous walls of any height above 32 in. ¶19 Guidance: walls — lower edge ≤ 2 in, ≥ 32 in high, common vertical face, contrasting sheeting at 32 in. **Figure 6K-2** (notes: ≥ 2-in gap; ≤ 2-in drainage gap; stripes vertical or 45°; edges/plates optional on continuous walls).
**6K.03** Cones. ¶1 **Standard: predominantly orange, struck without damaging vehicles; ≥ 18 in for daytime low-speed use; ≥ 28 in on freeways and high-speed highways, at night on all highways, or where more guidance is needed.** ¶2 **Standard: at night retroreflectorized or lit; 28–36-in cones — a 6-in white band 3–4 in from the top and a 4-in white band ~2 in below it.** ¶3 **Standard: cones > 36 in — alternating orange and white 4–6-in stripes, ≥ 2 of each, top orange, gaps ≤ 3 in.** ¶4, ¶6 Option: uses; doubled cones. ¶5, ¶8 Guidance: keep from blowing over; minimum ballast.
**6K.04** Tubular markers. ¶1 **Standard: predominantly orange, ≥ 18 in high and 2 in wide, struck without damage.** ¶2 **Standard: ≥ 28 in on freeways and high-speed highways, at night on all highways, or where more guidance is needed.** ¶3 **Standard: < 42 in — two 3-in white bands, the top ≤ 2 in from the top, ≤ 6 in between; ≥ 42 in — four 4–6-in alternating orange/white stripes, top orange.** ¶4 Guidance: only where space prevents more visible devices. ¶5 Guidance: stabilised; minimum ballast. ¶7 **Standard: attached to show the 2-in width to approaching traffic.**
**6K.05** Vertical panels. ¶1 **Standard: retroreflective striped material 8–12 in wide and ≥ 24 in high; alternating orange/white stripes sloping down at 45° toward the side traffic passes.** ¶2 **Standard: 6-in stripes where the material is ≥ 36 in high** (¶3 Option: 4-in below 36 in). ¶4 Option: where space is limited.
**6K.06** Drums. ¶1 **Standard: lightweight, deformable; ≥ 36 in high and ≥ 18 in wide; no metal drums; 4–6-in alternating orange/white stripes, ≥ 2 of each, top orange, gaps ≤ 3 in; closed tops.** ¶4 Guidance: not dangerously ballasted; drain holes where freezing occurs. ¶5 **Standard: no ballast on top.**
**6K.07** Type 1, 2, 3 barricades. ¶3 **Standard: alternating orange/white stripes sloping down at 45° toward the passing side; 6 in wide, except ¶4** (Option: 4 in for rails < 36 in). ¶5 **Standard: Type 1 and 2 ≥ 24 in long; Type 3 ≥ 48 in; rails 8–12 in wide; ≥ 270 sq in retroreflective area facing traffic on high-speed roads.** ¶6–¶8 Guidance: stripe direction toward the required turn(s), or toward the centre where no turn is intended. ¶9 Guidance: stable, visible. ¶10 Guidance: pedestrian widths (= 6C.02 ¶11D). ¶11 Guidance: supports ≤ 4-in projection between 27 and 80 in (ADA 307). ¶13–¶14: ballast on high-speed roads (sandbags low). ¶17–¶19: Type 1 on conventional roads; **Type 2 or 3 on freeways/high-speed roads; Type 3 to close roads** (Guidance); across the roadway (Option). ¶20 Guidance: someone responsible for closure each day where authorized access exists. ¶22 **Standard: a sign stating permissible use by local road users where closures allow local access (6G.05).**
**6K.08** Direction Indicator Barricade. ¶1 **Standard: a W1-6 One-Direction Large Arrow above a diagonally striped retroreflective rail.** ¶2 **Standard: black on orange; 4-in stripes at 45° toward the passing side; W1-6 24 × 12 in; rail 24 in long, 8 in high.** ¶3–¶4: in tapers and transitions, used in a series (Guidance).
**6K.09** Temporary traffic barriers as channelizing devices. ¶1 Support: not TTC devices themselves; they serve as such when placed and equipped like a line of channelizing devices. ¶2 Standard: then they meet Part 6's device requirements. ¶3 **Standard: never solely to channelize (also to protect the work space); when channelizing, supplemented with delineation, markings or channelizing devices.** ¶4–¶5 Guidance: **not for merging tapers except in low-speed urban areas**; taper length optimised then. ¶6 **Standard: such tapers delineated with channelizing devices, edge lines and/or barrier delineators.** ¶7 Guidance: light color.
**6K.10** Longitudinal channelizing devices. ¶2 **Standard: used singly as barricades, they meet the barricade characteristics.** ¶3 Guidance: supplemented at night. ¶4–¶6 Option: instead of cones/drums/barricades; water ballast; pedestrian control. ¶7 **Standard: for pedestrians — interlocked, with no gaps letting pedestrians stray.** ¶8 **Guidance: not crashworthy as barriers — not for shielding or positive protection.**
**6K.11** Temporary lane separators. ¶2 **Standard: a longitudinal base ≤ 4 in high and ≤ 1 ft wide with sloping sides, with tubular markers, vertical panels or W6-4 signs on flexible supports.** ¶3 Standard: those devices retroreflectorized. ¶4–¶5 Guidance: stabilised; not for protection. ¶6 **Standard: at pedestrian crossings — an opening or shortening giving ≥ 60 in.**
**6K.12** Other devices (Option by study; Guidance: general characteristics of this Chapter).

## Chapter 6L — Other TTC zone traffic control devices (PDF 865–…)
**6L.01** Temporary signals. ¶1 **Standard: per Part 4.** ¶3 **Standard: one-lane two-way sections per 4O.02.** ¶4 Guidance: conflict monitors. ¶6 Option: portable or fixed. ¶7 Guidance: only where preferable to restaging, flaggers, STOP/YIELD or warning devices. ¶8 Support: factors A–U. ¶10–¶12 Guidance: supplement with signs, markings, channelizing devices; cover or remove when unused; **interconnect within ½ mile of another signal**. ¶13 **Standard: not within 200 ft of a grade crossing unless preempted (4F.18, 4F.19, 8D.09) or an officer or flagger prevents stopping on the crossing.**
**6L.02** AFADs — general. ¶2: two types (STOP/SLOW; red/yellow lens with gate arm). ¶3: short- and intermediate-term. ¶4 **Standard: only where one lane approaches in the controlled direction.** ¶5 **Standard: illuminated at night (6D.06).** ¶6 Guidance: not for long-term stationary work. ¶7 **Standard: AFADs are not traffic control signals and never substitute for a continuously operating temporary signal.** ¶8 **Standard: crashworthy.** ¶9 Guidance: before the one-lane taper, downstream of the stopping point. ¶10 **Standard: all controlling items visible to the first driver; advance warning signs for others.** ¶11 **Standard: operated only by a trained flagger, never unattended while in use.** ¶12 **Standard: Method 1 (AFAD at each end) or Method 2 (AFAD one end, flagger the other).** ¶13 **Standard: two flaggers, except ¶14** (Option: one flagger with unobstructed views of the AFAD(s) and of traffic both ways). ¶15 Guidance: W20-1, W20-4, W3-4 in advance. ¶16 **Standard: AFAD-related signs removed or covered when not in use.** ¶17 Guidance: agency policy A–I.
**6L.03** STOP/SLOW AFAD. ¶1 **Standard: alternately displays STOP (R1-1) and SLOW (W20-8).** ¶2 **Standard: octagonal, rigid, bottom ≥ 6 ft above the pavement, ≥ 24 × 24 in with letters ≥ 8 in; STOP red with white; SLOW an orange diamond with black; both retroreflectorized.** ¶3 Standard: positively locked in each position. ¶4 **Standard: active conspicuity — A lights within the faces (per 6D.02), or B a Stop Beacon ≤ 24 in above the STOP face and a Warning Beacon ≤ 24 in from the SLOW face, each dark while the other face shows.** ¶5–¶6 Option/Standard: Type B warning or strobe lights instead of the Warning Beacon, flashing only with SLOW. ¶7–¶8 Option/Standard: louvers keeping the full face visible at ≥ 50 ft. ¶9 Guidance: a gate arm down on STOP, up on SLOW. ¶10 Option: sign on a moving mast arm. ¶11 **Standard: gate arms fully retroreflectorized both sides, vertical red/white stripes at 16-in intervals (Figure 8D-1); ≥ 2-in vertical aspect; reaching at least the lane centre.** ¶12 **Standard: WAIT ON STOP (R1-7) sign displayed.** ¶13–¶14 Option: GO ON SLOW (R1-8); R1-7a combined. ¶15 **Standard: on or beside the AFAD, in the same line of view.** ¶16 **Standard: STOP with its lights/beacon flashing to stop; SLOW with its lights/beacon flashing to proceed.** ¶17 **Standard: safeguards preventing SLOW at both ends at once; SLOW only after oncoming vehicles have cleared.** **Figure 6L-1** (Method 1; letter codes per Table 6B-1).
**6L.04** Red/Yellow Lens AFAD. ¶1 **Standard: alternately a steady CIRCULAR RED and a flashing CIRCULAR YELLOW, with no flagger needed at the device or in the road.** ¶2 **Standard: at least one set of 12-in lenses, red above yellow, per Part 4; post-mounted bottom ≥ 7 ft; over the roadway ≥ 15 ft.** ¶3 Option: additional sets over the road or on the left. **Figure 6L-2** (Method 2).

Notes (read together)
- **Tubular markers — two contexts**: permanent ≥ 28 in (3I.02 ¶1); TTC ≥ 18 in, ≥ 28 in on high-speed roads, at night, or where more guidance is needed (6K.04 ¶1–¶2). Not to be merged.
- **Direction Indicator Barricade**: its W1-6 "shall be 24 × 12 in" (6K.08 ¶2), whereas Table 6H-1 lists W1-6 at 48 × 24 (conventional). The barricade Standard is the specific provision for that mounting.
- **200-ft grade-crossing threshold across Parts**: temporary signals — Standard (6L.01 ¶13); PHBs and EVHBs — Guidance to preempt (4J.03 ¶14, 4N.03 ¶7). Same distance, authority by device.
- **Red/Yellow lens AFAD** post-mounted lenses ≥ 7 ft (6L.04 ¶2) vs 4D.09 ¶2A's 8 ft for faces not over the roadway — the device-specific rule; over the roadway both require 15 ft (= 4D.09 ¶1).
- CHECK passed: gate-arm stripes at 16-in intervals — AFADs (6L.03 ¶11), movable bridge gates (4Q.02 ¶13), Part 2 gate arms, Figure 8D-1; Figure 6K-1's stripe note = 6K.07 ¶3–¶4; 6K.07 ¶10 = 6C.02 ¶11D (60 in; 60 × 60 passing space every 200 ft); 6L.03 ¶4B's ≤ 24-in beacon distance matches 4S.05 ¶3's 12–24-in range; 6L.02 ¶7 agrees with 4D.11 (temporary signals are traffic control signals; AFADs are not).
- MEAN device dimensions are Standards with size thresholds that switch the marking pattern (cones at 36 in; tubular markers at 42 in; vertical panels at 36 in; barricade rails at 36 in) — computable from device height or length.

**6L.04 (cont.)** ¶4 **Standard: a gate arm down on red, up on flashing yellow; fully retroreflectorized, red/white stripes at 16-in intervals; ≥ 2-in vertical aspect; reaching at least the lane centre.** ¶5 **Standard: a Stop Here On Red (R10-6 / R10-6a) sign on the right at the stopping point.** ¶6 Standard: stop = steady red + arm down; proceed = flashing yellow + arm up. ¶7 **Standard: safeguards against flashing yellow at both ends; yellow only after oncoming vehicles clear.** ¶8 **Standard: a steady yellow change interval between flashing yellow and steady red, arm up during it.** ¶9 **Standard: no change interval from steady red to flashing yellow.** ¶10 Guidance: **steady yellow ≥ 5 s** unless another duration within 4F.17's range is justified.
**6L.05** Portable changeable message signs. ¶1 Support: **follow Chapter 2L in most cases; this Section describes where PCMS differ.** ¶6 Guidance: components; protective face. ¶7 **Standard: 2A principles; only traffic operational, regulatory, warning and guidance information; no advertising.** ¶9 **Standard: legend colors per Table 2A-2.** ¶11 Guidance: **visible from ½ mile day and night.** ¶13 Guidance: **3 lines × 8 characters, or full matrix.** ¶14 Guidance: **letters ≥ 18 in**, except ¶15 (Option: ≥ 10 in on service patrol or incident vehicles; shorter on low-speed facilities if legible from ≥ 650 ft). ¶17 Guidance: ≤ 2 phases, ≤ 3 lines per phase, each understood alone, centred; only one sequential sign visible at a time. ¶19 **Standard: no animation, rapid flashing, dissolving, exploding, scrolling or travelling displays, except when simulating an arrow board (6L.06).** ¶20 Guidance: **each phase ≥ 2 s, both together ≤ 8 s.** ¶21 Guidance: three thoughts (problem, location, action); more PCMS for more than two phases — same side, **≥ 1,000 ft apart on freeways/expressways, ≥ 500 ft elsewhere**. ¶22 **Standard: abbreviations per 1D.08.** ¶23 **Standard: automatic brightness.** ¶24 **Standard: a preview display screen; memory kept without power.** ¶25 **Standard: a power source with battery back-up.** ¶26 **Standard: bottom ≥ 7 ft above the roadway in urban areas, ≥ 5 ft in rural areas, when operating.** ¶27–¶33 Guidance: supplement, not substitute; far enough ahead of diversions; sited for legibility; off the shoulder behind barrier or outside the clear zone, else delineated; **only TTC messages in TTC zones**; relocated or turned away when idle; permanent conspicuity material on trailers.
**6L.06** Arrow boards. ¶1 **Standard: a matrix sign with flashing or sequential displays for merging and control.** ¶2–¶4 Guidance: arrow/chevron mode for lane closures on major multi-lane roads (heavy volume, high speed, limited sight); with signs and channelizing devices; on the shoulder or farther, delineated; removed, shielded or delineated when idle. ¶5 **Standard: minimum size, legibility distance, elements and other specifications per Figure 6L-3.** ¶6 Support: Type A low-speed urban; B intermediate-speed and mobile/maintenance on high-speed; C high-speed high-volume projects; D on agency-authorized vehicles. ¶7 **Standard: Types A–C solid rectangular; Type D arrow-shaped.** ¶8 **Standard: non-reflective black finish; on a vehicle, trailer or support.** ¶9 Guidance: **bottom ≥ 7 ft** (vehicle-mounted as high as practicable). ¶10 Guidance: remote controls. ¶11 **Standard: elements dim ≥ 50 %; dimmed mode at night.** ¶12 Guidance: full brilliance by day. ¶13 **Standard: yellow elements.** ¶14 Guidance: recessed or ≥ 180° hoods. ¶15 **Standard: flashing on-time ≥ 50 %; sequential phases 25 % each; 25–40 flashes per minute.** ¶16 **Standard: modes — A flashing arrow, sequential arrow or sequential chevron; B flashing double arrow; C flashing caution or alternating diamond.** ¶17 **Standard: arrow or chevron mode only for stationary or moving lane closures on multi-lane roadways.** ¶18 **Standard: shoulder work, blocking the shoulder, roadside work near it, or closing one lane of a two-lane two-way road — caution mode only.** ¶19–¶20 Guidance: on the shoulder at the start of the merging taper; in the closed lane where the shoulder is narrow. ¶21 **Standard: a separate arrow board for each closed lane.** ¶22–¶23 Guidance: positions for multi-lane closures (Figure 6P-37); separation for mobile operations. ¶24 **Standard: a vehicle with an arrow board has high-intensity rotating, flashing, oscillating or strobe lights.** ¶25 **Standard: only for lane closures; never for lane shifts.** ¶26 Option: a PCMS simulating an arrow board.
**Figure 6L-3** (read from the page) Arrow board specifications — **A 48 × 24 in, ½ mile, 12 elements; B 60 × 30 in, ¾ mile, 13; C 96 × 48 in, 1 mile, 15; D none\* (\* arrow 48 in long, head 24 in wide), ½ mile, 12**; modes — 1 flashing/sequential arrow or chevron, 2 flashing double arrow **"shall be provided"**, 3 **"at least one of"** flashing caution or alternating diamond **"shall be provided"**.
**6L.07** Flashing beacons and warning lights. ¶1 Guidance: by judgement. ¶2 Option: supplement signs, barriers, channelizing devices. ¶3 Support: Types A–D. ¶4 **Standard: per ITE Equipment and Materials Standards, Chapter 13 (1998).** ¶5 **Standard: mounted so as not to penetrate windshields if struck.** ¶6 Guidance: spacing as for channelizing devices. ¶8 Option: steady or flashing. ¶9 **Standard: flash when on devices used alone or in a cluster to warn of a condition.** ¶10 **Standard: steady-burn on devices used in a series to channelize (except ¶12).** ¶11 **Standard: flashing lights never for delineation (except ¶12).** ¶12 **Standard: sequential flashing on merging tapers runs upstream → downstream, each light 55–75 times per minute.** ¶13 **Standard: Types A, C, D visible 3,000 ft on a clear night; Type B visible 1,000 ft on a sunny day.** ¶14 **Standard: ≥ 30 in to the bottom of the lens.** ¶15–¶20: Type A (night), Type B (day and night, 24 h), Types C and D (night delineation of the traveled way edge; Guidance: only on the outside of curves).
**6L.08** High-level warning device (flag tree). ¶3 **Standard: ≥ 2 flags, with or without a Type B light; light lens and lowest flag ≥ 8 ft above the roadway; flags ≥ 16 in square, orange or fluorescent red-orange.** ¶4 Option: a warning sign below.

## Chapter 6M — Other TTC zone design features and safety devices (PDF 878–881)
**6M.01** Support: lighting, barriers, dividers, crash cushions and screens are **not traffic control devices** (1D.04).
**6M.02** Positive protection and temporary barriers. ¶2 Guidance: consider barriers/positive protection where workers face increased risk. ¶3 Support A–E: no escape (tunnels, bridges); **long-term zones of two weeks or more**; **anticipated operating speeds ≥ 45 mph** with high volumes; workers, pedestrians or bicyclists near open lanes; overnight roadside hazards. ¶5: **23 CFR 630.1108(a)** (external). ¶7 **Standard: channelizing barriers supplemented with delineation, markings or devices; delineation color matching the marking color.** ¶8 **Standard: barriers and end treatments crashworthy.** ¶9 **Standard: no short intermittent segments; segments properly connected.** ¶10 Option: steady-burn lights on barriers.
**6M.03** Temporary raised islands. ¶1 **Standard: only with pavement striping and other channelizing devices.** ¶2 Option: **two-lane two-way operations with ADT 4,000–15,000; freeways with ADT 22,000–60,000.** ¶4 Guidance: **4 in high, ≥ 12 in wide, rounded corners.** ¶5 Guidance: not causing loss of control or dangerous debris. ¶6 **Standard: ≥ 60-in openings at pedestrian crossings.**
**6M.04** Detectable edging (Support: individual devices, tape, rope, markings are not detectable). ¶2 **Guidance: continuous detectable edging throughout temporary pedestrian facilities — top ≥ 8 in, bottom ≤ 2 in above the surface; gaps only at crossings; firmly attached and interconnected.** ¶3 Support examples A–G. ¶4 Guidance: orange, white or yellow matching adjacent devices.
**6M.05** Crash cushions. ¶2 **Standard: crashworthy; designed per application; periodically inspected; damaged ones promptly repaired or replaced.** ¶4 Standard: stationary cushions designed for the specific application. ¶5 **Standard: truck-mounted attenuators energy-absorbing, per manufacturer's specifications; the shadow vehicle placed in advance of the work.** ¶7 Guidance: distance neither too short nor so long that vehicles pass around it.
**6M.06** Rumble strips. ¶3 **Standard: colored longitudinal rumble strips match the line they supplement.** ¶4 **Standard: colored transverse rumble strips in travel lanes — white, black or orange.** ¶5 Option: decreasing intervals; warning sign. ¶6–¶11 Guidance: transverse to traffic; skid resistance; urban design; not on sharp curves; **not through pedestrian crossings or on bicycle routes**; **≥ 4-ft clear path for bicyclists** at edges or on shoulders.
**6M.07** Screens (Guidance: not restricting visibility; Roadside Design Guide Ch. 9; Option: on barriers between opposing traffic).
**6M.08** Lighting for night work (Guidance: floodlights, minimal glare, checked by driving through at night; identify workers as persons; avoid shadows). ¶5 Support: **~5 foot-candles general, ~20 for precision tasks.** ¶6 **Standard: flagger stations illuminated at night except in emergencies** (= 6D.06 ¶5).

## Chapter 6N — Types of TTC zone activities (PDF 882–…)
**6N.01** Work duration. ¶2 **Standard: five categories — A long-term stationary > 3 days; B intermediate-term stationary > one daylight period up to 3 days, or night work > 1 hour; C short-term stationary — daytime work > 1 hour within a single daylight period; D short duration — up to 1 hour; E mobile — moving intermittently or continuously.** ¶4, ¶6 **Standard: retroreflective and/or illuminated devices in long-term and intermediate-term stationary zones (they extend into night).** ¶9 Guidance: short-duration and mobile safety not compromised by fewer devices. ¶11 Option: simplified control for short duration, offset by dominant devices (vehicle lights). ¶13–¶14 Option: flags, devices moved along; flaggers. ¶16–¶18 Guidance: **a shadow vehicle with arrow board or sign following mobile work** (especially at high speeds/volumes); off-peak scheduling; **arrow boards for mobile operations in high-speed lanes of multi-lane divided highways**. ¶19 **Standard: mobile operations have appropriate devices on the equipment (high-intensity lights, signs, special lighting) or a separate warning vehicle; hazard warning flashers may supplement but never replace them.** ¶20 Option: **below 3 mph**, mobile or repositioned stationary signs. ¶21 Support: rolling roadblocks.
**6N.02** Location. ¶2 Support: outside the shoulder; on it (no or minor encroachment); median; traveled way — closer work needs more devices. ¶3 **Standard: work within the traveled way (except short-duration and mobile) — advance warning with a general message and highway-condition information; devices clearly delineating the path.**
**6N.03** Modifications (Option: add devices, adjust spacing, fewer devices for simpler cases; Guidance: for complex conditions — A added devices (1–12), B upgrades (1–5), C geometrics, D longer distances, E lighting, F pedestrian routes, G bicycle diversions).
**6N.04** Pedestrian and bicycle facilities. ¶3–¶5 Guidance: modify typical applications where usage is high (6C, 6K.02, 6M.04); separate pedestrians with accessible, detectable devices; no exposure to excavations, open utility access or overhanging equipment. ¶6 **Guidance: except short-term and mobile operations, SHOULDER WORK (W21-5) before an occupied shoulder; shoulder taper for work on paved shoulders ≥ 8 ft wide; signs not narrowing pedestrian passages below 48 in.**

Notes (read together)
- **Three flash-rate ranges by device (all Standards)**: arrow boards **25–40/min** (6L.06 ¶15); beacons and flashing warning lights, paddle lights **50–60/min** (4S.01 ¶3, 4G.01 ¶1, 6D.02 ¶9); sequential warning lights on merging tapers **55–75/min** (6L.07 ¶12). Not interchangeable.
- **PCMS and Chapter 2L**: 2L.01 ¶2 applies Chapter 2L to portable CMS; 6L.05 ¶1 says 6L.05 covers where PCMS differ. On phases the content is the same (≤ 2 phases, ≤ 3 lines), so 2L.05 ¶4's Standard authority carries over to PCMS; 6L.05 ¶17's Guidance restates it. Genuine differences: **3 lines × 8 characters** (6L.05 ¶13) vs 20 characters (2L.04 ¶7) — matching Table 2L-4's 8-character note; letter height ≥ 18 in generally (6L.05 ¶14) vs speed-based (2L.04 ¶9); PCMS mounting heights (6L.05 ¶26).
- **MEAN COMPUTED work-duration categories (6N.01 ¶2)**: short duration ≤ 1 h; short-term > 1 h within one daylight period; intermediate > one daylight period and ≤ 3 days, or night work > 1 h; long-term > 3 days; mobile. The partition has no gaps; the 3-day boundary matches 6F.02 ¶11 (low portable signs ≤ 3 days).
- CHECK passed: 3K.02 ¶4 (transverse rumble strips black or white "except as otherwise provided in Section 6M.06 for TTC zones") ↔ 6M.06 ¶4 (adds orange); 6M.04 ¶2's 8 in / 2 in edging = 6K.02 ¶8's detection-plate dimensions (Standard there, Guidance here); 6M.08 ¶6 = 6D.06 ¶5; 6L.05 ¶19's arrow-board exception = 2L.04 ¶2; 6L.05 ¶20 = 2L.05 ¶7A–B (2 s / 8 s); 6L.04 ¶10's ≥ 5-s yellow sits within 4F.17 ¶13's 3–6 s; 6L.06 ¶5 gives Figure 6L-3 Standard authority (R21).
- Pedestrian widths: 60 in target with 60 × 60 passing spaces every 200 ft (6C.02 ¶11D, 6K.07 ¶10); signs must not narrow passages below **48 in** (6N.04 ¶6); 60-in openings through lane separators and raised islands (6K.11 ¶6, 6M.03 ¶6, Standards).
- Flag colors by device: flagger flags "red or fluorescent orange-red" (6D.03 ¶2); flag-tree flags "orange or fluorescent red-orange" (6L.08 ¶3) — different lists; "red-orange" and "orange-red" are recorded as printed.

**6N.04 (cont.)** ¶7 Guidance: avoid pedestrian detours; work so as not to need them. ¶8 **Standard: alternate pedestrian routes where routes are closed.** ¶9 **Standard: temporary facilities detectable with the existing accessibility features** (= 6C.03 ¶4). ¶10, ¶14–¶16 Guidance: bikeway continuity; short, direct bike detours; not onto pedestrian paths (except shared-use paths or no alternative); full detour plans with W21 and W4-9 signs for complex detours. ¶11–¶13 Support: continuity matters beside general lanes ≥ 35 mph; lane closures to keep bike lanes. ¶18 Option: **R9-20 where lanes are reduced below 14 ft.** ¶19 **Standard: TTC signs on shared-use paths per Table 9A-1; on on-street bikeways per 6G–6I.**
**6N.05** Work outside the shoulder. ¶1 Support: **TTC generally not needed for work ≥ 15 ft from the traveled way**, unless distractions exist. Guidance: a single warning sign (W20-1); equipment on the road with flags, lights or W21-4; W21-5 for vehicles on the shoulder; W21-3 for occasional shoulder use. Option: MOWING AHEAD; W21-5 repeated every mile over 2 miles; NEXT XX MILES.
**6N.06** Shoulder work, no encroachment. ¶2 **Standard: closing paved shoulders ≥ 8 ft wide — at least one advance warning sign, and channelizing devices closing the shoulder in advance.** ¶3–¶6 Guidance: freeways — W20-1 then W21-5a (with NEXT XX FEET/MILES if beyond view), on the affected side only; improved high-speed shoulders treated as road closures — **taper ⅓ L**; W8-9 / W8-4 for damaged shoulders, repeated every mile beyond 1 mile. ¶9 **Standard: arrow boards for shoulder work in caution mode only** (= 6L.06 ¶18).
**6N.07** Minor encroachment. ¶2–¶3 Guidance: **close the lane unless 10 ft remains** (consider truck off-tracking). ¶4 Option: **9 ft** for short-term work on low-volume, low-speed roads without large commercial vehicles.
**6N.08** Median work (Guidance: **within 15 ft of either traveled way → advance warning and channelizing devices**).
**6N.09** Two-lane highways (Guidance: periodic detour signs; closures warned in advance, R11-3a for local access; detours signed throughout; Option: flaggers, STOP/YIELD on low volumes, temporary signals — Figures 6P-7 to 6P-12).
**6N.10** Urban streets. ¶4 **Standard: adequate access for bicyclists to the roadway or shared-use paths (Part 9).** ¶5 **Standard: pedestrian and vehicular access to affected or relocated transit stops.** Guidance: signed alternate bicycle routes, not onto pedestrian paths; detectable devices against pedestrian incursion at intersections; high-visibility devices for utility work.
**6N.11** Multi-lane non-access-controlled highways. ¶3 **Standard: lane closures (other than mobile) use a transition with a merging taper.** ¶4 Guidance: barriers when justified by study. ¶7–¶8 Guidance: uneven peak volumes — lend an opposing lane; closely spaced devices for temporary center lines. ¶10 **Standard: left-lane closures on undivided roads — devices along the center line and the adjacent lane.** ¶11–¶12 Guidance: consider closing an adjacent lane; capacity analysis; **move one lane at a time, tapers 2L apart**. ¶13 Option: **a single continuous taper at ≤ 40 mph operating speed** where space is short. ¶14 **Standard: closed directional roadways — inapplicable WRONG WAY signs, markings and intersection devices in the two-way section covered, removed or obliterated.**
**6N.12** Intersections. ¶2: near side, far side, in the intersection. ¶4 Guidance: adjust signal phasing, detectors, head locations (Part 4). ¶5 **Standard: contact the agency with jurisdiction where operational, capacity or accessibility problems are expected.** ¶6 Guidance: warning on all cross streets; **more advance signs where the posted limit, off-peak 85th-percentile or anticipated speed exceeds 40 mph.** ¶7 Guidance: accessible, detectable separation of crossings. ¶9–¶10, ¶13, ¶15 Option: mandatory turn lanes for through traffic; two advance signs plus an action sign in short blocks; strategies A–D in the intersection. ¶12 Guidance: **close the near-side lane where the far-side lane is closed.** ¶16 Guidance: flaggers or officers as conditions require.
**6N.13** Freeways and expressways (Guidance: queue-length assessment in the plan; Figures 6P-33 to 6P-38 — 6P-37 when capacity allows, 6P-38 when needed).
**6N.14** Two-way traffic on one roadway of a divided highway. ¶2 **Standard: opposing traffic separated throughout by temporary barriers, channelizing devices, W6-4 signs on flexible supports, or a temporary raised island; markings and signs alone shall not be used.**
**6N.15** Crossovers (Guidance A–D: tapers separated from crossovers; **design speed no lower than 10 mph below the posted, off-peak 85th-percentile or anticipated speed**; devices and full markings; accommodate trucks and buses). ¶2 Support: devices cannot compensate for poor geometry.
**6N.16** Interchanges (Guidance: maintain ramp access; clearly delineated exits; remove conflicting markings on long-term projects; coordinate before ramp closures; Option: close with signs and Type 3 barricades).
**6N.17** Grade crossings. ¶1 **Standard: no queuing across the tracks from lane restrictions or flagging; if unavoidable, a uniformed officer or flagger at the crossing even where automatic devices exist.** ¶4 Guidance: early railroad coordination.
**6N.18** Night work. Guidance: enhanced controls (6N.03), lights and retroreflective markings on workers and equipment, officers where speeds or impaired drivers are a concern. ¶4 Option: close and detour. ¶6 **Standard: except emergencies, temporary lighting at all night flagger stations.** ¶7 Support: **5 fc general, 10 fc around equipment, 20 fc for precision tasks.**
**6N.19** Late Merge (Support; Option: static or PCMS; Guidance: **R4-9a and W9-2a static signing**; Option: PCMS messages A–E upstream and "MERGE HERE / TAKE TURNS" at the merge point). **Figure 6N-1** (letter codes per Table 6B-1).

## Chapter 6O — Traffic incident management areas (PDF 895–897)
**6O.01** Support: NIMS requires ICS; definitions (incident; incident management area — a type of TTC zone). ¶4 **Classes: major > 2 h; intermediate 30 min to 2 h; minor < 30 min.** ¶6–¶9 Guidance: joint planning; responder training; move incidents off the roadway; high-visibility apparel for responders and news media; notify agencies early for probable major incidents; position emergency vehicles to protect and minimise disruption; estimate magnitude, duration and queue. ¶10 Option: **fluorescent pink warning and guide signs** (Figure 6O-1). ¶12 Option: on-hand devices for initial response. ¶14–¶16 Guidance: devices ready for major and intermediate incidents; warn the back of the queue; manual control by qualified flaggers or officers. ¶17, ¶19 Option: available devices for flaggers; flares supplementing devices. ¶18, ¶20 Guidance: replace flares with channelizing devices soon; remove all after the incident.
**6O.02** Major (> 2 h). ¶2 Guidance: **incidents expected to last > 24 hours use the other Part 6 procedures and devices.** **Figure 6O-1**.
**6O.03** Intermediate (30 min – 2 h; Support). **6O.04** Minor (< 30 min; Support; Guidance: move vehicles to the shoulder quickly).
**6O.05** Emergency-vehicle lighting (Support: warning only, no traffic control; too many lights distract). Guidance: reduce lighting once the scene is secured, especially forward-facing on divided roads; turn off unneeded floodlights and headlights at night.

## Chapter 6P — Typical applications (PDF 898–1009)
**6P.01** ¶1: combine typical applications as needed. ¶2 **Support: the procedures shown are minimum solutions; except for the notes (classified by their own Standard / Guidance / Option / Support headings), the typical applications can generally be regarded as Guidance.** ¶3–¶4 Option: deviate for site or jurisdiction conditions; add devices, adjust spacing; fewer devices by field conditions. ¶6: Table 6P-1 (index of 54), Table 6P-2 (symbol legend); spacing letters per Table 6B-1; tapers per Table 6B-4. ¶7: most show one direction.

Notes (read together)
- **R25 (new reading rule) — typical application authority (6P.01 ¶2)**: the drawings are Guidance; each note carries the authority of its own heading. A "shall" in a TA note under a Standard heading is a Standard; the drawing itself is not.
- MEAN COMPUTED incident classes (6O.01 ¶4): minor < 30 min; intermediate 30 min – 2 h (both ends inclusive); major > 2 h; > 24 h → full Part 6 treatment (6O.02 ¶2).
- Thresholds used consistently: 15 ft from the traveled way (6N.05 ¶1, 6N.08 ¶2); ⅓ L shoulder tapers (6N.06 ¶4 = 6B.08 ¶10); arrow boards in caution mode for shoulder work (6N.06 ¶9 = 6L.06 ¶18); night flagger-station lighting stated three times as a Standard (6D.06 ¶5, 6M.08 ¶6, 6N.18 ¶6); 6N.18 ¶7 adds 10 fc to 6M.08 ¶5's 5 / 20 fc.
- The three-way TTC speed (posted, off-peak 85th-percentile, or anticipated) recurs in 6N.12 ¶6 and 6N.15 ¶1B — the same unranked key as Tables 6B-2 and 6B-4 (item 7).
- Lane-width floors: 10 ft (Guidance), 9 ft (Option, low-volume low-speed short-term) (6N.07); bikeway shared-lane signing below 14 ft (6N.04 ¶18).

**Table 6P-1** (read from the pages; 2 sheets) Index to Typical Applications — TA-1 to TA-54 grouped by Section: outside the shoulder (TA-1 beyond shoulder, TA-2 blasting); shoulder (TA-3 to TA-6); two-lane traveled way (TA-7 to TA-17); urban street (TA-18 to TA-20); intersections and sidewalks (TA-21 to TA-29); multi-lane non-access-controlled (TA-30 to TA-35); freeways/expressways (TA-36 to TA-45); grade crossing (TA-46); bicycle lanes and shared-use paths (TA-47 to TA-51); circular intersections (TA-52 to TA-54).

(The text layer shows only 19 — the four in bold are missing from it; the tables file has all 23.)

**Typical Application notes (TA-1 to TA-54)** — read from the pages; drawings are Guidance per 6P.01 ¶2 and were read through their text labels and notes (not image by image). Standards and thresholds recorded:
- **Recurring Standards**: vehicle hazard warning signals **never instead of** high-intensity rotating, flashing, oscillating or strobe lights (TA-1, 3, 4, 6, 15, 17, 21, 26, 27 — = 6N.01 ¶19); **shadow and work vehicles display high-intensity lights** (TA-6, 17, 35); vehicle-mounted signs not obscured and covered when work is not in progress (TA-4, 6, 17, 35); **flagger stations illuminated at night except emergencies** (TA-10, 14, 27, 46, 52, 53); devices for the opposite direction where only one is shown (TA-7, 9); **arrow boards in caution mode for shoulder and two-lane operations** (TA-4, 17); **"An arrow board shall be used when a freeway lane is closed; a separate arrow board for each closed lane"** (TA-33, 35, 37, 38, 39, 42, 44, 45); **barrier never placed along the merging or shifting taper — the lane is first closed/shifted with channelizing devices and markings** (TA-34, 36, 38); barriers per 6M.02 (TA-5, 28, 34, 36, 38); temporary facilities detectable with existing accessibility features (TA-28, 29, 53, 54); Street Name / Bike Route Name sign **above** the Detour / Bike Detour sign when used (TA-19, 20, 48, 50).
- **TA-1** Option: ROAD WORK AHEAD omitted where work is behind a barrier, > 24 in behind the curb, or **≥ 15 ft** from any roadway edge; signs omitted for short-term/short-duration/mobile work with vehicle lights.
- **TA-2** **Standard: signing whenever blasting caps are within 1,000 ft of a roadway; covered when no explosives; side roads within the zone or within 1,000 ft of a cap get similar signing; the blaster in charge decides whether traffic is stopped during blasting.**
- **TA-3** Option: signs eliminated for **short-duration operations of 60 minutes or less** with vehicle lights; **Standard (note 8) = 6N.06 ¶2** (≥ 8-ft shoulders).
- **TA-4** Guidance: advance sign **≤ 5 miles** from the work; distance plaque at 2–5 miles; Option: G20-1 when work spans **> 2 miles**.
- **TA-6** Guidance: lanes **≥ 10 ft** to the near face of devices; Option: **9 ft** short-term on low-volume low-speed roads.
- **TA-7** Standard: inapplicable markings removed before new patterns open; barriers and end treatments crashworthy. Guidance: **tangent > 600 ft → Reverse Curve signs (Reverse Turn if ≤ 30 mph) instead of Double Reverse Curve**.
- **TA-10** Guidance: grade-crossing provisions (extend the zone; inform flaggers; left-side warning); Option: flagger/officer to keep vehicles from stopping **within 15 ft** of the crossing (from both outside rails).
- **TA-12** **Standard: Part 4 compliance; timing by authorized officials; red clearance adequate to clear the one-lane section; flashing mode → red to both approaches; stop lines for long-term closures, conflicting markings removed and restored afterwards; safeguards against conflicting indications** (= 4O.02, 4O.03). Support: signals preferable to flaggers for long-term and night work.
- **TA-13** Support: planned closure **≤ 20 minutes** in daytime; **Standard: a flagger or uniformed officer**; flaggers per 6D.05–6D.06.
- **TA-14** Haul roads. **Standard: the same method both directions; unused haul roads closed with Type 3 barricades (flagger signs covered; or signals flashing yellow/red or covered with signs hidden, or green to the main road only with actuation); signals control both roads per Part 4; stop lines; markings removed and restored.**
- **TA-16** **Standard: surveying on the center line of a high-volume road — close one lane per Figure 6P-10.** Guidance: cones 6–12 in from the center line.
- **TA-18** **Standard: only for low-speed, low-volume facilities; one or two flaggers where traffic cannot self-regulate.**
- **TA-21** **Standard: the merging taper directs traffic into the right or left lane, not both.**
- **TA-28** **Standard: temporary ramps 12:1 (8.33 %) or flatter, slip-resistant; landings ≥ 48 × 48 in with ≤ 2 % cross-slope; LCDs per 6K.02; SIDEWALK CLOSED CROSS HERE signs with audible information devices; audible devices where midblock closings or changed crosswalks leave inadequate communication.** Option: route 48 in wide with a 60-in passing area every 200 ft.
- **TA-29** **Standard: curb parking prohibited ≥ 50 ft before the midblock crosswalk**; audible devices as TA-28. Guidance: cover or deactivate pedestrian signals at closed crosswalks. Option: FYG pedestrian/bicycle/school signs in TTC (= 6H.01 ¶5).
- **TA-31** **Standard: only when volumes require two lanes in the direction with the closure; lanes illustrated on Reverse Curve signs = through lanes (except ALL LANES plaque).** Guidance: **devices at ≤ ½ S ft where markings conflict**; < 600 ft tangent → Double Reverse Curve.
- **TA-32** **Standard: inapplicable markings removed as soon as practical; temporary markings except intermediate/short-term, where very close device spacing makes channelization dominant.** Guidance: ≤ ½ S spacing instead of markings.
- **TA-33** **Standard: also for median-lane work (LEFT LANE CLOSED and Lane Ends signs substituted); added devices at side roads within the zone.**
- **TA-34** Standard: movable barrier — no temporary white edge line; when the lane reopens, signs and devices show only the shoulder closed; arrow board at the shoulder taper in caution mode.
- **TA-35** **Standard: arrow boards at least Type B (60 × 30 in).** Guidance: shadow vehicles 1 (arrow board + TMA) and 2; on the shoulder where it is ≥ 10 ft wide for interior-lane work.
- **TA-36** Support: a lane shift meeting design speed, full cross-section and full markings needs only the initial warning sign. **Standard: shoulder lane ≥ 10 ft; long-term — conflicting markings removed and temporary markings installed before the change.** Guidance: > 600 ft → two sets of Reverse Curve signs; < 600 ft → Double Reverse Curve; solid white lane lines with STAY IN LANE.
- **TA-37 / TA-38** Guidance: second arrow board positions (= 6L.06 ¶22); Option: shoulder ≥ 10 ft may carry traffic.
- **TA-39** **Standard: channelizing devices or barriers separate opposing traffic.** Guidance: barrier for long-term high-speed high-volume work; AASHTO Green Book 2018 for curved crossovers; **extend devices 2 × S beyond the transition** (= 6K.01 ¶5); **Two-Way Traffic signs every 1 mile**.
- **TA-41 / TA-42** **Standard: a temporary EXIT sign in the temporary gore, bottom ≥ 7 ft above the pavement** (black on orange or white on green, Option). Guidance: EXIT CLOSED panels diagonally across guide signs.
- **TA-43** Guidance: truck off-tracking and the 10-ft lane (6N.07).
- **TA-44** **Standard: where acceleration distance is inadequate, the YIELD sign is replaced by STOP signs on both sides.** Guidance: temporary stop line; arrow-board taper well ahead of the ramp; paired advisory-speed signs on sharp ramps. Option: Stop Beacon or red Type B flasher; NO MERGE AREA plaque.
- **TA-45** Movable-barrier reversible lane (Support: phases A, A→B, B, B→A; Guidance: transfer-vehicle procedure).
- **TA-46** Guidance: the grade crossing = **15 ft either side of the closest and farthest rail**; buffer extended upstream of the crossing; DO NOT STOP ON TRACKS on all approaches in the zone. **Standard: officer or flagger if queuing across active tracks cannot be avoided** (= 6N.17 ¶1).
- **TA-47 / 48 / 50** Bicycle lanes: Guidance — **≥ 35 mph** and not appropriate to share → separate facility or detour; Option — **≤ 30 mph** with the adjacent lane **< 14 ft** → R9-20; **≥ 14 ft** throughout → bicycle warning signs with IN STREET / IN ROAD plaques.
- **TA-49** Guidance: temporary path at least as wide as the closed one. **TA-51** Guidance: for paved shoulders closed on bikeway-system roads **≥ 45 mph**.
- **TA-52** **Standard: with a quadrant closed, only one approach released at a time; WRONG WAY signs covered.** Guidance: cover YIELD, ONE WAY, directional arrows and misleading guide or lane-use signs; truck detour; **close and detour for intermediate or long-term work**.
- **TA-53 / TA-54** Standard: flaggers per 6D.05–6D.06; accessible temporary facilities. Guidance: one entry at a time; wide-vehicle turning; ≤ ½ S device spacing where markings conflict; close and detour for intermediate/long-term work if traffic cannot be accommodated.

**Chapter 6P complete. PART 6 complete.**

Notes (read together)
- **Arrow boards on freeways are required by the typical-application notes**: 6L.06 ¶2 is Guidance ("should be used … along major multi-lane roadways"); the TA notes carry a **Standard** — "An arrow board shall be used when a freeway lane is closed" — under 6P.01 ¶2's rule that notes keep their own headings. For freeway lane closures the arrow board is a Standard.
- **Barriers and tapers**: 6K.09 ¶4 (Guidance: not for merging tapers except low-speed urban) is made a Standard in the freeway TAs (TA-34, 36, 38: never along the merging/shifting taper).
- **Device spacing where markings conflict**: ≤ ½ S ft (TA-31, 32, 53, 54) — tighter than 6K.01 ¶4's 1 × S (taper) / 2 × S (tangent).
- **600-ft tangent split**: Double Reverse Curve for tangents **< 600 ft** (6H.31 ¶1, TA-31, 32, 36); two Reverse Curve sets for **> 600 ft** (TA-7, 36). A tangent of exactly 600 ft is in neither statement (minor; same family as item 7).
- CHECK passed: TA-3's "60 minutes or less" = 6N.01 short duration (≤ 1 h); TA-1's 15 ft = 6N.05 ¶1; TA-35's Type B 60 × 30 = Figure 6L-3; TA-12 = 4O.02–4O.03; TA-46 = 6N.17; TA-39 note 7 = 6K.01 ¶5; TA-29's FYG option = 6H.01 ¶5; the bicycle 30/35-mph split leaves no gap for posted limits (multiples of 5, 2B.21 ¶13).
