# PART 7 — TRAFFIC CONTROL FOR SCHOOL AREAS

## Chapter 7A — General (PDF 1010–1011)
**7A.01** Support: standards for all devices and other controls (including adult crossing guards) for school-area pedestrian conditions.
**7A.02** School route plans (Guidance): a plan for each school (elementary to high school) by school, law enforcement and traffic officials — a map of streets, school, controls, walk routes and crossings (Figure 7A-1); consider bicycling; device type related to vehicle volume and speed, street width, number and age of students; devices in a school traffic control plan; walk routes use existing controls; factors A–D for longer walks to controlled crossings. Support: gaps; Warrant 5 (4C.06).

## Chapter 7B — Signs (PDF 1012–…)
**7B.01** Design. ¶1 **Standard: sizes on conventional roadways in school areas per Table 7B-1 (except 2A.07).** ¶2 **Standard: Oversized column on expressways in school areas.** ¶3 Guidance: Oversized on roads with ≥ 4 lanes and posted limits ≥ 40 mph. ¶4 Option: larger. ¶5 **Standard: school warning signs (including the SCHOOL part of S5-1 and their plaques) fluorescent yellow-green with black legend and border, unless otherwise provided for a specific sign.** ¶6 **Standard: retroreflective or illuminated.** ¶11 Option: in-roadway signs per 2B.20 and 7B.03.
**Table 7B-1** (read from the page) School Area Sign and Plaque Sizes — conventional · minimum · oversized: S1-1, S3-1, S3-2, S4-5/5a 36×36 · 30×30 · 48×48; S5-1 24×48 · — · 36×72; S5-2, S5-3 24×30 · — · 36×48; R1-5a/5c 36×36; R1-6 series 12×36; R1-9b/9c 90×24; **R2-1 (school use) 24×30 · — · 36×48**; R2-10, R2-11 24×30 · 36×48; plaques S4-1P, S4-2P, S4-4P, S4-6P 24×12 · 36×18; S4-3P 24×9 · 36×12; S4-7P 24×12 · 30×18; R2-6P/6aP/6bP 24×18 · 36×24; W16-2P 24×18 · 30×24; W16-2aP 24×12 · 30×18; W16-5P/6P/7P 21×15 · 30×21; W16-9P 24×12 · 30×18. Notes: larger allowed; W×H inches; **3 "Minimum sign sizes for multi-lane conventional roads shall be as shown in the Conventional Road column."**
**7B.02** School area signs. ¶2 Support: four uses of S1-1 — A school area, B school zone start, C School Advance Crossing assembly (with AHEAD or XX FEET), D School Crossing assembly (with W16-7P). ¶3 Option: S1-1 with W16-5P/6P on approaches to a nearby cross-street school area. ¶4 **Standard: where a school zone is designated by State or local statute, S1-1 at its beginning point(s).** ¶5–¶7 Option: SCHOOL plaque; ALL YEAR plaque for 12-month schools; END SCHOOL ZONE at the end. **Figures 7B-1** (2 sheets; notes: **WHEN FLASHING assemblies shall be accompanied by a Speed Limit Sign Beacon (4S.04)**; reduced in-street sizes S1-1 12×12, S4-3P 12×4, W16-7P 12×6, W16-9P 12×6; STATE LAW optional; SCHOOL plaque optional above R1-6), **7B-2** (note: School Advance Crossing assembly optional within a signed school zone).
**7B.03** School crossing signs. ¶1 **Standard: School Advance Crossing assembly = S1-1 + AHEAD or XX FEET.** ¶2 **Standard: used in advance (Table 2C-3) of the first School Crossing assembly in each direction, except ¶3** (Option: omitted where a School Zone S1-1 sign precedes the crossing). ¶4–¶5 Option: arrow plaques for nearby cross-street crosswalks; 12-in in-street S1-1 (per 2B.20) with 12×6 AHEAD. ¶6 **Standard: School Crossing assembly (S1-1 + W16-7P) at or as close as possible to the crossing.** ¶7 **Standard: only at crossings adjacent to schools or on established school pedestrian routes.** ¶8 **Standard: not on STOP- or YIELD-controlled approaches, except ¶9–¶10** (Option: roundabout approaches and YIELD-controlled channelized right-turn lanes where the crosswalk is **≥ 20 ft** before the yield point). ¶11 Option: R1-5a/R1-5c before marked crosswalks on uncontrolled multi-lane approaches in school zones (2B.19). ¶12–¶14 Option: R1-6/6a or R1-6b/6c in-street signs (with 12×4 SCHOOL plaque; STATE LAW omissible) where not controlled by signals, PHBs or EVHBs; with flashing beacons; R1-9b/9c overhead. ¶15 **Standard: at uncontrolled crossings, in-street or overhead signs only supplement a School Crossing assembly with W16-7P.** ¶16 Option: 12-in in-street S1-1 instead of R1-6 series. ¶17 **Standard: in-roadway signs meet 2B.20's mounting height and support requirements.** ¶18 **Standard: in-street, overhead and in-street S1-1 signs never on approaches controlled by a traffic control signal, PHB or EVHB.** **Figure 7B-3**.
**7B.04** School bus stops (Guidance: S3-1 where a stopped bus is not visible for an adequate distance and cannot be relocated; Option: S3-2 SCHOOL BUS TURN AHEAD where a turnaround is not visible for the **Table 2C-3 Condition B "0" distance**).
**7B.05** School speed limits. ¶1 **Standard: a School Speed Limit assembly or S5-1 where a reduced school speed limit is established by engineering study or statute, at or near the start of the zone.** ¶2 **Standard: an S1-1 in advance (Table 2C-3) of the first school speed limit sign each way.** ¶3 **Standard: END SCHOOL SPEED LIMIT (S5-3) at the downstream end, except ¶4** (Option: END SCHOOL ZONE where both end together; a Speed Limit sign above). ¶5 **Guidance: begin ≥ 200 ft before the school grounds or crossing (more if the school limit is ≥ 30 mph); no more than 500 ft before.** ¶6 **Standard: static, blank-out or CMS.** ¶7 **Standard: static assembly — SCHOOL plaque, R2-1, and a bottom plaque (S4-1P, S4-2P, S4-4P or S4-6P) giving periods/days.** ¶8 **Standard: S5-1 or R2-1 with WHEN FLASHING — a Speed Limit Sign Beacon (4S.04) identifies the periods.** ¶9 **Standard: "SCHOOL" on a CMS uses fluorescent yellow-green pixels.** ¶10–¶11 Option: blank-out; Vehicle Speed Feedback W13-20aP (¶12 Guidance: only while the limit is in effect). ¶13 Guidance: **S4-5/S4-5a where the limit drops by more than 10 mph** or by judgement. ¶14–¶15 **Standard: followed by the school speed limit sign, with the identical speed.** **Figure 7B-4**.
**7B.06** Higher fines. ¶2 **Standard: increased fines in a designated school zone — A R2-10 or an R2-6P/6aP/6bP plaque supplementing the S1-1 at the start; B R2-11 or S5-2 at the end.** ¶3 **Standard: fines only for speeding — the fines plaque goes with the S5-1 sign, never beneath the S1-1.** ¶4 **Standard: where higher fines begin elsewhere than the S1-1 — R2-10 at that point.** ¶5–¶6 Option: plaque beneath S1-1 too; S4-1P, S4-2P or S4-4P (with a yellow beacon) below the beginning sign. ¶7 Guidance: omit the duplicate fines plaque on the S5-1 when other violations are also fined. ¶8–¶9 Option: END SCHOOL ZONE for coinciding ends; R2-10 and plaques omissible where the zone is set by statute. **Figure 7B-5**.
**7B.07** Parking and stopping (Option; Support: typical school-day regulations; 2B.53–2B.55).

Notes (read together)
- **School Speed Limit (R2-1) on multi-lane conventional roads**: Table 2B-1 gives 24×30 (single lane) / **30×36 (multi-lane)**; Table 7B-1 gives **24×30** with note 3 — "Minimum sign sizes for multi-lane conventional roads **shall** be as shown in the Conventional Road column". Settled: 7B.01 ¶1 (Standard) makes Table 7B-1 govern sizes in school areas — the specific provision. The design fits: the School Speed Limit assembly stacks a 24-wide SCHOOL plaque, R2-1 and a 24-wide time plaque. **In school areas the graph uses Table 7B-1, not Table 2B-1's multi-lane column** (contrast Table 6G-1, which refers multi-lane roads back to Table 2B-1).
- CHECK passed: 7B.03 ¶9–¶10's 20-ft rule = 2C.54 ¶11 / 2C.55 ¶6 and 3C.09 ¶3; 7B.03 ¶18 = 2B.20 (in-street signs not at signals, PHBs, EVHBs); 7B.05 ¶13–¶15 = 2C.40 (more than 10 mph; identical speed); 7B.05 ¶8 and the Figure 7B-1 note = 4S.04; 7B.06 ¶9 = 2B.25 ¶3 (statutory zones may omit R2-10); 7B.01 ¶5 (FYG) = 2C.02 and Table 2A-2.
- MEAN COMPUTED school speed zone start: ≥ 200 ft before the school/crossing (increased when the school limit ≥ 30 mph), ≤ 500 ft (7B.05 ¶5, Guidance).
- X statute-dependent: designated school zones and statutory school speed limits (7B.02 ¶4, 7B.05 ¶1).


## Chapter 7C — Markings (PDF 1025)
**7C.01** Guidance: mark crosswalks at intersections on established school routes with substantial conflict, where students would not know where to cross, or where drivers would not expect them; an engineering study (3C.02 factors) away from signals and STOP/YIELD approaches; **warning signs (7B.03) at all non-intersection marked school crosswalks**; parking prohibitions for visibility.
**7C.02** Option: two-lane SCHOOL word marking; Guidance: **letters ≥ 10 ft** if two lanes wide. **Figure 7C-1**.

## Chapter 7D — Crossing supervision (PDF 1026)
**7D.01** Option: adult crossing guards where a study shows gaps must be created and where authorized by law. Guidance: jurisdiction policies on qualifications, selection, training.
**7D.02** ¶1 **Standard: officers doing school crossing supervision and adult crossing guards wear apparel labeled ANSI 107-2020, Class 2, Type R, as described in 6C.05.** ¶2 **Standard: guards do not direct traffic in the law-enforcement sense; they pick opportune times to create gaps and stand in the roadway to show that pedestrians are crossing and vehicles must stop.** ¶3–¶4 **Standard: a STOP paddle is the primary device — a 6D.02 paddle with STOP on both sides.** ¶5 **Standard: retroreflective or illuminated in darkness.**

**PART 7 complete.**

# PART 8 — TRAFFIC CONTROL FOR RAILROAD AND LIGHT RAIL TRANSIT GRADE CROSSINGS

## Chapter 8A — General (PDF 1028–1033)
**8A.01** Support: LRT; Part 8 applies to highway-rail and highway-LRT crossings unless stated; Chapter 8E for pathway/sidewalk crossings; the joint right-of-way; **Diagnostic Team** (highway agency, regulatory agency, railroad/transit agency); "traffic control system"; "grade crossing warning system" (AREMA 2023, external). ¶9 **Standard: Part 8 devices and practices at all grade crossings open to public travel — except crossings of privately-owned roadways, pathways and sidewalks — consistent with Federal, State and local law.** ¶10: 23 CFR 655.603 for private crossings.
**8A.02** LRT alignments (Support: exclusive — no grade crossings; semi-exclusive — limited access at designated points; mixed-use — LRT without right-of-way priority). ¶5 **Guidance: LRT crossings with flashing-light signals within 200 ft of a signal, PHB or EVHB — rail preemption (4F.19, 8D.09) unless the Diagnostic Team decides otherwise.** ¶6 Option: priority or preemption on mixed-use alignments. ¶7 **Standard: where LRT and railroads share tracks or run on adjacent tracks, highway-rail devices and practices apply.**
**8A.03** Systems and practices. ¶2 **Standard: approval by the highway agency, the regulatory agency (if any) and the railroad/transit agency before any new or modified system.** ¶3 **Standard: the Diagnostic Team's recommendation documented in an engineering study (8A.05), based on site visits, meetings or calls.** ¶4 **Standard: operational changes evaluated by a Diagnostic Team, except ¶7** (Option: maintenance or minor changes without negative impact, as the agencies determine). ¶5 **Standard: Diagnostic Team engineering studies for — lane changes approaching or crossing; track additions or removals; significant changes in train or vehicle numbers or speeds; new nearby vehicle access; sidewalk or bicycle-lane changes (especially counter-flow lanes on one-way streets); one-way/reversible conversions; new or significantly changed nearby signals.** ¶6 Option: Quiet Zone studies. ¶8 Support: AREMA 2023, FHWA/FRA Highway-Rail Crossing Handbook (3rd ed.), ITE Preemption (2nd ed.) (external).
**8A.04** LRT systems. ¶3 **Standard: semi-exclusive LRT crossings (except median tracks) have flashing-light signals, with or without gates, unless the Diagnostic Team finds Crossbuck Assemblies, signals (8D.04), or STOP or YIELD signs alone adequate.**
**8A.05** Engineering studies. ¶1 **Standard: the traffic control system is determined by an engineering study of a Diagnostic Team.** ¶2 Option: regulatory approval. ¶3 Guidance: factors (geometry, stopping and clearing sight distance, nearby intersections, driveways, traffic and train volumes, queuing, pedestrians and bicycles, passenger trains and stations, train speeds and variability, multiple tracks, high-speed operation, school buses and hazmat haulers, crash history). ¶4 Option: HRI components of the National ITS architecture.
**8A.06** Uniform provisions. ¶1 **Standard: signs retroreflective or illuminated (2A.21).** ¶2 **Standard: no sign or signal in the centre of an undivided highway unless crashworthy or on a raised island.** ¶3 Guidance: **≥ 2 ft** from the island edge (except 2A.16). ¶4–¶5 Guidance: median islands sized for queues; **TWLTLs discontinued near crossings** (islands, one-direction left turns, or yellow diagonals with tubular markers). ¶6 Option: gates extended across that lane.
**8A.07** Minimum track clearance and clear storage distances (Support): upstream point — the gate arm tip farthest from the nearest rail; else the stop line; unpaved — **10 ft** from the nearest rail; downstream point — **6 ft beyond the farthest rail** (or the downstream intersection edge if closer), or clear of an exit gate; adjacent crossings within 200 ft measured through both; clear storage distance lane by lane to the downstream stop/yield line.
**8A.08** Adjacent crossings (Guidance, measured along the highway between the inside rails): **≤ 100 ft → treated as one crossing; > 100 and < 200 ft → added signs or devices, extra clearance time for active devices; > 200 ft → separate crossings with devices between.**
**8A.09** Elimination. ¶1–¶3 Option/Guidance: engineering study incl. diverted traffic. ¶4 **Standard: devices at an eliminated crossing are removed, and covered or turned in the interim.** ¶5–¶7 Guidance: relocate devices when tracks are removed; remove roadway approaches with end markers (2C.73); remove or pave over eliminated tracks. ¶8 Option: TRACKS OUT OF SERVICE (R8-9).
**8A.10** Illumination (Support: ANSI/IES RP-8-22).
**8A.11** Quiet Zones. Support: 49 CFR Part 222. ¶2 **Standard: devices used in a Quiet Zone comply with the MUTCD.**
**8A.12** Circular intersections. ¶2 **Standard: where a circular intersection includes or is within 200 ft of a grade crossing, an engineering study of queuing.** ¶3 Guidance: Diagnostic Team measures (Support A–F).
**8A.13** TTC zones. ¶2 **Standard: per Part 6.** Guidance: railroad/transit in planning where lanes reverse over a crossing; **a uniformed officer at all times rail traffic may approach if the warning system is not modified**; a TTC plan for detours over passive crossings; coordination; prior notice.

## Chapter 8B — Signs (PDF 1034–…)
**8B.01** Support: passive systems (signs and markings); Part 2 applies unless provided here.
**8B.02** ¶1 **Standard: minimum sizes per Table 8B-1.** ¶2 Option: larger.
**Table 8B-1** (read from the pages; 2 sheets; Rev. 1 marks) Grade Crossing Sign and Plaque Minimum Sizes — conventional single · multi-lane · expressway · minimum · oversized: **R1-1 30×30 · 36×36 · 36×36 · — · 48×48; R1-2 30×30×30 · 36×36×36 · 36×36×36 · — · 48×48×48**; R3-1a/2a 24×30 · 30×36; R8-8 24×30 · 24×30 · 36×48 · — · 36×48; R8-9 24×24 · 24×24 · 36×36 · — · 36×36; R8-10 24×36 · 24×36 · — · — · 36×48; R8-10a 24×30 · 24×30 · — · — · 36×42; R10-6 24×36 …36×48; R10-6a 24×30 … 36×42; R10-10b/c 24×30 · 30×36 · — · 24×30; **R15-1 48×9**; **R15-2P 27×18**; R15-3P 24×12; R15-4a/b/c, R15-5, R15-5a, R15-6a 24×30; R15-6, R15-7, R15-7a 24×24; R15-8 — · — · — · 18×9; **W10-1 36 dia. · 36 dia. · 48 dia. · — · 48 dia.**; W10-1aP 24×12; W10-2/3/4, W10-5, W10-8, W10-9, W10-11, W10-12 36×36 · 36×36 · 48×48 · — · 48×48; W10-5P, W10-9P, W10-13P, W10-14P/14aP 30×24; W10-7 24×24; W10-11a/11b 30×36; W10-15P 30×24 … 36×30; W10-16 30×30; W10-21 36×36 · 36×36 · 48×48 · — · 48×48; W10-21aP 30×24; I13-1 — · 12×9 (minimum); I13-2 — · 18×9. Notes: larger allowed; W×H inches; **3 Table 9A-1 gives minimum sizes for signs facing shared-use paths and pedestrian facilities.**
**8B.03** Crossbuck R15-1 and Number of Tracks R15-2P. ¶1 **Standard: retroreflective white with black RAILROAD CROSSING, mounted per Figure 8B-2.** ¶2 Support: in most States the Crossbuck requires yielding to rail traffic. ¶3 **Standard: at least one Crossbuck on each approach to every highway-rail crossing.** ¶4 **Standard: at least one on each approach to every highway-LRT crossing with flashing-light signals or gates** (¶5 Option: at other LRT crossings). ¶6 **Standard: two or more tracks → an inverted-T R15-2P plaque below.** ¶7 **Standard: on the right side of each approach; an additional one on the left where sight distance is restricted, geometry is unfavorable, or the approach is one-way multi-lane.** ¶8 **Standard: a ≥ 2-in retroreflective white strip on the back of each blade at all passive crossings, except back-to-back or double-faced installations** (¶9 Option: at active crossings). ¶10–¶11 Guidance: clearances per the railroad and regulatory agency; **centre ~9 ft above the pavement edge** (¶12 Option: varied, raised for signs below).
**8B.04** Crossbuck Assemblies with YIELD or STOP at passive crossings. ¶1 **Standard: Crossbuck (+ R15-2P if two or more tracks) with a YIELD or STOP sign on the same support (except ¶10), installed per 2B.18 and Figures 8B-2, 8B-3.** ¶2 **Standard: at all public highway-rail crossings without Chapter 8D active systems (except where an authorized person on the ground stops traffic whenever a train approaches) — a Crossbuck Assembly on the right of each approach.** ¶3 **Standard: highway-LRT passive crossings using a Crossbuck — likewise.** ¶4 **Standard: an additional assembly on the left for restricted sight distance, unfavorable geometry or one-way multi-lane approaches.** ¶5 **Standard: YIELD is the default; STOP only if an engineering study by the agency with jurisdiction determines it appropriate.** ¶6 Guidance: STOP limited to unusual conditions found by a Diagnostic Team (sight lines incl. seasonal crops, tracks, speeds, crashes). ¶7 Guidance: stop-controlled approaches with clear storage shorter than the design vehicle — consider the STOP at the Crossbuck instead of the intersection. ¶8 **Standard: a passive crossing on an approach to a signalized intersection not interconnected or preempted — a Diagnostic Team determines the devices; no STOP sign on the Crossbuck Assembly.** ¶10 Option: the YIELD/STOP on a separate support at the stopping point, still part of the assembly. **Figures 8B-1, 8B-2** (notes: YIELD/STOP only at passive crossings, STOP only by study; **mounting ≥ 4 ft on existing Crossbuck supports; ≥ 5 ft for new rural installations; ≥ 7 ft where parking or pedestrians are likely**), **8B-3** (notes: faces in one plane, YIELD/STOP nearer the traveled way, **≥ 2-in separation**).

Notes (read together)
- **Crossing-guard apparel (7D.02 ¶1 vs 6C.05 ¶1)**: 7D.02 names ANSI 107-**2020** Class 2 Type R "as described in Section 6C.05"; 6C.05 names 107-**2015** "or equivalent revisions" (Class 2 or 3). Reconciled by 6C.05's revision clause; Part 7 narrows crossing guards to Class 2.
- **Adjacent grade crossings (8A.08)**: ≤ 100 ft one crossing; > 100 and < 200 ft added devices; > 200 ft separate — **exactly 200 ft is in none of the three** (item 7 family).
- **The 200-ft threshold across Parts**: LRT flashing-light crossings near signals/PHBs/EVHBs (8A.02 ¶5), circular intersections (8A.12 ¶2), temporary signals (6L.01 ¶13), PHB/EVHB preemption (4J.03 ¶14, 4N.03 ¶7), adjacent crossings (8A.07 ¶3, 8A.08).
- CHECK passed: 8A.01 ¶9 agrees with 1B (the MUTCD does not govern private-road grade crossings); 8A.07's 6-ft downstream point = Figure 4C-9's 6-ft mark and the D definition used in Warrant 9; 7D.02 ¶3–¶4 STOP/STOP paddle = 6D.02 ¶3 Option; 7C.02 = 3B.20 ¶8 (SCHOOL may exceed one lane) and 3B.21 ¶1 (≥ 6 ft letters); 8B.03–8B.04 with 2B.18 (Crossbuck assemblies with YIELD/STOP); Table 8B-1 W10-1 36 dia. = Table 6H-1 W10-1; Figure 8B-2's "shall" notes take Standard authority from 8B.04 ¶1 (R21).
- Part 9 cross-check to make: Table 8B-1 note 3 → Table 9A-1 (path- and pedestrian-facing grade crossing signs).

**8B.04 (cont.)** ¶11 **Standard: YIELD/STOP on an existing Crossbuck support — bottom ≥ 4 ft above the curb top (or traveled-way edge).** ¶12 **Standard: new supports or separate supports — ≥ 5 ft rural, ≥ 7 ft where parking or pedestrians are likely.** ¶13 Guidance: separate YIELD/STOP in the Crossbuck's plane, nearer the road, ≥ 2 in apart. ¶14–¶16 Support: meanings — YIELD: prepare to slow and yield; certain commercial vehicles and school buses must stop at all crossings (49 CFR 392.10); **STOP: full stop not less than 15 ft short of the nearest rail**. ¶17 **Standard: at passive crossings, a ≥ 2-in retroreflective white strip on the back of each Crossbuck support from the sign/plaque to within 2 ft of the roadway edge (wrapping round supports ≥ 2 in diameter satisfies it), except ¶18** (Option: omitted on one-way streets and pathway/sidewalk crossings). ¶19 Option: red or white strip on the front below a YIELD/STOP. ¶20 **Standard: supports without a YIELD/STOP — a white strip on the front likewise.** ¶21 **Standard: Yield Ahead / Stop Ahead where 2C.35's criteria are met.**
**8B.05** STOP or YIELD without Crossbucks at LRT crossings (Guidance: only where a Diagnostic Team finds all of A–E — minor, low-volume, low-speed roads (thresholds by local agencies); LRT operator sight line to sound and stop; road user sight distance; signal warrants not met; no queuing on tracks). ¶2 **Standard: placement per 2B.18; advance signs per 2C.35.**
**8B.06** Grade Crossing Advance Warning. ¶1 **Standard: W10-1 on each highway in advance of every grade crossing, except A parallel-highway approaches < 100 ft with W10-2/3/4 on the parallel highway; B low-volume, low-speed roads over infrequently used spurs with a flagging person; C business/commercial areas with active systems; D where no effective display is possible; E LRT crossings without Crossbucks.** ¶2 **Standard: placement per 2C.04 and Table 2C-3.** ¶3 **Standard: with YIELD/STOP — Yield/Stop Ahead where 2C.35 is met, the W10-1 upstream of it, spacing per Table 2C-3.** ¶4 Option: left-side W10-1 on divided/one-way roads. ¶5 **Standard: parallel highway < 100 ft from the nearest rail → W10-2, -3 or -4 on each parallel-highway approach; no W10-1 required between tracks and parallel highway.** ¶6 **Standard: their placement per Table 2C-3's Intersection Warning guidelines at the through speed, measured from the intersection.** ¶7 Guidance: **≥ 100 ft → W10-1, not W10-2/3/4.** **Figure 8B-4** (note: W10-11 is a W10-3 modified for geometry).
**8B.07** DO NOT STOP ON TRACKS R8-8 (Guidance: where queues are likely to reach the tracks; right side, near or far; Option: both sides; left side on divided/one-way).
**8B.08** TRACKS OUT OF SERVICE R8-9 (Option: instead of Crossbuck/assembly until tracks are removed or covered). ¶2 **Standard: out-of-service tracks — devices and gate arms removed; signal heads removed, hooded or turned (except ¶3–¶4).** ¶3 **Standard: the I13-1 Emergency Notification sign retained and visible (unless the number is no longer in service).** ¶4 Guidance: physical-condition warnings (W10-5, W10-12) stay while the condition remains. ¶5 **Standard: R8-9 removed when tracks are removed/paved or service returns; I13-1 removed when tracks are removed/paved.**
**8B.09–8B.10** STOP HERE WHEN FLASHING (Option); STOP HERE ON RED (Option; Guidance: stop lines with adequate sight along the track).
**8B.11** EXEMPT plaques (Option where authorized by law: R15-3P white below the Crossbuck; W10-1aP yellow below W10-1–4; R15-3P alone at LRT crossings without either). Support: exempt vehicles need not stop unless a train approaches or view is blocked.
**8B.12–8B.15** LRT signs: Only Lane R15-4 series (Option; Guidance: on posts beside or overhead; preferential markings only with these signs); Do Not Pass R15-5 / R15-5a (Option; Guidance: just before the boarding area); No Motor Vehicles On Tracks R15-6 / R15-6a (Guidance: DO NOT ENTER for wrong entry to LRT-only streets; Option: on 3-ft flexible posts, 12×12 between tracks); ¶6 **Standard: R15-6 no smaller than 12 × 12 in**; Divided Highway with LRT R15-7 (Guidance: actual number of tracks); ¶3 **Standard: R15-7 at four-leg, R15-7a at T-intersections.**
**8B.16** Low Ground Clearance W10-5. ¶1 Guidance: where profiles can hang up long-wheelbase or low trailers. ¶2 **Standard: always with the W10-5P educational plaque, kept ≥ 3 years after installation (2A.09).** ¶3–¶7 Guidance: vehicle-specific word and exclusion signs (2B.45); AHEAD / NEXT CROSSING / USE NEXT CROSSING plaques at detour points; W13-1P if speeds must be below the limit; a signed detour. Support: AREMA 2023, AASHTO 2018; crossing inventories.
**8B.17** LRT Approaching blank-out W10-7 (Option; 2L applies). **8B.18** ANOTHER TRAIN COMING W10-16 (Guidance: Diagnostic Team decision; Option: with a Warning Beacon).
**8B.19** TRAINS MAY EXCEED 80 MPH W10-8 (Guidance: where trains may exceed **80 mph**, between W10-1 and the crossing on all approaches).
**8B.20** ¶1 **Standard: in each direction at every highway-rail crossing in a 49 CFR Part 222 Quiet Zone — a W10-9 sign or a W10-9P plaque directly below the W10-1–4.**
**8B.21** Storage Space W10-11 series (Guidance: where a Diagnostic Team finds inadequate design-vehicle storage between the intersection and the dynamic envelope; Option: W10-11b beyond the crossing). ¶4 **Standard: never a replacement for W10-1; in addition, on a separate post.**
**8B.22** Skewed Crossing W10-12 (Option; Guidance: symbol matches the skew). ¶3 **Standard: never a replacement for W10-1; in addition, on a separate post.**
**8B.23–8B.25** NO GATES OR LIGHTS, NEXT CROSSING, USE NEXT CROSSING, ROUGH CROSSING plaques (Option). **8B.26** LRT Station I3-8 (Option).
**8B.27** Emergency Notification System I13-1. ¶1 **Standard: on each approach to all highway-rail crossings and to highway-LRT crossings with gates or flashing-light signals.** ¶2 **Standard: rail — at least the USDOT crossing inventory number and the emergency telephone number.** ¶3 **Standard: LRT — a unique crossing identifier and the phone number.** ¶4 **Standard: ≥ 12 × 9 in; numerals and upper-case letters ≥ 1 in.** ¶5 **Standard: retroreflective.** ¶6 **Standard: white on blue, except ¶7** (Option: the 7-character inventory number black on white). ¶8–¶11 Guidance: on the right-side Crossbuck or signal mast (not equipment housings), parallel or perpendicular to the road, not blocked by gates; not obstructing devices or views; no larger than needed; conspicuous at stations. ¶12–¶14 Option: separate post (larger); yellow NOTICE header; more signs. **Figure 8B-5**.

## Chapter 8C — Markings (PDF 1050–…)
**8C.01** Support: Part 3 applies unless provided here.
**8C.02** Grade crossing pavement markings. ¶1 **Standard: on paved roads — an X, the letters RR, a no-passing zone marking (two-lane two-way roads with center lines, 3B.01), and transverse lines per Figures 8C-1, 8C-2.** ¶2 **Standard: in each approach lane on all paved approaches to highway-rail crossings with signals or gates, and at all other crossings where the posted or statutory speed is ≥ 40 mph, except ¶3–¶4.** ¶3 **Standard: not required below 40 mph if the Diagnostic Team finds other devices suitable.** ¶4 **Standard: not required in urban areas if the Diagnostic Team finds other devices suitable.** ¶5 **Standard: required in each lane at highway-LRT crossings with a Crossbuck.** ¶6 **Standard: identical markings in each multi-lane approach lane.** ¶7 **Standard: retroreflective white.** ¶8 Guidance: part of the X opposite the W10-1. ¶9–¶10 Option/Guidance: supplemental symbols, the downstream transverse line **≥ 50 ft** upstream of the stop/yield line. **Figures 8C-1** (note: not all devices shown; supplemental symbols ≥ 50 ft), **8C-2** (standard and narrow symbols).
**8C.03** Stop and yield lines. ¶1 Guidance: stop lines with STOP signs at passive crossings. ¶2 **Option: yield line or stop line with YIELD at passive crossings.** ¶3 Guidance: at right angles, **≥ 15 ft** before the nearest rail. ¶4 **Standard: stop lines at crossings with flashing-light signals, gates or traffic signals.** ¶5 Guidance: **~8 ft before the signals or gate (whichever is farther from the tracks), ≥ 15 ft from the nearest rail.** ¶6 Guidance: signal-controlled — ≥ 15 ft. ¶7 **Standard: with traffic signal control, placed so the approach's faces meet 4D.07 and 4D.08.**
**8C.04** ¶1 **Standard: no turn lane-use arrows between the crossing stop line and the tracks.** ¶2 Guidance: **≥ 100 ft upstream of the stop line or ≥ 20 ft beyond the farthest rail.**
**8C.05** Lines across crossings. ¶1 Guidance: edge, lane and center lines extend up to and across the crossing, except ¶3–¶5 (Option: omitted on/between rails per railroad requirements, abrupt profiles, or surfaces that will not hold markings). ¶2 Guidance: coordinate maintenance with the railroad. ¶6–¶8: RPMs and tubular markers if a Diagnostic Team recommends (railroad clearances). ¶9 **Standard: RPMs and tubular markers match the line color day and night.**
**8C.06** Dynamic envelope and Do Not Block. ¶1 Option. ¶2 **Standard: dynamic envelope markings per Part 3 — solid white, 4–24 in wide.** ¶3 Option: contrasting color or texture. ¶4 Guidance: outside the envelope, parallel to the nearest rail per railroad requirements, across the roadway, not perpendicular to the road at skews. ¶5–¶8 Option: cross-hatching within (supplement only) and adjacent no-stopping areas; along semi-exclusive trackways; continuous in mixed-use alignments; adjacent lane lines instead where outside the envelope. **Figures 8C-3 to 8C-5**.

Notes (read together)
- **Telephone numbers on signs**: 2A.04 ¶18 bans them "unless otherwise provided in this Manual for a specific sign"; the Emergency Notification sign **requires** one (8B.27 ¶2–¶3) — with 2F.19 ¶2 and 2I.14 ¶2 the provided exceptions.
- **Stop lines with YIELD**: 3B.19 ¶3 ("except as provided in Section 8C.03") ↔ 8C.03 ¶2 (stop or yield line with YIELD at passive grade crossings). The cross-reference checks.
- **15 ft from the nearest rail** is consistent: STOP meaning (8B.04 ¶16), stop/yield lines (8C.03 ¶3, ¶5, ¶6), TA-10 and TA-46 (grade crossing = 15 ft either side).
- Clean partitions: parallel highway **< 100 ft** → W10-2/3/4; **≥ 100 ft** → W10-1 (8B.06 ¶5, ¶7). Crossing pavement markings **≥ 40 mph** required; **< 40 mph** may be omitted by Diagnostic Team (8C.02 ¶2–¶3); both "posted or statutory" speed (not 85th-percentile).
- CHECK passed: 8B.04 ¶11–¶12 = Figure 8B-2 notes 2–3 and 2A.15 (5 ft / 7 ft); 8B.16 ¶2's ≥ 3-year educational plaque = 2A.09; Table 8B-1's I13-1 12×9 = 8B.27 ¶4; 8C.02 ¶1's no-passing marking = 3B.03 ¶2C; 8C.04 ¶2's 20 ft beyond the rail ↔ the pre-signal/queue provisions (4F.01 ¶13).
- MEAN several "Standards" hinge on Diagnostic Team determinations (8C.02 ¶3–¶4, 8A.03–8A.05) — an actor-dependent condition the graph must model as an input, not a computation.


## Chapter 8D — Flashing-light signals, automatic gates, traffic control signals (PDF 1057–…)
**8D.01** Support: active systems (exit gates, gates, flashing lights, signals, blank-out/variable signs); LRT speed = maximum permitted. ¶4 Option: post-mounted/overhead, with or without gates, by Diagnostic Team. ¶5 **Standard: meanings per UVC 11-701 and 11-703.** ¶6 **Standard: locations per Figure 8D-1.** ¶7 **Standard: with a curb — ≥ 2 ft from the curb face to the signal or upright gate arm; cantilevered signals ≥ 17 ft above the crown.** ¶8 **Standard: with a shoulder and no curb — ≥ 2 ft from the paved shoulder edge and ≥ 6 ft from the traveled way.** ¶9 **Standard: no curb or shoulder — ≥ 6 ft from the traveled way.** ¶10 Standard: track clearances per the railroad/transit and regulatory agency. ¶11 Guidance: **cabinets ≥ 30 ft from the highway edge and ≥ 25 ft from the nearest rail** where possible. ¶12–¶14 Guidance: pedestrian clearance; an escape route free of guardrail; same treatment both sides. ¶15 Option: guardrail in low-speed industrial areas. ¶16–¶17: coordinate signals and flashing lights at LRT crossings; shared overhead structures. **Figure 8D-1** (notes: median gates need counterweight clearance; **foundation ≤ 4 in above ground at crown elevation**, re-grade or adjust to keep **17 ft** clearance).
**8D.02** Flashing-light signals. ¶2 **Standard: the side assembly includes a Crossbuck and, for more than one track, an R15-2P.** ¶3 Guidance: plaque and Crossbuck low above the light backgrounds. ¶5 Option: bells at highway-rail crossings. ¶6 **Standard: two red lights in a horizontal line flashing alternately toward approaching traffic.** ¶7 **Standard: on the right of every approach, located per Figure 8D-1 unless visibility suffers.** ¶8 **Standard: two-way traffic — back-to-back signals each side of the tracks; multi-lane one-way and divided — both sides on the approach or overhead.** ¶9 **Standard: each lamp 35–65 flashes per minute, alternating, equal on-times; together lit the whole operating time.** ¶10 **Standard: 8-in or 12-in lenses** (¶11 Guidance: per 4E.02). ¶12–¶15 Guidance: **one pair per approach lane; ~30 in centre to centre; centre 8–9 ft above the crown; foundation ≤ 4 in above ground.** ¶16 **Standard: low voltage with storage batteries (primary or standby) and charging.** ¶17–¶19: extra signals toward parallel-road turns; toward turners where storage < design vehicle (Guidance); for pedestrians. ¶20 Standard: "lenses" do not limit optical units (LEDs allowed). ¶22–¶23 Option: overhead or cantilevered by Diagnostic Team; more units. ¶24 **Standard: no breakaway or frangible bases for overhead or cantilever supports.**
**8D.03** Automatic gates. ¶2 **Standard: drive mechanism and a fully retroreflective red-and-white gate arm with lights, extending across the approach lanes when down.** ¶3 **Standard: lights activate immediately on detection; the arm starts down ≥ 3 s after the flashing lights start, is horizontal ≥ 5 s before the train arrives, and stays down until the train clears.** ¶4 Standard: then ascends; lights stop. ¶5 **Standard: arms retroreflective both sides, vertical red/white stripes at 16-in intervals; front sheeting ≥ 4 in wide for the first 32 ft from the mast centre, ≥ 2 in beyond.** ¶6 Support: vertical or matching diagonal replacements (1B.03 ¶13). ¶7 **Standard: ≥ 3 red lights.** ¶8 **Standard: the tip light steady; the others flash alternately in unison with the flashing-light signals (left with left, right with right).** ¶10 **Standard: entrance arms fail-safe down.** ¶11–¶15 Guidance: **up in ≤ 12 s**; vertical when up; timing for large/slow vehicles; cover the approach so vehicles cannot go around without crossing the center line; **3.5–4.5 ft above the crown when down**; counterweights clear of the traveled way and sidewalks. ¶16–¶19: channelizing devices or medians (Option), **medians ≥ 60 ft** (Guidance); median counterweight width; cantilevered flashing lights.
**8D.04** LRT crossings. ¶1 **Standard: LRT speeds > 25 mph → active traffic control systems.** ¶2 **Standard: LRT speeds > 40 mph → including automatic gates.** ¶3 Option: gates at ≤ 40 mph by Diagnostic Team. ¶4 Guidance: ≤ 25 mph — active systems unless the Diagnostic Team finds Crossbuck Assemblies, signals, or STOP/YIELD alone adequate. ¶5 Guidance: **non-intersection, non-median crossings with LRT > 20 mph — no traffic control signals in lieu of flashing lights.** ¶7 **Standard: flashing-light signals at LRT crossings used by pedestrians or bicyclists include a bell or similar audible device.**
**8D.05** Exit Gate and Four-Quadrant systems. ¶1 Option: where gates and medians are not effective (Diagnostic Team). ¶2–¶3 Support: definitions (four-quadrant = full closure). ¶4–¶5 **Standard: series of gates with lighted striped arms across entrance and exit lanes; 8D.02 and 8D.03 apply.** ¶7 **Standard: entrance arms ≥ 3 s after lights, horizontal ≥ 5 s before arrival; exit gate timing by the Diagnostic Team; exit gate clearance time long enough for a maximum-length design vehicle to clear the minimum track clearance distance; down while the train occupies.** ¶8 Standard: ascend after clearing. ¶9 **Standard: exit arms fail-safe up, except ¶21** (Option: fail down with remote health monitoring). ¶10 **Standard: median islands where arms are offset enough to drive between them.** ¶11–¶20 Guidance: ≤ 12 s ascent; constant warning time; operating mode by Diagnostic Team (Timed — exit gate clearance time; Dynamic — vehicle intrusion detection, independent per direction); exit clearance time in minimum warning time; **Dynamic mode near intersections that queue into the clearance distance**; back-up power and gate-down interlock with interconnected signals; remote health monitoring. ¶22–¶23: medians ≥ 60 ft. **Figure 8D-2**.
**8D.06** Wayside horns. Option per 49 CFR 222. ¶2 **Standard: where the locomotive horn is not sounded, per Appendix E to 49 CFR 222.** ¶3 Guidance: 8D.01 clearances; **≥ 15 ft from the nearest track centre**; not blocking views of flashing lights.
**8D.07** Rail traffic detection. ¶1 **Standard: active devices actuated by rail traffic detection.** ¶2 **Standard: detection circuits fail-safe as far as practical.** ¶3 **Standard: flashing-light signals operate ≥ 20 s before arrival, except ¶4** (Option: shorter where all rail traffic runs < 20 mph and a person on the ground stops road users). ¶5 Option: more time by study. ¶6 Guidance: uniform notice for varying speeds; suppress activations from station stops and switching.
**8D.08** ¶1 **Standard: traffic control signals not instead of flashing-light signals at highway-rail crossings, except ¶2** (Option: industrial crossings and places where **maximum train speed ≤ 10 mph**). ¶4 **Standard: Part 4 applies where signals are so used.**
**8D.09** Preemption. ¶1–¶2 Support: combined systems; FRA Technical Bulletin S-12-01 and Safety Advisory 2010-02 (external). ¶3 **Guidance: crossings with flashing-light signals ≤ 200 ft from a signal, PHB or EVHB — rail preemption (4F.19) unless the Diagnostic Team decides otherwise.** ¶4 Guidance: > 200 ft — consider queue detection, queue cutters, blank-out signs (factors listed). ¶5 Guidance: joint determination of preemption operation and timing. ¶6 **Guidance: a signal installed ≤ 200 ft from a passive crossing → an active warning system to provide preemption** (unless the Diagnostic Team decides otherwise). ¶7 Guidance: interconnected flashing lights get gates unless the Diagnostic Team decides otherwise. ¶10 **Standard: preemption type and timing information given to the railroad/transit agency.** ¶11 **Standard: if preemption is provided, the normal sequence is preempted to give a track clearance interval before the train arrives** (unless the Diagnostic Team determines otherwise). ¶12 Option: reduced time where switching or restarts occur. ¶13 **Standard: with flashing-light signals, any highway signal faces within 50 ft of any rail are preempted; the Diagnostic Team sets their indications (4F.19) to avoid conflicts.** ¶14 Guidance: flashing yellow beacons within 50 ft considered. ¶15 **Standard: a supervised, fail-safe preemption interconnection; the train's approach de-energizes it or sends a fail-safe message (IEEE 1570-2002); preemption held while the warning system is active; with gates, not ended until the gates start upward.** ¶16–¶21 Support: advance / simultaneous preemption; maximum preemption time (right-of-way transfer + queue clearance + separation time); supervised interconnection. ¶22–¶23 Option: double-break circuits. ¶24–¶31 Guidance: passive crossings with detection treated as active; protected left turns during track clearance where needed; preemption time analysis; gate-descending-on-vehicle analysis; extended warning time with simultaneous preemption; variability analysis with advance preemption. ¶32 **Standard: with gates and downstream greens during track clearance, the greens are not ended until the gate toward the intersection is fully lowered.** ¶33–¶34 Support: gate-down circuitry vs timing correction (mutually exclusive). ¶35 **Standard: with gate-down circuitry, a broken or not-lowered gate never ends track clearance before the train enters.** ¶36 **Standard: with timing correction, a timing circuit caps the interval between advance preemption and warning activation when trains decelerate.** ¶37–¶39 Guidance: advance preemption with exit gates; analyse stop-and-restart operations; immediate re-service of repeated preemption calls. ¶40 **Standard: flashing-mode dwell does not begin until the train has entered the crossing.** ¶41 **Standard: the Diagnostic Team sets priority between boat and train preemption calls; the US Coast Guard or waterway authority invited.**
**8D.10** Movements prohibited during preemption. ¶1 Guidance: **signalized intersections within 100 ft of a crossing** — prohibit permissive turns toward the crossing (blank-out R3-1a/R3-2a), steady red arrows to protected turns, red to through movements toward the crossing. ¶2 Option: all movements toward the track even beyond 100 ft. ¶5 Guidance: LRT-activated blank-out turn prohibitions where A no active warning, B turns across tracks within 100 ft or in the median, C no separate red-arrow face. ¶6 **Standard: preemption blank-out signs display only while a preemption signal is received or the gate is activated.** (2L applies.)
**8D.11** Pre-signals. ¶1 Guidance: where clear storage distance < design vehicle length near a signal. ¶2 **Guidance: flashing lights without gates within 200 ft of a signalized intersection → a pre-signal.** ¶3 Support: pre-signals generally < 200 ft; queue cutters (8D.12) beyond. ¶5 **Standard: pre-signals show steady red during the track clearance interval.** ¶6 **Standard: no pre-signal greens while the crossing lights flash red.** ¶7 Guidance: visibility-limited downstream faces. ¶8–¶9 Option: extended green by detection; offset timing. ¶10 Guidance: queue clearance time for a maximum-length design vehicle. ¶12 Guidance: separate turn-lane pre-signal faces where turn storage crosses the tracks and turn greens differ from through greens. ¶13 **Standard: separately controlled adjacent lanes — faces show CIRCULAR RED, CIRCULAR YELLOW, straight-through GREEN ARROW; no turn GREEN ARROWs; no CIRCULAR GREEN.** ¶14 Option: CIRCULAR GREEN where all lanes are controlled together. ¶15 **Standard: separate turn-lane faces exclusively for the turn lane, visibility-limited, or with R10-10b / R10-10c signs.** ¶16–¶17 Support: the pre-signal approach is separate for 4D.05–4D.08. ¶18 Guidance: R10-6 at the pre-signal stop line. ¶19 **Standard: No Turn on Red sign at the pre-signal where turns on red would otherwise be permitted downstream.** ¶20–¶22 Option: R8-8; faces upstream or downstream; on the flashing-light structure.
**8D.12** Queue cutter signals. ¶1 Support: independent of the downstream signal. ¶2–¶3 Option: where downstream queues regularly reach the crossing; modes A actuated, B non-actuated, C variable. ¶4 Support: **non-actuated generally 200–400 ft from the bottleneck; actuated beyond 400 ft** (< 200 ft → pre-signal). ¶5 **Standard: separately controlled lanes — CIRCULAR RED, CIRCULAR YELLOW, straight GREEN ARROW; no turn arrows; no CIRCULAR GREEN.** ¶6–¶8 Option. ¶9 Guidance: R10-6. ¶10 Option: R8-8. ¶11–¶14 Guidance: detector placement; hold red while queued; intermediate queues; coordination. ¶15–¶16 Option: flashing when queues are not expected; detector-extended red. ¶17 **Standard: interconnected with the flashing-light signals.** ¶18 **Standard: no greens while the crossing lights flash red.** ¶19 **Standard: on preemption — immediate steady CIRCULAR YELLOW (4F.17) then steady CIRCULAR RED until the train clears.** ¶20 **Standard: actuated mode — straight GREEN ARROW except on queue actuation (finish minimum green, yellow, red) or preemption; return to green when no queue and no preemption (after minimum red).** ¶21 **Standard: fail-safe design; if a queue detector fails, flashing CIRCULAR RED until restored.** ¶23–¶24: separate turn-lane faces (Guidance) exclusively controlled, visibility-limited or with R10-10b/10c signs (Standard).

Notes (read together)
- **A fourth flash-rate range**: grade crossing flashing-light signals **35–65/min per lamp, alternating** (8D.02 ¶9) — alongside arrow boards 25–40, beacons 50–60, sequential lights 55–75. Each a Standard for its device.
- **Alternating vs simultaneous reds**: grade crossing signals alternate (8D.02 ¶6, ¶9); intersection control beacons and Stop Beacons flash horizontal pairs **simultaneously "to avoid being confused with grade crossing flashing-light signals"** (4S.02 ¶5, 4S.05 ¶2). Consistent by design.
- **MEAN COMPUTED gate timing (Standards)**: flashing ≥ 20 s before arrival (8D.07 ¶3); gate starts down ≥ 3 s after flashing begins; horizontal ≥ 5 s before arrival (8D.03 ¶3, 8D.05 ¶7); ascent ≤ 12 s (Guidance).
- **Clean partitions**: LRT speed ≤ 25 mph (Guidance: active) / > 25 (Standard: active) / > 40 (Standard: gates) (8D.04); rail distance thresholds 50 ft (signal faces preempted, 8D.09 ¶13), 100 ft (turn prohibitions, 8D.10), 200 ft (preemption, pre-signals, 8D.09 ¶3, ¶6, 8D.11 ¶2), 200–400 / > 400 ft (queue cutters, Support).
- CHECK passed: gate-arm stripes 16 in (8D.03 ¶5) = 6L.03 ¶11, 6L.04 ¶4, 4Q.02 ¶13; gate height 3.5–4.5 ft (8D.03 ¶15) ≈ movable bridge warning gate top ~4 ft (4Q.02 ¶14); pre-signal and queue-cutter indications (8D.11 ¶13, 8D.12 ¶5) = 4F.01 ¶5D and ¶13 (straight GREEN ARROW; allowed combinations); 8D.09 ¶3 = 8A.02 ¶5 (200 ft); 8D.10 ¶6 with 2L blank-out provisions; 8D.02 ¶10–¶11 lens sizes with 4E.02.

**8D.12 (cont.)** ¶25–¶26 Support: the queue-cutter approach is separate for 4D.05–4D.08; 4D.07 turn-face positioning applies. ¶27 Support: queue cutters ≠ transit queue-jump signals.
**8D.13** Warning Beacons or LED-enhanced signs. ¶1 Option: **Warning Beacons (4S.03) or LEDs within legend, symbol or border (see 2A.12)** on grade crossing warning signs, continuous or rail-activated. ¶2–¶3 Support: physical-condition signs usually continuous; W3-4 + WHEN FLASHING or "TRAIN WHEN FLASHING" only when the condition exists. ¶4 **Standard: if a Warning Beacon or LEDs … activated by rail traffic accompany a warning sign with WHEN FLASHING (on the sign or a plaque), activation is by a supervised fail-safe preemption interconnection (8D.09).** ¶5 Support: fail state = operating with no train. ¶6 Option: run on after the train for the queue. ¶7–¶8 Guidance: start before the crossing lights by the travel time to the stop line; back-up power on AC.
**8D.14** Traffic signals at LRT crossings. ¶2 **Standard: Part 4 and 8D.08–8D.12 apply, incl. interconnection.** ¶3 **Standard: a bell or similar audible device with traffic signals at LRT crossings used by pedestrians or bicyclists.** ¶4–¶5 Guidance: coordinate emergency preemption with LRT; wide-median LRT — protected far-side left-turn clearance. ¶6–¶8 Option: non-conflicting movements during LRT phases; signals with gates at intersections if warranted (4C); **non-intersection LRT crossings < 25 mph — signals in lieu of flashing lights.** ¶12 Guidance: separate turn faces across the tracks. ¶13 **Standard: separate turn faces toward the crossing show steady red during the LRT's approach/passage.**
**8D.15** LRT signals. ¶1–¶2 Option: at mixed-use crossings/intersections for LRT phases; for bus phases. ¶3 **Standard: separate LRT and intersection control are interconnected; the LRT phase not ended until the LRT vehicle clears.** ¶4 **Standard: standard R/Y/G indications controlling LRT are not visible to motorists, pedestrians or bicyclists (4D.06).** ¶5 Guidance: else the Figure 8D-3 LRT indications. ¶6 **Standard: Figure 8D-3 LRT indications are white.** ¶7 Option: clustered. ¶8 Guidance: **≥ 3 ft from the nearest highway signal face.** **Figure 8D-3** (notes: **all rectangular bar indications shall be white**; go indications may flash to prepare to stop).

## Chapter 8E — Pathway and sidewalk grade crossings (PDF 1076–1087)
**8E.01** Support: pathway vs sidewalk crossings (Figure 8E-1); Chapter 3C governs crosswalks across mixed-use LRT at intersections.
**8E.02** Guidance: consider users' ability to detect trains; a Diagnostic Team including the pathway/sidewalk agency; re-align skewed sidewalk crossings. Support: flangeway gaps 2.5 in (LRT) / 3 in (railroad); consistent alignment; refuge areas; sidewalks outside vehicular device areas (Figure 8E-2); ADA 2010.
**8E.03** Signs and markings. ¶1 **Standard: standard shape, legend and color.** ¶2 **Standard: minimum sizes per "the shared-use path column in Table 9A-1".** ¶3 Guidance: nothing protruding into the crossing; railroad clearances. ¶4 Guidance: **post-mounted signs ≥ 4 ft** (Figure 9A-1). ¶5–¶6 Guidance: **overhead above pathways ≥ 8 ft; ≥ 10 ft with equestrians.** ¶7 **Standard: overhead above sidewalks ≥ 7 ft.** ¶8 Guidance: **devices below 8 ft — ≥ 2-ft lateral offset** from pathways. ¶9 Guidance: advance warning where bicyclists or skaters use the path (Figure 8E-3). ¶10–¶12: W10-12; LOOK (R15-8) on a separate post farther from the path than the Crossbuck (Guidance). **Figure 8E-2** (note: **a ≤ 25 ft → flashing lights may be omitted**, 8E.07).
**8E.04** Stop lines, edge lines, detectable warnings. ¶1 Guidance: stop lines at pathway crossings (Option at sidewalks) where surfaces retain markings. ¶3 Guidance: full width, **≥ 2 ft before gates, counterweights, flashing lights or Crossbuck; ≥ 12 ft from the nearest rail** (without detectable warnings). ¶4–¶5 Option: edge lines across. ¶7 **Standard: detectable warnings at pathway crossings where pedestrians are permitted and at sidewalk crossings, full width.** ¶8–¶9 Guidance: **≥ 2 ft deep**; just beyond or part of the stop line; downstream edge **≥ 2 ft before devices and ≥ 12 ft from the nearest rail.** ¶10 Guidance: **tracks ≥ 30 ft apart → detectable warnings at the refuge area.** ¶11–¶12: LRT — may be < 12 ft (Option), **≥ 6 ft** and ≥ 2 ft before devices (Guidance). **Figures 8E-3, 8E-4**.
**8E.05** Crossbuck Assemblies. ¶1 **Standard: passive pathway/sidewalk crossings whose nearest edge is > 25 ft from the nearest warning device's centre — a Crossbuck Assembly on each approach** (measured perpendicular to the traveled way). ¶2–¶4 Option: ≤ 25 ft; omitted at station crossings; no back strip. ¶5 **Standard: YIELD/STOP bottom ≥ 4 ft where the lateral offset is ≥ 2 ft; ≥ 7 ft where < 2 ft.** ¶6 **Standard: minimum lateral offset 0 ft for sidewalks, 2 ft for pathways.** **Figure 8E-5** (notes: ≥ 7 ft sidewalks with < 2-ft offset, ≥ 4 ft with ≥ 2 ft, ≥ 4 ft for pathways).
**8E.06** Channelizing devices (Support: swing gates opening away from tracks, self-closing; latches operable by all; maze fencing passable by wheelchairs and dismounted cargo/tandem bicycles). ¶7 Guidance: **PUSH TO EXIT (I13-2) on the track side and DO NOT ENTER (R5-1) on the other side of swing gates.**
**8E.07** Active systems. ¶1 **Standard: pathway/sidewalk-LRT crossings on semi-exclusive alignments with LRT > 25 mph → active systems (except ¶5).** ¶2 **Standard: > 40 mph → with automatic gates (except ¶5).** ¶3 **Standard: active systems include flashing-light signals on each approach.** ¶4 Guidance: a bell. ¶5 Option: **omit flashing lights and bells within 25 ft of an equipped active device.** ¶6 Option: extra units facing the back side. ¶7 Guidance: refuge between parallel tracks and roadway, with pedestrian signal features. **Figure 8E-7** (notes: mounting ≥ 4 ft beside, **≥ 7 ft above sidewalks (shall)**, ≥ 8 ft above pathways; 2-ft pathway offset; none for sidewalks).
**8E.08** Signals. ¶1 Support: pedestrian signal heads are not appropriate where rail traffic does not stop. ¶2 **Standard: no WALK/DONT WALK pedestrian signal heads at pathway or sidewalk grade crossings, except ¶3** (Option: where LRT movement is controlled by traffic signals or LRT signals). ¶4 **Standard: horizontal flashing lights, units ≥ 4 in; 4-in units ~16 in apart with backgrounds ≥ 8 in.** ¶5 **Standard: alternating, 35–65 flashes per minute per lamp** (= 8D.02 ¶9). ¶6 **Standard: bottom of lights ≥ 4 ft above the path.** ¶7–¶8 Option/Standard: between tracks — **≥ 1 ft**. ¶9–¶11 Guidance: add pedestrian gates where behavior or notice is insufficient; flashing lights with Crossbuck and audible device on semi-exclusive LRT where sight distance is insufficient.
**8E.09** Automatic pedestrian gates. ¶1 Option. ¶2 **Standard: tracks with trains permitted at ≥ 80 mph → pedestrian gates and an escape area with swing gates and fencing, unless the Diagnostic Team finds other treatments more appropriate.** ¶3 Guidance: emergency escape routes. ¶4 **Standard: at least one steadily lit red light on the arm while active (except ¶6).** ¶5 **Standard: additional lights in pairs, flashing alternately with the other units.** ¶6 Option: omit within 25 ft of an equipped highway crossing. ¶7–¶8 Guidance: **arm 3–4 ft above the path when down**; full-width coverage with swing gates. ¶9 **Standard: sidewalks between the road edge and a vehicular gate support — vehicular gate location, placement and height (8D.03).** ¶10–¶11: separate mechanism (Guidance) or interlocked auxiliary arm (Option). ¶12–¶13: hanging bar **≤ 26 in** (Guidance).
**8E.10** Multiple tracks (Guidance: avoid pedestrians waiting between tracks).

**PART 8 complete.**

# PART 9 — TRAFFIC CONTROL FOR BICYCLE FACILITIES

## Chapter 9A — General (PDF 1088–…)
**9A.01** Support: scope (roadways, separated bikeways, shared-use paths; micromobility where allowed); bicycles are vehicles on roadways; FHWA Bikeway Selection Guide (external); bicycles generally allowed without bicycle-specific devices. Guidance: maintenance; a designated maintaining agency.
**9A.02** Signing. ¶2 **Standard: bicycle signs per the Manual's standard shape, legend and color.** ¶3 **Standard: all signs on bikeways retroreflective (incl. shared-use paths, bike lanes).** ¶4 **Standard: signs serving bicyclists and others — height and lateral placement per 2A.15 and 2A.16.** ¶5–¶8 Guidance: **shared-use paths — ≥ 2 ft lateral from the path edge, ≥ 8 ft vertical over the whole path; post-mounted ≥ 4 ft; bicycle-only signs not confusing others; more clearance for equestrians or maintenance vehicles.** ¶9 **Standard: signs applying to motorists and bicyclists — conventional-road sizes in Tables 2B-1, 2C-1, 2D-1, 8B-1.** ¶10 **Standard: bicycle-only and shared-use-path signs — Table 9A-1 minimums, used only for bicyclist applications.** ¶11–¶12 Option: larger; **18 × 18 in diamonds applicable only to path or separated-bike-lane users.** ¶13 Guidance: design identical to street signs except size. **Figure 9A-1**.
**9A.03** Markings. Guidance: word/symbol/arrow markings in bikeways; low-slip materials. ¶4 **Standard: markings needing night visibility retroreflective unless lighting suffices.** ¶5 **Standard: colors, widths, patterns, symbols, arrows per Part 3.** ¶8–¶9 Guidance: **no RPMs on bike lanes or shared-use paths**, nor immediately adjacent to their travel paths.
