Notes (read together)
- **QUESTION FOR THE ENGINEER (Medium, with proposed reading) — 2A.12 vs 8D.13 ¶4**: 2A.12 (Standard, no exception clause): "LED units shall not be used within the legend or border of a sign in conjunction with the phrase WHEN FLASHING …". 8D.13 ¶4 (Standard) sets an interconnection requirement "if a Warning Beacon **or LEDs within the legend, symbol, or border** … is activated by the approach … of rail traffic in conjunction with a warning sign that includes the legend WHEN FLASHING". **Proposed reading: 2A.12 governs** — 8D.13 ¶1 permits LEDs "(see Section 2A.12)", importing its limits, and ¶4 is a condition, not a permission; rail-activated WHEN FLASHING signs therefore use Warning Beacons. The cost of this reading is that ¶4's LED branch never applies; an engineer could instead read 8D.13 as a grade-crossing exception. 4P.03 ¶3 (no flashing LEDs in W3-8's legend) supports the 2A.12 reading.
- **8E.03 ¶2 "shared-use path column" vs Table 9A-1's "Off Roadway" column** — settled by Table 9A-1 note 1 (Off Roadway includes shared-use paths).
- **CHECK passed (planned Part 4 ↔ Part 9)**: Bicycle Signal signs 12×21 post-mounted / 24×36 overhead (Table 9A-1) = 4H.03 ¶2.
- **CHECK passed (planned Part 8 ↔ Part 9)**: Table 8B-1 note 3 → Table 9A-1: R15-1 24×4.5 (half of 48×9), R15-2P 13.5×9 (half of 27×18), R15-8 18×9 (= Table 8B-1 minimum), W10-1 36 dia. on roadways (= Table 8B-1).
- Thresholds with different comparison forms: W10-8 where trains may **exceed** 80 mph (8B.19, > 80); pedestrian gates where trains are permitted at **80 mph or higher** (8E.09 ¶2, ≥ 80). At exactly 80 mph gates are required but W10-8 is not.
- Clean partitions in 8E: ≤ 25 ft / > 25 ft (Crossbuck Assemblies, 8E.05; flashing-light omission, 8E.07 ¶5, Figure 8E-2); LRT > 25 / > 40 mph (8E.07 = 8D.04).
- Stop/detectable-warning distances differ by user: vehicles ≥ 15 ft from the nearest rail (8C.03); pathways/sidewalks ≥ 12 ft (railroad), ≥ 6 ft (LRT) (8E.04).
- CHECK passed: 8E.08 ¶5 flash rate = 8D.02 ¶9; 8E.03 ¶7 (7 ft over sidewalks) = 6F.02 ¶6, 2A.15; 8E.03 ¶4–¶8 and 9A.02 ¶5–¶6 agree (4 ft, 8 ft, 2 ft); Table 9A-1 note 2 = 9A.02 ¶9.


## Chapter 9B — Regulatory signs (PDF 1093–1105)
**9B.01** STOP and YIELD. ¶1 **Standard: STOP signs on bicycle facilities where bicyclists must stop.** ¶2 **Standard: YIELD signs where bicyclists have an adequate view of conflicting traffic and must yield.** ¶3 **Standard: no STOP or YIELD with a bicycle signal face (4H).** ¶4 Option: larger on paths. ¶5 Guidance: path-only STOP/YIELD shielded from roadway users. ¶6–¶8 Guidance: priority at path/road intersections by relative speed, volume and importance (speed not the sole factor); least-restrictive control; **no STOP where YIELD suffices** (2B.06, 2B.08). **Figure 9B-1**.
**9B.02** EXCEPT BICYCLES R3-7bP. ¶2 Guidance: where study or judgement supports exemption. ¶5 **Standard: never to exempt bicyclists from STOP, YIELD, Yield/Stop Here to/for Pedestrians signs, or a signal indication.** ¶6 **Standard: not with a regulatory sign on a post that carries a STOP or YIELD sign.** ¶7 **Standard: below the sign it supplements.** **Figure 9B-2**.
**9B.03** R3-8 series with bike lanes. ¶1 Option. ¶3 Guidance: complex approaches — separate R3-5/R3-7 signs and bicycle guide signs. ¶4 **Standard: the bike-lane portion shows only the lane's relationship to other lanes — no extensions, buffers, turn boxes or bike boxes.** ¶5–¶7 **Standard: no counter-flow bike lanes, shared-lane marking symbols or shared-use paths on these signs.** ¶8 **Standard: the bike lane in white on black (Figure 2B-4); no green to match colored pavement.**
**9B.04** Bike Lane R3-17 and plaques. ¶1 **Standard: only with marked bicycle lanes (9E.01, 9E.06, 9E.07).** ¶2 Guidance: at the start and before the end. ¶3 Option: periodic intervals. ¶6–¶7 Option: BIKE LANE plaque above Optional or Mandatory Movement Lane Control signs.
**9B.05** BEGIN RIGHT TURN LANE YIELD TO BIKES R4-4 (Option where motorists weave across bike lanes into a right-turn lane; Guidance: not where bicyclists must move left at a lane drop).
**9B.06** Bicycle WRONG WAY R5-1b with RIDE WITH TRAFFIC R9-3cP (Option; Guidance: the plaque only with and directly below R5-1b).
**9B.07–9B.08** NO MOTOR VEHICLES R5-3; selective exclusion (R5-6, R9-3, R9-13 to R9-16; R5-10a combined) — Options.
**9B.09** ¶1 **Standard: parking/standing/stopping restrictions in bike lanes by 2B.53–2B.55 signs or R7-9 / R7-9a.** **9B.10** Back-In Parking R7-10 (Option; Figure 9B-3).
**9B.11** Bicycles Use Ped Signal R9-5 (Option; R10-15/15a/12b reminders; Guidance: near the crossing).
**9B.12** Bicycles Yield to Peds R9-6 (Option). ¶2 **Standard: with a yield line, the sign and marking adjacent.** ¶3 **Standard: not as a corridor-wide regulation without a yielding point.** ¶4 **Standard: not with STOP, YIELD or pedestrian yield/stop signs.**
**9B.13** Shared-Use Path Restriction R9-7 (Option with a solid white separating line, 9E.13; Guidance: each two-way mode's area wide enough).
**9B.14** R9-20 (Support: UVC "substandard width lane"; Option: no bike lane or usable shoulder and lanes too narrow to share; to inform that bicyclists may occupy the lane; shared-lane markings in addition or instead, 9E.09).
**9B.15** Bicycle Passing Clearance R4-19. Option: where law or ordinance defines a clearance (value adjustable). ¶3 **Standard: not in jurisdictions without a defined passing clearance.** ¶4 Guidance: not where bike lanes or usable shoulders exist.
**9B.16** Bicycles Use Shoulder Only R9-21 (Option; Guidance: freeways/expressways only, after entrance ramps where the full shoulder resumes).
**9B.17** Freeways. ¶1 **Standard: Bicycles Must Exit R9-22 in advance of a freeway/expressway segment prohibited to bicycles, before the exit or intersection preceding it** (Figure 9B-4). ¶2 Option: below a post-mounted Exit Direction sign. ¶3 **Standard: then a No Bicycles R5-6 downstream where the prohibition begins; never below the Exit Gore sign.** ¶4 Option: ON FREEWAY R5-10dP plaque.
**9B.18** Two-stage turn boxes. Support: when required (A–C). ¶3 **Standard: required use → the R9-23 series.** ¶4 **Standard: R9-23 or R9-23a in advance and at least one R9-23b or R9-23c at the intersection.** ¶5–¶6 **Standard: R9-23b near side; R9-23c far side.** ¶7–¶8 Option: guide series (9D.13) where optional; D3-1 below. **Figure 9B-5**.
**9B.19** Bicycle jughandles R9-24 to R9-27 series (Options by movement; RIGHT for left-side facilities; Guidance: brief independent alignments; not for two-stage turn boxes). **Figure 9B-6** (green pavement optional).
**9B.20** Actuation signs (Options: R10-4, R10-24, R10-26 where not controlled by pedestrian signals; R10-25 with in-roadway or other warning lights; R10-22 at detector markings, 9E.15; Guidance: near the crossing / beside the marking).
**9B.21** Left Turn Yield to Bicycles R10-12b (Option for unexpected bicycle movements).
**9B.22** Bicycle Signal signs. ¶3 **Standard: R10-40/40a (mandatory) or R10-41 series (optional movement) require turns, permit otherwise-prohibited turns, require straight-through, or indicate allowed movements on GREEN BICYCLE.** (4H.03.)
**9B.23** LOOK R15-8 (Option on the Crossbuck support or nearby at path/bikeway crossings; Guidance: not on a Crossbuck Assembly with YIELD/STOP on the same support). **9B.24** Other 2B and 8B signs (Option).

## Chapter 9C — Warning signs and object markers (PDF 1106–1109)
**9C.01** W1 series (Guidance: for unexpected path direction changes; **W1-1 to W1-5 ≥ 50 ft before the change**). **Figure 9C-1** (note: FYG allowed; plaque background matches its sign — should).
**9C.02** W2 series (Option; Guidance: where path-approach visibility is limited; **not where the path approach is STOP-, YIELD- or signal-controlled**).
**9C.03** W8-10 Bicycle Surface Condition and BUMP, DIP, PAVEMENT ENDS (Option).
**9C.04** W11-1, W11-15 (Support; Option: TRAIL X-ING plaque). ¶4–¶5 Guidance: AHEAD or XX FEET in advance; Table 2C-3 placement. ¶6 **Standard: at the crossing location, supplemented with a W16-7P arrow plaque.** ¶7–¶8: FYG option; one background color systematically within a zone (Guidance).
**9C.05** EXCEPT BICYCLES warning plaque W16-20P (Option; e.g., DEAD END, NO OUTLET).
**9C.06** Two-Way Bicycle Cross Traffic W16-21P. ¶1 **Standard: when used, below a STOP or YIELD sign.** ¶4 Guidance: with counter-flow or two-way facilities against motorists' usual scanning.
**9C.07** W9-5 Bicycle Lane Ends / W9-5a Bicycles Merging (Option; Guidance: not where the lane drops at an intersection and resumes just beyond; Option: R9-20/shared-lane markings downstream; W16-2aP distance).
**9C.08** Other bicycle warnings (Option: W5-4a, W7-5; W11-1 with IN ROAD/IN STREET plaques; Guidance: **≥ 50 ft in advance**; Part 6 signs for TTC on bikeways).
**9C.09** ¶1 **Standard: obstructions in a shared-use path marked with retroreflective material or object markers (2C.70).** ¶2 Option: Type 1–3 markers on adjacent objects; smaller Type 3 for path-only viewing (Table 9A-1).

## Chapter 9D — Guide and service signs (PDF 1110–…)
**9D.01** Bicycle Destination and Distance signs. Support: purposes A–E; 2D.36 and 2D.43 for shared signs. ¶4 **Standard: not a substitute for vehicular destination signs when the message is also for motorists.** ¶5–¶7 Option: multiple destinations with arrows and distances; D1-1/D1-1a, D3-1, D2 series instead or as well where they apply to both. ¶8 Guidance: separate directions clearly. ¶9 **Guidance: < ½ mile → distance to the nearest ¼ mile; < ¼ mile → in feet to the nearest 50 ft.** ¶10 Option: nearest 1/10 mile. ¶12 **Standard: right arrows at the extreme right; left or up arrows at the extreme left; distance numerals to the right of names.** ¶13 **Standard: a bicycle symbol next to each destination or group, except ¶14.**

Notes (read together)
- **MEAN COMPUTED bicycle destination distances (9D.01 ¶9–¶10)**: d < ¼ mile → feet, nearest 50 ft; ¼ ≤ d < ½ mile → nearest ¼ mile; finer 1/10 mile allowed (Option); fractions per 2A.08.
- CHECK passed: 9B.03 ¶8 (white on black, not green) with Figure 2B-4 and 3H.05 (green pavement's own meaning); 9B.01 ¶3 (no STOP/YIELD with bicycle signal faces) consistent with 2B.06 ¶8's limits on STOP signs at signals; 9B.17 ¶3 (No Bicycles not below the Exit Gore sign) with 2E exit gore rules; 9C.04 ¶6 (W16-7P at the crossing) = 2C.54 / 2C.55 pattern; 9C.09 = 2C.70; 9B.12 ¶4 and 9B.02 ¶5 keep bicycle-specific signs from overriding STOP/YIELD.
- Advance placement on paths is a flat ≥ 50 ft (9C.01 ¶2, 9C.08 ¶3), not Table 2C-3's speed-based distances (which 9C.04 ¶5 uses for road-facing Bicycle/Trail Crossing signs).

**9D.01 (cont.)** ¶14 Option: an oversized bicycle symbol as the top line. ¶15 **Standard: with a left arrow at the extreme left, the bicycle symbol to the right of the arrow.** ¶16–¶18 Guidance: symbol left of the legend with right arrows; horizontal or vertical arrows; equal widths in assemblies. ¶19 **Guidance: no travel times on Bicycle Destination signs** (¶20: bicyclist times vary). **Figure 9D-1** (3 sheets).
**9D.02** Bike Route Guide D11-1 / D11-1c (Option: unnumbered, unnamed routes; repeated; D11-1c word legends; BEGIN/END plaques; Guidance: no travel times). **Figure 9D-2**.
**9D.03** BIKE ROUTE plaque D11-1bP (Option: with D11-1c, D11-11 on paths, or D3-1; bicycle symbol on the plaque). ¶3 **Standard: no bicycle symbol on a Street Name sign.** ¶4 **Standard: a BIKE ROUTE plaque with a Street Name sign on a network street → one of D11-1, D11-1c, M1-8/8a, M1-8b/8c or M1-9 used systematically to establish the route.** ¶5 **Standard: no replicas of route signs on the plaque.** ¶6 Option: widths may differ. **Figure 9D-3** (note: D1-2 signs larger to be legible to motorists).
**9D.04** Numbered bikeway systems (Support: USBRs numbered by AASHTO; local by authorities). Guidance: coordination; avoid duplicate numbers; minimal overlaps; continuous routing. ¶7 **Standard: preference US, then State, then county/local — highest priority at the top or left of the assembly.** ¶8 **Standard: concurrent systems per Figure 9D-4.** ¶9–¶11: route signs and plaques; at direction changes; on roads, paths, separated bikeways (Option). **Figure 9D-4**.
**9D.05** M1-8 / M1-8a (Option). ¶2 **Standard: M1-8 — route designation, white legend and border on green.** ¶3 **Standard: M1-8a — the same plus a pictograph or words on the upper portion.** ¶4 **Standard: on a roadway, include a bicycle symbol.** ¶5–¶6 Guidance: **pictograph ≤ 2 × numeral height, within a green border, width ⅔–9⁄10 of the sign; bicycle symbol ≥ ¼ of the panel height.**
**9D.06** M1-8b / M1-8c. ¶1 **Standard: on roadways — green background, white border, words identifying the route (or words and a pictograph/bicycle symbol), except ¶2** (Option: words omitted where the pictograph clearly shows a bicycle). ¶5–¶6 Option: named routes; **green background or white border may be omitted on shared-use paths**. ¶9 Guidance: statewide policy for independent organizations.
**9D.07** M1-9. ¶1 Guidance: coordinated State submittals to AASHTO. ¶2 **Standard: green legend and border on white, with the AASHTO-assigned designation.**
**9D.08** Route sign assemblies. ¶2 Guidance: bikeway route assemblies independent of highway route assemblies. ¶3 **Standard: route signs for bikeways not on guide signs or overhead.** ¶4–¶5 Option: locations by bicycle operation; auxiliary plaques. ¶6–¶8 Guidance: M2-1P, M3, M4 above; M5, M6 below; colors match (except M4-8P). ¶9 **Guidance: plaques ≥ 12 × 6 in (words), ≥ 12 × 9 in (arrows).** ¶10 **Standard: junction/cardinal/alternative plaques above, arrow plaques below, on the same assembly.** ¶11–¶12 Option: enlarged plaques not wider than the route sign; **route sign and plaques combined on a single sign as a guide sign.** ¶14 **Standard: an assembly = route sign + plaques identifying the route and direction.** ¶15 Guidance: assemblies on all approaches where numbered routes intersect. ¶16 **Standard: left-intersecting routes at the left/top-or-centre; right at right/bottom; straight-through at centre (horizontal) or top (vertical).** ¶17 **Standard: Junction assembly = M2-1P + the intersected route's sign.** ¶19 **Standard: Advance Route Turn assembly = route sign + advance turn arrow or word plaque (+ cardinal direction if needed), before turns needed to stay on the route.** ¶21 **Standard: Directional assembly = cardinal direction (if needed) + route sign + directional arrow.** ¶22–¶30 Guidance: uses A–D; not for through movements alone; near right corner; Confirming and Reassurance assemblies and spacing. ¶23 Option: M6-3P through.
**9D.09** Bicycle parking, sharing station, lockers D4-3, D4-4, D4-4a (Options; Guidance: D4-4 for regulated systems, D4-3 for unregulated). ¶8 **Standard: no promotional advertising, logos or public-private partnership identification (1D.07).**
**9D.10** Reference location signs D10 series. Option: along paths; tenth-mile intermediates. ¶4 Guidance: integer signs show ".0" when intermediates are used. ¶5 **Guidance: mounting ≥ 2 ft — not governed by 9A.02's heights.** ¶6–¶7 Option: one side, back to back; **moved up to 50 ft**. ¶8 Guidance: **omit if not within 50 ft**. ¶9 Guidance: **zero at the south and west termini.**
**9D.11** Mode-specific directional D11-11 to D11-14 (Options; white-on-green arrow plaques; D11-11 + D11-1bP instead of D11-1 on paths; combined with exclusion signs). **Figure 9D-5**.
**9D.12** Destination guide signs for paths D11-10 series. ¶2 **Standard: retroreflective where bicycles are allowed.** ¶3 Guidance: independent assemblies. ¶4 Option: other SHS mode symbols. ¶5 **Standard: symbols only for allowed modes supported by regulatory signs; no facility/activity symbols.** ¶7–¶9 Guidance: rectangular; **≤ 3 destinations** (2D.06); minimal abbreviations. ¶11 **Standard: arrows and priority per 2D.08 and 2D.36.** ¶12 **Standard: destinations in mixed case with initial capitals; other words upper case.** ¶13 **Standard: lettering per 2D.04, except ¶15** (Option: another style if a study shows equal or better legibility). ¶16 **Standard: no alternative lettering within the highway right-of-way or where the path crosses a street.** ¶17–¶18 Option/Standard: pictographs ≤ 2 × upper-case letter height. ¶19 **Standard: no logos, commercial graphics or advertising.** ¶20–¶22: identification markers (Option), **smaller than the sign and not resembling a TCD (Standard)**, **≤ ⅕ of the sign's area** (Guidance). ¶23 **Standard: white legend and border on green or brown, except ¶26** (Option: other colors off the highway right-of-way). ¶24 **Standard: no color coding or pictographs to distinguish destination types; color coding by square/rectangular panels left of the area named, ≤ 2 × letter height.** ¶27 **Standard: never red, orange, yellow, purple (or fluorescent versions), FYG or fluorescent pink backgrounds.** ¶28 Option: phone numbers, internet and e-mail addresses, URLs. ¶29 **Standard: only the path authority's direct contact or emergency contact; no advertising contacts.** **Figure 9D-6**.
**9D.13** Two-stage turn box guide signs D11-20 series (Option). ¶5 **Standard: D11-20 in advance of the intersection.** ¶6 **Standard: D11-20a on the far side.** ¶7–¶8 Option: another D11-20 near side; D3-1 below. **Figure 9D-7** (note: **R10-11 series required**, 9E.11).
**9D.14** General service signs on bikeways (Option). ¶2 **Standard: signs for bicyclists and others — Table 2I-1 sizes.** ¶3 Option: reduced for bicyclists only.

## Chapter 9E — Markings (PDF 1127–…)
**9E.01** Bicycle lanes. ¶2 **Standard: longitudinal markings and bicycle symbol or word markings define bicycle lanes.** ¶3 Guidance: first marking at the start, after major intersections, periodic. ¶4 Option: arrows downstream of the symbol. ¶5 Option: R3-17 signs, not at every marking. ¶7 **Standard: no bicycle symbol, BIKE LANE word or arrow in a shoulder.** ¶8 **Standard: no roadway portion both a shoulder and a bicycle lane.** **Figures 9E-1, 9E-2**.
**9E.02** Intersection approaches. ¶1 **Standard: no through bicycle lane right of a right-turn-only lane or left of a left-turn-only lane, except ¶2** (Option: with bicycle signal indications, 4H). ¶4 Guidance: **bike lane markings stop ≥ 100 ft before a dropped-lane turn lane and resume on its other side.** ¶5 Guidance: no optional through-turn lane beside a mandatory turn lane with a through bike lane. ¶6 **Standard: a bike lane between general-purpose lanes on an approach has at least one symbol/word and one arrow marking.** ¶7 **Standard: no bike lane marked within a general-purpose lane (dotted or otherwise).** ¶8 Option: shared-lane markings in narrow turn/general lanes. ¶9 **Standard: signalized turn lanes accommodate through bicycles only if the turn always runs with the adjacent through movement.** ¶11 Guidance: dotted bike-lane line where turning vehicles cross.

Notes (read together)
- **9D.08 ¶3 vs ¶12 (minor, settled)**: ¶3 (Standard) bars bikeway route signs *on* guide signs or overhead; ¶12 (Option) lets a route sign and its plaques be *made as* a single guide sign. ¶3 concerns placing route markers on highway guide signs; ¶12 concerns fabricating the bicycle route assembly as one sign.
- **Telephone numbers / URLs**: 9D.12 ¶28–¶29 is a further specific-sign exception to 2A.04 ¶18 (with 2F.19, 2I.14, 8B.27), limited to authority or emergency contacts.
- **CHECK passed (Part 4 ↔ Part 9)**: 4H.01 ¶1B's reference to "Paragraph 1 of Section 9E.02" = 9E.02 ¶1–¶2 (through bike lane right of a right-turn-only lane only with bicycle signal indications).
- 9D.10 ¶5 names its own exception to 9A.02's mounting heights (2 ft for reference location signs).
- CHECK passed: 9D.08 ¶9 plaque sizes = Table 9A-1 (12×6, 12×9); 9D.12 ¶8 three destinations = 2D.06; 9D.12 ¶27 forbidden colors = the regulatory/warning color meanings of 1D.05 / Table 2A-2; 9D.09 ¶8 and 9D.12 ¶19 = 1D.07 (no advertising); 9D.04 ¶7 route priority parallels 2D route-assembly priority; 9D.14 ¶2 = Table 2I-1.

**Figures 9E-2 to 9E-4**. **9E.02 (cont.)** ¶12–¶17 Support/Option: buffer-separated and separated lanes at intersections — shifted toward (visibility) or away (queue space, isolated scanning) from the general lane; staggered stop lines (Figure 9E-7 D, E). ¶18 Option: **mixing zones** where a mandatory turn lane meets a separated/buffered bike lane. ¶19 **Standard: mixing zones only where the bike lane is one-way in the adjacent lane's direction.** ¶20 **Standard: mixing zones with yielding areas have yield markings where entering traffic yields to bicyclists.** ¶21 **Standard: where the mixing zone continues to the intersection, shared-lane markings and turn arrows in the lane.** ¶23 Guidance: buffered or separated space where the bike lane re-establishes. **Figure 9E-5**.
**9E.03** Extensions through intersections. Support: width and color in 3B.11; care next to crosswalks for pedestrians with vision disabilities. ¶4–¶5 Option: symbol/arrow/words; green pavement (3H.06). ¶6 **Standard: no shared-lane or chevron markings in bike lanes or their extensions.** ¶7 **Standard: extensions use dotted lines.** ¶9–¶10 Guidance: extend buffered/separated lanes; two lines. ¶11 **Standard: next to a crosswalk — two dotted lines; the crosswalk's transverse line (or high-visibility pattern edge) is never one side of the extension.**
**9E.04** Driveways (Option: solid or dotted lines; symbols; green background for the symbol).
**9E.05** Circular intersections. ¶1 **Standard: no bike lanes in the circulatory roadway of unsignalized circular intersections with entry/exit conflicts, except ¶4** (Option: separated bike lanes). ¶2 Guidance: **bike lane markings stop ≥ 100 ft before the crosswalk (or yield line, or circulatory edge).** ¶3 Guidance: **bicycle crossings ≥ 20 ft from the circulatory roadway.** Support: AASHTO Bike Guide 2012; FHWA-SA-22-017.
**9E.06** Buffer-separated bike lanes. ¶3 **Standard: the buffer marked with a solid white line along both edges where crossing is discouraged, except ¶5** (Option: without lines where adjacent markings define it). ¶4 Guidance: breaks for driveways, transit stops, parking. ¶6 **Standard: no through buffer-separated lane right of a mandatory right-turn lane (left of left-turn), except ¶7** (Option: **only with a bicycle signal face and phasing and signing eliminating the conflict**). ¶8 Guidance: **buffer ≥ 3 × the line width.** ¶9 Guidance: **2–3 ft buffers — chevrons or diagonals.** ¶10 Option: **< 2 ft — diagonals or none.** ¶11 **Standard: diagonals slant away from adjacent motor traffic.** ¶12 Guidance: **spacing ≥ 10 ft.** ¶14 **Standard: buffers > 3 ft — chevrons or diagonals required.** ¶15 Guidance: extension markings. **Figure 9E-6**.
**9E.07** Separated bike lanes (vertical elements: tubular markers, islands, parked vehicles). ¶5–¶6 Guidance: R3-17 signs; door buffer beside parking. ¶7–¶9 Option: one- or two-way; one or both sides or median. ¶11 **Standard: edge and lane line colors per Chapter 3A.** ¶12 **Standard: directional arrows downstream of the symbol or word marking.** ¶13 **Standard: turns on red prohibited across separated bike lanes while bicyclists may proceed.** ¶15 **Standard: buffer edges marked with solid lines.** ¶16 **Standard: buffers ≥ 2 ft (incl. with tubular markers) — chevrons or diagonals, unless islands, dividers or parking occupy most of the buffer.** ¶17–¶18 Guidance: spacing ≥ 10 ft; crosswalks consistent with the adjacent lanes. **Figure 9E-7** (notes: "Diagonal or chevron markings **shall be used** if buffer width is 2 feet or greater for separated bicycle lanes"; parking as the vertical element).
**9E.08** Counter-flow bike lanes. ¶3 Guidance: bicyclists on their right with opposing traffic on the left. ¶4 **Standard: at the roadway edge with speed limits ≤ 30 mph — double yellow center line, painted or raised median island, or physical separation.** ¶5 **Standard: speed limits ≥ 35 mph — a buffer (3B.25), painted or raised median island, or physical separation.** ¶6–¶7 Guidance: extensions at intersections; not between a general lane and a parking lane. ¶9 **Standard: turns onto such roadways regulated by Movement Prohibition signs with EXCEPT BICYCLES, not ONE WAY signs.** ¶10 **Standard: DO NOT ENTER at egress points with EXCEPT BICYCLES beneath.** ¶11 **Standard: intersection controls (STOP, YIELD, signals) also face counter-flow bicyclists.** ¶12 **Standard: at signals — bicycle signalization (9F) facing them, with actuation.** ¶14 Guidance: W16-21P below crossroad STOP signs. **Figure 9E-8**.
**9E.09** Shared-lane marking. ¶2 Option uses A–F (door zone, narrow lanes, alert, passing, wrong-way, mixing zones). ¶3 Guidance: **not on roads with speed limits ≥ 40 mph.** ¶4 **Standard: never in A shoulders; B bike lanes or their extensions; C lanes with LRT; D weave transitions into mandatory turn lanes across bike lanes; E two-stage turn boxes; F bicycle boxes; G shared-use paths or their crossings; H physically separated bikeways.** ¶5 **Standard: no green background.** ¶6 Option: black contrast background. ¶7–¶10 Guidance: **centres ≥ 12 ft from the curb with parking; ≥ 4 ft without parking where the outside lane < 14 ft; spacing 50–250 ft; first ≤ 50 ft after an intersection.** ¶12 Guidance: lane centre with R9-20. ¶13–¶14 Option: where bike lanes cannot continue; in narrow turn lanes. **Figure 9E-9**.
**9E.10** Circular intersections (Option: in the circulatory roadway; Guidance: lane centre).
**9E.11** Two-stage turn boxes. ¶4 **Standard: locations A–D (between through movement and parallel crosswalk / pedestrian movement; inside the bike facility where parallel traffic does not run; at T-intersections by the pedestrian facility).** ¶5 **Standard: at least one bicycle symbol and one arrow.** ¶6 **Standard: turn arrow for one-way bike lanes; through arrow for two-way bikeways.** ¶7 **Standard: bounded on all sides by solid white lines.** ¶8 **Standard: symbol precedes the turn arrow for one-way bikeways.** ¶9 **Standard: passive bicycle detection in the box where the second-stage phase is actuated.** ¶10 Guidance: size by judgement (volumes, geometry). ¶11–¶12 Option/Standard: **green pavement, if used, covers the entire box.** ¶13 **Standard: where turns on red would cross the box — a full-time No Turn on Red for the crossroad approach** (2B.60). **Figures 9E-10, 9E-11**.
**9E.12** Bicycle box. ¶1 Option. ¶2–¶5 Guidance: by judgement or study where green-interval turning conflicts are demonstrated; consider other treatments; bike lane on the approach; not contiguous with a crosswalk; stop line at its downstream end. ¶6 **Standard: ≥ 10 ft deep; at least one bicycle symbol.** ¶7 **Standard: moving a motor-vehicle stop line upstream → recalculate and reprogram yellow and red clearance intervals (4F.17).** ¶8 **Standard: boxes across more than one lane → countdown pedestrian signals for the crossing movement, displaying the countdown without actuation.** ¶9 **Standard: no turns on red from the lane with the box.** ¶11 Guidance: countdowns for single-lane boxes where late-red arrivals occur. ¶12 Option: green pavement. **Figure 9E-12**.

Notes (read together)
- **DATA (chunks) — Figure 9E-7 notes**: the two identical notes ("Diagonal or chevron markings shall be used if buffer width is 2 feet or greater for separated bicycle lanes") were merged and interleaved with a third note, producing "shall be be used" and a truncated chunk ("… markings shall"), both filed as **9E.08 Standard** paragraphs. Printed page (read from the image): "shall be used", in Figure 9E-7 (with 9E.07). Same failure as the Figure 2G-32 notes.
- **Chevron/diagonal buffer thresholds differ by facility (both Standards)**: buffer-separated lanes — required only > 3 ft (9E.06 ¶14; Guidance 2–3 ft, Option < 2 ft); separated lanes — required ≥ 2 ft (9E.07 ¶16, Figure 9E-7 notes). Clean partitions within each.
- **Bike lane right of a right-turn-only lane**: conventional — allowed with bicycle signal indications (9E.02 ¶2); buffer-separated — only with a bicycle signal face **and** phasing/signing eliminating the conflict (9E.06 ¶7). The specific rule is stricter.
- **Mixing zones vs the shared-lane exclusions**: 9E.02 ¶21 requires shared-lane markings where a mixing zone runs to the intersection; 9E.09 ¶4D bans them in weave transitions into turn lanes across bike lanes, and ¶2F allows them in mixing zones. The markings belong in the shared portion, not the weave transition.
- **Counter-flow separation**: ≤ 30 mph (double yellow, island, separation) / ≥ 35 mph (buffer, island, separation) — no gap for posted limits (multiples of 5).
- **Bicycle box countdowns (9E.12 ¶8)**: required with multi-lane boxes, and displayed **without actuation** — a specific Standard beyond 4I.04 ¶1's > 7-s change-interval rule.
- CHECK passed: 9E.05 ¶3's 20 ft = 3C.09 ¶3 and 7B.03 ¶9; 9E.11 ¶13 = Figure 9D-7 note (R10-11 required); 9E.03 ¶6 and 9E.09 ¶4B agree (no shared-lane markings in bike lanes); 9E.12 ¶7 applies 4F.17; 9E.07 ¶13 and 9E.12 ¶9 NTOR through 2B.60; Chapter 9F exists (PDF 1154), so 9E.08 ¶12, 4C.01 ¶16 and 4H.01 ¶7 references resolve.

**9E.12 (cont.)** ¶13 **Standard: green pavement, if used, fills the full limits of the bicycle box.** ¶14 Support: EXCEPT BICYCLES under R10-6 to exempt bicyclists from the advance stop line (9B.02). **Figure 9E-12** (2 sheets; NTOR sign per 2B.60).
**9E.13** Shared-use paths. ¶1 Option: solid yellow center line where passing is not permitted, broken where permitted (paths wide enough for two lanes). ¶2 **Guidance: broken lines on paths — 3-ft segments, 9-ft gaps.** ¶3–¶4 Option: solid white line separating user types (with R9-7); smaller words/symbols; half-size arrows (3B.20). ¶5 **Standard: crosswalk markings where a shared-use path crosses a roadway (3C).** ¶6–¶7: parallel bicycle and pedestrian crossing markings (Option); green bicycle crossing areas at high-volume crossings (Guidance). **Figures 9E-13, 9E-14**.
**9E.14** Bicycle route pavement markings (Option). ¶2 **Standard: only on shared-use paths, separated or buffer-separated lanes; never in standard bike lanes or shared lanes.** ¶3–¶5 Guidance: systematic with signs; elongated; low-slip. **Figure 9E-15**.
**9E.15** Bicycle detector symbol (Option: symbol, WAIT HERE FOR GREEN words, R10-22; green background per 3H.06). **Figure 9E-16** (word markings optional).
**9E.16** Obstructions (Guidance: Figure 9E-17 markings at path/bikeway obstructions incl. bollards; white markings around roadway drain grates). **Figure 9E-17** (**L = W·S, W = offset (ft), S = bicycle approach speed (mph); raised obstructions add 1 ft: L = (W + 1)·S**).
**9E.17** Raised devices (Support: 3I; inflexible devices beside bicyclists without a buffer create collision risk). ¶3 Option: channelizing devices per 3I, not preventing lawful turning merges. ¶4–¶5 Guidance: tubular markers; consider post and base collision potential. ¶7 **Standard: no green in channelizing devices or their retroreflective elements to supplement green pavement.** ¶8 Guidance: in the buffer, **≥ 1 ft from the bike-lane line**.

# APPENDICES (PDF 1156–1162)
**Appendix A1 — Congressional actions** (read from the pages): PL 102-240 §1077 (1991) — States/locals may install STOP or YIELD at passive rail-highway crossings with ≥ 2 trains per day; PL 102-388 §406 (1992) — retroreflectivity minimums for markings and signs on all public roads, and a standard for roads needing center/edge lines; PL 104-59 §205 (1995) — no metric sign mandates; §306 — **motorist call boxes on NHS rights-of-way: sponsorship logos approved by the agency; ≤ the box size or 12 × 18 in on the box; ≤ 12 × 30 in on the identification sign; no more often than 1 per 5 miles; ≥ 20 % outside urbanized areas > 50,000**; call boxes consistent with the MUTCD; **§353(a) the Oregon December 1991 experimental signs deemed compliant; §353(b) a red, white and blue center line on Main Street, Bristol, Rhode Island, deemed compliant**; PL 115-141 Div. L §125 (2018) — Interim Approval IA-5: **Option: Series E(modified)-Alternate for names on freeway/expressway guide signs; Standard: only for names of places, streets and highways on those signs; mixed case; SHS letter design; lower-case loop height 84 % of the initial capital; interline spacing ≥ 96 % of the initial capital; edge spacing per 2E.13; signs enlarged; numerals in names in Series E(modified); other legend in Series B–F; no other use.**
**Appendix A2 — Metric conversions** (read from the page; Rev. 1 marks): English units govern throughout. **Table A2-1** inches → mm at a rounded **25 mm per inch** (0.25→6 … 3→75 … 12→300 … 36→900, 120→3000; note: 1 in = 25.4 mm). **Table A2-2** feet → m at **~0.3 m per foot** (1→0.3 … 10→3 … 100→30, 330→100, 1,000→300, 3,000→900; note: 1 ft = 0.3048 m). **Table A2-3** miles → km (0.25→0.4, 0.5→0.8, 0.6→1, 1→1.6, 2→3.2, 3→4.8, 5→8, 10→16, 15→25, 70→110). **Table A2-4** mph → km/h: 3→5, 10→16, 15→20, 20→30, 25→40, 30→50, **35→60, 40→60**, 45→70, 50→80, 55→90, 60→100, **65→105**, 70→110, 80→130 (note: 1 mph = 1.609 km/h).

Notes (read together)
- **Appendix A2 values are rounded equivalents, not exact conversions.** Tables A2-1/A2-2 use 25 mm per inch and 0.3 m per foot (their notes give 25.4 and 0.3048). Table A2-4 mostly rounds to the nearest 10 km/h, so **35 and 40 mph both map to 60 km/h**, but 65 mph → 105 (nearest 5) and 10 mph → 16 (nearest 1) break the pattern. Since the Manual's dimensions are all English (A2 preamble), the graph should not convert through these tables; if a conversion is ever needed, use the exact factors in the notes. (Low.)
- **Statutory deemed-compliance (A1 §353)**: two site-specific items are compliant by law regardless of Part 2/3 provisions (the Oregon 1991 experimental signs; Bristol, RI's red-white-blue center line). Out of scope for general rules, but the graph must not flag them as violations.
- Appendix A1 carries its own Option and Standard (Series E(modified)-Alternate), which are binding like any other Standard; they match 2D.04 and 2E.13 cross-references.
- CHECK passed: 9E.13 ¶2 path broken lines (3/9 ft) vs 3A.04's 10/30 ft on roadways — a specific rule for paths; 9E.17 ¶7 (no green devices) with 3H.06 and 3I.01; 9F.01 ¶2 = 4C.01 ¶16; 9F.03 = 4D.03; Figure 9E-17's taper L = W·S matches the form of Part 3's ≥ 45-mph taper formula but with bicycle approach speed (a separate context).
- MEAN COMPUTED Figure 9E-17 taper: L = W·S, or (W + 1)·S for raised obstructions (S = bicycle approach speed).

# ENGINEER RULINGS — session of 22 September 2026

## Ruling 1 (answers open item 7, part a) — which speed
**The maximum of the speeds the provision names.** Engineering basis given by the engineer: design to
the maximum risk factor; higher speed = higher risk. This applies whether or not the manual ranks them:
- Where the manual already says "whichever is higher" (3B.12 ¶8, 3B.13 ¶2, 4M.01 ¶5) — unchanged.
- Where the manual joins them with "or" and does not rank (Table 2C-3 "Posted or 85th-Percentile Speed";
  Tables 6B-2 and 6B-4 "Posted speed, off-peak 85th-percentile speed prior to work starting, or the
  anticipated operating speed"; 4D.05 ¶7; 4D.06 ¶17; 4J.01 ¶6–¶7; 6N.12 ¶6; 6N.15 ¶1B; 4S.02 ¶7) —
  **take the maximum of those named.**
- Where the manual names only one basis (Table 4D-2: 85th-percentile only; 4E.02 ¶5C: posted/statutory
  limit or operating speed; 4F.16 ¶4 and 8C.02 ¶2: posted or statutory only) — **that one basis only.**
  The rule picks the maximum among the speeds a provision names; it never adds a basis the provision
  does not name.

## Ruling 2 (answers open item 7, part b) — values between rows
**Round to a row; the direction is per table, as the engineer stated:**
- **Table 6B-4 (TTC taper formulas)**: a speed below 45 mph uses the "40 mph or less" branch,
  L = W·S²/60. This closes the 41–44 mph gap for measured or anticipated speeds. The formula still
  uses the actual S (e.g. S = 43, W = 12 → L = 12 × 43² ÷ 60 = 370 ft; the ≥ 45 branch would give 516 ft).
- **Table 4D-2 (signal visibility sight distance)**: **round up** to the row above — e.g. an
  85th-percentile speed of 43 mph uses the 45-mph row, 460 ft. (Engineer's decision of 22 Sep, after
  the direction check below: the longer visibility is the safer side.)
- **Table 4C-7 (high-occupancy buses)**: round **up** to the next row — e.g. 1 % uses the 2 % row;
  by the same rule 3 % uses 4 %, 5 % uses 6 %.

Recorded as the engineer's decision. Item 7 is closed except for the two exact-boundary cases below.

**Default direction (generalised from the two rulings, to be vetoed if wrong):** round **up** to the
next row, because that is the safer side; the one stated exception is Table 6B-4, where the engineer
ruled that any speed below 45 mph uses the "40 mph or less" branch. Tables not named above
(2C-3, 3B-1, 4C-8, Figures 4C-9/4C-10) therefore round up until the engineer says otherwise.

**Settled by the engineer (22 Sep):** Table 4D-2 rounds **up** — the longer visibility distance.

## Ruling 3 (22 Sep) — the two exact-boundary cases, both approved by the engineer
- **Exactly 600 ft of tangent** between reverse curves (6H.31 ¶1 "< 600 ft" → Double Reverse Curve;
  TA-7 and TA-36 "> 600 ft" → two Reverse Curve sign sets). **Ruled: two separate sign sets.**
- **Exactly 200 ft between adjacent grade crossings** (8A.08: "≤ 100 ft" one crossing; "> 100 and
  < 200 ft" added devices; "> 200 ft" separate crossings). **Ruled: the middle band (added signs and
  devices, with added clearance time for active systems).**

**Item 7 is now closed.** Ruling 1 (maximum of the speeds a provision names, never a basis it does not
name), Ruling 2 (round up by default; Table 6B-4 below 45 mph uses the "40 mph or less" branch;
Table 4C-7 rounds up), Ruling 3 (the two boundary values above).


## Ruling 4 (22 Sep) — occupancy on preferential-lane guide signs (was question 2)
**Settled from the figures, at the engineer's prompting.** Chapter 2G shows no occupancy legend on any
green guide-sign field. Figure 2G-17's guide signs are green with **white header panels** (HOV EXIT,
HOV LANE, HOV EXITS) carrying the white-on-black diamond; Figure 2G-19 shows a green EXPRESS LANE
ENTRANCE sign whose **white header panel** reads "Toll Pass OR / HOV 2+ ONLY", exactly as 2G.19 ¶3
requires ("the header panel shall be modified to a regulatory format … with a black legend on a white
background"). The 2+/3+/4+ legends elsewhere in the chapter are all on Figure 2G-1, the **regulatory**
sign plate. **Recorded: the occupancy requirement goes in a regulatory-format panel (black on white)
within the guide sign assembly, never on the green guide-sign field; the diamond remains the guide
sign's indication that an occupancy requirement exists (2G.10 ¶11).** Figure 2G-17 note 3 is read as
referring to that panel.

**Confirmed during the figure pass (22 Sep):** Figure 2G-17's own guide signs, examined at high zoom,
carry a **white header panel with a black legend ("HOV EXIT", "HOV LANE") and the black-and-white
diamond square at its left**, above a green body holding the route shield, cardinal direction and
arrow. The occupancy level added by note 3 therefore goes **in that white header panel** (e.g.
"HOV 2+ EXIT"), which is exactly the black-on-white regulatory panel that **2F.12 ¶11** prescribes for
regulatory messages incorporated within a guide sign. The green field carries no occupancy, so
2G.10 ¶11 stands. 2F.12 ¶10 states the principle generally: regulatory or warning messages may be
combined with guide signs "using plaques, header panels, or rectangular regulatory or warning panels
incorporated within the guide signs, as long as the proper legend and background colors are
preserved".

## Ruling 5 (22 Sep) — bar-pair crosswalk spacing (was question 3)
**Settled by Figure 3C-1**, which states the spacing for all three high-visibility types in the same
form: longitudinal bar "12 inches to 60 inches (not to exceed 2.5 times width of longitudinal bar)";
ladder, the same; **bar pair "24 inches to 60 inches (not to exceed 2.5 times width of bar pair)"**.
So 3C.08 ¶4's "or 2.5 times …" is the same second cap the sister sections state in a separate sentence.
**Recorded: both limits apply and the lesser governs; for bar pairs 2.5 × pair width is 60–90 in, so the
60-in limit governs in practice.** Note the types are distinct: a bar pair counts as **one** longitudinal
element for the three-element minimum (3C.05 ¶4), which is why its lower bound is 24 in, not 12.

## Ruling 6 (22 Sep) — LEDs and WHEN FLASHING at grade crossings (was question 4)
**No conflict after checking the related sections.** The manual ties the WHEN FLASHING plaque to a
**Warning Beacon** in every place it speaks:
- 2A.12 ¶13 (Standard, no exception): "LED units shall not be used within the legend or border of a sign
  in conjunction with the phrase WHEN FLASHING … (see Item E in Paragraph 1 of Section 4S.03 for the use
  of Warning Beacons to indicate when a regulatory or warning message is in effect)."
- 2C.35 ¶8 (Option): a Warning Beacon **or** yellow LEDs within the border may be used with a
  BE PREPARED TO STOP sign; ¶10 (Guidance): when **the Warning Beacon** is interconnected with a signal
  or queue detection system, the sign should be supplemented with the WHEN FLASHING plaque.
- 2C.55 ¶14–¶15: a Warning Beacon may be used with any Non-Vehicular Warning sign; the WHEN FLASHING
  plaque may be used with any such sign "that is supplemented with a Warning Beacon".
- 4P.03 ¶3 (Standard): no flashing LED units in the legend or border of the W3-8 RAMP METERED WHEN
  FLASHING sign — the same rule applied to a named sign.
**Recorded: 8D.13 ¶4 names both devices because that is its section's scope ("Warning Beacons or
LED-Enhanced Warning Signs at Grade Crossings"); it requires the supervised fail-safe rail
interconnection for whichever device is used, and for a WHEN FLASHING sign the only lawful device is the
Warning Beacon. 2A.12 ¶13 stands; nothing in 8D.13 is dead text.**

**All four questions from the reading are now closed.**

## Method note (22 Sep) — figures
The engineer identified a real gap: during the reading, **tables were read from the page image but
figures were read from the text layer and notes only**. Questions 2, 3 and 4 were all answerable from
the drawings (white header panels; the Figure 3C-1 spacing callouts; the sign plates). A figure pass is
now planned: every figure to be viewed as an image, checked against the section it serves, with findings
logged the same way as the reading.
